对斯特拉·门多萨来说，这次会面是她一生中最要紧、最性命攸关的一场。她特意推迟行期，指望格雷戈里·彭尼对自己的义务能慷慨些看待，尽管对他会在钱这个头等大事上回心转意，她本没抱多少指望。而如今，奇迹似的，他软了下来；电话里的口吻近乎友善；还取笑她的种种保留，以及她答应要采取的防范；到头来，她把天然的恐惧压了下去。
他接待她的地方不在书房，而在正上方那间大套间。套间更深，因为它把楼下小客厅占的那片地面也收了进来；可说到陈设，却大不相同。斯特拉只进过一回他那所谓的"辉煌厅"（The Splendid Hall）。它的空旷幽暗吓着过她，他为款待她而安排的那一场，是她记忆里不愉快的一桩。
大屋铺着厚厚的黑地毯，地面上不见一件家具，毫无生趣。四下里摆着长沙发椅，墙上挂满东方式的帷幔；屋子两侧各立着一排猩红的柱子，仅有的光亮来自三盏罩得严严实实的黑色吊灯，在地毯上投下一汪汪黄光，却给屋子添不了一丝欢快。
彭尼盘腿坐在一张绸面软榻上，眼睛盯着一个土著姑娘的旋转腾挪——她在屋内暗角三名板着脸的汉子弹出的古怪琴声中扭动盘旋。格雷戈里穿一套火红的绸睡衣裤，他那呆滞的目光和兽类的嘴，把斯特拉想了解的她这位恶友的一切都告诉了她。

===Original===
Sir Gregory Penne was no less and no more than a slave to his appetites. Born a rich man, he had never known denial of his desires. Money had grown to money in a sort of cellular progression, and when the normal pleasures of life grew stale, and he was satiated by the sweets of his possessions, he found his chiefest satisfaction in taking that which was forbidden. The raids which his agents had made from time to time in the jungles of his second home gave him trophies, human and material, that lost their value when they were under his hand.
Stella, who had visions of becoming mistress of Griff Towers, became less attractive as she grew more complaisant. And at last her attraction had vanished, and she was no more to him than the table at which he sat.
A doctor had told him that drink would kill him﻿—he drank the more. Liquor brought him splendid visions, precious stories that wove themselves into dazzling fabrics of dreams. It pleased him to place, in the forefront of his fuddled mind, a slip of a girl who hated him. A gross bully, an equally gross coward, he could not or would not argue a theme to its logical and unpleasant conclusion. At the end there was always his money that could be paid in smaller or larger quantities to settle all grievances against him.
The native who had conducted Stella Mendoza to the apartment had disappeared, and she waited at the end of the divan, looking at the man for a long time before he took any notice of her. Presently he turned his head and favoured her with a stupid, vacant stare.
“Sit down, Stella,” he said thickly, “sit down. You couldn’t dance like that, eh? None of you Europeans have got the grace, the suppleness. Look at her!”
===Chinese===
格雷戈里·彭尼爵士不多不少，正是自己欲望的奴隶。生而富家，他的欲求从不曾遭过拒绝。钱像细胞分裂一般钱生钱；待到人生的寻常乐趣变了味，占有的甜头也吃腻了，他最大的快意便落在了攫取禁脔上。他的代办们在他第二故乡的丛林里时不时来一场劫掠，给他带来人与物的战利品——一到他手里，便都失了价值。
斯特拉曾一心做着格里夫塔楼女主人的梦，可她越是柔顺，便越失魅力。到头来魅力荡然无存，她于他，同他手边那张桌子再无分别。
有医生告诫过他，酒会要他的命——他偏喝得更凶。酒浆给他带来壮丽的幻象，带来一个个珍贵的故事，自己织成炫目的梦之锦缎。把一个恨他的纤瘦姑娘摆在他那醉醺醺头脑的最前台，这叫他心满意足。他是个十足的恶霸，又是个同样十足的懦夫，不能、也不肯把一个题目推到那合乎逻辑却不愉快的结论上去。到头来，反正总有他的钱，多多少少掏出来，便可摆平世人对他的怨怼。
把斯特拉·门多萨领进套间的那名土著已经退下了，她候在软榻一头，望着那个男人望了半晌，他才理会她。过了一会儿，他转过头来，赏给她一记呆滞空茫的凝视。
"坐呀，斯特拉。"他口齿含混地说，"坐呀。你可跳不出那个样子，嗯？你们欧洲人，没一个有那份风韵、那份柔韧。瞧瞧她！"

===Original===
The dancing girl was twirling at a furious rate, her scanty draperies enveloping her like a cloud. Presently, with a crash of the guitars, she sank, face downward, on the carpet. Gregory said something in Malayan, and the woman showed her white teeth in a smile. Stella had seen her before: there used to be two dancing girls, but one had contracted scarlet fever and had been hurriedly deported. Gregory had a horror of disease.
“Sit down here,” he commanded, laying his hand on the divan.
As if by magic, every servant in the room had disappeared, and she suddenly felt cold.
“I’ve left my chauffeur outside, with instructions to go for the police if I’m not out in half an hour,” she said loudly, and he laughed.
“You ought to have brought your nurse, Stella. What’s the matter with you nowadays? Can’t you talk anything but police? I want to talk to you,” he said in a milder tone.
===Chinese===
舞女旋转得令人眼花缭乱，单薄的衣纱裹着她，像一团云。不多时，琴声戛然一响，她面朝下瘫倒在地毯上。格雷戈里用马来语说了句什么，那女人咧开一口白牙笑了。斯特拉从前见过她：本来有两个舞女，一个染上猩红热，被火速遣送走了。格雷戈里最怕病症。
"坐这儿。"他手按着软榻，命令道。
仿佛变戏法一般，屋里的仆役霎时没了踪影，她突然觉出寒意。
"我把司机留在了外头，吩咐过他，半小时内我不出来就去报警。"她朗声说，他笑了。
"你该把你的奶妈也带来，斯特拉。你如今是怎么了？除了警察就不会说点别的？我想同你谈谈。"他的口气温和了些。

===Original===
“And I want to talk to you, Gregory. I am leaving Chichester for good, and I don’t want to see the place again.”
“That means you don’t want to see me again, eh? Well, I’m pretty well through with you, and there’s going to be no weeping and wailing and gnashing of teeth on my part.”
“My new company﻿—” she began, and he stopped her with a gesture.
“If your new company depends upon my putting up the money, you can forget it,” he said roughly. “I’ve seen my lawyer﻿—at least, I’ve seen somebody who knows﻿—and he tells me that if you’re trying to blackmail me about Tjarji, you’re liable to get into trouble yourself. I’ll put up money for you,” he went on. “Not a lot, but enough. I don’t suppose you’re a beggar, for I’ve given you sufficient already to start three companies. Stella, I’m crazy about that girl.”
She looked at him, her mouth open in surprise.
===Chinese===
"我也正想同你谈谈，格雷戈里。我要永远离开奇切斯特，这地方我不想再见。"
"那就是说，不想再见的人是我，嗯？好啊，你我也算缘尽了，我这厢绝不啼哭哀号、咬牙切齿。"
"我的新公司——"她刚开口，他一抬手止住了她。
"要是你的新公司指望我出钱，趁早死心。"他粗暴地说，"我见过我的律师——至少，见过一个懂行的人——他告诉我，你若想拿查尔吉（Tjarji）的事敲诈我，你自己也脱不了麻烦。钱我可以出，"他接着说，"不多，但够用。我想你还不至于乞讨，我已经给你的，够开三家公司的了。斯特拉，我迷上那姑娘了，迷得发疯。"
她望着他，惊讶得张开了嘴。

===Original===
“What girl?” she asked.
“Adele. Isn’t that her name?﻿—Adele Leamington.”
“Do you mean the extra girl that took my place?” she gasped.
He nodded, his sleepy eyes fixed on hers.
“That’s it. She’s my type, more than you ever were, Stella. And that isn’t meant in any way disparaging to you.”
She was content to listen: his declaration had taken her breath away.
===Chinese===
"哪个姑娘？"她问。
"阿黛尔。她不是叫这个名字吗？——阿黛尔·利明顿。"
"你是说顶了我位置的那个临时演员？"她倒抽一口气。
他点了点头，惺忪的眼睛盯住她。
"就是她。她对我的路数，比你这辈子都合，斯特拉。这话说出来，可没有半点贬损你的意思。"
她只管听着：他这番宣言惊得她喘不上气。

===Original===
“I’ll go a long way to get her,” he went on. “I’d marry her, if that meant anything to her﻿—it’s about time I married, anyway. Now you’re a friend of hers﻿—”
“A friend!” scoffed Stella, finding her voice. “How could I be a friend of hers when she has taken my place? And what if I were? You don’t suppose I should bring a girl to this hell upon earth?”
He brought his eyes around to hers﻿—cold, malignant, menacing.
“This hell upon earth has been heaven for you. It has given you wings, anyway! Don’t go back to London, Stella, not for a week or two. Get to know this girl. You’ve got opportunities that nobody else has. Kid her along﻿—you’re not going to lose anything by it. Speak about me; tell her what a good fellow I am; and tell her what a chance she has. You needn’t mention marriage, but you can if it helps any. Show her some of your jewels﻿—that big pendant I gave you﻿—”
He rambled on, and she listened, her bewilderment giving place to an uncontrollable fury.
===Chinese===
"为了得手，我什么都干得出来。"他接着说，"只要对她有点意思，我连娶她都肯——反正我也到该成家的年纪了。你呢，是她的一位朋友——"
"朋友！"斯特拉找回声音，讥讽道，"她占了我的位置，我还做得成她的朋友？就算是，又怎样？你以为我会把一个姑娘带进这处人间地狱？"
他把目光转到她身上——冰冷、狠毒、带着威吓。
"这处人间地狱，于你曾是天堂。至少，它给过你翅膀！别回伦敦去，斯特拉，一两个星期都别。去亲近这姑娘。你的机会谁都没有。哄着她——你少不了半根毫毛。多提提我；告诉她我人是多好；告诉她这于她是多大的造化。婚事不必提，若提了管用，提也无妨。给她看看你的首饰——我给你的那件大坠子——"
他絮絮地说下去，她听着，茫然化作一股按捺不住的怒火。

===Original===
“You brute!” she said at last. “To dare suggest that I should bring this girl to Griff! I don’t like her﻿—naturally. But I’d go down on my knees to her to beg her not to come. You think I’m jealous?” Her lips curled at the sight of the smile on his face. “That’s where you’re wrong, Gregory. I’m jealous of the position she’s taken at the studio, but, so far as you’re concerned”﻿—she shrugged her shoulders﻿—“you mean nothing to me. I doubt very much if you’ve ever meant more than a steady source of income. That’s candid, isn’t it?”
She got up from the divan and began putting on her gloves.
“As you don’t seem to want to help me,” she said, “I’ll have to find a way of making you keep your promise. And you did promise me a company, Gregory; I suppose you’ve forgotten that?”
“I was more interested in you then,” he said. “Where are you going?”
“I’m going back to my cottage, and tomorrow I’m returning to town,” she said.
===Chinese===
"你这畜生！"她终于开口，"竟敢撺掇我把这姑娘带进格里夫！我不喜欢她——天经地义。可我宁可跪下来求她千万别来。你以为我吃醋？"见他脸上的笑，她的嘴唇撇了撇。"这你就错了，格雷戈里。摄影棚里她占的位置，我确实眼红；至于你嘛"——她耸耸肩——"你在我心里一文不值。我很怀疑，你从来就不过是一份进项罢了。够坦白了吧？"
她从软榻上起身，开始戴手套。
"你既然不打算帮我，"她说，"我只好想法子叫你兑现承诺。你答应过我一家公司的，格雷戈里；你怕是忘了吧？"
"那时候我对你还有兴致些。"他说，"你去哪儿？"
"回我的小屋，明天回城。"她说。

===Original===
He looked first at one end of the room and then at the other, and then at her.
“You’re not going back to your cottage; you’re staying here, my dear,” he said.
She laughed.
“You told your chauffeur to go for the police, did you? I’ll tell *you* something! Your chauffeur is in my kitchen at this moment, having his supper. If you think that he’s likely to leave before you, you don’t know me, Stella!”
He gathered up the dressing-gown that was spread on the divan and slipped his arms into the hanging sleeves. A terrible figure he was in the girl’s eyes, something unclean, obscene. The scarlet pyjama jacket gave his face a demoniacal value, and she felt herself cringing from him.
===Chinese===
他先望望屋子这头，又望望那头，再望望她。
"你不回小屋了；你留下，亲爱的。"他说。
她笑了。
"你叫司机去报警，是吧？我也告诉你一件事！你那位司机，这会儿正在我厨房里吃晚饭呢。你要以为他赶在你前头走得了，那你就是不了解我，斯特拉！"
他拾起摊在软榻上的晨袍，两臂伸进垂着的袖筒。在姑娘眼里，这是一副可怕的形象，某种不洁的、猥亵的东西。猩红的睡衣上装衬得他那张脸愈发狰狞，她觉出自己在朝后畏缩。

===Original===
He was quick to notice the action, and his eyes glowed with a light of triumph.
“Bhag is downstairs,” he said significantly. “He handles people rough. He handled one girl so that I had to call in a doctor. You’ll come with me without﻿—assistance?”
She nodded dumbly; her knees gave way under her as she walked. She had bearded the beast in his den once too often.
Halfway along the corridor he unlocked a door of a room and pushed it open.
“Go there and stay there,” he said. “I’ll talk to you tomorrow, when I’m sober. I’m drunk now. Maybe I’ll send you someone to keep you company﻿—I don’t know yet.” He ruffled his scanty hair in drunken perplexity. “But I’ve got to be sober before I deal with you.”
===Chinese===
他立刻察觉了这个动作，两眼燃起得胜的光。
"巴格就在楼下。"他话里有话，"它对付人可不含糊。有个姑娘经它招呼过，害我得去请医生。你是自己跟我走呢，还是要——帮忙？"
她默默点头；走动时双膝直打软。她闯进兽窟撩拨那头野兽，这一回想是撩过头了。
走到走廊中段，他打开一间房的门锁，把门推开了。
"进去，待在那儿。"他说，"明天再同你谈，等酒醒了。我现在醉着。也许会派个人陪你——还没定。"他醉态可掬地抓着稀疏的头发，"不过整治你之前，我得先醒醒酒。"

===Original===
The door slammed on her and a key turned. She was in complete darkness, in a room she did not know. For one wild, terrified moment she wondered if she was alone.
It was a long time before her palm touched the little button projecting from the wall. She pressed it. A lamp enclosed in a crystal globe set in the ceiling flashed into sparkling light. She was in what had evidently been a small bedroom. The bedstead had been removed, but a mattress and a pillow were folded up in one corner. There was a window, heavily barred, but no other exit. She examined the door: the handle turned in her grasp; there was not even a keyhole in which she could try her own key.
Going to the window, she pulled up the sash, for the room was stuffy and airless. She found herself looking out from the back of the house, across the lawn to a belt of trees which she could just discern. The road ran parallel with the front of the house, and the shrillest scream would not be heard by anybody on the road.
Sitting down in one of the chairs, she considered her position. Having overcome her fear, she had that in her possession which would overcome Gregory if it came to a fight. Pulling up her skirt, she unbuckled the soft leather belt about her waist, and from the Russian leather holster it supported, she took a diminutive Browning﻿—a toy of a weapon but wholly businesslike in action. Sliding back the jacket, she threw a cartridge into the chamber and pulled up the safety-catch; then she examined the magazine and pressed it back again.
“Now, Gregory,” she said aloud, and at that moment her face went round to the window, and she started up with a scream.
Two grimy hands gripped the bars; glaring in at her was the horrible face of a tramp. Her trembling hand shot out for the pistol, but before it could close on the butt, the face had disappeared; and though she went round to the window and looked out, the bars prevented her from getting a clear view of the parapet along which the uncouth figure was creeping.
===Chinese===
门在她身后砰地关上，钥匙一转。她陷在彻底的黑暗里，在一间陌生的屋子。有一瞬间，她在狂乱惊恐中疑心屋里不止她一人。
过了好半天，她的手掌才摸到墙上凸起的小按钮。她按下去。天花板上一盏罩在水晶球里的灯霎时亮起，光华闪烁。这屋子显然原是一间小卧室。床架已撤走，一角折叠着一床垫褥和一只枕头。有一扇窗，装着粗重的铁栏，除此之外别无出口。她查验了房门：门柄在她手里转得动；门上连个锁眼都没有，她想试试自己的钥匙也无处可插。
她走到窗前，推起窗扇——屋里实在闷不透风。她发现自己正从宅子背面望出去，越过草坪，勉强辨得出一带树影。大路同宅子正面平行，任凭喊破嗓子，路上的人也听不见。
她在一张椅子上坐下，盘算自己的处境。恐惧既已压下，她手里也有足以制住格雷戈里的东西，真到动手时用得上。她撩起裙子，解开腰间的软皮带，从皮带上的俄式枪套里取出一柄小巧的勃朗宁（Browning）——玩具似的小枪，动起手来却是十足的真家伙。她推开套筒，顶弹上膛，拨起保险；又查验了弹匣，复又合上。
"来吧，格雷戈里。"她出声道。就在这一刻，她的脸转向窗户，惊叫着跳了起来。
两只污黑的手扒着铁栏；朝屋里瞪视的，是一张流浪汉的可怖面孔。她颤着手去抓手枪，还没握上枪柄，那张脸已经没了；她赶到窗前向外张望，铁栏却挡着，看不真切那粗野的身形正沿着女儿墙爬向何处。

## XXXI John Percival Liggitt / XXXI 约翰·珀西瓦尔·利吉特

===Original===
It was the second day of Michael’s visit to town, and, for a reason which she could not analyse, Adele felt “out” with the world. And yet the work was going splendidly, and Jack Knebworth, usually sparing of his praise, had almost rhapsodized over a little scene which she had acted with Connolly. So generous was he in his praise, and so comprehensive, that even Reggie came in for his share, and was willing and ready to revise his earlier estimate of the leading lady’s ability.
“I’ll be perfectly frank and honest, Mr. Knebworth,” he said, in this moment of candour, “Leamington is good. Of course, I’m always on the spot to give her tips, and there’s nothing quite so educative﻿—if I may use the term﻿—”
“You may,” said Jack Knebworth.
“Thanks,” said Connolly. “﻿—﻿—﻿—as having a finished artiste playing opposite to you. It doesn’t do me much good, but it helps her a lot; it inspires courage and all that sort of thing. And though I’ve had a perfectly awful, dreadful time, I feel that she pays for the coaching.”
“Oh, do you?” growled the old man. “And I’d like to say the same about you, Reggie! But unfortunately, all the coaching you’ve had or ever will get is not going to improve you.”
===Chinese===
迈克尔进城已是第二天。不知为了什么缘故——她自己也分析不出——阿黛尔觉得同全世界赌着气。其实工作进行得顺极了，杰克·尼布沃斯一向吝于夸奖，这回却对她同康诺利合演的一小场戏几乎赞不绝口。他夸得如此慷慨、如此周全，连雷吉也分到了一份，心甘情愿地修正自己先前对这位女主角实力的估价。
"我打算彻底坦白、以诚相告，尼布沃斯先生，"他趁着这坦诚的一刻说道，"利明顿是不错。当然，有我随时在旁点拨她，再没有比这更有教益的了——如果可以用这个词——"
"可以。"杰克·尼布沃斯说。
"多谢。"康诺利说，"——好比有一位炉火纯青的艺术家同你演对手戏。这于我益处不多，于她却帮衬极大；能鼓起勇气，还有诸如此类的好处。虽说这几日我着实受了些可怕的罪，我却觉得，这指点费她付得值。"
"哦，是吗？"老头儿咆哮道，"我也想把这话原样奉还给你呢，雷吉！可惜啊，你已经领教过、将来还会领教的种种点拨，都改不好你。"

===Original===
Reggie’s superior smile would have irritated one less equable than the director.
“You’re perfectly right, Mr. Knebworth,” he said earnestly. “I can’t improve! I’ve touched the zenith of my power, and I doubt whether you’ll ever look upon the like of me again. I’m certainly the best juvenile lead in this, and possibly in any country. I’ve had three offers to go to Hollywood, and you’ll never believe who is the lady who asked me to play against her﻿—”
“I don’t believe any of it,” said Jack even-temperedly, “but you’re right to an extent about Miss Leamington. She’s fine. And I agree that it doesn’t do you much good playing against her, because she makes you look like a large glass of heavily diluted beer.”
Later in the day, Adele herself asked her grey-haired chief whether it was true that Reggie would soon be leaving England for another and a more ambitious sphere.
“I shouldn’t think so,” said Jack. “There never was an actor that hadn’t a better contract up his sleeve and was ready to take it. But when it comes to a showdown, you find that the contracts they’re willing to tear up in order to take something better, are locked away in a lawyer’s office and can’t be got out. In the picture business all over the world, there are actors and actresses who are leaving by the first boat to show Hollywood how it’s done. I guess these liners would sail empty if they waited for ’em! That’s all bluff, part of the artificial life of make-believe in which actors and actresses have their being.”
===Chinese===
雷吉那副高人一等的浅笑，换个性子不如导演平和的人，早被他惹恼了。
"您说得千真万确，尼布沃斯先生。"他一脸恳切地说，"我是改不好了！我的功力已然登峰造极，我怀疑您此生还能不能再见到我这样的材料。不折不扣，我是这个国家、兴许还是任何国家最好的小生。好莱坞向我招过三回首，还有一位女士邀我与她演对手戏——您永远猜不出是谁——"
"这些我一个字也不信。"杰克心平气和地说，"不过关于利明顿小姐，你倒有几分说对了。她是好。我也承认，同她配戏于你益处不大，因为有你衬着，她把你照得像一大杯兑了过量水的啤酒。"
当天晚些时候，阿黛尔亲自问起她那位头发花白的上司：雷吉果真即将离开英国、另投高枝吗？
"我看未必。"杰克说，"天底下就没有一个演员，袖子里不揣着一份更好的合同、随时准备改换门庭的。可真到摊牌的时候你才发现，他们甘愿撕毁去就高枝的那几份合同，原是锁在律师的保险柜里，根本拿不出来。全世界的电影这一行，总有那么些男演员女演员，明天就要搭头班船去好莱坞露一手。依我看，这些邮船要是真等他们，恐怕就得空着开啦！全是唬人，是红男绿女们装虚作假生活里的一景。"

===Original===
“Has Mr. Brixan come back?”
He shook his head.
“No, I’ve not heard from him. There was a tough-looking fellow called at the studio half an hour ago to ask whether he’d returned.”
“Rather an unpleasant-looking tramp?” she asked. “I spoke to him. He said he had a letter for Mr. Brixan which he would not deliver to anybody else.”
She looked through the window which commanded a view of the entrance drive to the studio. Standing outside on the edge of the pavement was the wreck of a man. Long, lank black hair, streaked with grey, fell from beneath the soiled and dilapidated golf cap; he was apparently shirtless, for the collar of his indescribable jacket was buttoned up to his throat; and his bare toes showed through one gaping boot.
===Chinese===
"布里克森先生回来了吗？"
他摇了摇头。
"没有，他音信全无。半小时前有个样子很横的家伙到摄影棚来，打听他回没回来。"
"是不是那个模样很不讨喜的流浪汉？"她问，"我同他说过话。他说他有一封给布里克森先生的信，除本人之外谁也不交。"
她透过那扇能望见摄影棚门前车道的窗户看去。人行道边上站着一副人的残骸。又长又直的黑发夹着灰白，从那顶污渍斑斑、破烂不堪的高尔夫帽底下披散下来；他身上显然没穿衬衫，因为那件说不清样式的夹克领口直扣到喉咙；一只靴子张着大口，露出光脚趾。

===Original===
He might have been a man of sixty, but it was difficult to arrive at his age. It looked as though the grey, stubbled beard had not met a razor since he was in prison last. His eyes were red and inflamed; his nose that crimson which is almost blue. His hands were thrust into the pockets of his trousers, and seemed to be their only visible means of support, until you saw the string that was tied around his lean waist; and as he stood, he shuffled his feet rhythmically, whistling a doleful tune. From time to time he took one of his hands from his pockets and examined the somewhat soiled envelope it held, and then, as if satisfied with the scrutiny, put it back again and continued his jigging vigil.
“Do you think you ought to see that letter?” asked the girl, troubled. “It may be very important.”
“I thought that too,” said Jack Knebworth, “but when I asked him to let me see the note, he just grinned.”
“Do you know who it’s from?”
“No more than a crow, my dear,” said Knebworth patiently. “And now let’s get off the all-absorbing subject of Michael Brixan, and get back to the fair Roselle. That shot I took of the tower can’t be bettered, so I’m going to cut out the night picture, and from now on we’ll work on the lot.”
===Chinese===
他也许有六十岁，可实在拿不准年纪。看那副灰白的胡茬，像是打上回蹲监狱起就没见过剃刀。两眼红肿发炎；鼻子红得发紫。双手插在裤兜里，似乎全靠它们撑着身子——直到你看见他精瘦的腰上还系着一根麻绳。他站在那里，脚底有节奏地蹭来蹭去，吹着一支哀伤的调子。他不时从兜里抽出一只手，端详手里那只沾了污渍的信封，端详够了，仿佛甚为满意，又揣回去，接着他那跳跳跶跶的守望。
"您说，这封信您是不是该看一眼？"姑娘忧心地问，"说不定要紧得很。"
"我也这么想来着，"杰克·尼布沃斯说，"可我请他把条子给我瞧瞧，他只朝我咧嘴一笑。"
"您知道信是谁写的吗？"
"我一无所知，亲爱的，乌鸦知道的不比我多。"尼布沃斯耐着性子说，"好了，咱们别再抱着迈克尔·布里克森这个引人入胜的话题不放，回到美丽的罗塞尔身上来吧。塔那场戏我拍得再好不过了，夜景索性取消，从现在起就在厂棚里干。"

===Original===
The production was a heavy one, unusually so for one of Knebworth’s; the settings more elaborate, the crowd bigger than ever he had handled since he came to England. It was not an easy day for the girl, and she was utterly fagged when she started homeward that night.
“Ain’t seen Mr. Brixan, miss?” said a high-pitched voice as she reached the sidewalk.
She turned with a start. She had forgotten the existence of the tramp.
“No, he hasn’t been,” she said. “You had better see Mr. Knebworth again. Mr. Brixan lives with him.”
“Don’t I know it? Ain’t I got all the information possible about him? I should say I had!”
===Chinese===
这部戏工程浩大，在尼布沃斯的制作里算得上反常；布景更考究，群众演员之多，超过他来英国后经手的任何一部。这一天对姑娘并不轻松，夜里动身回家时，她已累得浑身散架。
她刚踏上人行道，一个尖利的嗓音问道："没见着布里克森先生吧，小姐？"
她吓了一跳，转过身去。她早把那流浪汉忘到脑后了。
"没有，他没回来过。"她说，"你还是再去见见尼布沃斯先生吧。布里克森先生住在他家。"
"我能不知道？他的底细我哪样没打听全？该打听的都打听了！"

===Original===
“He is in London: I suppose you know that?”
“He ain’t in London,” said the other disappointedly. “If he was in London, I shouldn’t be hanging around here, would I? No, he left London yesterday. I’m going to wait till I see him.”
She was amused by his pertinacity, though it was difficult for her to be amused at anything in the state of utter weariness into which she had fallen.
Crossing the market square, she had to jump quickly to avoid being knocked down by a car which she knew was Stella Mendoza’s. Stella could be at times a little reckless, and the motto upon the golden mascot on her radiator﻿—“Jump or Die”﻿—held a touch of sincerity.
She was in a desperate hurry now, and cursed fluently as she swung her car to avoid the girl, whom she recognized. Sir Gregory had come to his senses, and she wanted to get at him before he lost them again. She pulled up the car with a jerk at the gates of Griff Towers, flung open the door and jumped out.
“If I don’t return in two hours, you can go into Chichester and fetch the police,” she said.
===Chinese===
"他在伦敦：这你总知道吧？"
"他不在伦敦。"对方失望地说，"他要在伦敦，我干嘛在这儿干耗着，嗯？不，他昨天离开伦敦了。我就守在这儿，非见着他不可。"
他那股死缠的韧劲逗得她直想笑，虽说以她眼下筋疲力竭的地步，要被什么事逗笑本也难得。
穿过集市广场时，她慌忙一跳，才没被一辆汽车撞倒——她认得那是斯特拉·门多萨的车。斯特拉有时开得颇为莽撞，她散热器上那尊金色吉祥物刻着的座右铭——"跳开或死"——倒有几分真心实意。
这会儿她正十万火急，一面麻利地打轮躲开那姑娘——她也认出了她——一面流利地咒骂着。格雷戈里爵士神志清醒过来了，她要赶在他再度昏头之前抓住他。她把车猛地停在格里夫塔楼大门前，推开车门，跳了下来。
"我要是两个钟头里不出来，你就进奇切斯特去，把警察带来。"她说。

## XIV Mendoza Makes a Fight / XIV 门多萨的反击

===Original===
Jack stared at him in amazement.
“You’re joking!” he said.
“On the contrary, I am very much in earnest,” said Michael quietly. “But to know the Headhunter, and to bring his crimes home to him, are quite different matters.”
Jack Knebworth sat at his desk, his hands thrust into his trousers pockets, a look of blank incredulity on the face turned to the detective.
“Is it one of my company?” he asked, troubled, and Michael laughed.
“I haven’t the pleasure of knowing all your company,” he said diplomatically, “but at any rate, don’t let the Headhunter worry you. What are you going to do about Mr. Reggie Connolly?”
===Chinese===
杰克愕然盯着他。
"你在说笑吧！"他说。
"恰恰相反，我说得极其认真，"迈克尔平静地说，"不过，知道猎头者是谁，同把他的罪证钉死在他身上，完全是两回事。"
杰克·尼布沃斯坐在写字台前，双手插在裤兜里，转向侦探的那张脸上一片茫然的难以置信。
"是我们班组里的人？"他不无忧虑地问，迈克尔笑了。
"贵班组的各位，我还没福气全都认识，"他圆滑地说，"不过无论如何，别让猎头者的事烦心。雷吉·康诺利先生那边，您打算怎么办？"

===Original===
The director shrugged.
“He doesn’t mean it, and I was a fool to get wild,” he said. “That kind of ninny never means anything. You wouldn’t dream, to see him on the screen, full of tenderness and love and manliness, that he’s the poor little jellyfish he is! As for Mendoza—” he swept his hands before him, and the gesture was significant.
Miss Stella Mendoza, however, was not accepting her dismissal so readily. She had fought her way up from nothing, and was not prepared to forfeit her position without a struggle. Moreover, her position was a serious one. She had money—so much money that she need never work again; for, in addition to her big salary, she enjoyed an income from a source which need not be too closely inquired into. But there was a danger that Knebworth might carry the war into a wider field.
Her first move was to go in search of Adele Leamington, who, she learnt that morning for the first time, had taken her place. Though she went in a spirit of conciliation, she choked with anger to discover that the girl was occupying the star’s dressing-room, the room which had always been sacred to Stella Mendoza’s use. Infuriated, yet preserving an outward calm, she knocked at the door. (That she, Stella Mendoza, should knock at a door rightfully hers was maddening enough!)
Adele was sitting at the bare dressing-table, gazing, a little awestricken, at the array of mirrors, lights and the vista of dresses down the long alleyway which served as a wardrobe. At the sight of Mendoza she went red.
===Chinese===
导演耸了耸肩。
"他不是那个意思，我发火才是傻瓜，"他说，"那种蠢货从来当不得真。你在银幕上看他，柔情似水，爱意绵绵，一身男子气概，做梦也想不到他实际上是条可怜的小海蜇！至于门多萨嘛——"他把手在面前一挥，这个手势意味深长。
不过，斯特拉·门多萨小姐可不打算这么乖乖认栽。她是白手起家一路打拼上来的，没准备不战而弃自己的地位。何况，她的处境非同小可。她有钱——钱多得下半辈子不用再干活；因为除了高额片酬，她还有一注进项，来源嘛，不便深究。可危险在于，尼布沃斯可能把战火烧到更大的地盘去。
她的第一步棋是去找阿黛尔·利明顿——那天早晨她才头一回听说，顶替她的就是这个姑娘。她本是抱着和解的心思去的，可一发现姑娘占着明星专用化妆间——那间向来只许斯特拉·门多萨一人使用的屋子——气得她直噎。她怒火中烧，面上却维持着平静，敲了敲门。（她斯特拉·门多萨，竟要敲自己名正言顺的房门，这已经够让人发疯了！）
阿黛尔正坐在光秃秃的梳妆台前，带着几分敬畏，望着那一面面镜子、一盏盏灯，以及长长一条充作衣帽间的廊道里层层叠叠的裙装。一看见门多萨，她的脸红了。

===Original===
“Miss Leamington, isn’t it?” asked Stella sweetly. “May I come in?”
“Do, please,” said Adele, hastily rising.
“Please *do* sit down,” said Stella. “It’s a very uncomfortable chair, but most of the chairs here are uncomfortable. They tell me you have been ‘doubling’ for me?”
“ ‘Doubling’?” said Adele, puzzled.
“Yes, Mr. Knebworth said he was ‘doubling’ you. You know what I mean: when an artiste can’t appear, they sometimes put in an understudy in scenes where she’s not very distinctly shown—long shots—”
“But Mr. Knebworth took me close up,” said the girl quietly. “I was only in one long shot.”
===Chinese===
"是利明顿小姐吧？"斯特拉甜甜地问，"我能进来吗？"
"请进，"阿黛尔说着慌忙起身。
"您千万*坐*下，"斯特拉说，"这椅子很不舒服，不过这儿的椅子大多不舒服。听说您一直在替我'替演'？"
"'替演'？"阿黛尔不解。
"是啊，尼布沃斯先生说让您'替演'。您明白我的意思：演员不能出场时，他们有时会在她不太露脸的镜头里塞个替角——远景什么的——"
"可尼布沃斯先生给我拍的是特写，"姑娘平静地说，"我只上过一个远景。"

===Original===
Miss Mendoza masked her anger and sighed.
“Poor old chap! He’s very angry with me, and really, I oughtn’t to annoy him. I’m coming back tomorrow, you know.”
The girl went pale.
“It’s fearfully humiliating for you, I realize, but, my dear, we’ve all had to go through that experience. And people in the studio will be very nice to you.”
“But it’s impossible,” said Adele. “Mr. Knebworth told me I was to be in the picture from start to finish.”
Mendoza shook her head smilingly.
“You can never believe what these fellows tell you,” she said. “He’s just told me to be ready to shoot tomorrow morning on the South Downs.”
===Chinese===
门多萨小姐掩住怒气，叹了口气。
"可怜的老头儿！他正生我的气呢，说真的，我不该惹他。我明天就回来了，您知道吧。"
姑娘的脸白了。
"我知道这叫您屈辱极了，可是亲爱的，这种滋味我们都尝过。棚里的人会好好待您的。"
"可这不可能，"阿黛尔说，"尼布沃斯先生亲口说，这部片子从头到尾都用我。"
门多萨含笑摇了摇头。
"这些人说的话，你永远不能当真，"她说，"他刚刚还吩咐我，明早去南唐斯丘陵（South Downs）准备开机呢。"

===Original===
Adele’s heart sank. She knew that was the rendezvous, though she was not aware of the fact that Stella Mendoza had procured her information from the disgruntled Mr. Connolly.
“It *is* humiliating,” Stella went on thoughtfully. “If I were you, I would go up to town and stay away for a couple of weeks till the whole thing has blown over. I feel very much to blame for your disappointment, my dear, and if money is any compensation—” She opened her bag and, taking out a wad of notes, detached four and put them on the table.
“What is this for?” asked Adele coldly.
“Well, my dear, you’ll want money for expenses—”
“If you imagine I’m going to London without seeing Mr. Knebworth and finding out for myself whether you’re speaking the truth—”
Mendoza’s face flamed.
===Chinese===
阿黛尔的心沉了下去。她知道那正是集合地点，只是她并不知道，斯特拉·门多萨这消息是从满腹怨气的康诺利先生那里弄来的。
"这确实屈辱，"斯特拉若有所思地接着说，"我要是你，就进城去躲两个星期，等这事整个儿风平浪静。亲爱的，你的落空我有很大责任，要是钱能算点补偿——"她打开手包，抽出一沓钞票，数出四张放在桌上。
"这是什么意思？"阿黛尔冷冷地问。
"喏，亲爱的，你路上总要花销——"
"要是你以为我会不亲眼见尼布沃斯先生、不亲自核实你说的是真是假就上伦敦去——"
门多萨的脸腾地烧红了。

===Original===
“Do you suggest I’m lying?”
She had dropped all pretence of friendliness and stood, a veritable virago, her hands on her hips, her dark face thrust down into Adele’s.
“I don’t know whether you’re a liar or whether you are mistaken,” said Adele, who was less afraid of this termagant than she had been at the news she had brought. “The only thing I’m perfectly certain about is that for the moment this is my room, and I will ask you to leave it!”
She opened the door, and for a moment was afraid that the girl would strike her; but the broad-shouldered Irish dresser, a silent but passionately interested spectator and audience, interposed her huge bulk and good-humouredly pushed the raging star into the corridor.
“I’ll have you out of there!” she screamed across the woman’s shoulder. “Jack Knebworth isn’t everything in this company! I’ve got influence enough to fire Knebworth!”
===Chinese===
"你是说我撒谎？"
她已抛开一切友好的伪装，站在那儿活脱脱一个泼妇，双手叉腰，那张黧黑的脸直逼到阿黛尔脸上。
"我不知道你是撒谎还是搞错了，"阿黛尔说，比起刚才那消息，她倒不那么怕这个悍妇了，"我只确知一件事：此刻这是我的房间，请你出去！"
她拉开门，有一瞬还真怕那姑娘会动手；好在那位宽肩膀的爱尔兰服装员——一个不出声却看得津津有味的看客兼听众——把高大的身躯往中间一横，笑呵呵地把怒气冲冲的明星推出了走廊。
"我会把你从那儿撵出去的！"她隔着服装员的肩膀尖叫，"这家公司不是杰克·尼布沃斯一个人的天下！我有的是门路，炒了尼布沃斯都够！"

===Original===
The unrepeatable innuendoes that followed were not good to hear, but Adele Leamington listened in scornful silence. She was only too relieved (for the girl’s fury was eloquent) to know that she had not been speaking the truth. For one horrible moment Adele had believed her, knowing that Knebworth would not hesitate to sacrifice her or any other member of the company if, by so doing, the values of the picture could be strengthened.
Knebworth was alone when his ex-star was announced, and his first instinct was not to see her. Whatever his intentions might have been, she determined his action by appearing in the doorway just as he was making up his mind what line to take. He fixed her with his gimlet eyes for a second, and then, with a jerk of his head, called her in. When they were alone:
“There are many things I admire about you, Stella, and not the least of them is your nerve. But it is no good coming to me with any of that let-bygones-be-bygones stuff. You’re not appearing in this picture, and maybe you’ll never appear in another picture of mine.”
===Chinese===
随后那些没法复述的影射话不堪入耳，阿黛尔·利明顿只是轻蔑地默默听着。得知她方才并非实言，她着实松了口气（那姑娘的狂怒本身就说明了一切）。因为曾有可怕的一瞬，阿黛尔真信了她——她知道，只要能让片子的成色更好，尼布沃斯会毫不迟疑地牺牲她或者班组里的任何人。
前明星求见时，尼布沃斯正独自一人，他的第一反应是不见。不论他原本打什么主意，就在他拿定主意怎么办的那一刻，她出现在门口，替他做了决定。他用锥子般的眼睛盯了她一秒，然后头一摆，把她叫了进来。两人独处时，他说：
"你身上有许多让我佩服的东西，斯特拉，脸皮厚不算最小的一样。不过，跟我来'既往不咎'那一套没用。这部片子你不上镜了，兴许我的任何片子你都不会再上。"

===Original===
“Is that so?” she drawled, sitting down uninvited, and taking from her bag a little gold cigarette case.
“You’ve come in to tell me that you’ve got influence with a number of people who are financially interested in this corporation,” said Jack, to her dismay. She wondered if there were telephone communication between the dressing-room and the office, then remembered there wasn’t.
“I’ve handled a good many women in my time,” he went on, “and I’ve never had to fire one but she didn’t produce the President, Vice-President or Treasurer and hold them over my head with their feet ready to kick out my brains! And, Stella, none of those holdups have ever got past. People who are financially interested in a company may love you to death, but they’ve got to have the money to love you with; and if I don’t make pictures that sell, somebody is short of a perfectly good diamond necklace.”
===Chinese===
"是吗？"她慢悠悠地说，也不等人让，自顾自坐下，从包里拿出一只小金烟盒。
"你进来是要告诉我，你在本公司几位出资人那里说得上话，"杰克说，正戳中她的痛处。她疑心化妆间和办公室之间有电话相通，随即想起并没有。
"我这辈子打过交道的女人多了，"他接着说，"没有一个是被我开掉时不搬出董事长、副董事长或者财务主任，把他们悬在我头顶上，脚都举起来要踢碎我的脑壳！可是斯特拉，那些要挟一回也没得逞过。在公司里投了钱的人也许能爱你爱到死，可爱人总得有钱来爱；我要是拍不出卖座的片子，哪位就要少一条顶好的钻石项链。"

===Original===
“We’ll see if Sir Gregory thinks the same way,” she said defiantly, and Jack Knebworth whistled.
“Gregory Penne, eh? I didn’t know you had friends in that quarter. Yes, he is a stockholder in the company, but he doesn’t hold enough to make any difference. I guess he told you that he did. And if he held ninety-nine percent of it, Stella, it wouldn’t make any difference to old Jack Knebworth, because old Jack Knebworth’s got a contract which gives him carte blanche, and the only getting out clause is the one that gets *me* out! You couldn’t touch me, Stella, no, ma’am!”
“I suppose you’re going to blacklist me?” she said sulkily.
This was the one punishment she most feared—that Jack Knebworth should circulate the story of her unforgivable sin of letting down a picture when it was half-shot.
“I thought about that,” he nodded, “but I guess I’m not vindictive. I’ll let you go and say the part didn’t suit you, and that you resigned, which is as near the truth as any story I’ll have to crack. Go with God, Stella. I guess you won’t, because you’re not that way, but—behave!”
===Chinese===
"格雷戈里爵士是不是也这么想，咱们走着瞧，"她挑衅地说，杰克·尼布沃斯吹了声口哨。
"格雷戈里·彭尼，嗯？我不知道你在那头还有朋友。不错，他是公司股东，可他那点股份翻不起浪。我猜他跟你说他握着大头。就算他握着百分之九十九，斯特拉，对老杰克·尼布沃斯也照样没用，因为老杰克·尼布沃斯的合同给了他全权，唯一的解约条款，也是把*我*解出去的那条！你动不了我，斯特拉，动不了，女士！"
"我猜你要把我拉进黑名单？"她绷着脸说。
这正是她最怕的一记惩罚——杰克·尼布沃斯把她那桩不可饶恕的罪过四处传扬：片子拍到一半她撂了挑子。
"这个我想过，"他点头道，"不过我不记仇。放你走，对外就说不适合这个角色，是你请辞——这跟我将来不得不编的任何说辞相比，算最接近实情的了。愿上帝与你同行，斯特拉。我料你不会，因为你不是那路人，可还是——规矩点！"

===Original===
He waved her out of the office and she went, somewhat chastened. Outside the studio she met Lawley Foss, and told him the result of the interview.
“If it’s like that you can do nothing,” he said. “I’d speak for you, Stella, but I’ve got to speak for myself,” he added bitterly. “The idea of a man of my genius truckling hat in hand to this damned old Yankee is very humiliating.”
“You ought to have your own company, Lawley,” she said, as she had said a dozen times before. “You write the stuff and I’ll be the leading woman and put it over for you. Why, you could direct Kneb’s head off. I *know*, Lawley! I’ve been to the only place on God Almighty’s earth where art is appreciated, and I tell you that a four-flusher like Jack Knebworth wouldn’t last a light-mile at Hollywood!”
“Light-mile” was a term she had acquired from a scientific admirer. It had the double advantage of sounding grand and creating a demand for an explanation. To her annoyance, Foss was sufficiently acquainted with elementary physics to know that she meant the period of time that a ray of light would take to traverse a mile.
===Chinese===
他挥挥手打发她出办公室，她走了，气焰收敛了几分。摄影棚外她遇上劳利·福斯，把面谈的结果告诉了他。
"要是这样的话，你无计可施了，"他说，"我倒想替你说话，斯特拉，可我得先替我自己说话，"他苦涩地补了一句，"我这个天才人物，竟要手捧帽子去巴结这该死的美国老头，实在屈辱。"
"你该有自己的公司，劳利，"她说，这话她已说过十几回了，"你来写本子，我当女主角，替你把它唱红。嗐，论导演，你能把尼布甩出十条街。我*懂行*，劳利！全能上帝的地球上只有一处地方懂得赏识艺术，我去过，我告诉你，杰克·尼布沃斯这种绣花枕头，在好莱坞连一光英里都撑不下来！"
"光英里"这个词，是她从一位搞科学的仰慕者那里学来的。它有双重好处：听着气派，还能引来别人请教。让她恼火的是，福斯对基础物理颇为熟悉，知道她指的不过是一束光跑完一英里所需的那点时间。

===Original===
“Is he in his office now?”
She nodded, and without any further word Lawley Foss, in some trepidation, knocked at his chief’s door.
“The truth is, Mr. Knebworth, I want to ask a favour of you.”
“Is it money?” demanded Jack, looking up from under his bushy brows.
“Well, it was money, as a matter of fact. There have been one or two little bills I’ve overlooked, and the bailiffs have been after me. I’ve got to raise fifty pounds by two o’clock this afternoon.”
Jack pulled open a drawer, took out a book and wrote a cheque, not for fifty pounds, but for eighty.
“That’s a month’s salary in advance,” he said. “You’ve drawn your pay up to today, and by the terms of your contract you’re entitled to one month’s notice or pay therefore. You’ve got it.”
===Chinese===
"他这会儿在办公室？"
她点了点头。劳利·福斯二话不说，怀着几分忐忑，敲响了上司的房门。
"实话说，尼布沃斯先生，我想求您一件事。"
"是要钱？"杰克从浓眉底下抬起眼问。
"嗯，还真是钱的事。有一两张小账单我一时疏忽了，法警已经追上门。今天下午两点之前，我必须凑出五十英镑。"
杰克拉开抽屉，取出支票簿开了张支票——不是五十英镑，而是八十。
"这是预支一个月薪水，"他说，"你的工钱已领到今天，按合同条款，你应得一个月的通知期，或者折成工钱。你拿到了。"

===Original===
Foss went an ugly red.
“Does that mean I’m fired?” he asked loudly.
Jack nodded.
“You’re fired, not because you want money, not because you’re one of the most difficult men on the lot to deal with, but for what you did last night, Foss.”
“What do you mean?”
“I mean I am taking Mr. Brixan’s view, that you fastened a white label to the window of Miss Leamington’s room in order to guide an agent of Sir Gregory Penne. That agent came and nearly kidnapped my leading lady.”
The man’s lip curled in a sneer.
===Chinese===
福斯的脸涨成了难看的紫红。
"这是说我被开除了？"他大声问。
杰克点了点头。
"你被开除了，不是因为你缺钱，不是因为你是全片场最难缠的人之一，而是因为你昨晚干的事，福斯。"
"什么意思？"
"我的意思是，我采纳布里克森先生的看法：你在利明顿小姐房间的窗上贴了白色标记，为格雷戈里·彭尼爵士的一名爪牙引路。那爪牙昨夜来了，差点劫走我的女主角。"
那人的嘴角撇出一声冷笑。

===Original===
“You’ve got melodrama in your blood, Knebworth,” he said. “Kidnap your leading lady! Those sort of things may happen in the United States, but they don’t happen in England.”
“Close the door as you go out,” said Jack, preparing for his work.
“Let me say this—” began Foss.
“I’ll let you say nothing,” snarled Knebworth. “I won’t even let you say ‘goodbye.’ Get!”
And, when the door slammed behind his visitor, the old director pushed a bell on his table, and, to his assistant who came:
“Get Miss Leamington down here,” he said. “I’d like contact with something that’s wholesome.”
===Chinese===
"你血液里全是情节剧，尼布沃斯，"他说，"绑架你的女主角！这种事美国兴许有，英格兰可没有。"
"出去时把门带上，"杰克说，着手准备干活。
"容我说一句——"福斯开口。
"我一句也不容你说，"尼布沃斯怒道，"连'再见'都不让。滚！"
客人摔门而去之后，老导演按了按桌上的铃，对进来的助理说：
"把利明顿小姐请下来，"他说，"我想接触点干净的东西。"

## XXXIX Michael Knows for Sure / XXXIX 迈克尔确知真相

===Original===
It was that same voice that had brought Michael Brixan racing across the garden to the postern gate. A car stood outside, its lights dimmed. Standing by its bonnet was a frightened little brown man who had brought the machine to the place.
“Where is your master?” asked Michael quickly.
The man pointed.
“He went that way,” he quavered. “There was a devil in the big machine﻿—it would not move when he stamped on the little pedal.”
Michael guessed what had happened. At the last moment, by one of those queer mischances which haunt the just and the unjust, the engine had failed him and he had fled on foot.
“Which way did he go?”
===Chinese===
正是那声呼救，引得迈克尔·布里克森飞奔着穿过园地直扑后门。门外停着一辆汽车，灯光昏暗。车头旁站着一个吓坏了的矮小棕色男人，正是他把车开到这儿的。
"你的主人在哪儿？"迈克尔急问。
那人指了指。
"他往那边去了。"他颤声道，"大机器里有个恶魔——他踩那个小踏板，它就是不动。"
发生的事，迈克尔猜到了。千钧一发之际，碰上那种专跟好人坏人一视同仁地过不去的离奇意外，引擎罢了工，他只得徒步逃窜。
"他往哪边跑了？"

===Original===
Again the man pointed.
“He ran,” he said simply.
Michael turned to the detective who was with him.
“Stay here: he may return. Arrest him immediately and put the irons on him. He’s probably armed, and he may be suicidal; we can’t afford to take any risks.”
He had been so often across what he had named the “Back Field” that he could find his way blindfolded, and he ran at top speed till he came to the stile and to the road. Sir Gregory was nowhere in sight. Fifty yards along the road, the lights gleamed cheerily from an upper window in Mr. Longvale’s house, and Michael bent his footsteps in that direction.
===Chinese===
那人又指了指。
"他跑来着。"他简单地说。
迈克尔转向随行的侦探。
"你守在这儿：他可能回来。一见人就逮捕，给他上铐。他身上多半有枪，还可能寻短见；咱们冒不起任何风险。"
那片被他称作"后田"的地界，他已走熟得闭着眼也认得路。他全速奔到栅栏梯凳和大路上，哪里也不见格雷戈里爵士的影子。沿路五十码外，朗维尔先生家楼上的一扇窗透出暖亮的灯光，迈克尔折步朝那个方向去了。

===Original===
Still no sight of the man, and he turned through the gate and knocked at the door, which was almost immediately opened by the old gentleman himself. He wore a silken gown, tied with a sash about the middle, a picture of comfort, Michael thought.
“Who’s that?” asked Mr. Sampson Longvale, peering out into the darkness. “Why, bless my life, it’s Mr. Brixan, the officer of the law! Come in, come in, sir.”
He opened the door wide and Michael passed into the sitting-room, with its inevitable two candles, augmented now by a small silver reading-lamp that burnt some sort of petrol vapour.
“No trouble at the Towers, I trust?” said Mr. Longvale anxiously.
“There was a little trouble,” said Michael carefully. “Have you by any chance seen Sir Gregory Penne?”
===Chinese===
仍不见那人踪影，他进了院门，敲响房门，开门的几乎立刻就是老先生本人。他穿一件绸袍，腰间系着带子，好一幅安适图，迈克尔想。
"是哪位？"桑普森·朗维尔先生望着外面的黑暗问道，"哎呀，我的老天，是布里克森先生，执法官员！快请进，快请进，先生。"
他把门敞开，迈克尔走进起居室。屋里照例点着两支蜡烛，如今又添了一盏小小的银质阅读灯，燃着某种汽油蒸气。
"塔楼那边没出乱子吧，我希望？"朗维尔先生担忧地说。
"出了点小乱子。"迈克尔斟酌着说，"您可巧见过格雷戈里·彭尼爵士吗？"

===Original===
The old man shook his head.
“I found the night rather too chilly for my usual garden ramble,” he said, “so I’ve seen none of the exciting events which seem inevitably to accompany the hours of darkness in these times. Has anything happened to him?”
“I hope not,” said Michael quietly. “I hope, for everybody’s sake, that﻿—nothing has happened to him.”
He walked across and leant his elbows on the mantelpiece, looking up at the painting above his head.
“Do you admire my relative?” beamed Mr. Longvale.
“I don’t know that I admire him. He was certainly a wonderfully handsome old gentleman.”
===Chinese===
老人摇了摇头。
"我觉得今夜太凉，不宜照例去园中漫步，"他说，"所以近来这些似乎总要伴随黑夜而来的热闹事，我一件也没赶上。他出什么事了？"
"但愿没有。"迈克尔平静地说，"为了大家的缘故，我希望——他什么事故都没有。"
他走过去，双肘撑在壁炉台上，仰头望着头顶上方的画像。
"您欣赏我这位先人吗？"朗维尔先生满面春风地问。
"说不上欣赏。不过他无疑是位相貌堂堂的老先生。"

===Original===
Mr. Longvale inclined his head.
“You have read his memoirs?”
Michael nodded, and the old man did not seem in any way surprised.
“Yes, I have read what purport to be his memoirs,” said Michael quietly, “but latter-day opinion is that they are not authentic.”
Mr. Longvale shrugged his shoulders.
“Personally, I believe every word of them,” he said. “My uncle was a man of considerable education.”
===Chinese===
朗维尔先生微微颔首。
"您读过他的回忆录？"
迈克尔点了点头，老人似乎毫不惊讶。
"读过，读过那部传为他回忆录的东西。"迈克尔平静地说，"不过现今的看法是，那并非真本。"
朗维尔先生耸了耸肩。
"我个人对他一字一句都信。"他说，"我的叔父是位极有学识的人。"

===Original===
It would have amazed Jack Knebworth to know that the man who had rushed hotfoot from the tower in search of a possible murderer, was at that moment calmly discussing biography; yet such was the incongruous, unbelievable fact.
“I sometimes feel that you think too much about your uncle, Mr. Longvale,” said Michael gently.
The old gentleman frowned.
“You mean﻿—?”
“I mean that such a subject may become an obsession and a very unhealthy obsession, and such hero-worship may lead a man to do things which no sane man would do.”
Longvale looked at him in genuine astonishment.
===Chinese===
杰克·尼布沃斯若是得知，那个为搜捕杀人凶犯而从塔楼一路狂奔而来的人，此刻正心平气和地谈着传记，定会大吃一惊；然而这正是那桩不协调得难以置信的事实。
"我有时觉得，您对您那位叔父想得太多了，朗维尔先生。"迈克尔温和地说。
老先生皱起了眉。
"您是说——？"
"我是说，这样的题目可能变成一种痴念，而且是很不健康的痴念，这样的英雄崇拜，可能引得一个人做出任何心智健全者都不会做的事。"
朗维尔真切地愕然望着他。

===Original===
“Can one do better than imitate the deeds of the great?” he asked.
“Not if your sense of values hasn’t got all tangled up, and you ascribe to him virtues which are not virtues﻿—unless duty is a virtue﻿—and confuse that which is great with that which is terrible.”
Michael turned and, resting his palms on the table, looked across to the old man who confronted him.
“I want you to come with me into Chichester this evening.”
“Why?” The question was asked bluntly.
“Because I think you’re a sick man, that you ought to have care.”
===Chinese===
"效法伟人的行止，还有比这更好的吗？"他问。
"若是你的价值标尺没缠成一团乱麻便罢；可你把并非德性的东西当作德性安在他身上——除非本分也算德性——又把伟大之物同可怕之物混为一谈，那就没有比这更糟的了。"
迈克尔转过身，双手按在桌上，望向对面迎着他的老人。
"我要您今晚随我进城去奇切斯特。"
"为什么？"这问题问得毫不客气。
"因为我认为您是个病人，应当得到照护。"

===Original===
The old man laughed and drew himself even more erect.
“Sick? I was never better in my life, my dear sir, never fitter, never stronger!”
And he looked all that he said. His height, the breadth of his shoulders, the healthy glow of his cheeks, all spoke of physical fitness.
A long pause, and then:
“Where is Gregory Penne?” asked Michael, emphasizing every word.
“I haven’t the slightest idea.”
The old man’s eyes met his without wavering.
===Chinese===
老人笑了，身姿挺得更直。
"病人？我这辈子从没这么好过，亲爱的先生，从没这么健壮，从没这么硬朗！"
而且他的模样也全然印证这话。他的个头、宽阔的肩膀、双颊健康的红润，无一不说着体魄的强健。
长久的沉默，然后：
"格雷戈里·彭尼在哪儿？"迈克尔一字一顿地问。
"我毫无头绪。"
老人的目光迎上他的，毫不动摇。

===Original===
“We were talking about my great-uncle. You know him, of course?” he asked.
“I knew him the first time I saw his picture, and I thought I had betrayed my knowledge, but apparently I did not. Your great-uncle”﻿—Michael spoke deliberately﻿—“was Sanson, otherwise Longval, hereditary executioner of France!”
Such a silence followed that the ticking of a distant clock sounded distinctly.
“Your uncle has many achievements to his credit. He hanged three men on a gallows sixty feet high, unless my memory is at fault. His hand struck off the head of Louis of France and his consort Marie Antoinette.”
The look of pride in the old man’s face was startling. His eyes kindled, he seemed to grow in height.
“By what fantastic freak of fate you come to have settled in England, what queer kink of mind decided you secretly to carry on the profession of Sanson and seek far and wide for poor, helpless wretches to destroy, I do not know.”
===Chinese===
"咱们方才谈的是我的叔祖。您当然知道他？"他问。
"我一见他的画像就认出来了，当时还以为自己露了口风，如今看来并没有。您的叔祖"——迈克尔一字一句地说——"就是桑松（Sanson），又名朗瓦尔（Longval），法兰西世袭刽子手！"
随之而来的一片寂静里，远处一座钟的滴答声清晰可闻。
"您这位叔父名下功业颇多。他曾在一座六十英尺高的绞架上绞死过三个人，除非我记错了。法国的路易（Louis）及其王后玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette），也丧生在他手里。"
老人脸上那副骄色触目惊心。他双眼燃亮，人仿佛也高大了。
"凭着怎样荒诞的命运作弄，您落脚到了英国；又是怎样古怪的心思乖僻，叫您暗中重操桑松的旧业，四处搜寻可怜无助的倒霉鬼来毁灭——我不知道。"

===Original===
Michael did not raise his voice, he spoke in a calm, conversational tone; and in the same way did Longvale reply.
“Is it not better,” he said gently, “that a man should pass out of life through no act of his own, than that he should commit the unpardonable crime of self-murder? Have I not been a benefactor to men who dared not take their own lives?”
“To Lawley Foss?” suggested Michael, his grave eyes fixed on the other.
“He was a traitor, a vulgar blackmailer, a man who sought to use the knowledge which had accidentally come to him, to extract money from me.”
“Where is Gregory Penne?”
A slow smile dawned on the man’s face.
===Chinese===
迈克尔没有提高嗓门，他语调平缓，如叙家常；朗维尔的回答也是同样从容。
"一个人不经自己动手便离开人世，"他温和地说，"岂不比犯下那不可赦免的自杀之罪更好？对那些不敢亲手了断的人，我岂不是一位恩人？"
"对劳利·福斯也是？"迈克尔提了一句，严肃的目光盯住对方。
"他是个叛徒，一个庸俗的敲诈者，一个想拿偶然到手的秘密从我身上榨钱的人。"
"格雷戈里·彭尼在哪儿？"
那人脸上缓缓绽开一个笑容。

===Original===
“You will not believe me? That is unkind, sir! I have not seen Sir Gregory.”
Michael pointed to the hearth, where a cigarette was still smouldering.
“There is that,” he said. “There are his muddy footprints on the carpet of this room. There is the cry I heard. Where is he?”
Within reach of his hand was his heavy-calibred Browning. A move on the old man’s part, and he would lie maimed on the ground. Michael was dealing with a homicidal lunatic of the most dangerous type, and would not hesitate to shoot.
But the old man showed no sign of antagonism. His voice was gentleness itself. He seemed to feel and express a pride in crimes which, to his brain, were not crimes at all.
===Chinese===
"您不肯信我？这可不够厚道，先生！我没有见过格雷戈里爵士。"
迈克尔指了指壁炉边，那儿还有一支香烟燃着余烬。
"这就是凭据。"他说，"这屋子的地毯上有他带泥的脚印。还有我听见的那声呼救。他在哪儿？"
他那把大口径勃朗宁就垂手可得。老人但凡动一动，便会伤残倒地。迈克尔面对的是最危险的那类杀人狂，开起枪来绝不迟疑。
可老人没有露出半分敌意。他的声音温和至极。他似乎对那些罪行感觉并表达着一种自豪——在他的头脑里，那些根本不是罪行。

===Original===
“If you really wish me to go into Chichester with you tonight, of course I will go,” he said. “You may be right in your own estimation, even in the estimation of your superiors, but, in ending my work, you are rendering a cruel disservice to miserable humanity, to serve which I have spent thousands of pounds. But I bear no malice.”
He took a bottle from the long oaken buffet against the wall, selected two glasses with scrupulous care, and filled them from the bottle.
“We will drink our mutual good health,” he said with his old courtesy, and, lifting his glass to his lips, drank it with that show of enjoyment with which the old-time lovers of wine marked their approval of rare vintages.
“You’re not drinking?” he said in surprise.
“Somebody else has drunk.”
===Chinese===
"您若当真要我今晚随您进城，我自然去。"他说，"按您自己的评判，甚至按您上级的评判，您也许是对的；可您终结了我的事业，便是对苦难众生做下一件残酷的坏事——为造福他们，我已花去数千英镑。不过我并无怨怼。"
他从靠墙的长橡木餐具柜上取过一瓶酒，一丝不苟地挑了两只杯子，斟满了。
"为彼此的健康干一杯。"他保持着惯常的礼数说道，举杯到唇边，带着旧时好酒之徒称许珍酿时的那种欣然，一饮而尽。
"您不喝？"他讶道。
"另有别人喝过了。"

===Original===
There was a glass half empty on the buffet: Michael saw it for the first time.
“He did not seem to enjoy the wine.”
Mr. Longvale sighed.
“Very few people understand wine,” he said, dusting a speck from his coat. Then, drawing a silk handkerchief from his pocket, he stooped and dusted his boots daintily.
Michael was standing on a strip of hearthrug in front of the fireplace, his hand on his gun, tense but prepared for the moment of trial. Whence the danger would come, what form it would take, he could not guess. But danger was there﻿—danger terrible and ruthless, emphasized rather than relieved by the suavity of the old man’s tone﻿—he felt in the creep of his flesh.
===Chinese===
餐具柜上放着一只半空的酒杯：迈克尔头一回注意到它。
"他像是没喝出这酒的妙处。"
朗维尔先生叹了口气。
"懂酒的人太少了。"他说着，从上衣掸去一粒尘屑。随后，他从口袋里抽出一方丝绸手帕，俯身斯斯文文地擦拭靴子。
迈克尔站在壁炉前的一条炉边地毯上，手按着枪，全身绷紧，静候那考验的一刻。危险从何而来、以何种面目出现，他猜不着。可危险就在那里——可怕而无情，老人语气愈谦和，那危险愈分明——他肌肤的战栗如此告诉他。

===Original===
“You see, my dear sir,” Longvale went on, still dusting his boots.
And then, before Michael could realize what had happened, he had grasped the end of the rug on which the detective was standing and pulled it with a quick jerk toward him. Before he could balance himself, Michael had fallen with a crash to the floor, his head striking the oaken panelling, his pistol sliding along the polished floor. In a flash, the old man was on him, had flung him over on his face and dragged his hands behind him. Michael tried to struggle, but he was as a child in that powerful grip, placed at such a disadvantage as he was. He felt the touch of cold steel on his wrists, there was a click, and, exerting all his strength, he tried to pull his other hand away. But gradually, slowly, it was forced back, and the second cuff snapped.
===Chinese===
"您瞧，亲爱的先生，"朗维尔接着说，手上仍擦着靴子。
话音未落——迈克尔还没弄清发生了什么，老人已攥住他脚下地毯的一头，猛地朝自己一拽。他还没稳住身子，已轰然摔倒在地，头撞上橡木护板，手枪滑过光亮的地板。电光石火间，老人已压到他身上，把他翻成俯卧，双手反剪到背后。迈克尔奋力挣扎，可在那双铁腕之下、在那样不利的境地中，他不过是个孩童。他觉出腕上一凉，咔哒一声；他用尽全身力气想抽回另一只手，可它被缓缓地、渐渐地硬扳了回去，第二只铐环咔地扣死。

===Original===
There were footsteps on the path outside the cottage. The old man straightened himself to pull off his silken gown and wrapped it round and round the detective’s head, and then a knock came at the door. One glance to see that his prisoner was safe, and Longvale extinguished the lamp, blew out one of the candles, and carried the other into the passage. He was in his shirtsleeves, and the Scotland Yard officer, who was the caller, apologized for disturbing a man who had apparently been brought down from his bedroom to answer the knock.
“Have you seen Mr. Brixan?”
“Mr. Brixan? Yes, he was here a few minutes ago. He went on to Chichester.”
===Chinese===
小屋外的小径上响起了脚步声。老人直起身，脱下绸袍，一圈一圈裹住侦探的头，随后门上响起了敲击声。他扫了一眼，确认俘虏万无一失，便熄了油灯，吹灭一支蜡烛，把另一支带进过道。他只穿着衬衫，那位来访的苏格兰场警员还连声道歉，怪自己搅扰了一位显然是从卧室里叫下来应门的人。
"您见到布里克森先生了吗？"
"布里克森先生？见了，几分钟前还在这儿。他往奇切斯特去了。"

===Original===
Michael heard the voices, but could not distinguish what was being said. The silken wrapper about his head was suffocating him, and he was losing his senses when the old man came back alone, unfastened the gown, and put it on himself.
“If you make a noise I will sew your lips together,” he said, so naturally and good-naturedly that it seemed impossible he would carry his threat into execution. But Michael knew that he was giving chapter and verse; he was threatening that which his ancestor had often performed. That beautiful old man, nicknamed by the gallants of Louis’ court “Monsieur de Paris,” had broken and hanged and beheaded, but he had also tortured men. There were smoke-blackened rooms in the old Bastille where that venerable old hangman had performed nameless duties without blenching.
“I am sorry in many ways that you must go on,” said the old man, with genuine regret in his voice. “You are a young man for whom I have a great deal of respect. The law to me is sacred, and its officers have an especially privileged place in my affections.”
===Chinese===
迈克尔听得见说话声，却辨不清说的什么。裹在头上的绸袍闷得他透不过气，正当知觉将失，老人独自回来了，解开绸袍，穿回自己身上。
"你若出声，我就把你的嘴唇缝起来。"他说得那样自然、那样和气，仿佛绝不可能将威胁付诸实行。可迈克尔知道，他这话说得有根有据；他威胁的，正是他的先人屡屡做过的事。那位俊美的老人，路易宫廷的风流人物赠他雅号"巴黎先生"（Monsieur de Paris）——他折骨、绞人、断头，可他也拷问过人。古老的巴士底狱（Bastille）里，有几间熏黑的屋子，那位可敬的老刽子手曾在其中面不改色地履行过无名之职。
"你非走这一步不可，我在许多方面都很遗憾。"老人说，声音里透着真切的惋惜，"你是个年轻人，我对你极为敬重。法律于我是神圣的，执法官在我心里占着一个格外优待的位置。"

===Original===
He pulled open a drawer of the buffet and took out a large serviette, folded it with great care and fixed it tightly about Michael’s mouth. Then he raised him up and sat him on a chair.
“If I were a young and agile man, I would have a jest which would have pleased my uncle Charles Henry. I would fix your head on the top of the gates of Scotland Yard! I’ve often examined the gates with that idea in my mind. Not that I thought of you, but that some day providence might send me a very high official, a Minister, even a Prime Minister. My uncle, as you know, was privileged to destroy kings and leaders of parties﻿—Danton, Robespierre, every great leader save Murat. Danton was the greatest of them all.”
There was an excellent reason why Michael should not answer. But he was his own cool self again, and though his head was aching from the violent knock it had received, his mind was clear. He was waiting now for the next move, and suspected he would not be kept waiting long. What scenes had this long dining-room witnessed! What moments of agony, mental and physical! It was the very antechamber to death.
===Chinese===
他拉开餐具柜的一只抽屉，取出一方大餐巾，细心叠好，紧紧蒙住迈克尔的嘴。随后把他扶起来，安置在椅子上。
"我若是还年轻灵便，倒要开一个我叔父查尔斯·亨利（Charles Henry）准会赏识的玩笑。把你的头安在苏格兰场大门顶上！我常揣着这个念头打量那两扇大门。倒不是想着你，而是盼着有朝一日上天赐我一位大员，一位大臣，甚至一位首相。我的叔父，你也知道，是有幸处决过帝王和党魁的人——丹东（Danton）、罗伯斯庇尔（Robespierre），除米拉（Murat）之外的每一位伟人。丹东是他们中间最伟大的。"
迈克尔不便回答，自有十足的理由。但他已恢复了素日的冷静，头虽因那一记重撞作痛，头脑却清明如常。他如今只等下一手棋，而且料定不会久等。这间长长的餐厅见识过多少场面！多少精神与肉体的极痛时刻！它简直就是死亡的前厅。

===Original===
Here, then, Bhag must have been rendered momentarily unconscious. Michael guessed the lure of drugged wine, that butyl chloride which was part of the murderer’s equipment. But for once Longvale had misjudged the strength of his prey. Bhag must have followed the brown folk to Dower House﻿—the man and woman whom the old man in his cunning had spared.
Michael was soon to discover what was going to happen. The old man opened the door of the buffet and took out a great steel hook, at the end of which was a pulley. Reaching up, he slipped the end of the hook into a steel bolt, fastened in one of the overhead beams. Michael had noticed it before and wondered what purpose it served. He was now to learn.
From the cupboard came a long coil of rope, one end of which was threaded through the pulley and fastened dexterously under the detective’s armpits. Stooping, Longvale lifted the carpet and rolled it up, and then Michael saw that there was a small trapdoor, which he raised and laid back. Below he could see nothing, but there came to him the sound of a man’s groaning.
“Now I think we can dispense with that, sir,” said Mr. Longvale, and untied the serviette that covered the detective’s mouth.
===Chinese===
这么说，巴格必是在这儿被一时弄昏的。迈克尔猜到了下了药的酒的诱饵，那丁基氯，凶手行头的一部分。只是这一回，朗维尔失算了自己猎物的膂力。巴格必是跟着那对棕色男女到了寡妇居——那两个被老人狡诈地留了活口的男人和女人。
迈克尔很快便要知道接下来会发生什么了。老人打开餐具柜的门，取出一枚大钢钩，钩尾装着一只滑轮。他踮身把钩头套进一枚钢螺栓——螺栓固定在一根顶梁上。这东西迈克尔先前就留意过，还纳闷它是做什么用的。现在他要见识了。
柜子里取出一长盘绳子，一端穿过滑轮，利落地拴在侦探的腋下。朗维尔俯身掀起地毯，卷到一旁，迈克尔这才看见下面有一扇小小的活动门。老人把门掀开搁好。门下什么也看不见，却有一声男人的呻吟传上来。
"我想这个现在用不着了，先生。"朗维尔先生说着，解开了蒙住侦探嘴的餐巾。

===Original===
This done, he pulled on the rope, seemingly without an effort, and Michael swung in midair. It was uncomfortable; he had an absurd notion that he looked a little ridiculous. The old man guided his feet through the opening and gradually paid out the rope.
“Will you be good enough to tell me when you touch ground,” he asked, “and I will come down to you?”
Looking up, Michael saw the square in the floor grow smaller and smaller, and for an unconscionable time he swung and swayed and turned in midair. He thought he was not moving, and then, without warning, his feet touched ground and he called out.
“Are you all right?” said Mr. Longvale pleasantly. “Do you mind stepping a few paces on one side? I am dropping the rope, and it may hurt you.”
===Chinese===
事毕，他拽动绳子，似乎毫不费力，迈克尔便悬在了半空。姿势很不好受；他荒唐地觉得自己这副样子有点可笑。老人引着他的双脚穿过洞口，缓缓放着绳。
"劳驾您落地时知会一声，"他问，"我这就下来陪您，可好？"
仰头望去，迈克尔只见地板上那个方口越缩越小，好一段没道理的长时间里，他在半空晃着、荡着、转着。他以为自己没动，随后毫无预兆地，双脚触了地，他喊了一声。
"您还好吗？"朗维尔先生愉快地说，"可否劳驾往旁边挪几步？我要撒绳了，怕砸着您。"

===Original===
Michael gasped, but carried out instructions, and presently he heard the swish of the falling line and the smack of it as it struck the ground. Then the trapdoor closed, and there was no other sound but the groaning near at hand.
“Is that you, Penne?”
“Who is it?” asked the other in a frightened voice. “Is it you, Brixan? Where are we? What has happened? How did I get here? That old devil gave me a drink. I ran out﻿—and that’s all I remember. I went to borrow his car. My God, I’m scared! The magneto of mine went wrong.”
“Did you shout when you ran from the house?”
===Chinese===
迈克尔喘了口气，照办了，不多时便听见长绳坠落的嗖声和着地的啪嗒声。随后活动门合上了，除近旁那声声呻吟，再无别的响动。
"是你吗，彭尼？"
"是谁？"对方声音发颤地问，"是你吗，布里克森？咱们这是在哪儿？出什么事了？我怎么到这儿来的？那老魔鬼给了我一杯喝的。我跑了出去——往后就什么都不记得了。我是去借他的车的。我的上帝，我怕呀！我那车的磁电机坏了。"
"你从宅子里跑出来时，喊了吗？"

===Original===
“I think I did. I felt this infernal poison taking effect and dashed out﻿—I don’t remember. Where are you, Brixan? The police will get us out of this, won’t they?”
“Alive, I hope,” said Michael grimly, and he heard the man’s frightened sob, and was sorry he had spoken.
“What is he? Who is he? Are these the caves? I’ve heard about them. It smells horribly earthy, doesn’t it? Can you see anything?”
“I thought I saw a light just then,” said Michael, “but my eyes are playing tricks.” And then: “Where is Adele Leamington?”
“God knows,” said the other. He was shivering, and Michael heard the sound of his chattering teeth. “I never saw her again. I was afraid Bhag would go after her. But he wouldn’t hurt her﻿—he is a queer devil. I wish he was here now.”
===Chinese===
"喊了吧。我觉出这该死的毒药发作，就冲了出去——记不清了。你在哪儿，布里克森？警察会把咱们弄出去的，对吧？"
"活着出去，我希望。"迈克尔冷冷地说，随即听见那人惊惧的啜泣，后悔自己开了口。
"他到底是什么人？他是谁？这就是那些洞穴？我听说过。这土腥味冲得吓人，是不是？你能看见什么吗？"
"方才我仿佛看见一点光。"迈克尔说，"许是眼睛发花。"随后又问："阿黛尔·利明顿在哪儿？"
"天知道。"对方说。他在发抖，迈克尔听得见他牙齿打战的声音，"我再没见过她。我怕巴格追她去了。可它不会伤她——它是个古怪的东西。我倒盼着它这会儿在这儿。"

===Original===
“I wish somebody was here,” said Michael sincerely.
He was trying to work his wrists loose of the handcuffs, though he knew that barehanded he stood very little chance against the old man. He had lost his pistol, and although, in the inside of his waistcoat, there remained intact the long, razor-sharp knife that had cleared him out of many a Continental scrape, the one infallible weapon when firearms failed, he knew that he would have no opportunity for its employment.
Sitting down, he tried to perform a trick that he had seen on a stage in Berlin﻿—the trick of bringing his legs through his manacled hands and so getting his hands in front of him, but he struggled without avail. There came the sound of a door opening, and Mr. Longvale’s voice.
“I won’t keep you a moment,” he said. He carried a lantern in his hand that swung as he walked, and seemed to intensify the gloom. “I don’t like my patients to catch cold.”
His laughter came echoing back from the vaulted roof of the cave, intensified hideously. Stopping, he struck a match and a brilliant light appeared. It was a vapour lamp fixed on a shelf of rock. Presently he lit another, and then a third and a fourth, and, in the white, unwinking light, every object in the cave stood out with startling distinctness. Michael saw the scarlet thing that stood in the cave’s centre, and, hardened as he was, and prepared for that fearsome sight, he shuddered.
It was a guillotine!
===Chinese===
"我倒盼着随便什么人在这儿。"迈克尔诚恳地说。
他正试着把手腕从手铐里褪出来，尽管他知道，赤手空拳对付那老人，胜算微乎其微。手枪丢了，不过马甲内衬里，那柄曾助他脱身于欧陆多少场险境的长长的剃刀般锋利的匕首还完好无损——火器失灵时万无一失的兵器——可他知道，自己不会有拔出它的机会。
他坐下身，试着玩一套他在柏林舞台上见过的把戏——双腿穿过铐住的手环，好把双手挪到身前——可挣扎半天，徒劳无功。这时传来开门声，还有朗维尔先生的声音。
"让二位久等，即刻便来。"他说。他手里提着一盏提灯，走动间一晃一晃，那晃动似乎让幽暗愈发深了。"我不喜欢我的病人着凉。"
他的笑声从洞窟的拱顶荡回来，可怖地放大了。他停下脚，划着一根火柴，一团亮光亮起。那是一盏固定在石台上的汽灯。不多时他又点亮一盏，然后是第三盏、第四盏，在那一色雪亮、从不闪烁的光里，洞中每件东西都轮廓惊人地分明。迈克尔看见了立在洞穴中央的那件猩红之物；他虽久经风浪，也早有备于那可怖的一见，还是打了个寒噤。
那是一架断头台！

## XVII Mr. Foss Makes a Suggestion / XVII 福斯先生献策

===Original===
Immersed in her beloved script, Adele Leamington sat on her bed, a box of *marron glacé* by her side, her knees tucked up, and a prodigious frown on her forehead. Try as hard as she would, she found it impossible to concentrate upon the intricate directions with which Foss invariably tortured the pages of his scenarios. Ordinarily she could have mastered this handicap, but, for some reason or other, individual thoughts which belonged wholly to her and had no association with her art came flowing forth in such volume that the lines were meaningless and the page, for all the instruction it gave to her, might as well have been blank.
*What* Michael Brixan? He was not her idea of a detective, and why was he staying in Chichester? Could it be …? She flushed at the thought and was angry with herself. It was hardly likely that a man who was engaged in unravelling a terrible crime would linger for the sake of being near to her. Was the Headhunter, the murderer, living near Chichester? She dropped her manuscript to her knees at the appalling thought.
The voice of her landlady aroused her.
“Will you see Mr. Foss, miss?”
She jumped up from the bed and opened the door.
===Chinese===
阿黛尔·利明顿坐在床上，沉浸在她心爱的剧本里，手边放着一盒糖霜栗子（marron glacé），双膝蜷起，眉心拧出老大的一个疙瘩。她怎么努力，也无法把心思集中在福斯总是用来折磨剧本纸页的那一套繁复提示上。平常这个难关她本可以攻下，可不知怎么，那些全然属于她自己、与她这门艺术毫不相干的私人念头汹涌而来，字句都失了意义，那纸页给她的指引，跟一页白纸没什么两样。
迈克尔·布里克森*到底*是什么人？他可不像她想象中的侦探，他又为什么留在奇切斯特？难道会是……？想到这里她脸红了，又生自己的气。一个正忙着侦破可怕罪案的人，不太可能为了待在她近旁而流连不去。难道猎头者，那个凶手，就住在奇切斯特附近？这个可怕的念头让她把剧本放落在膝头。
房东太太的声音唤回了她。
"您见福斯先生吗，小姐？"
她从床上跳起来，开了门。

===Original===
“Where is he?”
“I’ve put him in the parlour,” said the woman, who had grown a little more respectful of late. Possibly the rise of the extra to stardom was generally known in that small town, which took an interest in the fortunes of its one ewe lamb of a production company.
Lawley Foss was standing by the window, looking out, when she came into the room.
“Good afternoon, Adele,” he said genially. (He had never called her by her Christian name before, even if he had known it.)
“Good afternoon, Mr. Foss,” she said with a smile. “I’m sorry to hear that you have left us.”
===Chinese===
"他在哪儿？"
"我让他坐在客厅里了，"那女人说——她近来恭敬了几分。也许是临时演员跃升明星的事已传遍那座小城，小城素来关心它独苗珍宝般那家制片公司的兴衰。
她进屋时，劳利·福斯正站在窗前朝外望。
"午安，阿黛尔，"他热络地说。（从前他从不叫她的名字，哪怕他其实知道。）
"午安，福斯先生，"她微笑着说，"听说您离开了我们，真遗憾。"

===Original===
Foss lifted his shoulders in a gesture of indifference.
“The scope was a little too limited for my kind of work,” he said.
He was wondering if Mike had told her about the disc of paper on her window, and surmised rightly that he had not. Foss himself did not attach any significance to the white disc, accepting Gregory’s explanation, which was that, liking the girl, he wished to toss some flowers and a present, by way of a peace offering, through a window which he guessed would be open. Foss had thought him a lovesick fool, and had obliged him. The story that Knebworth had told he dismissed as sheer melodrama.
“Adele, you’re a foolish little girl to turn down a man like Gregory Penne,” he said, and saw by her face that he was on dangerous ground. “There’s no sense in getting up in the air; after all, we’re human beings, and it isn’t unnatural that Penne should have a crush on you. There’s nothing wrong in that. Hundreds of girls have dinner with men without there being anything sinister in it. I’m a friend of Penne’s, in a way, and I’m seeing him tonight on a very important and personal matter—will you come along?”
===Chinese===
福斯漠然地耸了耸肩。
"那份天地，对我这一路的工作来说未免太窄了，"他说。
他寻思迈克有没有把窗上纸片的事告诉她，猜对了——没有。福斯自己并没把那白圆片当回事，他信了格雷戈里的说法：他喜欢那姑娘，想丢几束花和一件礼物进去，算作赔罪的表示，丢进一扇他估摸会开着的窗。福斯只当他是个害相思的傻子，帮了他这个忙。至于尼布沃斯讲的那一套，他斥为纯粹的情节剧。
"阿黛尔，拒绝格雷戈里·彭尼这样的人，你可是个傻姑娘，"他说，随即从她脸上看出自己正踩着险地。"犯不着炸毛；说到底，大家都是人，彭尼迷上你，没什么不自然。这又没什么错。成百上千的姑娘跟男人吃饭，里头没有一点见不得人的东西。怎么说我也算彭尼的朋友，今晚要去见他，谈一件顶要紧的私事——你愿意同去吗？"

===Original===
She shook her head.
“There may be no harm in it,” she said, “but there is no pleasure in it either.”
“He’s a rich man and a powerful man,” said Foss impressively. “He could be of service to you.”
Again she shook her head.
“I want no other help than my own ability,” she said. “I nearly said ‘genius,’ but that would have sounded like conceit. I do not need the patronage of any rich man. If I cannot succeed without that, then I am a hopeless failure and am content to be one!”
===Chinese===
她摇了摇头。
"这也许无害，"她说，"但也无趣。"
"他是个有钱有势的人，"福斯郑重地说，"他能帮上你的忙。"
她又摇了摇头。
"除了我自己的本事，我不需要别的帮助，"她说，"我差点说'天才'，可那听着像自夸。我不需要任何有钱人的提携。离了这个我就成不了事，那我就是个无可救药的失败者，而当个失败者我也认了！"

===Original===
Still Foss lingered.
“I think I can manage without you,” he said, “but I’d have been glad of your cooperation. He’s crazy about you. If Mendoza knew that, she’d kill you!”
“Miss Mendoza?” gasped the girl. “But why? Does she—she know him?”
He nodded.
“Yes: very few people are aware of the fact. There was a time when he’d have done anything for her, and she was a wise girl: she let him help! Mendoza has money to burn and diamonds enough to fill the Jewel House.”
Adele listened, horror-stricken, incredulous, and he hastened to insure himself against Stella’s wrath.
===Chinese===
福斯还在磨蹭。
"没有你我我想也办得成，"他说，"不过有你合作我还是高兴的。他为你神魂颠倒。门多萨要是知道了，会杀了你！"
"门多萨小姐？"姑娘倒抽一口气，"可是为什么？她——她认识他？"
他点了点头。
"认识：知道这层关系的没有几个人。有一阵子，为她他什么都肯干，她是个精明的姑娘：由着他出力！门多萨的钱多得烧手，钻石多得能塞满伦敦塔的珠宝库。"
阿黛尔听得又惊又骇，不敢相信，他赶紧先给自己上保险，防着斯特拉发作。

===Original===
“You needn’t tell her I told you—this is in strict confidence. I don’t want to get on the wrong side of Penne either,” he shivered. “That man’s a devil!”
Her lips twitched.
“And yet you calmly ask me to dine with him, and hold out the bait of Miss Mendoza’s diamonds!”
“I suppose you think she’s awful,” he sneered.
“I am very sorry for her,” said the girl quietly, “and I am determined not to be sorry for myself!”
She opened the door to him in silence, and in silence he took his departure. After all, he thought, there was no need for any outside help. In his breast pocket was a sheet of manuscript, written on the Headhunter’s typewriter. That ought to be worth thousands when he made his revelation.
===Chinese===
"可别告诉她是我说的——这话天知地知。我也不想站到彭尼的对立面去，"他打了个寒噤，"那人是个魔鬼！"
她的嘴唇抽动了一下。
"可你还平心静气地请我去跟他吃饭，又拿门多萨小姐的钻石当饵！"
"我猜你觉得她不堪，"他讥诮道。
"我很为她难过，"姑娘平静地说，"我也打定了主意，绝不让自己落到让人难过的地步！"
她默默为他开了门，他也默默离去。说到底，他想，外援其实用不着。他胸前的口袋里揣着一页稿纸，是用猎头者的打字机打的。等到他揭开底牌，这一页该值几千英镑。

## V Mr. Lawley Foss / V 劳利·福斯先生

===Original===
“What is wrong?” asked Adele, seeing the young man’s grave face.
“Where did this come from?”
He showed her the sheet of typewritten script.
“I don’t know: it was with the other sheets. I knew, of course, that it didn’t belong to ‘Roselle.’ ”
“Is that the play you’re acting in?” he asked quickly. And then: “Who would know?”
“Mr. Knebworth.”
===Chinese===
"出什么事了？"阿黛尔看到年轻人凝重的脸色，问道。
"这页纸是哪儿来的？"
他把那页打字的剧本递给她看。
"我不知道：它跟别的散页夹在一起。我当然看得出来，它不属于《罗塞尔》。"
"那是你演的那部戏？"他急问。接着又问："谁知道内情？"
"尼布沃斯先生。"

===Original===
“Where shall I find him?”
“You go through that door,” she said, “and you will find him on the studio floor.”
Without a word, he walked quickly into the building. Instinctively he knew which of the party was the man he sought. Jack Knebworth looked up under lowering brows at the sight of the stranger, for he was a stickler for privacy in business hours; but before he could demand an explanation, Michael was up to him.
“Are you Mr. Knebworth?”
Jack nodded.
“I surely am,” he said.
===Chinese===
"上哪儿能找到他？"
"穿过那道门，"她说，"摄影棚里就能找到他。"
他二话不说，快步走进厂房。他本能地认出了人群里他要找的那个人。杰克·尼布沃斯眉峰压低，抬眼看见这个闯进来的陌生人——上班时间他最忌讳旁人打扰；可他还没来得及质问，迈克尔已经到了他跟前。
"您是尼布沃斯先生？"
杰克点了点头。
"正是，"他说。

===Original===
“May I speak to you for two minutes?”
“I can’t speak to anybody for one minute,” growled Jack. “Who are you, anyway, and who let you in?”
“I am a detective from the Foreign Office,” said Michael, lowering his voice, and Jack’s manner changed.
“Anything wrong?” he asked, as he accompanied the detective into his sanctum.
Jack laid down the sheet of paper with its typed characters on the table.
“Who wrote that?” he asked.
===Chinese===
"能跟您说两分钟的话吗？"
"我连一分钟都匀不出来，"杰克咆哮道，"你到底是谁？谁放你进来的？"
"我是外交部的侦探，"迈克尔压低声音说，杰克的态度立刻变了。
"出了什么事？"他一面问，一面把侦探让进自己的私室。
杰克把那页打满字的纸放在桌上。
"这是谁写的？"他问。

===Original===
Jack Knebworth looked at the manuscript and shook his head.
“I’ve never seen it before. What is it all about?”
“You’ve never seen this manuscript at all?”
“No, I’ll swear to that, but I dare say my scenario man will know all about it. I’ll send for him.”
He touched a bell, and, to the clerk who came:
“Ask Mr. Lawley Foss to come quickly,” he said.
===Chinese===
杰克·尼布沃斯看了看稿子，摇了摇头。
"我从没见过这东西。这是怎么回事？"
"这份稿子您确实从没见过？"
"没有，这一点我可以发誓，不过我那个编剧部的人多半知道底细。我派人去叫他。"
他按了按铃，对进来的职员吩咐：
"请劳利·福斯先生（Mr. Lawley Foss）马上过来，"他说。

===Original===
“The reading of books, plots and material for picture plays is entirely in the hands of my scenario manager,” he said. “I never see a manuscript until he considers it’s worth producing; and even then, of course, the picture isn’t always made. If the story happens to be a bad one, I don’t see it at all. I’m not so sure that I haven’t lost some good stories, because Foss”—he hesitated a second—“well, he and I don’t see exactly eye to eye. Now, Mr. Brixan, what is the trouble?”
In a few words Michael explained the grave significance of the typewritten sheet.
“The Headhunter!” Jack whistled.
There came a knock at the door, and Lawley Foss slipped into the room. He was a thinnish man, dark and saturnine of face, shifty of eye. His face was heavily lined as though he suffered from some chronic disease. But the real disease which preyed on Lawley Foss was the bitterness of mind that comes to a man at war with the world. There had been a time in his early life when he thought that same world was at his feet. He had written two plays that had been produced and had run a few nights. Thereafter, he had trudged from theatre to theatre in vain, for the taint of failure was on him, and no manager would so much as open the brown-covered manuscripts he brought to them. Like many another man, he had sought easy ways to wealth, but the Stock Exchange and the race track had impoverished him still further.
He glanced suspiciously at Michael as he entered.
===Chinese===
"小说、情节、电影剧本素材的审读，全在我那位剧本部主管手里，"他说，"他认为值得投拍的稿子，我才能见到；即便那样，片子也未必拍得成。要是故事碰巧不行，我干脆连看都不看。我说不准是不是因此错过了些好故事，因为福斯这个人"——他迟疑了一秒——"呃，他跟我看事情不怎么合拍。好了，布里克森先生，到底有什么麻烦？"
迈克尔三言两语讲明了那页打字稿的重大含义。
"猎头者！"杰克吹了声口哨。
有人敲门，劳利·福斯侧身进了屋。他是个瘦削的男人，面色黝黑阴沉，眼神游移。脸上刻着深深的皱纹，仿佛受着某种慢性病的折磨。但真正啃噬着劳利·福斯的病，是一个与全世界为敌的人心里的那种怨毒。年轻时他也曾以为世界就在自己脚下。他写过两部戏，都上演了，都只演了几个晚上。此后他一家剧院一家剧院地奔波，全属徒劳——失败的污名沾在了他身上，没有哪个经理肯翻开他送去的棕色纸包剧本。和许多人一样，他也寻过发财的捷径，可证券交易所和赛马场反而让他更加一贫如洗。
他进门时狐疑地瞥了迈克尔一眼。

===Original===
“I want to see you, Foss, about a sheet of script that’s got amongst the ‘Roselle’ script,” said Jack Knebworth. “May I tell Mr. Foss what you have told me?”
Michael hesitated for a second. Some cautioning voice warned him to keep the question of the Headhunter a secret. Against his better judgment he nodded.
Lawley Foss listened with an expressionless face whilst the old director explained the significance of the interpolated sheet, then he took the page from Jack Knebworth’s hand and examined it. Not by a twitch of his face or a droop of his eyelid did he betray his thoughts.
“I get a lot of stuff in,” he said, “and I can’t immediately place this particular play; but if you’ll let me take it to my office, I will look up my books.”
Again Michael considered. He did not wish that piece of evidence to pass out of his hands; and yet without confirmation and examination, it was fairly valueless. He reluctantly agreed.
===Chinese===
"我要见你，福斯，是为一张混进《罗塞尔》剧本里的稿纸，"杰克·尼布沃斯说，"我可以把你刚才告诉我的对福斯先生讲吗？"
迈克尔迟疑了一秒。心里有个告诫的声音提醒他把猎头者的事保密。他违背自己的明智判断，点了头。
老导演说明这页外来稿纸的分量时，劳利·福斯面无表情地听着，随后从杰克·尼布沃斯手里接过那页纸细看。脸上没有一丝抽动，眼皮没有一下低垂，他丝毫不曾泄露自己的心思。
"送到我手里的东西多得很，"他说，"这一页我一时对不上号；不过要是让我带回办公室，我可以查查登记簿。"
迈克尔又斟酌了一番。他不愿这件物证脱手；可不经查证核实，它也几乎一文不值。他勉强同意了。

===Original===
“What do you make of that fellow?” asked Jack Knebworth when the door had closed upon the writer.
“I don’t like him,” said Michael bluntly. “In fact, my first impressions are distinctly unfavourable, though I am probably doing the poor gentleman a very great injustice.”
Jack Knebworth sighed. Foss was one of his biggest troubles, sometimes bulking larger than the temperamental Mendoza.
“He certainly is a queer chap,” he said, “though he’s diabolically clever. I never knew a man who could take a plot and twist it as Lawley Foss can—but he’s—difficult.”
“I should imagine so,” said Michael dryly.
===Chinese===
"你觉得那人怎么样？"那位编剧走出门去后，杰克·尼布沃斯问。
"我不喜欢他，"迈克尔直率地说，"说实在的，我的第一印象坏透了，尽管我很可能大大冤枉了这位可怜的先生。"
杰克·尼布沃斯叹了口气。福斯是他最头疼的难题之一，有时比那位艺术脾气发作起来的门多萨还难对付。
"他确实是个怪人，"他说，"不过鬼聪明鬼聪明的。我从没见过谁能像劳利·福斯那样，拿起一个情节就能翻出百般花样——可他就是——难缠。"
"可以想见，"迈克尔淡淡地说。

===Original===
They passed out into the studio, and Michael sought the troubled girl to explain his crudeness. There were tears of vexation in her eyes when he approached her, for his startling disappearance with a page of the script had put all thoughts of the play from her mind.
“I am sorry,” he said penitently. “I almost wish I hadn’t come.”
“And I quite wish it,” she said, smiling in spite of herself. “What was the matter with that page you took—you *are* a detective, aren’t you?”
“I admit it,” said Michael recklessly.
“Did you speak the truth when you said that my uncle—” she stopped, at a loss for words.
“No, I did not,” replied Michael quietly. “You uncle is dead, Miss Leamington.”
===Chinese===
两人走进摄影棚，迈克尔去找那位心绪不宁的姑娘，为自己的鲁莽赔罪。他走近时，她眼里噙着恼火的泪花——他抱着剧本里的一页纸突然失踪，把她满脑子关于这部戏的念头全都吓跑了。
"对不起，"他悔疚地说，"我几乎后悔不该来。"
"我还真巴不得呢，"她说，不由自主地笑了，"你拿走的那页纸到底怎么回事——你*真是*个侦探，对吧？"
"我承认，"迈克尔豁出去地说。
"你先前说我伯伯的事，是真话吗——"她停住了，不知如何措辞。
"不，不是真话，"迈克尔平静地答道，"你伯伯已经死了，利明顿小姐。"

===Original===
“Dead!” she gasped.
He nodded.
“He was murdered, in extraordinary circumstances.”
Suddenly her face went white.
“He wasn’t the man whose head was found at Esher?”
“How did you know?” he asked sharply.
“It was in this morning’s newspaper,” she said, and inwardly he cursed the sleuthhound of a reporter who had got on to the track of this latest tragedy.
She had to know sooner or later: he satisfied himself with that thought.
===Chinese===
"死了！"她倒抽一口气。
他点了点头。
"他是被谋杀的，情形极不寻常。"
她的脸霎时变白了。
"他不会就是在埃舍尔被人发现脑袋的那个人吧？"
"你怎么知道的？"他厉声问。
"今天早上的报纸登了，"她说。他心里暗暗咒骂那个嗅探到这桩最新惨案踪迹的猎犬般的记者。
她迟早总要知道的——他用这个念头宽解自己。

===Original===
The return of Foss relieved him of further explanations. The man spoke for a while with Jack Knebworth in a low voice, and then the director beckoned Michael across.
“Foss can’t trace this manuscript,” he said, handing back the sheet. “It may have been a sample page sent in by a contributor, or it may have been a legacy from our predecessors. I took over a whole lot of manuscript with the studio from a bankrupt production company.”
He looked impatiently at his watch.
“Now, Mr. Brixan, if it’s possible I should be glad if you would excuse me. I’ve got some scenes to shoot ten miles away, with a leading lady from whose little head you’ve scared every idea that will be of the slightest value to me.”
Michael acted upon an impulse.
“Would you mind my coming out with you to shoot—that means to photograph, doesn’t it? I promise you I won’t be in the way.”
Old Jack nodded curtly, and ten minutes later Michael Brixan was sitting side by side with the girl in a charabanc which was carrying them to the location. That he should be riding with the artistes at all was a tribute to his nerve rather than to his modesty.
===Chinese===
福斯回来了，省得他再多作解释。那人和杰克·尼布沃斯低声说了一阵，随后导演朝迈克尔招手让他过去。
"福斯查不出这份稿子的来历，"他说着把那页纸递了回来，"也许是有投稿人寄来的样页，也许是前任们留下的旧物。我从一家破产的电影制作公司手里连片场带一大批剧本一起接了过来。"
他不耐烦地看了看表。
"好了，布里克森先生，要是方便的话，恕我失陪。我十英里外还有几场戏要拍，对手是位领衔女主角——她那颗小脑袋里对我稍有价值的一点子，都被你吓跑了。"
迈克尔一时心血来潮。
"您介意我跟你们一起去拍——'拍'就是拍照的意思，对吧？我保证不碍事。"
老杰克生硬地点了点头。十分钟后，迈克尔·布里克森已和那姑娘并肩坐在一辆开往外景地的游览车里。他能跟演员们坐在一处，靠的是他的脸皮厚，而非谦逊。

## II Mr. Sampson Longvale Calls / II 桑普森·朗维尔先生来访

===Original===
Adele Leamington waited till the studio was almost empty before she came to where the white-haired man sat crouched in his canvas chair, his hands thrust into his trousers pockets, a malignant scowl on his forehead.
It was not a propitious moment to approach him: nobody knew that better than she.
“Mr. Knebworth, may I speak to you?”
He looked up slowly. Ordinarily he would have risen, for this middle-aged American in normal moments was the soul of courtesy. But just at that moment, his respect for womanhood was something below zero. His look was blank, though the director in him instinctively approved her values. She was pretty, with regular features, a mop of brown hair in which the sunshine of childhood still lingered. Her mouth firm, delicately shaped, her figure slim—perfect in many ways.
Jack had seen many beautiful extras in his career, and had passed through stages of enthusiasm and despair as he had seen them translated to the screen—pretty wooden figures without soul or expression, gauche of movement, hopeless. Too pretty to be clever, too conscious of their beauty to be natural. Dolls without intelligence or initiative—just “extras” who could wear clothes in a crowd, who could smile and dance mechanically, fit for extras and nothing else all the days of their lives.
===Chinese===
阿黛尔·利明顿一直等到摄影棚几乎走空了，才走到白发老头蜷坐的帆布椅旁——他双手插在裤兜里，额头拧着一股凶巴巴的怒纹。
此刻去找他绝非吉时：这一点没有人比她更清楚。
"尼布沃斯先生，我能跟您说句话吗？"
他缓缓抬起头来。换作平常他会站起身，因为这位中年美国人在正常时候是礼节的化身。可就在那一刻，他对女性的敬意跌到了零点以下。他目光漠然，不过骨子里的导演本能还是认可了她的各项条件。她生得漂亮，五官端正，一头蓬松的棕发里还残留着童年的阳光。嘴唇坚定而秀气，身材苗条——从许多方面看都无可挑剔。
杰克职业生涯里见过的漂亮临时演员多了，眼看着她们被搬到银幕上，他也经历过从热望到绝望的各个阶段——漂亮的木头人，没有灵魂，没有表情，动作笨拙，无可救药。漂亮得过了头所以不聪明，太在意自己的美貌所以不自然。没有头脑、没有主动性的洋娃娃——不过是能在人群里穿衣裳、能机械地微笑和跳舞的"临时演员"，一辈子只配当临时演员，别的什么都不成。

===Original===
“Well?” he asked brusquely.
“Is there a part I could play in this production, Mr. Knebworth?” she asked.
His shaven lips curled.
“Aren’t you playing a part, Miss—can’t remember your name—Leamington, is it?”
“I’m certainly playing—I’m one of the figures in the background,” she smiled. “I don’t want a big part, but I’m sure I could do better than I have done.”
“I’m mighty sure you couldn’t do worse than some people,” he growled. “No, there’s no part for you, friend. There’ll be no story to shoot unless things alter. That’s what!”
===Chinese===
"什么事？"他没好气地问。
"这部片子里有没有我能演的角色，尼布沃斯先生？"她问。
他刮得光光的嘴唇撇了撇。
"你不是正在演着角色吗，小姐——名字想不起来了——利明顿，是吧？"
"我当然在演——我是背景里的人影之一，"她笑道，"我不要多大的角色，可我肯定自己能比现在演得好。"
"我倒敢肯定你不会比某些人演得更糟，"他咆哮道，"不，没你的角色，朋友。除非情况有变，否则连要拍的故事都没有。就是这么回事！"

===Original===
She was going away when he recalled her.
“Left a good home, I guess?” he said. “Thought picture-making meant a million dollars a year an’ a new automobile every Thursday? Or maybe you were holding down a good job as a stenographer and got it under your toque that you’d make Hollywood feel small if you got your chance? Go back home, kid, and tell the old man that a typewriter’s got a sunlight arc beaten to death as an instrument of commerce.”
The girl smiled faintly.
“I didn’t come into pictures because I was stage-struck, if that is what you mean, Mr. Knebworth. I came in knowing just how hard a life it might be. I have no parents.”
He looked up at her curiously.
===Chinese===
她正要走，他又把她叫住了。
"抛下了好好的家，我猜？"他说，"以为拍电影就是一年一百万美元、每个星期四换一辆新汽车？要不就是你本来稳稳当当做着速记员的好差事，脑袋一热，觉得只要有机会就能让好莱坞相形见绌？回家去吧，小姑娘，告诉你家老爷子：论做生意的家伙什，打字机可比太阳弧光灯强出一百倍。"
姑娘淡淡一笑。
"我进电影这一行不是因为痴迷舞台，如果您是这个意思的话，尼布沃斯先生。我是明知这条路会有多苦才进来的。我没有父母。"
他好奇地抬起头看了她一眼。

===Original===
“How do you live?” he asked. “There’s no money in ‘extra’ work—not on this lot, anyway. Might be if I was one of those billion dollar directors who did pictures with chariot races. But I don’t. My ideal picture has got five characters.”
“I have a little income from my mother, and I write,” said the girl.
She stopped as she saw him looking past her to the studio entrance, and, turning her head, saw a remarkable figure standing in the doorway. At first she thought it was an actor who had made up for a film test.
The newcomer was an old man, but his great height and erect carriage would not have conveyed that impression at a distance. The tight-fitting tailcoat, the trousers strapped to his boots, the high collar and black satin stock belonged to a past age, though they were newly made. The white linen bands that showed at his wrists were goffered, his double-breasted waistcoat of grey velvet was fastened by golden buttons. He might have stepped from a family portrait of one of those dandies of the ’fifties. He held a tall hat in one gloved hand, a hat with a curly brim, and in the other a gold-topped walking-stick. The face, deeply lined, was benevolent and kind, and he seemed unconscious of his complete baldness.
===Chinese===
"那你怎么过日子？"他问，"'临时演员'这行当挣不着钱——至少在我这片场挣不着。要是我也是那种十亿美元身家、拍战车竞赛大场面的导演，或许还行。可我不是。我理想中的影片，一共五个角色。"
"我母亲留给我一小笔进项，另外我还写点东西，"姑娘说。
话说到一半她停住了，只见他的目光越过她投向摄影棚入口；她转过头，看见门口站着一个不同寻常的身影。起初她还以为是哪个演员化好了妆来试镜。
来的是位老人，但他那高大的身材和挺直的腰板，隔远了看绝显不出老态。紧身的燕尾服、用带子系在靴口上的裤脚、高耸的衣领和黑缎领巾，都是上一个时代的样式，衣料却是新做的。手腕处露出的白亚麻衬边烫着褶裥，灰色天鹅绒双排扣马甲由金纽扣扣着。他简直像是从十九世纪五十年代某位纨绔子弟的家族画像里走出来的人物。一只戴手套的手里拿着高顶礼帽，帽檐卷曲，另一只手拄一根金头手杖。那张刻满皱纹的脸慈祥和善，他似乎对自己的一头秃顶浑然不觉。

===Original===
Jack Knebworth was out of his chair in a second and walked toward the stranger.
“Why, Mr. Longvale, I am glad to see you—did you get my letter? I can’t tell you how much obliged I am to you for the loan of your house.”
Sampson Longvale, of the Dower House! She remembered now. He was known in Chichester as “the old-fashioned gentleman,” and once, when she was out on location, somebody had pointed out the big, rambling house, with its weed-grown garden and crumbling walls, where he lived.
