“I thought I would come over and see you,” said the big man.
His voice was rich and beautifully modulated. She did not remember having heard a voice quite as sweet, and she looked at the eccentric figure with a new interest.
===Chinese===
杰克·尼布沃斯（Jack Knebworth）一秒钟就从椅子里蹦了起来，朝那位陌生人走去。
"哎呀，朗维尔先生（Mr. Longvale），真高兴见到您——收到我的信了吗？您肯把房子借给我们，我真不知道该怎么谢您才好。"
桑普森·朗维尔（Sampson Longvale），遗孀宅（Dower House）的主人！她想起来了。他在奇切斯特有"老派绅士"之称，有一回她出外景，有人指给她看过那座杂乱铺展的大宅子，园中荒草丛生，院墙斑驳，他就住在那里。
"我想着过来瞧瞧你们，"大个子说。
他的嗓音醇厚，抑扬顿挫恰到好处。她不记得听过这么悦耳的嗓音，于是带着新的兴致打量起这位古怪的人物。

===Original===
“I can only hope that the house and grounds are suitable to your requirements. I am afraid they are in sad disorder, but I cannot afford to keep the estate in the same condition as my grandfather did.”
“Just what I want, Mr. Longvale. I was afraid you might be offended when I told you—”
The old gentleman interrupted him with a soft laugh.
“No, no, I wasn’t offended, I was amused. You needed a haunted house: I could even supply that quality, though I will not promise you that my family ghost will walk. The Dower House has been haunted for hundreds of years. A former occupant in a fit of frenzy murdered his daughter there, and the unhappy lady is supposed to walk. I have never seen her, though many years ago one of my servants did. Fortunately, I am relieved of that form of annoyance: I no longer keep servants in the house,” he smiled, “though, if you care to stay the night, I shall be honoured to entertain five or six of your company.”
Knebworth heaved a sigh of relief. He had made diligent inquiries and found that it was almost impossible to secure lodgings in the neighbourhood, and he was most anxious to take night pictures, and for one scene he particularly desired the peculiar light value which he could only obtain in the early hours of the morning.
===Chinese===
"但愿宅子和园子合乎你们的要求。恐怕它们都荒败失修了，我实在没财力把这份产业维持在我祖父当年那个样子。"
"正合我意，朗维尔先生。我还担心说出来您会见怪——"
老绅士轻声一笑，打断了他。
"不，不，我没有见怪，我是觉得有趣。你们需要一幢闹鬼的房子：这个我甚至也能供应，虽然我不能向你们保证我家的鬼魂会出来游荡。遗孀宅闹鬼已经闹了几百年了。早年间有位住户在狂怒之下在那里杀了自己的女儿，据说这位不幸的女士至今还在宅中出没。我从没见过她，不过许多年前，我的一个仆人见过。所幸这种烦恼已与我无缘：宅子里我已不再雇仆人了，"他微笑道，"不过，你们若肯留下过夜，能招待贵公司五六位客人，我将深感荣幸。"
尼布沃斯如释重负地舒了口气。他已四处打探过，发现这一带几乎租不到住处，而他一心想拍夜景，其中有一场戏，他特别需要那种只在凌晨时分才能获得的特殊光线效果。

===Original===
“I’m afraid that would give you a lot of trouble, Mr. Longvale,” he said. “And here and now I think we might discuss that delicate subject of—”
The old man stopped him with a gesture.
“If you are going to speak of money, please don’t,” he said firmly. “I am interested in cinematography; in fact, I am interested in most modern things. We old men are usually prone to decry modernity, but I find my chiefest pleasure in the study of those scientific wonders which this new age has revealed to us.”
He looked at the director quizzically.
“Some day you shall take a picture of me in the one role in which I think I should have no peer—a picture of me in the role of my illustrious ancestor.”
===Chinese===
"只怕那会给您添很多麻烦，朗维尔先生，"他说，"另外，咱们眼下正好可以把那件微妙的事谈谈——"
老人抬手止住了他。
"您要是想谈钱，那就免了，"他语气坚定地说，"我对电影摄影术很感兴趣；说实在的，我对大多数现代事物都感兴趣。我们这些老头子通常爱贬斥现代文明，可我最大的乐趣，恰恰是研究这个新时代向我们揭示的那些科学奇迹。"
他揶揄地望着导演。
"哪天你要给我拍一部片子，演那个我认为无人能与我匹敌的角色——让我扮演我那位显赫的先祖。"

===Original===
Jack Knebworth stared, half amused, half startled. It was no unusual experience to find people who wished to see themselves on the screen, but he never expected that little piece of vanity from Mr. Sampson Longvale.
“I should be glad,” he said formally. “Your people were pretty well known, I guess?”
Mr. Longvale sighed.
“It is my regret that I do not come from the direct line that included Charles Henry, the most historic member of my family. He was my great-uncle. I come from the Bordeaux branch of Longvales, which has made history, sir.” He shook his head regretfully.
“Are you French, Mr. Longvale?” asked Jack.
===Chinese===
杰克·尼布沃斯瞪大了眼，一半觉得好笑，一半感到惊愕。想上银幕的人他见得多了，可他万万没想到桑普森·朗维尔先生也会来这么一点虚荣。
"我很乐意，"他一本正经地说，"您家先辈想必相当有名吧？"
朗维尔先生叹了口气。
"遗憾的是，我并非查尔斯·亨利（Charles Henry）——我们家族最彪炳史册的人物——那一支嫡系的后人。他是我伯祖父。我出自朗维尔家的波尔多支系，先生，这一支也是创造过历史的。"他遗憾地摇了摇头。
"您是法国人，朗维尔先生？"杰克问。

===Original===
Apparently the old man did not hear him. He was staring into space. Then, with a start:
“Yes, yes, we were French. My great-grandfather married an English lady whom he met in peculiar circumstances. We came to England in the days of the directorate.”
Then, for the first time, he seemed aware of Adele’s presence, and bowed toward her.
“I think I must go,” he said, taking a huge gold watch from his fob pocket.
The girl watched them as they passed out of the hall, and presently she saw the “old-fashioned gentleman” pass the window, driving the oldest-fashioned car she had ever seen. It must have been one of the first motorcars ever introduced into the country, a great, upstanding, cumbersome machine, that passed with a thunderous sound and at no great speed down the gravel drive out of sight.
===Chinese===
老人显然没有听见。他怔怔地望着虚空。随后猛地一惊：
"是的，是的，我们本是法国人。我的曾祖父在特殊的境遇中结识了一位英国女士并娶了她。我们家是在督政府年代迁来英国的。"
这时他才仿佛第一次察觉阿黛尔（Adele）在场，朝她躬了躬身。
"我想我该走了，"他说着从表袋里掏出一块硕大的金表。
姑娘望着他们走出大厅，不一会儿，就见那位"老派绅士"驾车从窗前经过——那是她见过的式样最老的汽车。想必是这个国家最早引进的那批汽车之一，一部高大挺立、笨重臃肿的机器，轰隆隆地碾过砂砾车道，速度不快，渐渐消失在视线之外。

===Original===
Presently Jack Knebworth came slowly back.
“This craze for being screened certainly gets ’em—old or young,” he said. “Good night, Miss—forget your name—Leamington, ain’t it? Good night.”
She was halfway home before she realized that the conversation that she had plucked up such courage to initiate had ended unsatisfactorily for her, and she was as far away from her small part as ever.
===Chinese===
过了一会儿，杰克·尼布沃斯慢慢走了回来。
"上镜头这股瘾，确实老少通吃，"他说，"晚安，小姐——名字又忘了——利明顿，对吧？晚安。"
走到半路她才回过味来：她鼓足勇气开启的那场谈话，到头来还是没了下文，她离她的小角色依然和从前一样遥远。

## XXX The Advertisement / XXX 广告

===Original===
The question of the caves intrigued Michael more than any feature the case had presented. He bethought himself of Mr. Longvale, whose knowledge of the country was encyclopædic. That gentleman was out, but Michael met him, driving his antique car from Chichester. To say that he saw him is to mistake facts. The sound of that old car was audible long before it came into sight around a bend of the road. Michael drew up, Longvale following his example, and parked his car behind that ancient bus.
“Yes, it is rather noisy,” admitted the old man, rubbing his bald head with a brilliant bandana handkerchief. “I’m only beginning to realize the fact of late years. Personally, I do not think that a noiseless car could give me as much satisfaction. One feels that something is happening.”
“You ought to buy a —﻿—﻿—” said Michael with a smile, as he mentioned the name of a famous car.
“I thought of doing so,” said the other seriously, “but I love old things﻿—that is my eccentricity.”
Michael questioned him upon the caves, and, to his surprise, the old man immediately returned an affirmative.
===Chinese===
洞穴之谜比本案呈现过的任何疑点都更让迈克尔着迷。他想起朗维尔先生来，这位老先生对乡下风物的了解可谓包罗万象。他出门了，不过迈克尔在半路遇上了他——正开着那辆老爷车从奇切斯特回来。说"遇上"都嫌不准确：那辆老车的声响，早在它绕过路弯现身之前就先声夺人了。迈克尔把车停住，朗维尔也照办，把车泊在那辆老古董后面。
"是，它是吵了点儿。"老人承认道，用一条花色鲜艳的大手帕擦着秃头，"这话我近些年才慢慢觉出来。依我看，一辆无声的车未必能给我这么多满足。响着，人才觉得有事情在发生。"
"你该买一辆某某牌的。"迈克尔含笑说出一种名牌汽车的大名。
"我动过这个念头，"对方一本正经地说，"可我偏爱老物件——这是我的怪癖。"
迈克尔向他问起洞穴，不料老人立刻给了个肯定的答复。

===Original===
“Yes, I’ve heard of them frequently. When I was a boy, my father told me that the country round was honeycombed with caves, and that, if anybody was lucky enough to find them, they would discover great stores of brandy. Nobody has found them, as far as I know. There used to be an entrance over there.” He pointed in the direction of Griff Tower. “But many years ago﻿—”
He retold the familiar story of the landslide and of the passing out of two companies of gallant knights and squires, which probably the old man had got from the same source of information as Michael had drawn upon.
“The popular legend was that a subterranean river ran into the sea near Selsey Bill﻿—of course, some distance beneath the surface of the water. But, as you know, country people live on such legends. In all probability it is nothing but a legend.”
Inspector Lyle was waiting for the detective when he arrived, with news of a startling character.
===Chinese===
"听过，常听。我还是孩子的时候，父亲就告诉我，这一带乡下的地底下密布着洞穴，谁要有福气找到，就能发现大批窖藏的白兰地。就我所知，谁也没找到过。从前那儿有一个洞口。"他朝格里芬塔的方向指了指，"可是许多年前——"
他把那次地陷、两队英武骑士与乡绅葬身其下的老故事又讲了一遍——多半老人与迈克尔引的是同一来源。
"民间传说是有一条地下河，在塞尔西角（Selsey Bill）附近入海——当然，是在水面之下相当深的地方。不过你也知道，乡下人就靠这类传说过活。十有八九，不过是个传说罢了。"
他回来时，莱尔探长正等着他，带来一条惊人的消息。

===Original===
“The advertisement appeared in this morning’s *Daily Star*,” he said.
Michael took the slip of paper. It was identically worded with its predecessor.

> “Is your trouble of mind or body incurable? Do you hesitate on the brink of the abyss? Does courage fail you? Write to Benefactor, Box﻿—”

“There will be no reply till tomorrow morning. Letters are to be readdressed to a shop in the Lambeth Road, and the chief wants you to be ready to pick up the trail.”
===Chinese===
"广告上了今天早晨的《每日星报》（*Daily Star*）。"他说。
迈克尔接过那片剪报。措辞与先前那则一字不差。

> "你身心的疾苦是否已无可救药？你是否在深渊边缘踌躇不前？你是否失去了勇气？请致函'恩主'（Benefactor），信箱——"

"明天早上之前不会有动静。来信将转投兰贝斯路（Lambeth Road）的一家店铺，长官要你做好准备，随时接手跟踪。"

===Original===
The trail indeed proved to be well laid. At four o’clock on the following afternoon, a lame old woman limped into the newsagent’s shop on the Lambeth Road and inquired for a letter addressed to Mr. Vole. There were three waiting for her. She paid the fee, put the letters into a rusty old handbag and limped out of the shop, mumbling and talking to herself. Passing down the Lambeth Road, she boarded a tramcar en route for Clapham, and near the Common she alighted and, passing out of the region of middle-class houses, came to a jumble of tenements and ancient tumbledown dwellings.
Every corner she turned brought her to a street meaner than the last, and finally to a low, arched alleyway, the paving of which had not been renewed for years. It was a little cul-de-sac, its houses, built in the same pattern, joined wall to wall, and before the last of these she stopped, took out a key from her pocket and opened the door. She was turning to close it when she was aware that a man stood in the entrance, a tall, good-looking gentleman, who must have been on her heels all the time.
“Good afternoon, mother,” he said.
The old woman peered at him suspiciously, grumbling under her breath. Only hospital doctors and workhouse folk, people connected with charity, called women “mother”; and sometimes the police got the habit. Her grimy old face wrinkled hideously at this last unpleasant thought.
===Chinese===
这条线索果真布得周全。第二天下午四点，一个瘸腿老太婆一瘸一拐走进兰贝斯路的报店，询问有没有寄给沃尔先生（Mr. Vole）的信。有她三封。她付了邮资，把信塞进一只锈迹斑斑的旧手提包，嘴里咕咕哝哝、自言自语地出了店门。她沿兰贝斯路走了一段，上了一辆开往克拉珀姆（Clapham）的电车，在公地附近下了车，走出中产阶级住宅的地界，来到一片租屋与破旧老屋杂陈的地带。
每拐一个弯，眼前的街就更寒碜一分，最后是一条低矮的拱顶小巷，路面多年不曾翻修。那是一条小小的死巷，两侧房屋一个式样，墙挨着墙。她在最末一栋前停下，从口袋里掏出钥匙开了门。正要回身关门，她发觉门口站着一个男人，一位高个子的漂亮绅士——想必一路都尾随着她。
"午安，大娘。"他说。
老太婆满腹狐疑地打量他，低声嘟囔着。只有医院的医生和济贫院的人——同慈善沾边的人——才管女人叫"大娘"；有时警察也沾上这习惯。想到这最后一种不祥的可能，她那张肮脏的老脸可怕地皱作一团。

===Original===
“I want to have a little talk with you.”
“Come in,” she said shrilly.
The boarding of the passageway was broken in half a dozen places and was indescribably dirty, but it represented the spirit of pure hygiene compared with the stuffy horror which was her sitting-room and kitchen.
“What are you, horspital or p’lice?”
“Police,” said Michael. “I want three letters you’ve collected.”
===Chinese===
"我想同你聊几句。"
"进来吧。"她尖声道。
过道的地板有六七处破损，脏得难以形容，可比起她那间兼作起居室和厨房的、闷臭熏人的屋子来，还称得上是纯洁卫生的典范。
"你是个啥，医院的还是警察的？"
"警察。"迈克尔说，"我要你取来的那三封信。"

===Original===
To his surprise, the woman showed relief.
“Oh, is that all?” she said. “Well, that’s a job I do for a gentleman. I’ve done it for years. I’ve never had any complaint before.”
“What is his name?”
“Don’t know his name. Just whatever name happens to be on the letters. I send ’em on to him.”
From under a heap of rubbish she produced three envelopes, addressed in typewritten characters. The typewriting Michael recognized. They were addressed to a street in Guildford.
===Chinese===
出乎他意料，老太婆反倒松了口气。
"哦，就为这个？"她说，"嗐，那是我替一位先生办的差事。办了好些年了，先前从没人说过什么。"
"他叫什么名字？"
"名字不知道。信上写什么名头就是什么名头。我取了信给他转寄过去。"
她从一堆破烂底下翻出三只信封，地址都是打字机打的。那笔打字迈克尔认得。收信地址是吉尔福德（Guildford）的一条街。

===Original===
Michael took the letters from her handbag. Two of them he read; the third was a dummy which he himself had written. The most direct cross-examination, however, revealed nothing. The woman did the work, receiving a pound for her trouble, in a letter from the unknown, who told her where the letters were to be collected.
“She was a little mad and indescribably beastly,” said Michael in disgust when he reported, “and the Guildford inquiries don’t help us forward. There’s another agent there, who sends the letters back to London, which they never reach. That is the mystery of the proceeding. There simply isn’t such an address at London, and I can only suggest that they are intercepted en route. The Guildford police have that matter in hand.”
Staines was very worried.
“Michael, I oughtn’t to have put you on this job,” he said. “My first thoughts were best. Scotland Yard is kicking, and say that the meddling of outsiders is responsible for the Headhunter not being brought to justice. You know something of interdepartmental jealousy, and you don’t need me to tell you that I’m getting more kicks than I’m entitled to.”
Michael looked down at his chief reflectively.
===Chinese===
迈克尔从她手提包里取出信。两封他读了；第三封是诱饵，出自他自己的手笔。然而再直截了当的盘问也问不出个所以然。老太婆办这差事，酬劳一英镑，装在一封无名氏寄来的信里，信上还告诉她去何处取信。
"她有点疯癫，邋遢得没法形容。"迈克尔汇报时厌恶地说，"吉尔福德那头的查问也帮不上忙。那儿另有一个代办，把信寄回伦敦——可信根本到不了。这正是这趟流程的神秘之处。伦敦压根没有这么个地址，我只能说，信在中途被人截走了。吉尔福德警方正在办这一头。"
斯坦斯忧心忡忡。
"迈克尔，我不该把这差事派给你。"他说，"我的第一直觉才是对的。苏格兰场正闹意见，说是外人来插手，才害得猎头者迟迟归不了案。部门之间的倾轧你也略知一二，不用我说你也明白，如今我挨的骂早超出了我该领的份。"
迈克尔低头望着上司，思忖片刻。

===Original===
“I can get the Headhunter, but more than ever I’m convinced that we cannot convict him until we know a little more about﻿—the caves!”
Staines frowned.
“I don’t quite get you, Mike. Which caves are these?”
“There are some caves in the neighbourhood of Chichester. Foss knew about them and suspected their association with the Headhunter. Give me four days, Major, and I’ll have them both. And if I fail”﻿—he paused﻿—“if I fail, the next time you say good morning to me, I shall be looking up to you from the interior of one of the Headhunter’s boxes!”
===Chinese===
"猎头者我能抓到，可我比从前任何时候都确信：不把那些洞穴再多摸清一分，我们就定不了他的罪！"
斯坦斯皱起眉头。
"我不大明白你的意思，迈克。哪门子洞穴？"
"奇切斯特一带有几处洞穴。福斯知道它们，还疑心它们同猎头者有牵连。给我四天，少校，人和洞我一并拿下。倘若我失手"——他顿了顿——"倘若我失手，下回你再同我道早安时，我正从猎头者的一只盒子里仰望着你呢！"

## IX The Ancestor / IX 先祖

===Original===
The Dower House was away from the main road. A sprawling mass of low buildings, it stood behind untidy hedges and crumbling walls. Once the place had enjoyed the services of a lodge-keeper, but the tiny lodge was deserted, the windows broken, and there were gaps in the tiled roof. The gates had not been closed for generations; they were broken, and leant crazily against the walls to which they had been thrust by the last person who had employed them to guard the entrance to the Dower House.
What had once been a fair lawn was now a tangle of weeds. Thistle and mayweed grew knee-deep where the gallants of old had played their bowls; and it was clear to Michael, from his one glance, that only a portion of the house was used. In only one of the wings were the windows whole; the others were broken or so grimed with dirt, that they appeared to have been painted.
His amusement blended with curiosity, Michael saw for the first time the picturesque Mr. Sampson Longvale. He came out to meet them, his bald head glistening in the afternoon sunlight, his strapped fawn-coloured trousers, velvet waistcoat and old-fashioned stock completely supporting Gregory Penne’s description of him.
“Delighted to see you, Mr. Knebworth. I’ve a very poor house, but I offer you a very rich welcome! I have had tea served in my little dining-room. Will you please introduce me to the members of your company?”
The courtesy, the old-world spirit of dignity, were very charming, and Michael felt a warm glow toward this fine old man who brought to this modern atmosphere the love and the fragrance of a past age.
===Chinese===
遗孀宅离大路有一段距离。那是一片低矮房屋杂乱铺开的建筑群，藏在不整的树篱和颓坏的院墙后面。这里也曾雇得起门房，如今小门房空置着，窗子破碎，瓦顶上豁着口子。那两扇大门已有几代人没关过；早已破败，歪歪斜斜地靠在墙上——那是最后一个用它们看守遗孀宅入口的人把它们推过去靠住的。
昔日一片好草坪，如今杂草丛生。蓟草和母菊长到膝盖深，正好长在当年风流子弟们玩滚木球的地方；迈克尔一眼便看出，宅子只有一部分在使用。只有一侧翼房的窗子完好；其余的不是破碎，就是积垢厚得像是涂过漆。
迈克尔觉得有趣，又掺着好奇，第一次见到了风采别致的桑普森·朗维尔先生。他出来迎接他们，光头在午后的阳光下闪闪发亮，绑带的浅黄褐色裤子、天鹅绒马甲和老式领巾，把格雷戈里·彭尼对他的那番描摹印证了个十足。
"见到您真高兴，尼布沃斯先生。寒舍简陋，我给各位的欢迎却十二分殷勤！小饭厅里已备好茶点。可否请您把我引见给贵公司各位？"
这份礼数，这种旧世界的庄重气度，非常动人，迈克尔对这位高雅的老人涌起一阵暖意——他把往昔时代的温情与芬芳带进了这现代的空气里。

===Original===
“I should like to shoot a scene before we lose the light, Mr. Longvale,” said Knebworth, “so, if you don’t mind the meal being a scrambling one, I can give the company a quarter of an hour.” He looked round. “Where is Foss?” he asked. “I want to change a scene.”
“Mr. Foss said he was walking from Griff Towers,” said one of the company. “He stopped behind to speak to Sir Gregory.”
Jack Knebworth cursed his dilatory scenario man with vigour and originality.
“I hope he hasn’t stopped to borrow money,” he said savagely. “That fellow’s going to ruin my credit if I’m not careful.”
He had overcome his objection to his new extra; possibly he felt that there was nobody else in the party whom he could take into his confidence without hurt to discipline.
“Is he that way inclined?”
===Chinese===
"趁天光还没暗，我想先拍一场戏，朗维尔先生，"尼布沃斯说，"所以要是您不嫌这顿茶吃得仓促，我只能给全班组一刻钟。"他环顾四周，"福斯呢？"他问，"我要改一场戏。"
"福斯先生说是从格里夫塔楼步行过来，"班组里有人说，"他留下来跟格雷戈里爵士说话，落在了后头。"
杰克·尼布沃斯把他那磨磨蹭蹭的编剧骂了个淋漓尽致，用语既有力又新鲜。
"但愿他不是留下借钱去了，"他恶狠狠地说，"我不留神的话，这家伙非把我的信用毁掉不可。"
他对自己那位新临时演员的反感已然消退；也许是觉得，队里再没有第二个人，能让他交心而不伤纪律。
"他有这毛病？"

===Original===
“He’s always short of money and always trying to make it by some fool trick which leaves him shorter than he was before. When a man gets that kind of bug in his head he’s only a block away from prison. Are you going to stay the night? I don’t think you’ll be able to sleep here,” he said, changing the subject, “but I suppose you’ll be going back to London?”
“Not tonight,” said Michael quickly. “Don’t worry about me. I particularly do not wish to give you any trouble.”
“Come and meet the old man,” said Knebworth under his breath. “He’s a queer old devil with the heart of a child.”
“I like what I’ve seen of him,” said Michael.
Mr. Longvale accepted the introduction all over again.
“I fear there will not be sufficient room in my dining-room for the whole company. I have had a little table laid in my study. Perhaps you and your friends would like to have your tea there?”
===Chinese===
"他手头永远缺钱，又永远想靠什么蠢点子挣一笔，结果总比先前更缺。一个人脑子里钻进这种虫子，离监狱就只剩一个街区了。你今晚留下吗？这儿我怕是睡不下，"他岔开话题道，"不过我想你总要回伦敦去吧？"
"今晚不回，"迈克尔赶忙说，"别为我操心。我尤其不想给您添麻烦。"
"来见见那位老先生吧，"尼布沃斯压低声音说，"老家伙脾气怪，心肠却是孩子做的。"
"就我见到的而言，我喜欢他，"迈克尔说。
朗维尔先生把这一番引见从头又领受了一遍。
"恐怕我的饭厅坐不下全班组的人。我在书房里另摆了一张小桌。您和您的朋友们愿意在那儿用茶吗？"

===Original===
“Why, that’s very kind of you, Mr. Longvale. You have met Mr. Brixan?”
The old man smiled and nodded.
“I have met him without realizing that I’ve met him. I never remember names—a curious failing which was shared by my great-great-uncle Charles, with the result that he fell into extraordinary confusion when he wrote his memoirs, and in consequence many of the incidents he relates have been regarded as apocryphal.”
He showed them into a narrow room that ran from the front to the back of the house. Its ceilings were supported by black rafters; the open wainscoting, polished and worn by generations of hands, must have been at least five hundred years old. There were no swords over this mantelpiece, thought Michael with an inward smile. Instead, there was a portrait of a handsome old gentleman, the dignity of whose face was arresting. There was only one word with an adequate description: it was majestic.
He made no comment on the picture, nor did the old man speak of it till later. The meal was hastily disposed of, and, sitting on the wall, Michael watched the last daylight scene shot, and was struck by the plastic genius of the girl. He knew enough of motion pictures and their construction to realize what it meant to the director to have in his hands one who could so faithfully reproduce the movements and the emotions which the old man dictated.
===Chinese===
"哎呀，您太客气了，朗维尔先生。您见过布里克森先生吧？"
老人微笑着点头。
"我见过他，只是没意识到自己见过。我从来记不住名字——这个怪毛病我的高伯祖父查尔斯也有，结果他写回忆录时陷入了天大的混乱，他讲的许多事迹因此被人当作杜撰。"
他把他们让进一间狭长的屋子，从宅子前头直通到后头。天花板由黑色梁木支撑；敞露的护壁板被一代代人的手摩挲得油亮，至少有五百年了。这壁炉架上可没有挂刀剑，迈克尔心里暗笑。取而代之的是一位俊雅老绅士的画像，那张脸的威仪摄人心魄。只有一个词配得上它：气宇轩昂。
他没有对画像发表议论，老人也直到后来才提起它。茶点匆匆用过，迈克尔坐在墙头上看着最后一场日景拍完，被姑娘造型的天才打动了。他对电影及其门道的了解，足以让他明白：导演手里有这样一个人，能如此忠实地再现老人示范的一举一动、一颦一情，意味着什么。

===Original===
In other circumstances he might have thought it grotesque to see Jack Knebworth pretending to be a young girl, resting his elderly cheek coyly upon the back of his clasped hand, and walking with mincing steps from one side of the picture to the other. But he knew that the American was a mason who was cutting roughly the shape of the sculpture and leaving it to the finer artiste to express in her personality the delicate contours that would delight the eye of the picture-loving world. She was no longer Adele Leamington; she was Roselle, the heiress to an estate of which her wicked cousin was trying to deprive her. The story itself he recognized; a half-and-half plagiarism of “The Cat and the Canary,” with which were blended certain situations from “The Miracle Man.” He mentioned this fact when the scene was finished.
“I guess it’s a steal,” said Jack Knebworth philosophically, “and I didn’t inquire too closely into it. It’s Foss’s story, and I should be pained to discover there was anything original in it.”
Mr. Foss had made a tardy reappearance, and Michael found himself wondering what was the nature of that confidential interview which the writer had had with Sir Gregory.
Going back to the long sitting-room, he stood watching the daylight fade and speculating upon the one mystery within a mystery—the extraordinary effect which Adele had produced upon him.
Mike Brixan had known many beautiful women, women in every class of society. He had known the best and the worst, he had jailed a few, and had watched one face a French firing squad one grey wintry morning at Vincennes. He had liked many, nearly loved one, and it seemed, cold-bloodedly analysing his emotions, that he was in danger of actually loving a girl whom he had never met before that morning.
===Chinese===
换作别的场合，看见杰克·尼布沃斯扮作妙龄少女，把上了年纪的脸颊羞答答地靠在交叠的手背上，迈着碎步从画面这头走到那头，他也许会觉得滑稽。但他明白，这位美国人是个石匠，只管凿出雕像的大形，至于那能让爱电影的世人赏心悦目的细腻曲线，则留给更精致的艺术家用她本人去呈现。她已经不再是阿黛尔·利明顿；她是罗塞尔，一份产业的继承人，正被她恶毒的堂兄图谋夺产。这故事本身他认出来了：一半抄袭《猫与金丝雀》（The Cat and the Canary），又掺进了《奇迹人》（The Miracle Man）里的几个桥段。这场戏拍完时，他把这层点破了。
"我猜就是偷来的，"杰克·尼布沃斯达观地说，"我也没细究。那是福斯的故事，真要发现里面有什么原创的东西，我反倒要心疼了。"
福斯先生迟迟才露面，迈克尔不由得琢磨起这位编剧同格雷戈里爵士那番密谈的底细来。
回到那间狭长的客厅，他站在那儿望着天光暗下去，思忖着那谜中之谜——阿黛尔在他身上产生的异样效应。
迈克·布里克森认识的美貌女子多了，各个阶层的都有。最好的、最坏的他都领教过，送过几个进监狱，还在万塞讷（Vincennes）一个灰蒙蒙的冬日清晨，眼看一个女人面对法国行刑队。他喜欢过许多，差点爱过一个，此刻冷血地剖析自己的情感，他发现自己正面临真的爱上一个姑娘的危险——而就在那天早晨之前，他还从未见过她。

===Original===
“Which is absurd,” he said aloud.
“What is absurd?” asked Knebworth, who had come into the room unnoticed.
“I also wondered what you were thinking,” smiled old Mr. Longvale, who had been watching the young man in silence.
“I—er—well, I was thinking of the portrait.” Michael turned and indicated the picture above the fireplace, and in a sense he spoke the truth, for the thread of that thought had run through all others. “The face seemed familiar,” he said, “which is absurd, because it is obviously an old painting.”
Mr. Longvale lit two candles and carried one to the portrait. Again Michael looked, and again the majesty of the face impressed him.
“That is my great-great-uncle, Charles Henry,” said old Mr. Longvale with pride. “Or, as we call him affectionately in our family, the Great Monsieur.”
===Chinese===
"这真是荒唐，"他出声说道。
"什么荒唐？"尼布沃斯问，他进来时谁也没留意。
"我也正想知道你在想什么，"老朗维尔先生微笑道，他一直在默默打量这个年轻人。
"我——呃——我在想那幅画像。"迈克尔转过身，指了指壁炉上方的画。某种意义上他说的是实话，因为那条思绪一直贯穿着其余所有的念头。"那张脸似乎眼熟，"他说，"这就荒唐了，因为那显然是幅古画。"
朗维尔先生点亮两支蜡烛，把一支举到画像前。迈克尔再看，那张脸的威仪又一次震住了他。
"那是我的高伯祖父，查尔斯·亨利，"老朗维尔先生自豪地说，"或者，按我们家里亲昵的叫法——'大先生'（the Great Monsieur）。"

===Original===
Michael’s face was half-turned toward the window as the old man spoke.﻿ … Suddenly the room seemed to spin before his eyes. Jack Knebworth saw his face go white and caught him by the arm.
“What’s the matter?” he asked.
“Nothing,” said Michael unsteadily.
Knebworth was staring past him at the window.
“What was that?” he said.
With the exception of the illumination from the two candles and the faint dusk light that came from the garden, the room was in darkness.
“Did you see it?” he asked, and ran to the window, staring out.
===Chinese===
老人说话时，迈克尔的脸半朝着窗口。……突然，房间仿佛在他眼前旋转起来。杰克·尼布沃斯看见他脸色发白，一把抓住他的胳膊。
"怎么了？"他问。
"没什么，"迈克尔声音发飘地说。
尼布沃斯的目光越过他，直盯着窗户。
"那是什么？"他说。
除了两支蜡烛的光和花园透进来的一点微弱暮色，屋里一片漆黑。
"你看见了吗？"他问着奔到窗前，向外张望。

===Original===
“What was it?” asked old Mr. Longvale, joining him.
“I could have sworn I saw a head in the window. Did you see it, Brixan?”
“I saw something,” said Michael unsteadily. “Do you mind if I go out into the garden?”
“I hoped you saw it. It looked like a monkey’s head to me.”
Michael nodded. He walked down the flagged passage into the garden, and, as he did so, slipped a Browning from his hip, pressed down the safety-catch, and dropped the pistol into his jacket pocket.
He disappeared, and five minutes later Knebworth saw him pacing the garden path, and went out to him.
===Chinese===
"是什么？"老朗维尔先生也凑上来问。
"我可以发誓，我看见窗上有个脑袋。你看见了吗，布里克森？"
"我看见了个东西，"迈克尔声音发飘地说，"我出去到园子里看看，您介意吗？"
"但愿你也看见了。我看着像颗猴子的脑袋。"
迈克尔点了点头。他沿石板走廊走进花园，边走边从腰间抽出一支勃朗宁（Browning）手枪，按下保险，把枪顺势揣进外套口袋。
他的身影消失了。五分钟后，尼布沃斯看见他在园中小径上来回踱步，便走出去找他。

===Original===
“Did you see anything?”
“Nothing in the garden. You must have been mistaken.”
“But didn’t you see him?”
Michael hesitated.
“I thought I saw something,” he said with an assumption of carelessness. “When are you going to shoot those night pictures of yours?”
“You saw something, Brixan—was it a face?”
Mike Brixan nodded.
===Chinese===
"看见什么了吗？"
"园子里什么都没有。你一定是看错了。"
"可你不是也看见它了吗？"
迈克尔迟疑了。
"我以为自己看见了个东西，"他装出漫不经心的样子说，"你那些夜景打算什么时候拍？"
"你是看见东西了，布里克森——是张脸吗？"
迈克·布里克森点了点头。

## XVI The Brown Man from Nowhere / XVI 不知来处的棕色人

===Original===
With a brand-new grievance against life, Lawley Foss gathered his forces to avenge himself upon the world that had treated him so harshly. And first and most powerful of his forces was Stella Mendoza. There was a council of war held in the drawing-room of the pretty little house that Stella had taken when she joined the Knebworth Corporation. The third of the party was Mr. Reggie Connolly. And as they were mutually sympathetic, so were they mutually unselfish—characteristically so.
“We’ve been treated disgracefully by Knebworth, Mr. Foss, especially you. I think, compared with your case, mine is nothing.”
“It is the way he has handled you that makes me sore,” said Foss energetically. “An artiste of your standing!”
“The work you’ve done for him! And Reggie—he treated him like a dog!”
“Personally, it doesn’t matter to me,” said Reggie. “I can always find a contract—it’s you—”
“For the matter of that, we can *all* find contracts,” interrupted Stella with a taste of acid in her voice: “I can have my own company when I please, and I’ve got two directors mad to direct me, and two men I know would put up every cent of money to give me my own company—at least, they’d put up a lot. And Chauncey Seller is raving to play opposite me, and you know what a star he is; and he’d let me be featured and go into small type himself. He’s a lovely man, and the best juvenile in this country or any other.”
Mr. Connolly coughed.
===Chinese===
劳利·福斯怀着对生活的一腔新怨，集结起自己的力量，要向这个如此苛待他的世界复仇。他的力量里头一个、也是最强的一个，便是斯特拉·门多萨。军事会议在斯特拉那座漂亮小宅的客厅里召开——那是她加盟尼布沃斯公司时租下的。三人团伙的第三位是雷吉·康诺利先生。他们彼此同病相怜，也彼此无私——各有各的无私法。
"尼布沃斯待我们太不像话了，福斯先生，尤其是您。比起您的遭遇，我的算不了什么。"
"让我憋气的是他收拾您的手段，"福斯愤然道，"您这样身份的艺术家！"
"您为他出了多少力！还有雷吉——他拿他当狗使唤！"
"对我个人来说无所谓，"雷吉说，"我总能签到合同——是你们——"
"说到这个，咱们*谁*都签得到合同，"斯特拉打断他，话里带着酸味，"我高兴什么时候有自己的公司，就什么时候有。有两个导演发疯似的想导我，还有两个我认识的肯掏出每一分钱给我办公司——至少肯掏一大笔。昌西·塞勒（Chauncey Seller）更是抢着要跟我搭戏，他多大牌你们知道；他情愿挂我的头牌，把自己的名字排成小字。他是个可人，是全国乃至全世界最好的小生。"
康诺利先生咳了一声。

===Original===
“The point is, can we get the money *now*?” asked Foss, practical for once.
There was no immediate and enthusiastic assurance from the girl.
“Because, if not, I think I can get all I want,” said Foss surprisingly. “I won’t say from whom, or how I’m going to get it. But I’m certain I can get big money, and it will be easier to get it for some specific object than to ask for it for myself.”
“Less risky?” suggested Connolly, with a desire to be in the conversation.
It was an unfortunate remark, the more so since by chance he had hit the nail on the head. Foss went a dull red.
“What the hell do you mean by ‘less risky’?” he demanded.
Poor Reggie had meant nothing, and admitted as much in some haste. He had meant to be helpful, and was ready to sulk at the storm he had aroused. More ready because, as the conversation had progressed, he had faded more and more into the background as an inconsiderable factor. There is nothing quite so disheartening to a conspirator as to find the conspiring taken out of his hands, and Reggie Connolly felt it was the moment to make a complete volte face, and incidentally assert what he was pleased to call his “personality.”
===Chinese===
"要紧的是，钱*现在*能不能到手？"福斯问，难得实际了一回。
姑娘没有立刻热切地打包票。
"因为要是不能，我要的钱我自己就能弄到，"福斯出人意料地说，"我不说从谁手里、用什么法子。但我肯定我能弄到大钱，而且打着某个具体名目去要，比为我自己开口要容易。"
"风险小些？"康诺利提了一句，只为插上话。
这话说得不是时候，偏偏又一语中的。福斯的脸涨成了暗红色。
"你说'风险小些'，他妈的是什么意思？"他质问。
可怜的雷吉本无他意，也赶忙承认了这一点。他原想帮腔，如今惹出这场风暴，正准备怄气。尤其因为随着谈话深入，他作为一个无足轻重的角色，越来越退到了局外。对一个密谋者来说，最泄气的莫过于发现密谋已不再需要自己插手，雷吉·康诺利觉得，此刻正该来个一百八十度大转弯，顺便亮一亮他乐于称作"个性"的东西。

===Original===
“This is all very well, Stella,” he said, “but it looks to me as if I’m going to be left out in the cold. What with your thinking about Chauncey Seller—he’s let down more pictures than any two men I know—and all that sort of thing, I don’t see that I’m going to be much use to you. I don’t really. I know you’ll think I’m a fearful, awful rotter, but I feel that we owe something to old Jack Kneb, I do really. I’ve jeopardized my position for your sake, and I’m prepared to do anything in reason, but what with pulling Chauncey Seller—who is a bounder of the worst kind—into your cast, and what with Foss jumping down my throat, well, really—really!”
They were not inclined to mollify him, having rather an eye to the future than to the present, and he had retired in a huff before the girl realized that the holding of Reggie would at least have embarrassed Knebworth to the extent of forcing a retake of those parts of the picture in which he appeared.
“Never mind about Connolly. The picture is certain to fail with that extra: she’s bad. I have a friend in London,” explained Foss, after the discussion returned to the question of ways and means, “who can put up the money. I’ve got a sort of pull with him. In fact—well, anyhow, I’ve got a pull. I’ll go up tonight and see him.”
“And I’ll see mine,” said Stella. “We’ll call the company The Stella Mendoza Picture Corporation—”
Lawley Foss demurred. He was inclined to another title, and was prepared to accept as a compromise the Foss-Mendoza or F.M. Company, a compromise agreeable to Stella provided the initials were reversed.
===Chinese===
"这些都好，斯特拉，"他说，"可我看我是要被晾在一边了。你一心惦记着昌西·塞勒——他毁掉的片子比我认识的任何两个人毁掉的都多——再加上这些那些，我看我对你派不上多大用场。真的派不上。我知道你们会想我是个可怕透顶的混蛋，可我觉得咱们欠着老杰克·尼布一份情，真的欠着。我为了你把位子都押上了，合情合理的事我都肯干，可你们又要拉昌西·塞勒——顶下作的一个恶棍——进班子，福斯又冲我吹胡子瞪眼，唉，真是——真是！"
他们无意哄他——眼睛盯着将来而非眼下——等姑娘回过味来，他已经负气告退了：把雷吉留下，至少能让尼布沃斯难堪，逼他重拍他出场的那部分戏。
"别管康诺利了。用那个临时演员，这片子必败无疑：她不行。我在伦敦有个朋友，"等讨论回到钱的路数上，福斯解释道，"他出得起钱。我跟他有点交情。其实——唉，反正我有路子。我今晚就进城去见他。"
"那我去见我的人，"斯特拉说，"公司就叫斯特拉·门多萨影片公司——"
劳利·福斯不同意。他属意另一个名号，准备各退一步，叫福斯-门多萨公司，或者 F.M. 公司；斯特拉倒也认可这个折中，只要把缩写字母倒过来。

===Original===
“Who is Brixan?” she asked as Foss was leaving.
“He is a detective.”
She opened her eyes wide.
“A detective? Whatever is he doing here?”
Lawley Foss smiled contemptuously.
“He is trying to discover what no man of his mental calibre will ever discover, the Headhunter. I am the one man in the world who could help him. Instead of which,” he smiled again, “I am helping myself.”
With which cryptic and mystifying statement he left her.
===Chinese===
"布里克森是谁？"福斯临走时她问。
"是个侦探。"
她睁大了眼睛。
"侦探？他来这儿干什么？"
劳利·福斯轻蔑地一笑。
"他想揭开一个凭他那副脑髓永远揭不开的东西——猎头者。全世界只有一个人帮得了他，就是我。可我不但不帮，"他又一笑，"我还在帮我自己。"
丢下这句云山雾罩的话，他走了。

===Original===
Stella Mendoza was an ambitious woman, and when ambition is directed toward wealth and fame it is not attended by scruple. Her private life and her standard of values were no better and no worse than thousands of other women, and no more belonged to her profession than did her passion for good food and luxurious environment. The sins of any particular class or profession are not peculiar to their status or calling, but to their self-education in the matter of the permissible. As one woman would die rather than surrender her self-respect, so another would lose her self-respect rather than suffer poverty and hardship, and think little or nothing of the act or the deceit she practised to gain her ends.
After Foss had gone, she went up to her room to change. It was too early to make the call she intended, for Sir Gregory did not like to see her during the daytime. He, who had not hesitated to send Bhag on a fantastic mission, was a stickler for the proprieties.
Having some letters to post, she drove into Chichester late in the afternoon, and saw Mike Brixan in peculiar circumstances. He was the centre of a little crowd near the market cross, a head above the surrounding people. There was a policeman present: she saw his helmet, and for a moment was inclined to satisfy her curiosity. She changed her mind, and when she returned the crowd had dispersed and Michael had disappeared, and, driving home, she wondered whether the detective had been engaged professionally.
===Chinese===
斯特拉·门多萨是个野心勃勃的女人，而野心一旦冲着财富和名声而去，就不会带着良心同路。她的私生活和价值标尺，跟成千上万的女人不好也不坏，也跟她的职业无关，正如她贪恋美食华居跟职业无关一样。某一阶层或某一行业的罪，并不专属它们的身份或行当，而专属于每个人对"何者为可"的自我教育。有的女人宁死不舍自尊，另一种女人则宁舍自尊不受穷苦，而且对自己为达目的所做的手脚和欺骗，几乎不放在心上。
福斯走后，她上楼换衣裳。她打算去的那次拜访还太早，因为格雷戈里爵士不喜欢白天见她。这个连派巴格执行荒唐使命都毫不迟疑的人，在体面规矩上倒一丝不苟。
傍晚时分，她有几封信要寄，便开车进了奇切斯特，在一种奇特的情形下看见了迈克·布里克森。他是市场十字碑旁一小圈人的中心，比四周的人高出一头。现场有个警察：她看见了那顶头盔，一时想去满足一下好奇心。她改了主意，等她回来时人群已散，迈克尔也不见了；开车回家的一路，她琢磨那侦探是不是在办公事。

===Original===
Mike himself had been attracted by the crowd which was watching the ineffectual efforts of a Sussex policeman to make himself intelligible to a shock-haired, brown-faced native, an incongruous figure in an ill-fitting suit of store clothes and a derby hat which was a little too large for him. In his hand he carried a bundle tied up in a bright green handkerchief, and under his arm a long object, wrapped in linen and fastened with innumerable strings. At the first sight of him Michael thought it was one of Penne’s Malayan servants, but on second thoughts he realized that Sir Gregory would not allow any of his slaves to run loose about the countryside.
Pushing his way through the crowd, he came up to the policeman, who touched his helmet rim and grinned.
“Can’t make head or tail of this fellow’s lingo, sir,” he said. “He wants to know something, but I can’t make out what. He has just come into the city.”
The brown man turned his big dark eyes upon Mike and said something which was Greek to the detective. There was a curious dignity about the native that even his ludicrous garments could not wholly dissipate, an erectness of body, a carriage of head, an imponderable air of greatness that instantly claimed Michael Brixan’s attention.
Then suddenly he had an inspiration, and addressed the man in Dutch. Immediately the native’s eyes lit up.
“*Ja, mynheer*, I speak Dutch.”
===Chinese===
迈克尔自己，则是被围观的人群引过去的：大家正看着一名萨塞克斯警察徒劳地向一个头发蓬乱、棕色脸膛的土人解释什么。那人一身不合身的现成衣裤，头上扣一顶略大的圆顶硬礼帽，模样格格不入。他手里拎着一个用鲜绿手帕捆着的包袱，胳膊底下夹着一件长物，裹着亚麻布，缠了数不清的细绳。乍一看，迈克尔以为他是彭尼的马来仆人之一，转念一想，格雷戈里爵士决不会放自己的任何一个奴隶在乡间乱跑。
他从人群里挤过去，走到警察跟前；警察碰了碰帽檐，咧嘴一笑。
"这家伙的话我一个字也摸不着头脑，先生，"他说，"他想打听什么事，可我听不出是什么。他刚进城。"
棕色人把那双黑亮的大眼睛转向迈克，说了句什么，侦探如闻天书。这土人身上有种奇特的尊严，连他那身滑稽的衣装也无法完全掩盖：身板挺直，头颅端方，一股难以名状的贵气，一下子攫住了迈克尔·布里克森的注意。
突然他灵机一动，用荷兰语跟那人搭话。土人的眼睛顿时亮了。
"*Ja, mynheer*（是，先生），我会说荷兰话。"

===Original===
Mike had guessed that he came from Malaya, where Dutch and Portuguese are spoken by the better class natives.
“I am from Borneo, and I seek a man who is called Truji, an Englishman. No, *mynheer*, I wish to see his house, for he is a great man in my country. When I have seen his house I will go back to Borneo.”
Mike was watching him as he talked. It was a particularly good-looking face, except for the long and ugly scar that ran from his forehead to the point of his jaw.
A new servant for Gregory Penne, thought the detective, and gave him directions. Standing by the policeman’s side, he watched the queer figure with its bundles till it disappeared.
“Queer language, that, sir,” said the officer. “It was Dutch to me.”
“And to me,” chuckled Mike, and continued his way to the hotel.
===Chinese===
迈克方才就猜他来自马来亚，那里的上等土人会说荷兰语和葡萄牙语。
"我从婆罗洲来，我要找一个叫特鲁吉（Truji）的人，一个英国人。不，*mynheer*（先生），我想看他的宅子，他在我们国家是个大人物。看过了他的宅子，我就回婆罗洲去。"
他说话时，迈克尔端详着他。那是张格外端正的脸，只是从额头到下颌尖横着一道长长的丑疤。
是格雷戈里·彭尼新招的仆人吧，侦探想着，为他指了路。他站在警察身旁，望着那带着包袱的怪异身影，直到它消失。
"那语言真怪，先生，"警官说，"对我来说，那可是不折不扣的'荷兰话'——天书。"
"对我来说也是，"迈克轻笑着，继续朝旅馆走去。

## XXXVIII The Cavern of Bones / XXXVIII 白骨洞窟

===Original===
Down, down, down she fell, one hand clawing wildly at the soft earth, the other clenching unconsciously at the tiny pistol. She was rolling down a steep slope. Once her feet came violently and painfully into contact with an out-jutting rock, and the shock and the pain of it turned her sick and faint. Whither she was going she dared not think. It seemed an eternity before, at last, she struck a level floor and, rolling over and over, was brought up against a rocky wall with a jolt that shook the breath from her body.
Eternity it seemed, yet it could not have been more than a few seconds. For five minutes she lay, recovering, on the rock floor. She got up with a grimace of pain, felt her hurt ankle, and worked her foot to discover if anything was broken. Looking up, she saw a pale star above, and, guessing that it was the opening through which she had fallen, attempted to climb back; but with every step she took the soft earth gave under her feet and she slipped back again.
She had lost a shoe: that was the first tangible truth that asserted itself. She groped round in the darkness and found it after a while, half embedded in the earth. She shook it empty, dusted her stockinged foot, and put it on. Then she sat down to wonder what she should do next. She guessed that, with the coming of day, she would be able to examine her surroundings, and she must wait, with what philosophy she could summon, for the morning to break.
It was then that she became conscious that she was still gripping the earth-caked Browning, and, with a half-smile, she cleaned it as best she could, pressed down the safety-catch and, putting the weapon inside her blouse, thrust its blunt nose into the waistband of her skirt.
===Chinese===
她坠啊，坠啊，坠啊，一只手疯了似的抓挠松软的泥土，另一只手不知不觉死攥着那柄小手枪。她正顺着一道陡坡滚落。有一次，双脚狠狠撞上一块凸出的岩石，那一震一痛，叫她恶心发晕。滚向何处，她不敢想。仿佛熬过了无穷岁月，她才终于落上平地，又接连翻了几个滚，一头撞上石壁，震得她气息全无。
说是无穷岁月，其实不过几秒钟。她在岩石地上躺了五分钟，缓过劲来。她龇牙咧嘴地爬起来，摸摸受伤的脚踝，活动活动脚，看断了没有。抬头望去，头顶上方有一颗惨淡的星，她猜那便是自己坠落的洞口，便试着爬回去；可每迈一步，松软的泥土就在脚下塌陷，她又滑了下去。
她丢了一只鞋：这是头一个冒出来的、确凿的事实。她在黑暗中四下摸索，过了一阵找着了，半埋在土里。她倒净里面的土，掸掸穿袜子的脚，重新穿上。随后她坐下来，盘算下一步该怎么办。她想，天亮之后总可以把四周查看清楚，眼下只能搜刮出几分明达，静候破晓。
直到这时她才发觉，手里还攥着那柄糊满泥土的勃朗宁。她带着半个微笑，尽力把它擦拭干净，按下保险，把枪揣进衬衫里，将那钝钝的枪头别进裙腰。

===Original===
The mystery of Bhag’s reappearance was now a mystery no longer. He had been hiding in the cave, though it was her imagination that supplied the queer animal scent which was peculiarly his.
How far did the cave extend? She peered left and right, but could see nothing; then, groping cautiously, feeling every inch of her way, her hand struck a stone pillar, and she withdrew it quickly, for it was wet and clammy.
And then she made a discovery of the greatest importance to her. She was feeling along the wall when her hand went into a niche, and by the surface of its shelf she knew it was man-fashioned. She put her hand farther along, and her heart leapt as she touched something which had a familiar and homely feel. It was a lantern. Her other hand went up, and presently she opened its glass door and felt a length of candle, and, at the bottom of the lantern, a small box of matches.
It was no miracle, as she was to learn; but for the moment it seemed that that possibility of light had come in answer to her unspoken prayers. Striking a match with a hand that shook so that the light went out immediately, she at last succeeded in kindling the wick. The candle was new, and at first its light was feeble; but presently the wax began to burn, and, closing the lantern door, her surroundings came into view.
===Chinese===
巴格再度现身的谜，如今不再是谜了。它一直藏在这洞里，只是那股独属于它的怪异兽腥气，是她想象补出来的。
这洞穴究竟有多深？她左右张望，什么也看不见；随后她小心地摸索着，一寸一寸地探路，手碰上了一根石柱，赶紧缩了回来——柱子又湿又冷。
接着她有了一个对她至关重要的发现。她正贴着墙摸索，手伸进了一个壁龛，凭那搁板的表面，她断定是人工凿成的。她的手再往前探，触到一样东西，那熟悉而家常的手感让她心口一跳。是一盏提灯。她另一只手也伸上去，很快打开灯的玻璃门，摸到一截蜡烛，灯底还有一小盒火柴。
这并非奇迹——她日后自会明白；可那一刻，这一点光明，仿佛正是应了她未出口的祈求。她划火柴的手抖得厉害，火苗当即熄灭，试了几回才终于点着了灯芯。蜡烛是新的，起初光焰微弱；不多时蜡烧开了，她掩上灯门，四周的景物便映入眼帘。

===Original===
She was in a narrow cave, from the roof of which hung innumerable stalactites; but the dripping water which is inseparable from this queer formation was absent at the foot of the opening where she had tumbled. Farther along the floor was wet, and a tiny stream of water ran in a sort of naturally carved tunnel on one side of the path. Here, where the cave broadened, the stalactites were many, and left and right, at such regular intervals and of such even shape that they seemed almost to have been sculptured by human agency, were little caves within caves, narrow openings that revealed, in the light of her lantern, the splendour of nature’s treasures. Fairylike grottos, rich with delicate stone traceries; tiny lakes that sparkled in the light of the lantern. Broader and broader grew the cave, until she stood in a huge chamber that appeared to be festooned with frozen lace. And here the floor was littered with queer white sticks. There were thousands of them, of every conceivable shape and size. They showed whitely in the gleam of her lantern, in the crevices of the rocks. She stooped and picked one up, dropping it quickly with a cry of horror. They were human bones!
With a shuddering gasp she half walked, half ran across the great cavern, which began to narrow again and assumed the appearance of that portion of the cave into which she had fallen. And here she saw, in another niche, a second lantern, with new candle and matches. Who had placed them there? The first lantern she had not dared to think about: it belonged to the miraculous category. But the second brought her up with a jerk. Who had placed these lanterns at intervals along the wall of the cave, as if in preparation for an expected emergency? There must be somebody who lived down here. She breathed a little more quickly at the thought.
Going on slowly, she examined every foot of the way, the second lantern, unlighted, slung on her arm. At one part, the floor was flooded with running water; at another, she had to wade through a little subterranean ford, where the water came over her ankle. And now the cave was curving imperceptibly to the right. From time to time she stopped and listened, hoping to hear the sound of a human voice, and yet fearing. The roof of the cave came lower. There were signs in the roof that the stalactites had been knocked off to afford head room for the mysterious person who haunted these underground chambers.
===Chinese===
她置身的是一条狭洞，洞顶垂挂着数不清的钟乳石；可在这类奇特构造里须臾不离的滴水，在她滚落的洞口底下却不见踪影。再往前，地面就湿了，一线细流在路旁一条天然冲刷出的水道里流淌。洞穴到此豁然开阔，钟乳石林林总总，左右两侧每隔规整的间距便是一个，形状又那般齐整，几乎像是经了人工雕琢——洞中有洞，窄窄的洞口在灯光下显出自然宝藏的华彩。仙境般的石窟，缀满精巧的石缕石花；小小的水潭在灯光里闪闪发亮。洞越走越宽，最后她站进一座巨大的洞厅，四壁仿佛挂满了凝冻的蕾丝。就在这里，地上散落着古怪的白色短棍。成千上万根，形状大小无一雷同，在灯光里泛着白光，塞满岩缝。她俯身拾起一根，惊叫一声赶紧扔掉。那是人的骨头！
她倒抽着冷气，半走半奔地穿过大洞厅；洞厅又开始收窄，模样渐渐近似她跌落进来的那一段。就在这里，她又在另一个壁龛里看见了第二盏提灯，配着新蜡烛和火柴。是谁放在那儿的？头一盏她尚不敢深想：那属于神迹一类。可第二盏却叫她猛醒。是谁沿洞墙每隔一段便备下一盏灯，仿佛在为一场意料中的急难做准备？这底下必定住着什么人。想到这里，她的呼吸急促了几分。
她缓缓前行，一尺一尺查验着路，第二盏灯没有点，挎在臂弯里。有一段，地面漫着活水；又有一段，她得蹚过一处小小的地下浅滩，水没过脚踝。这时洞穴正不知不觉向右弯去。她不时驻足倾听，盼着听见人声，却又害怕听见。洞顶越来越低。顶上有钟乳石被敲落的痕迹——为那个出没于这些地下洞府的神秘人物腾出头顶的空间。

===Original===
Once she stopped, her heart thumping painfully at the sound of footsteps. They passed over her head, and then came a curious humming sound that grew in intensity, passed and faded. A motorcar! She was under the road! Of course, old Griff Tower stood upon the hillside. She was now near the road level, and possibly eight or nine feet above her the stars were shining. She looked wistfully at the ragged surface of the roof, and, steeling herself against the terrors that rose within her, she went on. She had need of nerve, need of courage beyond the ordinary.
The cave passage turned abruptly; the little grotto openings in the wall occurred again. Suddenly she stopped dead. The light of the lantern showed into one of the grottos. Two men lay side by side﻿—
She stifled the scream that rose to her lips, pressing her hands tight upon her mouth, her eyes shut tightly to hide the sight. They were dead﻿—headless! Lying in a shallow pool, the petrifying water came dripping down upon them, as it would drip down for everlasting until these pitiful things were stone.
===Chinese===
有一回她停住脚，一阵脚步声震得她心口生疼。脚步从她头顶过去，接着响起一种古怪的嗡嗡声，越来越响，掠过，又消退了。是汽车！她在路的下面！是啊，老格里芬塔本就立在山坡上。她此刻已接近路面高度，说不定头顶八九英尺之上，星星正闪着。她恋恋地望了望坑坑洼洼的洞顶，把心头涌起的恐怖强自压下，继续前行。她需要神经，需要超出常人的胆量。
甬道猛地一转；墙上那些小石窟的洞口又出现了。她突然僵住。灯光照进其中一窟。两个男人并排躺着——
她把涌到嘴边的尖叫硬生生压了回去，双手死死捂住嘴，双眼紧闭，不敢看。他们死了——没有头！躺在一片浅浅的水洼里，那能使人石化的水一滴一滴落在他们身上，还将这样滴到地老天荒，直到这些可怜的东西化为石头。

===Original===
For a long time she dared not move, dared not open her eyes, but at last her will conquered, and she looked with outward calm upon a sight that froze her very marrow. The next grotto was similarly tenanted, only this time there was one man. And then, when she was on the point of sinking under the shock, a tiny point of light appeared in the gloom ahead. It moved and swayed, and there came to her the sound of a fearful laugh.
She acted instantly. Pulling open the door of the lantern, she stooped and blew it out, and stood, leaning against the wall of the cave, oblivious to the grisly relics that surrounded her, conscious only of the danger which lay ahead. Then a brighter light blazed up and another, till the distant spaces wherein they burnt were as bright as day. As she stood, wondering, there came to her a squeal of mortal agony and a whining voice that cried:
“Help! Oh, God, help! Brixan, I am not fit to die!”
It was the voice of Sir Gregory Penne.
===Chinese===
许久她不敢动，不敢睁眼，但意志终于占了上风，她表面平静地望了望那冻彻骨髓的一幕。下一个石窟里住着同样的房客，只是这回只有一个男人。就在她几欲被这惊骇压垮之际，前方幽暗里亮起了一个小小的光点。它晃动着，摇摆着，随后传来一声可怕的笑声。
她立即行动。拉开灯门，俯身一口吹熄了灯，倚着洞壁站着，全然顾不上四周可怖的遗骸，心里只想着前方潜伏的危险。随后一团更亮的光燃起，又是一团，直到它们燃烧的远处亮如白昼。她正自惊疑，一阵垂死的惨叫传到她耳中，还有一个哀哀哭腔在喊：
"救命！哦，上帝，救命！布里克森，我不配死啊！"
那是格雷戈里·彭尼爵士的声音。

## XXVII The Caves / XXVII 洞穴

===Original===
Mr. Longvale’s two patients were removed to hospital that night, and, with a favourable report on the man’s condition from the doctors, Michael felt that one aspect of the mystery was a mystery no longer.
His old schoolmaster received a visit that night.
“More study?” he asked good-humouredly when Michael was announced.
“Curiously enough, you’re right, sir,” said Michael, “though I doubt very much whether you can assist me. I’m looking for an old history of Chichester.”
“I have one published in 1600. You’re the second man in the last fortnight who wanted to see it.”
“Who was the other?” asked Michael quickly.
===Chinese===
那天夜里，朗维尔先生的两位病人被送进了医院，医生对那男人伤情的报告颇为乐观，迈克尔觉得，这桩谜案的一个侧面已不再是谜。
他那当年的老校长当晚接待了他的来访。
"又来做学问？"迈克尔进屋通报时，他打趣道。
"说来也怪，还真让您说着了，先生，"迈克尔说，"不过我很怀疑您这回帮得上忙。我在找一部奇切斯特的老志书。"
"我藏有一部 1600 年刊行的。近两个星期里，你是第二个要看它的人。"
"另一个是谁？"迈克尔连忙问。

===Original===
“A man named Foss﻿—” began Mr. Scott, and Michael nodded as though he had known the identity of the seeker after knowledge. “He wanted to know about caves. I’ve never heard there were any local caves of any celebrity. Now, if this were Cheddar, I should be able to give you quite a lot of information. I am an authority on the Cheddar caves.”
He showed Michael into the library, and taking down an ancient volume, laid it on the library table.
“After Foss had gone I looked up the reference. I find it occurs only on one page﻿—385. It deals with the disappearance of a troop of horsemen under Sir John Dudley, Earl of Newport, in some local trouble in the days of Stephen. Here is the passage.” He pointed.
===Chinese===
"一个姓福斯的人——"斯科特先生（Mr. Scott）开口道，迈克尔点了点头，仿佛这位求知者的身份早在意料之中。"他想了解洞穴。我从没听说过本地有什么出了名的洞穴。若是在切达（Cheddar），我倒能给你提供不少材料。切达的洞穴，我算得上一名权威。"
他把迈克尔领进藏书室，取下一部古旧的卷册，摊在书桌上。
"福斯走后，我把那处引文查了出来。全书只有一页——385 页——提到此事。讲的是斯蒂芬王（Stephen）年间一场地方乱子中，纽波特伯爵（Earl of Newport）约翰·达德利爵士（Sir John Dudley）麾下一队骑兵失踪的始末。就是这一段。"他指了指。

===Original===
Michael read, in the old-fashioned type:

> “The noble Earl, deciding to await hi∫ arrival, carried two *companie∫* of hor∫e by night into the great caves which exi∫ted in the∫e times. By the merciful di∫pen∫ation of God, in Who∫e Hands we are, there occurred, at eight o’clock in the forenoon, a great land∫lide which entombed and de∫troyed all the∫e knights and ∫quires, and ∫ir John Dudley, Earl of Newport, ∫o that they were never more ∫een. And the place of this happening is nine miles in a line from this ∫ame city, called by the Romans Regnum, or Ciffancea∫ter in the ∫axon fa∫hion.”

“Have the caves ever been located?”
Mr. Scott shook his head.
“There are local rumours that they were used a century and a half ago by brandy smugglers, but then you find those traditions local to every district.”
===Chinese===
迈克尔对着那副旧式字体读道：

> "高贵之伯爵决意候其抵达，乃乘夜将两*队*骑兵带入彼时存有之大洞穴中。托上帝仁慈之安排——吾人皆在其掌握之中——上午八时骤发大滑坡，将诸骑士、乡绅并纽波特伯爵约翰·达德利爵士尽数埋殁，自此不复得见。事发生之地，距本城直线九英里；此城罗马人称为雷格努姆（Regnum），撒克逊人则谓 Ciffanceaster。"

"这些洞穴后来找到过吗？"
斯科特先生摇了摇头。
"当地有传闻，说一个半世纪前白兰地走私贩用过它们，不过这类传说，哪个乡里都有一套。"

===Original===
Michael took a local map of Chichester from his pocket, measured off nine miles, and with a pair of compasses encircled the city. He noted that the line passed either through or near Sir Gregory’s estate.
“There are two Griff Towers?” he suddenly said, examining the map.
“Yes, there is another besides Penne’s place, which is named after a famous local landmark﻿—the real Griffin Tower (as it was originally called). I have an idea it stands either within or about Penne’s property﻿—a very old, circular tower, about twenty feet high, and anything up to two thousand years old. I’m interested in antiquities, and I have made a very careful inspection of the place. The lower part of the wall is undoubtedly Roman work﻿—the Romans had a big encampment here; in fact, Regnum was one of their headquarters. There are all sorts of explanations for the tower. Probably it was a keep or blockhouse. The idea I have is that the original Roman tower was not more than a few feet high and was not designed for defence at all. Successive ages added to its height, without exactly knowing why.”
Michael chuckled.
===Chinese===
迈克尔从口袋里掏出一张奇切斯特本地地图，量出九英里，用圆规绕城画了个圈。他注意到，这条线或穿过、或紧挨着格雷戈里爵士的产业。
"格里夫塔楼有两座？"他端详着地图，忽然说道。
"是的，除了彭尼那处，另有一座，那才是得名于本地著名地标的正主——真正的格里芬塔（Griffin Tower，原名如此）。依我看，它就立在彭尼地产的界内或附近——一座非常古老的圆形塔，高约二十英尺，年头最老可上溯两千年。我素来留心古物，那地方我仔细踏勘过。墙体下半截无疑是罗马人的手笔——罗马人在这儿扎过大营；事实上，雷格努姆就是他们的总部之一。关于这座塔有种种说法，多半是座碉楼或堡垒。我个人的看法是，最初的罗马塔不过几英尺高，压根不是为了防御而建。后世历朝历代往上增筑，自己也不大说得清缘故。"
迈克尔轻轻一笑。

===Original===
“Now if my theory is correct, I shall hear more about this Roman castle before the night is out,” he said.
He gathered his trunks from the hotel and took them off to his new home. He found that the dinner-table was laid for three.
“Expecting company?” asked Michael, watching Jack Knebworth putting the finishing touches on the table﻿—he had a bachelor’s finicking sense of neatness, which consists of placing everything at equal distance from everything else.
“Yuh! Friend of yours.”
“Of mine?”
Jack nodded.
===Chinese===
"倘若我的推断不错，今夜未过，我就会再听到这座罗马城堡的消息。"他说。
他从旅馆收拾了行李箱，运去新居。到那儿一看，晚餐桌上摆了三副餐具。
"等客人来？"迈克尔问，一边看着杰克·尼布沃斯给餐桌做最后的收拾——他有一种单身汉式的讲究劲儿，讲究到样样东西彼此之间的距离都要相等。
"可不！你的朋友。"
"我的朋友？"
杰克点了点头。

===Original===
“I’ve asked young Leamington to come up. And when I see a man of your age turning pink at the mention of a girl’s name, I feel sorry for him. She’s coming partly on business, partly for the pleasure of meeting me in a human atmosphere. She didn’t do so well today as I wanted, but I guess we were all a little short of our best.”
She came soon after, and there was something about her that was very sweet and appealing; something that went straight to Michael’s heart and consolidated the position she had taken there.
“I was thinking as I came along,” she said, as Jack Knebworth helped her off with her coat, “how very unreal everything is﻿—I never dreamt I should be your guest to dinner, Mr. Knebworth.”
“And I never dreamt you’d be worthy of such a distinction,” growled Jack. “And in five years’ time you’ll be saying, ‘Why on earth did I make such a fuss about being asked to a skimpy meal by that punk director Knebworth?’ ”
===Chinese===
"我请了小利明顿来。眼瞧着你这个年纪的男人，一听人家姑娘的名字就红脸，我都替他难过。她来，一半是谈正事，一半是图个乐子，在居家过日子的气氛里见见我本人。今天她发挥得不如我期望的，不过我看咱们都有点儿不在状态。"
她不久便到了。她身上有一种十分甜美动人的东西，一种直入迈克尔心坎的东西，把她在那儿已经占下的位置又夯实了一层。
"来的路上我一直在想，"杰克·尼布沃斯替她宽去外衣时，她说，"这一切多么像做梦——我从没想过，自己竟会来您府上做客吃晚饭，尼布沃斯先生。"
"我也从没想过，你当得起这份抬举。"杰克粗声粗气地说，"再过五年，你就会说：'当年被那个蹩脚导演尼布沃斯请去吃了顿便饭，我干吗激动成那样？'"

===Original===
He put his hand on her shoulder and led her into the room, and then for the first time she saw Michael, and that young man had a momentary sense of dismay when he saw her face drop. It was only for a second, and, as if reading his thoughts, she explained her sudden change of mien.
“I thought we were going to talk nothing but pictures and pictures!” she said.
“So you shall,” said Michael. “I’m the best listener on earth, and the first person to mention murder will be thrown out of the window.”
“Then I’ll prepare for the flight!” she said good-humouredly. “For I’m going to talk murder and mystery﻿—later!”
Under the expanding influence of a sympathetic environment the girl took on a new aspect, and all that Michael had suspected in her was amply proven. The shyness, the almost frigid reserve, melted in the company of two men, one of whom she guessed was fond of her, while the other﻿—well, Michael was at least a friend.
===Chinese===
他把手搭在她肩上，领她进了房间。这时她才头一回看见迈克尔，见她脸色一沉，年轻人心里掠过一阵沮丧。不过只有一秒钟，她仿佛看透了他的心思，主动解释起自己神色的骤变。
"我还以为今晚只谈电影、除了电影还是电影呢！"她说。
"尽管谈。"迈克尔说，"我是天下头一号好听众，谁第一个提谋杀，就把谁扔出窗外。"
"那我可得先做好跳窗的准备！"她好脾气地说，"因为我待会儿就要谈谋杀谈疑案——待会儿就谈！"
在这份暖人环境的熏染之下，姑娘焕然换了副模样，迈克尔此前在她身上暗自揣度的种种，都得了充分的印证。那份羞怯、那种近乎冷若冰霜的矜持，在两个男人的陪伴下冰消雪融——其中一个，她猜是倾心于她的；另一个呢——嗯，迈克尔至少是个朋友。

===Original===
“I have been doing detective work this afternoon,” she said, after the coffee had been served, “and I’ve made amazing discoveries,” she added solemnly. “It started by my trying to track the motorcar, which I guessed must have come into my street through a lane which runs across the far end. It is the only motorcar track I’ve found, and I don’t think there is any doubt it was my white-handed man who drove it. You see, I noticed the back tyre, which had a sort of diamond-shaped design on it, and it was fairly easy to follow the marks. Halfway up the lane I found a place where there was oil in the middle of the road, and where the car must have stood for some time, and there﻿—I found this!”
She opened her little handbag and took out a small, dark-green bottle. It bore no label and was unstoppered. Michael took it from her hand, examined it curiously and smelt. There was a distinctive odour, pungent and not unpleasing.
“Do you recognize it?” she asked.
He shook his head.
“Let me try.” Jack Knebworth took the bottle from Michael’s hand and sniffed. “Butyl chloride,” he said quickly, and the girl nodded.
===Chinese===
"今天下午我可干了回侦探的活儿，"咖啡端上来之后，她说，"而且有了惊人的发现。"她又郑重其事地补了一句，"起初我是想追查那辆汽车的行踪，我猜它准是从横贯街尾的那条小巷开进我那条街的。这是我找到的唯一一条车辙，而且我看毫无疑问，开车的正是我那位白手先生。您瞧，我注意到那后轮胎上有种菱形花纹，循着印子走并不难。走到巷子半途，我发现路当中有一滩油迹，那车想必在那里停过一阵，就在那儿——我发现了这个！"
她打开小手包，取出一只深绿色的小瓶。瓶上没有标签，塞子也是开的。迈克尔从她手里接过，好奇地端详一番，又闻了闻。有一股特别的气味，冲鼻，却并不难闻。
"您认得它吗？"她问。
他摇了摇头。
"我来试试。"杰克·尼布沃斯从迈克尔手里拿过瓶子嗅了嗅。"丁基氯（butyl chloride）。"他脱口说道，姑娘点了点头。

===Original===
“I thought it was that. Father was a pharmaceutical chemist, and once, when I was playing in his dispensary, I found a cupboard open and took down a pretty bottle and opened it. I don’t know what would have happened to me, only daddy saw me. I was quite a child at the time, and I’ve always remembered that scent.”
“Butyl chloride?” Michael frowned.
“It’s known as the ‘death drop’ or the ‘knockout drop,’ ” said Knebworth, “and it’s a drug very much in favour with sharks who make a business of robbing sailors. A few drops of that in a glass of wine and you’re out!”
Michael took the bottle again. It was a commonplace bottle such as is used for the dispensation of poisons, and in fact the word “Poison” was blown into the glass.
===Chinese===
"我就猜是它。我父亲是药剂师，有一回我在他的配药室里玩，看见一口柜子开着，就取下一只好看的瓶子，打开了。要不是爹爹正好撞见，真不知我会出什么事。那时我还是个小不点儿，那股气味我一辈子都记得。"
"丁基氯？"迈克尔皱起眉头。
"这东西外号叫'夺命滴'，也叫'蒙汗药滴'，"尼布沃斯说，"专干劫掠水手营生的鲨鱼们最爱用它。酒里滴上那么几滴，你立马人事不省！"
迈克尔又拿起那只瓶子。这是盛放毒药常用的普通瓶子，事实上，"毒药"（Poison）一词就熔铸在瓶身上。

===Original===
“There is no trace of a label,” he said.
“And really there is no connection with the mysterious car,” admitted the girl. “My surmise is merely guesswork﻿—putting one sinister thing to another.”
“Where was it?”
“In a ditch, which is very deep there and is flooded just now, but the bottle didn’t roll down so far as the water. That is discovery number one. Here is number two.”
From her bag she took a curious-shaped piece of steel, both ends of which had the marks of a break.
===Chinese===
"一点儿标签的痕迹都没有。"他说。
"其实这同那辆神秘汽车未必有什么牵连，"姑娘承认道，"我的猜想不过是瞎琢磨——把一桩邪门的事安到另一桩头上罢了。"
"在哪儿找到的？"
"在一条沟里，那一段沟很深，眼下又积满了水，好在瓶子没有一路滚到水边。这是发现第一号。这是第二号。"
她又从包里取出一块形状古怪的钢件，两头都有断口的痕迹。

===Original===
“Do you know what that is?” she asked.
“It beats me,” said Jack, and handed the find to Michael.
“*I* know what it is, because I’ve seen it at the studio,” said the girl, “and you know too, don’t you, Mr. Brixan?”
Mike nodded.
“It’s the central link of a handcuff,” he said, “the link that has the swivel.”
It was covered with spots of rust, which had been cleaned off﻿—by the girl, as she told him.
===Chinese===
"您知道这是什么吗？"她问。
"这可难住我了。"杰克说着，把这件发现递给迈克尔。
"*我*知道这是什么，我在摄影棚里见过，"姑娘说，"您也知道，对不对，布里克森先生？"
迈克点了点头。
"这是手铐的中央链环，"他说，"带转轴的那个环。"
上面布满锈斑，已被擦拭干净——是她擦的，她告诉他。

===Original===
“Those are my two finds. I am not going to offer you my conclusions, because I have none!”
“They may not have been thrown from the car at all,” said Michael, “but, as you say, there is a possibility that the owner of the car chose that peculiarly deserted spot to rid himself of two articles which he could not afford to have on the premises. It would have been safer to throw them into the sea, but this, I suppose, was the easier, and, to him, the safer method. I will keep these.”
He wrapped them in paper, put them away in his pocket, and the conversation drifted back to picture-taking, and, as he had anticipated:
“We’re shooting at Griff Tower tomorrow﻿—the real tower,” said Jack Knebworth. “It is one of the landmarks﻿—what is there amusing in Griff Tower?” he demanded.
“Nothing particularly amusing, except that you have fulfilled a prediction of mine,” said Michael. “I knew I should hear of that darned old tower!”
===Chinese===
"这就是我的两项发现。我不打算向您奉献什么结论，因为我压根没有结论！"
"它们也许根本不是从那辆车里扔出来的，"迈克尔说，"不过正如你所说，车主也有可能特意挑了那个僻静无人的地方，甩掉两件不敢留在手里的东西。扔进海里原是更稳妥的，可我想，这么办更省事，在他看来也更保险。这两样我收下了。"
他用纸把它们包好，收进口袋，谈话又飘回了拍电影的话题上。果然不出他所料：
"我们明天去格里芬塔拍外景——就是那座真塔。"杰克·尼布沃斯说，"那是本地地标之一——格里芬塔有什么好笑的？"他质问道。
"没什么特别好笑的，只是你应验了我的一句预言，"迈克尔说，"我就知道，我准会再听到那座老掉牙的破塔！"

## XLI The Death / XLI 死亡

===Original===
The shriek of a man half crazy with fear is not nice to hear. Michael’s nerves were tough, but he had need to drive the nails into the palms of his manacled hands to keep his self-control.
“I warn you,” he found voice to say, as the shrieking died to an unintelligible babble of sound, “Longvale, if you do this, you are everlastingly damned!”
The old man turned his quiet smile upon his second prisoner, but did not make any answer. Lifting the half-conscious man in his arms as easily as though he were a child, he carried him to the terrible machine, and laid him, face downwards, on the tilted platform. There was no hurry. Michael saw, in Longvale’s leisure, an enjoyment that was unbelievable. He stepped to the front of the machine and pulled up one half of the lunette; there was a click, and it remained stationary.
“An invention of mine,” he said with pride, speaking over his shoulder.
Michael looked away for a second, past the grim executioner, to the farther end of the cave. And then he saw a sight that brought the blood to his cheeks. At first he thought he was dreaming, and that the strain of his ordeal was responsible for some grotesque vision.
Adele!
===Chinese===
半吓疯的人发出的尖叫，听来绝不是件好受的事。迈克尔神经够硬，可也得把指甲掐进铐住的掌心，才稳得住自己。
"我警告你，"待那尖叫低落成不知所云的呓语，他才找着嗓子说，"朗维尔，你若做下这事，你要万劫不复地堕入地狱！"
老人把他那副平静的微笑转向第二名囚徒，却没有答话。他把那半昏迷的人抱进怀里，轻松得像抱一个孩子，搬到那可怕的机器前，面朝下放倒在斜置的台板上。他毫不着急。从朗维尔的从容里，迈克尔看出一种令人难以置信的享受。他走到机器正面，掀起颈圈的一半；咔哒一响，它稳稳停住。
"我的一项发明。"他回头说，语气里带着自豪。
迈克尔把视线移开了一瞬，越过那冷峻的刽子手，投向洞穴的远端。随后他看见一幕景象，热血涌上面颊。起初他以为自己在做梦，以为是酷刑的煎熬生出了一场荒诞的幻象。
阿黛尔！

===Original===
She stood clear in the white light, so grimed with earth and dust that she seemed to be wearing a grey robe.
“If you move I will kill you!”
It was she! He twisted over on to his knees and staggered upright. Longvale heard the voice and turned slowly.
“My little lady,” he said pleasantly. “How providential! I’ve always thought that the culminating point of my career would be, as was the sainted Charles Henry’s, that moment when a queen came under his hand. How very singular!”
He walked slowly toward her, oblivious to the pointed pistol, to the danger in which he stood, a radiant smile on his face, his small, white hands extended as to an honoured guest.
===Chinese===
她清清楚楚立在雪亮的灯光里，满身泥尘，仿佛披了一件灰袍。
"你动一动我就打死你！"
真是她！他侧身撑膝，踉跄着站了起来。朗维尔听见声音，缓缓转身。
"我的小夫人。"他愉快地说，"真是天意！我一向认为，我事业的至高点，会像圣洁的查尔斯·亨利一样，是一位王后落到他手上的那一刻。多么不寻常啊！"
他慢慢朝她走去，全然不顾指着他的手枪、不顾自己身处何险，脸上放着光彩，一双白皙的小手向前伸出，仿佛迎一位贵客。

===Original===
“Shoot!” cried Michael hoarsely. “For God’s sake, shoot!”
She hesitated for a second and pressed the trigger. There was no sound﻿—clogged with earth, the delicate mechanism did not act.
She turned to flee, but his arm was round her, and his disengaged hand drew her head to his breast.
“You shall see, my dear,” he said. “The Widow shall become the Widower, and you shall be his first bride!”
She was limp in his arms now, incapable of resistance. A strange sense of inertia overcame her; and, though she was conscious, she could neither of her own volition, move nor speak. Michael, struggling madly to release his hands, prayed that she might faint﻿—that, whatever happened, she should be spared a consciousness of the terror.
===Chinese===
"开枪！"迈克尔嘶声喊道，"看在上帝的份上，开枪！"
她迟疑了一秒，扣下扳机。没有声响——精巧的机件被泥土塞住，失了灵。
她转身要逃，可他的手臂已圈住她，那只闲着的手把她的头搂到了自己胸前。
"你会明白的，亲爱的。"他说，"'寡妇'要变成'鳏夫'了，你就做他的第一位新娘！"
此刻她瘫软在他怀里，全无反抗之力。一种奇异的麻痹压倒了她；她神志清醒，却凭自己的意愿既动不得，也说不得。迈克尔疯狂地挣着双手，只祈求她昏过去——无论接下来发生什么，但愿她能免于清醒地领受那恐怖。

===Original===
“Now who shall be first?” murmured the old man, stroking his shiny head. “It would be fitting that my lady should show the way, and be spared the agony of mind. And yet﻿—” He looked thoughtfully at the prostrate figure strapped to the board, and, tilting the platform, dropped the lunette about the head of Gregory Penne. The hand went up to the lever that controlled the knife. He paused again, evidently puzzling something out in his crazy mind.
“No, you shall be first,” he said, unbuckled the strap and pushed the half-demented man to the ground.
Michael saw him lift his head, listening. There were hollow sounds above, as of people walking. Again he changed his mind, stooped and dragged Gregory Penne to his feet. Michael wondered why he held him so long, standing so rigidly; wondered why he dropped him suddenly to the ground; and then wondered no longer. Something was crossing the floor of the cave﻿—a great, hairy something, whose malignant eyes were turned upon the old man.
===Chinese===
"那么，谁先来呢？"老人抚着发亮的秃顶，喃喃道，"按理该由我的夫人引路，也免受心里那份煎熬。不过——"他若有所思地望着捆在台板上的那具身躯，掀起台板，把颈圈扣落到格雷戈里·彭尼的头颅上。他的手伸向控制铡刀的杠杆，又停住了，显然那颗疯癫的头脑里正琢磨着什么。
"不，还是你先来。"他说着，解开皮带，把那半疯的人推到地上。
迈克尔看见他抬起头侧耳听着。头顶传来空洞的声响，像是有人在走动。他又变了主意，俯身把格雷戈里·彭尼拽了起来。迈克尔纳闷他为何僵立着把他抱了那么久；又纳闷他为何突然把他撂在地上；随即就不再纳闷了。有什么东西正穿过洞底——一个庞大的、毛茸茸的东西，那双凶恶的眼睛正对着老人。

===Original===
It was Bhag! His hair was matted with blood; his face wore the powder mask which Michael had seen when he emerged from Griff Towers. He stopped and sniffed at the groaning man on the floor, and his big paw touched the face tenderly. Then, without preliminary, he leapt at Longvale, and the old man went down with a crash to the ground, his arms whirling in futile defence. For a second Bhag stood over him, looking down, twittering and chattering; and then he raised the man and laid him in the place where his master had been, tilting the board and pushing it forward.
Michael gazed with fascinated horror. The great ape had witnessed an execution! It was from this cave that he had escaped, the night that Foss was killed. His half-human mind was remembering the details. Michael could almost see his mind working to recall the procedure.
Bhag fumbled with the frame, touched the spring that released the lunette, and it fell over the neck of the Headhunter. And at that moment, attracted by a sound, Michael looked up, saw the trap above pulled back. Bhag heard it also, but was too intent upon his business to be interrupted. Longvale had recovered consciousness and was fighting to draw his head from the lunette. Presently he spoke. It was as though he realized the imminence of his fate, and was struggling to find an appropriate phrase, for he lay quiescent now, his hands gripping the edge of the narrow platform on which he lay.
===Chinese===
是巴格！它的毛发纠结着血块；脸上还罩着那层面粉面具——迈克尔见它从格里夫塔楼现身时便见过。它停下来，嗅了嗅地上呻吟的人，大手掌轻柔地碰了碰那张脸。随后，没有任何征兆，它朝朗瓦尔扑了过去，老人轰然倒地，双臂徒劳地挥舞。巴格在它身上站了片刻，低头望着，啾啾地叫着；然后它把那人举起，放到先前主人躺的位置上，掀起台板，往前一送。
迈克尔惊怖地凝视着。这头巨猿曾亲眼目睹过一场行刑！福斯被杀的那一夜，它就是从这个洞穴逃出去的。它那半人的头脑正回想着一个个细节。迈克尔几乎能看见它的脑子在转动，追忆着那套程序。
巴格在机架上摸索，碰到了松开颈圈的弹簧，颈圈落下，扣住了猎头者的脖颈。就在此刻，一声声响引得迈克尔抬头，看见头顶的活动门被拉开。巴格也听见了，可它一心办它的事，不受打搅。朗瓦尔已恢复知觉，正竭力把头从颈圈里拔出。过了一会儿，他开口了。仿佛他明白大限已至，正竭力寻一句得体的话，因为他此刻静静地躺着，双手攥住身下窄台的边沿。

===Original===
“Son of St. Louis, ascend to heaven!” he said, and at that moment Bhag jerked the handle that controlled the knife.
Inspector Lyle from above saw the blade fall, heard the indescribable sound of the thud that followed, and almost swooned. Then, from below:
“It’s all right, inspector. You may find a rope in the buffet. Get down as quickly as you can and bring a gun.”
The buffet cupboard contained another rope, and a minute later the detective was going down hand over hand.
“There’s no danger from the monkey,” said Michael.
Bhag was crooning over his senseless master, as a mother over her child.
===Chinese===
"圣路易（St. Louis）之子，升天去吧！"他说。就在这一刻，巴格猛地扳下了控制铡刀的手柄。
莱尔探长从上面望见铡刀落下，听见随之而来那声难以名状的闷响，险些晕厥。随后，下面传来声音：
"没事了，探长。餐具柜里兴许还有一条绳。尽快下来，带支枪。"
餐具柜里果然还有一条绳，一分钟后，那侦探便双手交替着坠了下去。
"那猴子没有危险。"迈克尔说。
巴格正俯在昏迷的主人身上低声哼着，像母亲哄着孩子。

===Original===
“Get Miss Leamington away,” said Michael in a low voice, as the detective began to unlock the handcuffs.
The girl lay, an inanimate and silent figure, by the side of the guillotine, happily oblivious of the tragedy which had been enacted in her presence. Another detective had descended the rope, and old Jack Knebworth, despite his years, was the third to enter the cave. It was he who found the door, and aided the detective to carry the girl to safety.
Unlocking the handcuffs from the baronet’s wrists, Michael turned him over on his back. One glance at the face told the detective that the man was in a fit, and that his case, if not hopeless, was at least desperate. As though understanding that the man had no ill intent toward his master, Bhag watched passively, and then Michael remembered how, the first time he had seen the great ape, Bhag had smelt his hands.
“He’s filing you for future reference as a friend,” had said Gregory at the time.
===Chinese===
"把利明顿小姐带走。"侦探动手开手铐时，迈克尔低声说。
姑娘躺在断头台旁，一动不动，悄无声息，所幸对眼前上演的惨剧一无所觉。又一名侦探顺绳而下，老杰克·尼布沃斯不顾年岁，第三个进了洞穴。门正是他找到的，他帮着侦探把姑娘抱到了安全处。
迈克尔解下从男爵腕上的手铐，把他翻成仰卧。只消看一眼那张脸，侦探便知此人癫痫发作，就算还有救，也已命悬一线。巴格似乎明白此人对它的主人并无恶意，只在一旁静静看着。迈克尔这才想起，头一回见这头巨猿时，巴格曾嗅过他的手。
"它这是把你的气味归档，留作日后查考的朋友。"格雷戈里当时这么说。

===Original===
“Pick him up,” said Michael, speaking distinctly in the manner that Gregory had addressed the ape.
Without hesitation, Bhag stooped and lifted the limp man in his arms, and Michael guided him to the stairway and led him up the stairs.
The house was full of police, who gaped at the sight of the great ape and his burden.
“Take him upstairs and put him on the bed,” ordered Michael.
Knebworth had already taken the girl off in his car to Chichester, for she had shown signs of reviving, and he wanted to get her away from that house of the dead before she fully recovered.
Michael went down into the cave again and joined the inspector. Together they made a brief tour. The headless figures in the niches told their own story. Farther on, Michael came to the bigger cavern, with its floor littered with bones.
===Chinese===
"把他抱起来。"迈克尔说，口齿清楚，学着格雷戈里对那猩猩说话的腔调。
巴格毫不迟疑，俯身把软瘫的人抱进怀里，迈克尔给它引到楼梯口，领它拾级而上。
宅子里满是警察，见了巨猿抱着那人的情景，个个目瞪口呆。
"把他抬上楼，放到床上。"迈克尔下令。
尼布沃斯已开车把姑娘送往奇切斯特，因为她已显出苏醒的迹象，他要赶在她完全醒来之前，把她带离这座死人之宅。
迈克尔又下到洞里，与探长会合。两人简略巡了一遍。壁龛里那些无头的身影，各自诉说着各自的故事。再往前，迈克尔来到那座更大的洞厅，地上撒满白骨。

===Original===
“Here is confirmation of the old legend,” he said in a hushed voice, and pointed. “These are the bones of those warriors and squires who were trapped in the cave by a landslide. You can see the horses’ skeletons quite plainly.”
How had Adele got into the cave? He was not long before he found the slide down which she had tumbled.
“Another mystery is explained,” he said. “Griff Tower was obviously built by the Romans to prevent cattle and men from falling through into the cave. Incidentally, it has served as an excellent ventilator, and I have no doubt the old man had this way prepared, both as a hiding-place for the people he had killed and as a way of escape.”
He saw a candle-lantern and matches that the girl had missed, and this he regarded as conclusive proof that his view was right.
They came back to the guillotine with its ghastly burden, and Michael stood in silence for a long time, looking at the still figure stretched on the platform, its hands still clutching the sides.
===Chinese===
"老传说在这里得了印证。"他压低声音说，指了指，"这些就是当年被地陷困死在洞中的骑士和乡绅的骸骨。马的骨架看得很清楚。"
阿黛尔是怎么进洞的？没费多久，他便找到了她滚落下来的那道滑坡。
"又一个谜解开了。"他说，"格里芬塔显然是罗马人所筑，为的是防止牲畜和人跌进洞里。顺带说一句，它还是个顶好的通风筒。我毫不怀疑，老人早把这条路备好了——既是藏匿他杀过的人的地方，也是一条退路。"
他看见了姑娘漏下的一盏烛灯和火柴，他认为这足证自己的看法不错。
他们回到断头台前，台上一副可怖的重负。迈克尔默默站了许久，望着那摊在台板上的静默身形，双手仍攥着台的两侧。

===Original===
“How did he persuade these people to come to their death?” asked the inspector in a voice little above a whisper.
“That is a question for the psychologist,” said Michael at last. “There is no doubt that he got into touch with many men who were contemplating suicide but shrank from the act, and performed this service for them. I should imagine his practice of leaving around their heads for identification arose out of some poor wretch’s desire that his wife and family should secure his insurance.
“He worked with extraordinary cunning. The letters, as you know, went to a house of call and were collected by an old woman, who posted them to a second address, whence they were put in prepared envelopes and posted, ostensibly to London. I discovered that the envelopes were kept in a specially lightproof box, and that the unknown advertiser had stipulated that they should not be taken out of that box until they were ready for posting. An hour after those letters were put in the mail the address faded and became invisible, and another appeared.”
===Chinese===
"他是怎么说动这些人前来赴死的？"探长的声音低得几近耳语。
"这个问题该留给心理学家。"迈克尔终于开口，"毫无疑问，他联系上了许多动了自尽之念却又下不去手的人，替他们操办了这项服务。我猜，他那套把头颅四处留放、供人辨认的做法，多半源于某个可怜虫的心愿——好叫他的妻儿领到保险金。
"他行事狡诈到了家。那些信，你也知道了，先寄到一家代收店，由一个老太婆取走，再寄往第二个地址，在那儿装进预备好的信封，表面上寄往伦敦。我发现那些信封收在一只有意遮光的特制盒子里，那位无名登报人还特别言明，信封在付邮之前不得取出盒子。那些信投进邮筒一个钟头后，地址便褪去，隐没不见，另一个地址浮现出来。"

===Original===
“Vanishing ink?”
Mike nodded.
“It is a trick that criminals frequently employ. The new address, of course, was Dower House. Put out the lights and let us go up.”
Three lamps were extinguished, and the detective looked round fearfully at the shadows.
“I think we’ll leave this down here,” he said.
“I think we will,” said Michael, in complete agreement.
===Chinese===
"隐形墨水？"
迈克点了点头。
"这是罪犯们常用的把戏。新地址，自然就是寡妇居。把灯灭了吧，我们上去。"
三盏灯熄了，侦探惶惶地环顾四周的暗影。
"我看这个就留在这儿吧。"他说。
"我看也是。"迈克尔说，深表赞同。

## XXI The Erasure / XXI 涂销

===Original===
Mike’s way back did not lead through the little street where Adele Leamington lived—at least, not his nearest road. Yet he found himself knocking at the door, and learnt, with a sense of disappointment, that the girl had been out since seven o’clock in the morning. Knebworth was shooting on the South Downs, and the studio, when he arrived, was empty, except for Knebworth’s secretary and the new scenario editor, who had arrived late on the previous evening.
“I don’t know the location, Mr. Brixan,” said Dicker, the secretary, “but it’s somewhere above Arundel. Miss Mendoza was here this morning, asking the same question. She wanted Miss Leamington to go out to lunch with her.”
“Oh, she did, did she?” said Michael softly. “Well, if she comes again, you can tell her from me that Miss Leamington has another engagement.”
The other nodded wisely.
===Chinese===
迈克归途本不经过阿黛尔·利明顿住的那条小街——至少，最近的一条路并不经过。可他还是发现自己敲响了那扇门，又带着几分失落得知，姑娘早晨七点就出门了。尼布沃斯在南唐斯拍戏；等他赶到摄影棚，里面空空荡荡，只有尼布沃斯的秘书和新来的剧本编辑（scenario editor）——后者是前一天夜里才到的。
"外景在哪儿我不知道，布里克森先生。"秘书迪克（Dicker）说，"只知道在阿伦德尔上游一带。门多萨小姐今天早上也来打听过同一句话。她想请利明顿小姐陪她出去吃午饭。"
"哦，是嘛？"迈克尔轻声说，"那好，她要是再来，你就替我捎句话：利明顿小姐另有约会。"
对方心领神会地点了点头。

===Original===
“I hope she won’t keep you waiting,” he said. “You never know, when Jack’s on location—”
“I did not say she had an engagement with me,” said Michael loudly.
“That reminds me, Mr. Brixan,” said the secretary suddenly. “Do you remember the fuss you made—I mean, there was—about a sheet of manuscript that by some accident had got into Miss Leamington’s script?”
Michael nodded.
===Chinese===
"但愿她不会让你久等。"他说，"杰克在外景地的时候，什么事都说不准——"
"我可没说她跟我有约会。"迈克尔提高了嗓门说。
"这一说倒提醒了我，布里克森先生。"秘书忽然说，"您还记得那场风波吗——我是说，曾经有那么一页手稿，不知怎么阴差阳错混进了利明顿小姐的剧本里？"
迈克尔点了点头。

===Original===
“Has the manuscript been found?” he asked.
“No, but the new scenario editor tells me that he was looking through the book where Foss kept a record of all the manuscripts that came in, and he found one entry had been blacked out with Indian ink.”
“I’d like to see that book,” said the interested Michael, and it was brought to him, a large foolscap ledger, ruled to show the name of the submitted scenario, the author, his address, the date received and the date returned. Mike put it down on the table in Knebworth’s private office and went carefully through the list of authors.
“If he sent one he has probably sent more,” he said. “There are no other erasures?”
===Chinese===
"那份手稿找到了吗？"他问。
"还没有。不过新来的剧本编辑告诉我，他翻检福斯登记所有来稿的那本簿子时，发现有一条记录被墨汁涂掉了。"
"那本簿子我想看看。"来了兴致的迈克尔说。簿子给取了来：一本大开本账簿，栏格分明，登记着投稿的剧本名、作者、地址、收稿日期和退稿日期。迈克把它摊在尼布沃斯私人办公室的桌上，细心地逐行排查作者姓名。
"他既然投过一篇，多半还投过不止一篇。"他说，"没有别的涂销了吧？"

===Original===
The secretary shook his head.
“That is the only one we’ve seen,” he said. “You’ll find lots of names of local people—there isn’t a tradesman in the place who hasn’t written a scenario or submitted an idea since we’ve been operating.”
Slowly Michael’s finger went up the column of names. Page after page was turned back. And then his finger stopped at an entry.
“The Power of Fear: Sir Gregory Penne,” he read, and looked round at Dicker.
===Chinese===
秘书摇了摇头。
"我们见到的就这一条。"他说，"本地人的名字你会查到一大堆——自打我们开张，镇上就没有哪个商家没写过剧本、没送来过点子的。"
迈克尔的指尖顺着姓名栏缓缓上移，一页一页往前翻。忽然，指尖停在一行字上。
"《恐惧的力量》（The Power of Fear），格雷戈里·彭尼爵士。"他念道，回头看迪克。

===Original===
“Did Sir Gregory submit scenarios, Mr. Dicker?”
Dicker nodded.
“Yes, he sent in one or two,” he said. “You’ll find his name farther back in the book. He used to write scenarios which he thought were suitable for Miss Mendoza. He’s not the man you’re looking for?”
“No,” said Michael quickly. “Have you any of his manuscript?”
===Chinese===
"格雷戈里爵士投过剧本吗，迪克先生？"
迪克点了点头。
"投过，来过一两篇。"他说，"再往前翻还能找到他的名字。他常写些自认为适合门多萨小姐的剧本。他不是你要找的那个人吧？"
"不是。"迈克尔急忙说，"他的手稿你们这儿还有吗？"

===Original===
“They were all sent back,” said Dicker regretfully. “He wrote awful mush! I read one of them. I remember Foss trying to persuade old Jack to produce it. Foss made quite a lot of money on the side, we’ve discovered. He used to take fees from authors, and Mr. Knebworth discovered this morning that he once took two hundred pounds from a lady on the promise that he’d get her into the pictures. He wrote Foss a stinging letter this morning about it.”
Presently Michael found Sir Gregory’s name again. It was not remarkable that the owner of Griff Towers should have submitted a manuscript. There was hardly a thinking man or woman in the world who did not believe he or she was capable of writing for the films.
He closed the book and handed it back to Dicker.
“It is certainly queer, that erased entry. I’ll speak to Foss about it as soon as I can find him,” he said.
===Chinese===
"全都退回去了。"迪克不无遗憾地说，"他写的东西糟透了！我读过一篇。记得福斯还极力撺掇老杰克把它拍出来。我们这才发现，福斯背地里捞了不少外快。他向来向作者收取费用，尼布沃斯先生今天早上还发现，他有一回收了一位太太两百英镑，许诺把她捧上银幕。今天早上他就这事给福斯写了封尖酸刻薄的信。"
不一会儿，迈克尔又找到了格雷戈里爵士的名字。格里夫塔楼的主人投过稿，这并不稀奇：世上几乎找不出哪个肯动脑筋的男人或女人，不自认有本事给电影写剧本的。
他合上簿子，递还给迪克。
"那条涂掉的记录，确实蹊跷。一找到福斯，我就要问问他这件事。"他说。

===Original===
He went immediately to the little hotel where Foss was staying, but he was out.
“I don’t think he came home last night,” said the manager. “If he did, he didn’t sleep in his bed. He said he was going to London,” he added.
Michael went back to the studio, for it had begun to rain, and he knew that that would drive the company from location. His surmise was correct: the big yellow charabanc came rumbling into the yard a few minutes after he got there. Adele saw him, and was passing with a nod when he called her to him.
“Thank you, Mr. Brixan, but we lunched on location, and I have two big scenes to read for tomorrow.”
===Chinese===
他随即去了福斯下榻的那家小旅馆，人却不在。
"我看他昨晚没回来过。"旅馆经理说，"就算回来过，也没在床上睡。他说过要去伦敦。"他又补了一句。
迈克尔回到摄影棚，因为天开始下雨，他知道雨会把全班人马从外景地赶回来。他料得不错：他到后没几分钟，那辆黄色大游览车便隆隆驶进院子。阿黛尔看见了他，正点头走过去，他把她叫住了。
"谢谢您，布里克森先生，我们在外景地吃过午饭了，再说明天还有两场重头戏的台词要背呢。"

===Original===
Her refusal was uncompromising, but Michael was not the type who readily accepted a “No.”
“What about tea? You’ve got to drink tea, my good lady, though you have fifty scenes to study. And you can’t read and eat too. If you do, you’ll get indigestion, and if you get indigestion—”
She laughed.
“If my landlady will loan me her parlour, you may come to tea at half-past four,” she said; “and if you have another engagement at five o’clock, you’ll be able to meet it.”
===Chinese===
她回绝得毫不含糊，可迈克尔不是那种轻易接受"不"字的人。
"那么喝茶呢？我的好小姐，茶总是要喝的，哪怕你有五十场戏要背。再说，你总不能一边啃剧本一边吃东西吧？非要那样，你会消化不良；你一消化不良——"
她笑了。
"要是房东太太肯把客厅借给我，你四点半可以来喝茶。"她说，"这样，你五点钟另有什么约会，也还赶得及。"

===Original===
Jack Knebworth was waiting for him when he went into the studio.
“Heard about that entry in the scenario book?” he asked. “I see you have. What do you think of it?” Without waiting for a reply: “It looks queer to me. Foss was an unmitigated liar. That fellow couldn’t see straight. I’ve got a little bone to pick with him on the matter of a fee he accepted from a screen-struck lady who wished to be featured in one of my productions.”
“How’s the girl?” asked Michael.
===Chinese===
他走进摄影棚时，杰克·尼布沃斯正等着他。
"剧本簿里那条记录，听说了吧？"他问，"哦，看样子是听说了。你怎么看？"不等回答，他又说下去："我看蹊跷得很。福斯是个彻头彻尾的骗子。那家伙眼睛长歪了，看什么都不对路。就他收下一位影迷成痴的太太的酬金、答应在我某部片子里让她领衔这事，我还得跟他小小地理论理论。"
"那姑娘怎么样？"迈克尔问。

===Original===
“You mean Adele? Really, she’s wonderful, Brixan! I’m touching wood all the time”—he put his hand on the table piously—“because I know that there’s a big shock coming to me somewhere and somehow. Those things do not happen in real life.
