The only stars that are born in a night are the fireworks produced by crazy vice-presidents who have promised to do something for Mamie and can’t break their word. And Mamie, supported by six hundred extras and half a million dollars’ worth of sets, two chariot races and the fall of Babylon, all produced regardless of expense, manages to get over by giving a fine imitation of what the Queen of Persia would look like if she’d been born a chorus girl and trained as a mannequin. And she’s either got so few clothes that you don’t look at her face, or so many clothes that you don’t notice her acting.
“Those kind of stars are like the dust of the Milky Way: there is so much splendour all round them that it wouldn’t matter if they weren’t there at all. But this girl Leamington, she’s getting over entirely and absolutely by sheer, unadulterated grey matter. I tell you, Brixan, it’s not right. These things do not happen except in the imagination of press agents. There’s something wrong with that kid.”
“Wrong?” said Michael, startled.
Knebworth nodded.
===Chinese===
"你说阿黛尔？说真的，她好得了不得，布里克森！我这颗心一直悬着，不停地敲木头"——他虔敬地把手按在桌上——"因为我知道，不知在哪儿、不知怎的，一场大惊吓正等着我。这种事在现实里是不会有的。一夜成名的明星，只有那些疯癫的副总经理造出来的烟花——他们答应过要为玛米（Mamie）做点事，又不能自食其言。于是玛米便靠六百名群众演员、值五十万美元的布景、两场战车竞赛外加一场巴比伦城的倾覆，样样不计成本地捧起来，居然也就立住了——活脱脱演出一副模样，仿佛波斯王后生下来原是个歌队姑娘，再按时装模特的路子调教出来会是什么样子。而她呢，衣裳不是少得你顾不上看她的脸，就是多得你看不出她的演技。
"那路明星就像银河里的尘埃：四下里光辉灿烂得很，少了她们也毫不见缺。可利明顿这姑娘，完完全全、彻头彻尾是靠着一副纯粹的、不掺假的脑瓜立住的。我跟你说，布里克森，这不合常理。这种事除非在宣传员的想象里，是不会有的。那孩子身上有点不对劲。"
"不对劲？"迈克尔吃了一惊。
尼布沃斯点点头。

===Original===
“Something radically wrong. There’s a snag somewhere. She’s either going to let me down by vanishing before the picture’s through, or else she’s going to be arrested for driving a car along Regent Street in a highly intoxicated condition!”
Michael laughed.
“I think she’ll do neither,” he said.
“Heard about Mendoza’s new company?” asked old Jack, filling his pipe.
===Chinese===
"根本性的不对劲。哪儿有个坎儿没迈过去。她要么会在片子拍完之前销声匿迹，撂下我不管；要么就会因为醉醺醺驾车开上摄政街（Regent Street），叫警察抓起来！"
迈克尔笑了。
"我看她两样都不会。"他说。
"门多萨要办新公司的事，听说了吗？"老杰克一边装烟斗一边问。

===Original===
Michael pulled up a chair and sat down.
“No, I haven’t.”
“She’s starting a new production company. There’s never a star I’ve fired that hasn’t! It gets all written out on paper, capital in big type, star in bigger! It’s generally due to the friends of the star, who tell her that a hundred thousand a year is a cruel starvation wage for a woman of her genius, and she ought to get it all. Generally there’s a sucker in the background who puts up the money. As a rule, he puts up all but enough, and then she selects a story where she is never off the screen, and wears a new dress every fifty feet of film. If she can’t find that sort of story, why, she gets somebody to write her one. The only time you ever see the other members of the company is in the long shots. Halfway through the picture the money dries up, the company goes bust, and all the poor little star gets out of it is the Rolls-Royce she bought to take her on location, the new bungalow she built to be nearer the lot, and about twenty-five percent of the capital that she’s taken on account of royalties.”
“Mendoza will not get a good producer in England?”
===Chinese===
迈克尔拖过一把椅子坐下。
"没有，还没听说。"
"她要办一家新的制片公司。我开掉过的明星，还没有一个不这么干的！章程全都落在纸面上：资本用大号字排，明星的名字排得更大！这多半是明星的那班朋友撺掇的，他们对她说，凭她那样的天才，一年十万进项简直是残忍的饥饿工钱，这钱她本该一个人全拿。背后一般有个冤大头掏钱。通常呢，他出的钱总是差着那么一口气不够，于是她挑一个自己永不离开银幕的本子，每五十英尺胶片就换一身新行头。找不到这样的本子？好办，雇人给她写一个就是。公司里其余的人，你只有在远景里才见得着。片子拍到一半，钱源枯竭，公司倒闭，可怜的小明星落得的全部好处，就是那辆载她去外景地的劳斯莱斯、那座为了离片场近点新盖的小洋房，外加她以版税名义预支走的大约百分之二十五的资本。"
"门多萨在英国就找不到一个像样的制片人？"

===Original===
“She may,” nodded Jack. “There *are* producers in this country, but unfortunately they’re not the men on top. They’ve been brought down by the craze for greatness. A man who produces with a lot of capital behind him can get easy money. He doesn’t go after the domestic stories, where he’d be found out first time; he says to the moneybags: ‘Let’s produce the Fall of Jerusalem. I’ve got a cute idea for building Ezekiel’s temple that’s never been taken before. It’ll only cost a mere trifle of two hundred thousand dollars, and we’ll have five thousand extras in one scene, and we’ll rebuild the Colosseum and have a hundred real lions in the arena! Story? What do you want a story for? The public love crowds.’ Or maybe he wants to build a new Vesuvius and an eruption at the rate of fifty dollars a foot. There’s many a big reputation been built up on sets and extras. Come in, Mr. Longvale.”
Michael turned. The cheery old man was at the door, hat in hand.
“I am afraid I am rather a nuisance,” he said in his beautiful voice. “But I came in to see my lawyer, and I could not deny myself the satisfaction of calling to see how your picture is progressing.”
“It is going on well, Mr. Longvale, thank you,” said Jack. “You know Mr. Brixan?”
===Chinese===
"或许会。"杰克点头道，"这个国家*是*有制片人的，只可惜当家的不是他们。那股贪大求狂的狂热把他们压倒了。背后有大资本撑腰的制片人，钱来得容易。他不去碰家庭伦理故事——那种片子头一回就会被看穿；他对财东们说：'咱们来拍《耶路撒冷的陷落》。造以西结的圣殿，我有个绝妙的主意，前人从没用过。不过破费区区二十万美元，一个场面就上五千名群众演员，再重建一座罗马斗兽场，场子里放一百头真狮子！故事？要故事干什么？观众就爱看人山人海。'要不然，他就想造一座新的维苏威火山，按每英尺五十美元的价码来一场喷发。靠布景和群众演员撑起来的大名头，多得是。请进，朗维尔先生。"
迈克尔转过身。那位快活的老先生已在门口，手里拿着帽子。
"恐怕我是相当讨人嫌的。"他用那副好听的嗓音说，"不过我今天进来是要见我的律师，实在按捺不住，想顺路来问一声，你们的片子拍得怎么样了。"
"进展很顺利，朗维尔先生，谢谢。"杰克说，"这位是布里克森先生，您认识吧？"

===Original===
The old man nodded and smiled.
“Yes, I came in to see my lawyer on what to you will seem to be a curious errand. Many years ago I was a medical student and took my final examination, so that I am, to all intents and purposes, a doctor, though I’ve not practised to any extent. It is not generally known that I have a medical degree and I was surprised last night to be called out by—er—a neighbour, who wished me to attend a servant of his. Now, I am so hazy on the subject that I wasn’t quite sure whether or not I’d broken the law by practising without registration.”
“I can relieve your mind there, Mr. Longvale,” said Michael. “Once you are registered, you are always registered, and you acted quite within your rights.”
“So my lawyer informed me,” said Longvale gravely.
===Chinese===
老先生含笑点头。
"是啊，我进来找律师，是为一件在你们看来想必很古怪的差事。许多年前我读过医科，也通过了毕业考试，所以不论从哪方面说，我都算个医生，只是从未真正行过医。我有医学学位这件事，外人并不都知道；昨天夜里，一位——呃——邻居来请我出诊，去看他家的一个仆人，我着实吃了一惊。这门学问我荒疏得太久，心里一直没底：未注册而行医，不知算不算犯了法。"
"这一点我可以给您吃颗定心丸，朗维尔先生。"迈克尔说，"注册一次，终身有效，您行使的完全是您的正当权利。"
"我的律师也是这样对我说的。"朗维尔一本正经地说。

===Original===
“Was it a bad case?” asked Michael, who guessed who the patient was.
“No, it was not a bad case. I thought there was blood poisoning, but I think perhaps I may have been mistaken. Medical science has made such great advance since I was a young man that I almost feared to prescribe. Whilst I am only too happy to render any service that humanity demands, I must confess that it was rather a disturbing experience, and I scarcely slept all night. In fact, it was a very disturbing evening and night. Somebody, for some extraordinary reason, put a motor-bicycle in my garden.”
Michael smiled to himself.
“I cannot understand why. It had gone this morning. And then I saw our friend Foss, who seemed very much perturbed about something.”
===Chinese===
"病情重吗？"迈克尔问，他心里已猜到病人是谁。
"不重。我起先疑心是血液中毒，不过多半是我看走了眼。打我年轻那会儿起，医学进步太大，我几乎不敢开方子。人道所需，我自然乐于效劳，可我也不瞒您说，这一遭实在搅得人心神不宁，我几乎一夜没合眼。说实在的，那晚整晚整夜都不安生。不知是哪个，也不知安的什么古怪心思，把一辆摩托车搁在了我家花园里。"
迈克尔暗自发笑。
"我实在想不明白。今天早晨，那车子又不见了。后来我还遇见了我们的朋友福斯，他好像为什么事心烦意乱得很。"

===Original===
“Where did you see him?” asked Michael quickly.
“He was passing my house. I was standing at the gate, smoking my pipe, and bade him good night without knowing who he was. When he turned back, I saw it was Mr. Foss. He told me he had been to make a call, and that he had another appointment in an hour.”
“What time was this?” asked Michael.
“I think it must have been eleven o’clock.” The old man hesitated. “I’m not sure. It was just before I went to bed.”
===Chinese===
"您在哪儿遇见他的？"迈克尔急忙问。
"他从我家门前过。我正站在大门口抽烟斗，随口道了声晚安，没认出他是谁。等他折回来，我才看清是福斯先生。他说他刚去出了一次诊，一个钟头后另有个约会。"
"这是几点钟的事？"迈克尔问。
"我想该是十一点钟。"老先生迟疑了一下，"说不准。那时我刚要上床。"

===Original===
Michael could easily account for Foss’s conduct. Sir Gregory had hurried him off and told him to come back after the girl had gone.
“My little place used to be remarkable for its quietness,” said Mr. Longvale, and shook his head. “Perhaps,” turning to Knebworth, “when your picture is finished you will be so good as to allow me to see it?”
“Why, surely, Mr. Longvale.”
“I don’t know why I’m taking this tremendous interest,” chuckled the old man. “I must confess that, until a few weeks ago, film-making was a mystery to me. And even today it belongs to the esoteric sciences.”
===Chinese===
福斯的行径，迈克尔不难解释：格雷戈里爵士把他打发走，叫等姑娘走后再回来。
"我这小小的宅子，从前是以清静出名的。"朗维尔先生说着摇了摇头。"或许，"他转向尼布沃斯，"等你们的片子拍完，可否劳驾让我看一看？"
"哎呀，那当然，朗维尔先生。"
"连我自己也不明白，怎么会对这个有这么大的兴致。"老先生呵呵笑道，"不瞒您说，直到几个星期以前，拍电影对我还是一门玄奥的学问；就是今天，它也仍属于秘传的方术。"

===Original===
Dicker thrust his head in the door.
“Will you see Miss Mendoza?” he asked.
Jack Knebworth’s expression was one of utter weariness.
“No,” he said curtly.
===Chinese===
迪克从门口探进头来。
"门多萨小姐求见，见吗？"他问。
杰克·尼布沃斯一脸说不出的厌倦。
"不见。"他没好气地说。

===Original===
“She says—” began Dicker.
Only the presence of the venerable Mr. Longvale prevented Jack from expressing his views on Stella Mendoza and all that she could say.
“There’s another person I saw last night,” nodded Mr. Longvale. “I thought at first you must be shooting—is that the expression?—in the neighbourhood, but Mr. Foss told me that I was mistaken. She’s rather a charming girl, don’t you think?”
“Very,” said Jack dryly.
“A very sweet disposition,” Longvale went on, unconscious of the utter lack of sympathy in the atmosphere. “Nowadays, the confusion and hurry which modernity brings in its trail do not make for sweetness of temper, and one is glad to meet an exception. Not that I am an enemy of modernity. To me, this is the most delightful phase of my long life.”
“Sweet disposition!” almost howled Jack Knebworth when the old man had taken a dignified farewell. “Did you get that, Brixan? Say, if that woman’s disposition is sweet, the devil’s made of chocolate!”
===Chinese===
"她说——"迪克刚开口。
全亏德高望重的朗维尔先生在场，杰克才没把他肚子里对斯特拉·门多萨的种种评价、连同她可能说出口的一切，一股脑儿倒出来。
"昨晚我还看见了一个人。"朗维尔先生点头道，"起先我当是你们——该怎么说来着？——在这一带取景拍戏，可福斯先生说我弄错了。那姑娘挺讨人喜欢，你们说是不是？"
"讨人喜欢得很。"杰克干巴巴地说。
"性情还格外温甜。"朗维尔接着说，浑然不觉四下里已毫无共鸣。"如今这个世道，现代文明裹挟来的纷乱与匆忙，最养不出好性子，所以遇到个例外，人就格外欢喜。这倒不是说我跟现代过不去。对我而言，眼下正是我漫长一生中最惬意的光景。"
"性情温甜！"老先生矜持地告辞之后，杰克·尼布沃斯几乎嚎叫起来，"布里克森，你听见没有？我说，那女人的性情要算温甜，魔鬼就是巧克力做的！"

## XXXVI The Escape / XXXVI 逃亡

===Original===
“Don’t start thanking God too soon,” said Stella with ominous calm. “Oh, you little fool, why did you come here?”
“He brought me. I didn’t want to come,” said Adele.
She was half hysterical in her fright. She tried hard to imitate the calm of her companion, biting her quivering lips to keep them still, and after a while she was calm enough to tell what had happened. Stella’s face clouded.
“Of course, he took my car,” she said, speaking to herself, “and he has caught the chauffeur, as he said he would. Oh, my God!”
“What will he do?” asked Adele in a whisper.
===Chinese===
"先别急着谢天谢地。"斯特拉说，平静得令人心悸，"哦，你这小傻瓜，何必上这儿来？"
"是他把我带来的。我本来不想来。"阿黛尔说。
她吓得半是歇斯底里。她竭力仿效同伴的镇定，咬住颤抖的嘴唇不让它抖，过了一阵，总算能平静地把经过讲了出来。斯特拉的脸沉了下来。
"不用说，他开走了我的车，"她自言自语道，"司机也叫他扣住了，果然像他说的那样。哦，我的天！"
"他会怎么样？"阿黛尔耳语般地问。

===Original===
Stella’s fine eyes turned on the girl.
“What do you think he will do?” she asked significantly. “He’s a beast﻿—the kind of beast you seldom meet except in books﻿—and locked rooms. He’ll have no more mercy on you than Bhag would have on you.”
“If Michael knows, he will kill him.”
“Michael? Oh, Brixan, you mean?” said Stella with newly awakened interest. “Is he fond of you? Is that why he hangs around the lot? That never struck me before. But what does he care about Michael or any other man? He can run﻿—his yacht is at Southampton, and he depends a lot upon his wealth to get him out of these kind of scrapes. And he knows that decent women shrink from appearance in a police court. Oh, he’s got all sorts of defences. He’s a worm, but a scaly worm!”
“What shall I do?”
===Chinese===
斯特拉那双秀目转向姑娘。
"你以为他会怎么样？"她意味深长地问，"他是头畜生——那种畜生，你除了在书里难得遇上，除非是在反锁的屋子里。他对你不会心慈手软，巴格对你也不会。"
"要是迈克尔知道了，他会杀了他的。"
"迈克尔？哦，你是说布里克森？"斯特拉说，兴味顿时被勾了起来，"他喜欢你？所以才老在摄影棚里转悠？这我从前竟没想到。可他哪会把迈克尔或任何男人放在眼里？他会跑——他的游艇就泊在南安普敦（Southampton），他一向仗着钱财替自己脱这一类的干系。他还知道，正经女人最怕上治安法庭抛头露面。哦，他的退路多得很。他是条虫，一条长着鳞甲的虫！"
"我该怎么办？"

===Original===
Stella was walking up and down the narrow apartment, her hands clasped before her, her eyes sunk to the ground.
“I don’t think he’ll hurt me.” And then, inconsequently, she went off at a tangent: “I saw a tramp at that window two hours ago.”
“A tramp?” said the bewildered girl.
Stella nodded.
“It scared me terribly, until I remembered his eyes. They were Brixan’s eyes, though you’d never guess it, the makeup was so wonderful.”
“Michael? Is he here?” asked the girl eagerly.
===Chinese===
斯特拉在狭窄的套间里来回踱步，双手交握在身前，眼睛盯着地面。
"我想他还不会害我。"接着她没头没脑地岔开一句："两个钟头前，我看见那扇窗外有个流浪汉。"
"流浪汉？"姑娘一头雾水。
斯特拉点了点头。
"把我吓得不轻，直到我想起那双眼睛。那是布里克森的眼睛——虽然你绝猜不出来，那化装实在太妙了。"
"迈克尔？他在这儿？"姑娘急切地问。

===Original===
“He’s somewhere around. That is your salvation, and there’s another.”
She took down from a shelf a small Browning.
“Did you ever fire a pistol?”
The girl nodded.
“I have to, in one scene,” she said a little awkwardly.
“Of course! Well, this is loaded. That”﻿—she pointed﻿—“is the safety catch. Push it down with your thumb before you start to use it. You had better kill Penne﻿—better for you, and better for him, I think.”
===Chinese===
"他就在附近什么地方。这是你的一条生路，还有一条。"
她从架子上取下一柄小巧的勃朗宁。
"你开过枪吗？"
姑娘点了点头。
"有一场戏里，我不得不开枪。"她说得有点别扭。
"可不是！喏，这把上了膛。那个"——她指了指——"是保险。动手之前，先用拇指把它拨下去。你最好毙了彭尼——于你更好，我想于他也更好。"

===Original===
The girl shrank back in horror.
“Oh, no, no!”
“Put it in your pocket﻿—have you a pocket?”
There was one inside the blue cloak the girl was wearing, and into this Stella dropped the pistol.
“You don’t know what sort of sacrifice I’m making,” she said frankly, “and it isn’t as though I’m doing it for somebody I’m fond of, because I’m not particularly fond of you, Adele Leamington. But I wouldn’t be fit to live if I let that brute get you without a struggle.”
And then impulsively she stooped forward and kissed the girl, and Adele put her arms about her neck and clung to her for a second.
===Chinese===
姑娘惊恐地缩了回去。
"哦，不，不行！"
"揣进兜里——你有兜吗？"
姑娘身披的蓝斗篷内侧有一个暗兜，斯特拉便把手枪塞了进去。
"你不知道我这是在做怎样的牺牲。"她坦白地说，"何况我也不是在为哪个心爱的人效劳——我对你谈不上多喜欢，阿黛尔·利明顿。可若让那畜生不费一刀一枪就得手，我还配活在世上吗。"
随后她一时冲动，俯身吻了姑娘，阿黛尔伸臂搂住她的脖颈，依偎了片刻。

===Original===
“He’s coming,” whispered Stella Mendoza, and stepped back with a gesture.
It was Gregory﻿—Gregory in his scarlet pyjama jacket and purple dressing-gown, his face aflame, his eyes fired with excitement.
“Come on, you!” He crooked his finger. “Not you, Mendoza: you stay here, eh? You can see her after, perhaps﻿—after supper.”
He leered down at the shrinking girl.
“Nobody’s going to hurt you. Leave your cloak here.”
“No, I’ll wear it,” she said.
===Chinese===
"他来了。"斯特拉·门多萨低语道，做一个手势退后了一步。
来的是格雷戈里——猩红睡衣上装外罩紫色晨袍的格雷戈里，满脸烧得通红，双眼亢奋冒火。
"来吧，你！"他勾了勾手指，"没你的事，门多萨：你待在这儿，嗯？回头兴许还能见着她——夜宵过后。"
他斜眼睨着瑟缩的姑娘。
"没人会伤你。斗篷搁这儿吧。"
"不，我穿着。"她说。

===Original===
Her hand went instinctively to the butt of the pistol and closed upon it.
“All right, come as you are. It makes no difference to me.”
He held her tightly by the hand and marched by her side, surprised and pleased that she offered so little resistance. Down into the hall they went, and then to the little drawing-room adjoining his study. He flung open the door and showed her the gaily decorated table, pushing her into the room before him.
“Wine and a kiss!” he roared, as he pulled the cork from a champagne bottle and sent the amber fluid splashing upon the spotless tablecloth. “Wine and a kiss!” He splashed the glass out to her so that it spilt and trickled down her cloak.
She shook her head mutely.
===Chinese===
她的手本能地摸向枪柄，握了个正着。
"行，就这样来吧。对我无所谓。"
他紧紧攥着她的手，并肩而行，见她几乎不作抵抗，又惊又喜。两人下楼进了门厅，再到他书房隔壁的小客厅。他推开门，把装点得喜气洋洋的餐桌指给她看，把她推到自己前头进了屋。
"美酒加一吻！"他大吼一声，拔开香槟的瓶塞，琥珀色的酒液泼溅在雪白的桌布上，"美酒加一吻！"他把斟满的酒杯朝她递去，酒泼了出来，顺着她的斗篷往下淌。
她默默摇头。

===Original===
“Drink!” he snarled, and she touched the glass with her lips.
Then, before she could realize what had happened, she was in his arms, his great face pressed down to hers. She tried to escape from the encirclement of his embrace, successfully averted her mouth and felt his hot lips pressing against her cheek.
Presently he let her go, and, staggering to the door, kicked it shut. His fingers were closing on the key handle when:
“If you turn that key I’ll kill you.”
He looked up in ludicrous surprise, and, at the sight of the pistol in the girl’s hand, his big hands waved before his face in a gesture of fear.
===Chinese===
"喝！"他咆哮道，她拿嘴唇碰了碰杯沿。
随即，她还没弄清发生了什么，人已在他怀里，那张大脸压了下来。她竭力想挣脱那双臂的环抱，总算把嘴偏开了，只觉他那滚烫的嘴唇贴上了她的脸颊。
不一会儿他放开她，踉跄着走到门边，一脚把门踢上。他的手指就要攥住钥匙柄时——
"你敢转那把钥匙，我就杀了你。"
他抬起头，那副惊愕滑稽至极；一看见姑娘手里的手枪，两只大手便在脸前乱摆，做出一副惧相。

===Original===
“Put it down, you fool!” he squealed. “Put it down! Don’t you know what you’re doing? The damned thing may go off by accident.”
“It will not go off by accident,” she said. “Open that door.”
He hesitated for a moment, and then her thumb tightened on the safety-catch, and he must have seen the movement.
“Don’t shoot, don’t shoot!” he screamed, and flung the door wide open. “Wait, you fool! Don’t go out. Bhag is there. Bhag will get you. Stay with me. I’ll﻿—”
But she was flying down the corridor. She slipped on a loose rug in the hall but recovered herself. Her trembling hands were working at the bolts and chains; the door swung open, and in another instant she was in the open, free.
===Chinese===
"放下，你这傻瓜！"他尖叫道，"放下！你知不知道自己在干什么？这该死的玩意儿走了火可不是玩的。"
"它不会走火。"她说，"开门。"
他迟疑了片刻，随后她拇指扣上保险，他必是看见了这动作。
"别开枪，别开枪！"他尖叫着把门甩开，"等等，你这傻瓜！别出去。巴格在外面。巴格会抓住你的。留下陪我。我会——"
可她已沿着走廊飞奔。她在门厅一块松动的地毯上滑了一下，随即稳住了。她颤抖的双手去解门闩门链；门荡开了，转眼她已在露天之下，自由了。

===Original===
Sir Gregory followed her. The shock of her escape had sobered him, and all the tragic consequences which might follow came crowding in upon him, until his very soul writhed in fear. Dashing back to his study, he opened his safe, took out a bundle of notes. These he thrust into the pocket of a fur-lined overcoat that was hanging in a cupboard and put it on. He changed his slippers for thick shoes, and then bethought him of Bhag. He opened the den, but Bhag was not there, and he raised his shaking fingers to his lips. If Bhag caught her!
Some glimmering of a lost manhood stirred dully in his mind. He must first be sure of Bhag. He went out into the darkness in search of his strange and horrible servant. Putting both hands to his mouth, he emitted a long and painful howl, the call that Bhag had never yet disobeyed, and then waited. There was no answer. Again he sent forth the melancholy sound, but, if Bhag heard him, for the first time in his life he did not obey.
Gregory Penne stood in a sweat of fear, but, so standing, recovered some of his balance. There was time to change. He went up to his ornate bedroom, flung off his pyjamas, and in a short space of time was down again in the dark grounds, seeking for the ape.
===Chinese===
格雷戈里爵士追了出去。她逃脱的震荡倒让他酒醒了，随之而来的种种可怕后果一齐涌上心头，逼得他的灵魂都蜷缩发颤。他冲回书房，打开保险柜，取出一沓钞票，塞进挂在柜里的一件皮里大衣口袋，穿上身。他换下拖鞋，蹬上厚底鞋，这才想起巴格。他打开兽窟，巴格却不在，他抬起发抖的手指放到唇边。要是巴格追上了她！
一点早已沦丧的男子气概，在他心里昏昏然动了动。他必须先弄清巴格的去向。他走进黑暗，去寻他那个怪异可怕的仆役。他双手拢在嘴边，发出一声悠长而凄厉的嚎叫——那是巴格从未违抗过的召唤——然后等着。没有回应。他又发出那声悲鸣，可巴格纵然听见了，生平头一遭，没有服从。
格雷戈里·彭尼站在那里，出了一身冷汗，可这么一站，倒又找回几分镇定。还来得及换装。他上楼进了那间华饰的卧室，扒下睡衣，不多时便又下到黑沉沉的园地里，寻那只猩猩去了。

===Original===
Dressed, he felt more of a man. A long glass of whisky restored some of his confidence. He rang for the servant who was in charge of his car.
“Have the machine by the postern gate,” he said. “Get it there at once. See that the gate is open: I may have to leave tonight.”
That he would be arrested he did not doubt. Not all his wealth, his position, the pull he had in the county, could save him. This latest deed of his was something more than eccentricity.
Then he remembered that Stella Mendoza was still in the house, and went up to see her. A glance at his face told her that something unusual had happened.
“Where is Adele?” she asked instantly.
===Chinese===
衣冠上身，他自觉更像个人了。一大杯威士忌又找回几分胆气。他按铃唤来管他汽车的仆役。
"把车备到后门去。"他说，"马上弄过去。记住门要开着：今夜我兴许得走。"
他毫不怀疑自己会被逮捕。他全部的财富、地位、在本郡的势力，都救不了他。他这最新的一手，已远不止是乖僻了。
这时他想起斯特拉·门多萨还在宅里，便上去看她。她一眼瞧他的脸色，便知出了非常之事。
"阿黛尔在哪儿？"她劈头便问。

===Original===
“I don’t know. She escaped﻿—she had a pistol. Bhag went after her. God knows what will happen if he finds her. He’ll tear her limb from limb. What’s that?”
It was the faint sound of a pistol shot at a distance, and it came from the back of the house.
“Poachers,” said Gregory uneasily. “Listen, I’m going.”
“Where are you going?” she asked.
“That’s no damned business of yours,” he snarled. “Here’s some money.” He thrust some notes into her hand.
“What have you done?” she whispered in horror.
===Chinese===
"不知道。她跑了——她有枪。巴格追她去了。天知道它找着她会出什么事。它会把她撕成碎块的。那是什么声音？"
那是一声遥远的枪响，微弱可闻，从宅子背面传来。
"偷猎的。"格雷戈里心神不宁地说，"听着，我要走了。"
"你去哪儿？"她问。
"他妈的与你无关。"他厉声道，"这儿有点钱。"他往她手里塞了几张钞票。
"你干了什么？"她惊恐地低声问。

===Original===
“I’ve done nothing, I tell you,” he stormed. “But they’ll take me for it. I’m going to get to the yacht. You’d better clear before they come.”
She was collecting her hat and gloves when she heard the door close and the key turn. Mechanically he had locked her in, and mechanically took no heed of her beating hand upon the panel of the door.
Griff Towers stood on high ground and commanded a view of the byroad from Chichester. As he stood in the front of the house, hoping against hope that he would see the ape, he saw instead two lights come rapidly along the road.
“The police!” he croaked, and went blundering across the kitchen garden to the gate.
===Chinese===
"我什么也没干，告诉你。"他怒喝道，"可他们会算在我头上。我要上游艇去。趁他们没来，你最好也快走。"
她正拾掇帽子手套，忽听得房门关上，钥匙一转。他下意识地把她锁在了里面，又下意识地不理会她拍打门板的巴掌。
格里夫塔楼立在高地上，俯瞰着奇切斯特来的小路。他站在宅子前面，明知无望仍盼着望见那猩猩，望见的却是两盏车灯沿路疾驰而来。
"警察！"他嗓音嘶哑地喊了一声，跌跌撞撞穿过菜园朝后门奔去。

## XVIII The Face in the Picture / XVIII 画面中的脸

===Original===
Mr. Sampson Longvale was taking a gentle constitutional on the strip of path before his untidy house. He wore, as usual—for he was a creature of habit—a long, grey silk dressing-gown, fastened by a scarlet sash. On his head was his silk nightcap, and between his teeth a clay churchwarden pipe, which he puffed solemnly as he walked.
He had just bidden a courteous good night to the help who came in daily to tidy his living-rooms and prepare his simple meals, when he heard the sound of feet coming up the drive. He thought at first it was the woman returning (she had a habit of forgetting things); but when he turned, he saw the unprepossessing figure of a neighbour with whom he was acquainted in the sense that Sir Gregory Penne had twice been abominably rude to him.
The old man watched with immobile countenance the coming of his unwelcome visitor.
“ ’Evening!” growled Penne. “Can I speak to you privately?”
Mr. Longvale inclined his head courteously.
===Chinese===
桑普森·朗维尔先生正在他那座凌乱宅子前的一条小径上悠悠地散步。他跟往常一样——他是个极有定规的人——穿一件长长的灰绸晨衣，腰间系一条猩红饰带。头上戴着绸睡帽，牙间咬着一支陶土长柄烟斗，边走边一本正经地吞云吐雾。
他刚跟那位每天来收拾起居室、备办简餐的帮工客气地道过晚安，就听见车道上传来脚步声。起初他以为是那女人折回来了（她有落东西的习惯）；可一回头，看见的是一位邻居那副不招人待见的身形——说相识，也就是格雷戈里·彭尼爵士曾两次对他无礼到家而已。
老人面无表情地看着这不速之客走近。
"晚上好！"彭尼瓮声瓮气地说，"能私下跟你谈谈吗？"
朗维尔先生客气地点了点头。

===Original===
“Certainly, Sir Gregory. Will you come in?”
He ushered the owner of Griff Towers into the long sitting-room and lit the candles. Sir Gregory glanced round, his lips curled in disgust at the worn poverty of the apartment, and when the old man had pushed up a chair for him, it was some time before he accepted the offer.
“Now, sir,” said Mr. Longvale courteously, “to what circumstances do I owe the pleasure of this visit?”
“You had some actors staying here the other day?”
Mr. Longvale inclined his head.
===Chinese===
"当然可以，格雷戈里爵士。请进？"
他把格里夫塔楼的主人让进那间狭长的客厅，点亮蜡烛。格雷戈里爵士环顾四周，看到屋里陈旧寒酸的样子，厌恶地撇了撇嘴；老人给他推过一把椅子后，他好半天才肯坐下。
"那么，先生，"朗维尔先生彬彬有礼地说，"是什么机缘让鄙舍有幸迎来这次造访？"
"前几天你这儿住过一班戏子？"
朗维尔先生点了点头。

===Original===
“There was some fool talk about a monkey of mine trying to get into the house.”
“A monkey?” said Mr. Longvale in gentle surprise. “That is the first I have heard of monkeys.”
Which was true. The other looked at him suspiciously.
“Is that so?” he asked. “You’re not going to persuade me you didn’t hear?”
The old man stood up, a picture of dignity.
“Do you suggest that I am lying, sir?” he said. “Because, if you do, there is the door! And though it hurts me to be in the least degree discourteous to a guest of mine, I am afraid I have no other course than to ask you to leave my house.”
“All right, all right,” said Sir Gregory Penne impatiently. “Don’t lose your temper, my friend. I didn’t come to see you about that, anyway. You’re a doctor, aren’t you?”
===Chinese===
"外头有些蠢话，说我的一只猴子想钻进这宅子。"
"猴子？"朗维尔先生温和地露出讶异，"猴子的事我还是头一回听说。"
这是实话。对方狐疑地打量他。
"是吗？"他问，"你不会想让我相信你没听说过吧？"
老人站起身来，一派庄重。
"先生，您是暗示我在撒谎？"他说，"因为您要是这个意思，门就在那儿！对自家客人有半分失礼都令我心痛，可恐怕我没有别的办法，只好请您离开寒舍。"
"行了，行了，"格雷戈里·彭尼爵士不耐烦地说，"别发火，我的朋友。反正我不是为这个来的。你当过医生，是吧？"

===Original===
Mr. Longvale was obviously startled.
“I practised medicine when I was younger,” he said.
“Poor, too?” Gregory looked round. “You haven’t a shilling in the world, I’ll bet!”
“There you are wrong,” said old Mr. Longvale quietly. “I am an extremely wealthy man, and the fact that I do not keep my house in repair is due to the curious penchant of mine for decaying things. That is an unhealthy, probably a morbid predilection of mine. How did you know I was a doctor?”
“I heard through one of my servants. You set the broken finger of a carter.”
“I haven’t practised for years,” said Mr. Longvale. “I almost wish I had,” he added wistfully. “It is a noble science—”
“Anyway,” interrupted Penne, “even if you can’t be bought, you’re a secretive old devil, and that suits me. There’s a girl up at my house who is very ill. I don’t want any of these prying country doctors nosing around my private affairs. Would you come along and see her?”
===Chinese===
朗维尔先生显然吃了一惊。
"我年轻时行过医，"他说。
"还穷？"格雷戈里环顾四周，"我打赌，你在世上连一个先令都没有！"
"这您可说错了，"老朗维尔先生平静地说，"我是个极其富有的人，宅子不加修葺，是因为我这人有个怪癖，偏爱衰败之物。这是一种不健康的、多半算病态的偏好。您怎么知道我行过医？"
"听我一个仆人说的。你给一个车夫接过断指。"
"我已经多年不行医了，"朗维尔先生说，"我倒差点希望自己一直行下去，"他怅然地补了一句，"那是一门崇高的科学——"
"反正，"彭尼打断他，"就算买不动你，你也是个嘴严的老家伙，这正合我意。我家楼上有个姑娘病得很重。我不想让那些爱管闲事的乡下医生在我私事里嗅来嗅去。你肯来一趟看看她吗？"

===Original===
The old man pursed his lips thoughtfully.
“I should be most happy,” he said, “but I am afraid my medical science is a little rusty. Is she a servant?”
“In a way,” said the other shortly. “When can you come?”
“I’ll come at once,” said Mr. Longvale gravely, and went out, to return in his greatcoat.
The baronet looked at the ancient garment with a smile of derision.
“Why the devil do you wear such old-fashioned clothes?” he asked.
“To me they are very new,” said the old man gently. “The garments of today are without romance, without the thrill which these bring to me.” He patted the overlapping cape and smiled. “An old man is entitled to his fancies: let me be humoured, Sir Gregory.”
===Chinese===
老人若有所思地抿了抿嘴唇。
"我非常乐意，"他说，"只怕我的医术有些生锈了。她是仆人？"
"也算吧，"对方简短地说，"你什么时候能来？"
"我这就去，"朗维尔先生郑重地说，出去换上大衣回来了。
从男爵看着那件老古董衣服，轻蔑地笑了。
"你他妈干吗穿这种老掉牙的衣裳？"他问。
"在我眼里它们崭新得很，"老人温和地说，"如今的衣裳没有罗曼史，没有这些衣裳带给我的那份激动。"他拍了拍层层相叠的披肩，笑了，"老头子有资格有自己的癖好：请容许我这份任性吧，格雷戈里爵士。"

===Original===
At the moment Mr. Sampson Longvale was driving to Griff Towers, Mike Brixan, summoned by messenger, was facing Jack Knebworth in his office.
“I hope you didn’t mind my sending for you, though it was a fool thing to do,” said the director. “You remember that we shot some scenes at Griff Towers?”
Michael nodded.
“I want you to see one that we took, with the tower in the background, and tell me what you think of—something.”
Wonderingly, Michael accompanied the director to the projection room.
“My laboratory manager pointed it out to me in the negative,” explained Jack as they seated themselves and the room went dark. “Of course, I should have seen it in the print.”
“What is it?” asked Michael curiously.
===Chinese===
就在桑普森·朗维尔先生驱车赶往格里夫塔楼的当口，被人捎信叫来的迈克·布里克森，正在办公室里面对杰克·尼布沃斯。
"派人来叫你，希望你不见怪，虽然这事办得冒失，"导演说，"记得我们在格里夫塔楼拍过几场戏吧？"
迈克尔点了点头。
"我想让你看一场我们拍的戏，塔楼做背景，然后告诉我你对——一样东西的看法。"
迈克尔满腹狐疑地跟着导演进了放映室。
"我的洗印主任在底片上给我指了出来，"两人落座、屋里暗下来时，杰克解释道，"当然，正片里我本该看出来的。"
"是什么？"迈克尔好奇地问。

===Original===
“That’s just what I don’t know,” said the other, scratching his head, “but you’ll see for yourself.”
There was a flicker and a furious clicking, and there appeared on the small screen which was used for projection purposes, a picture of two people. Adele was one and Reggie Connolly the other, and Michael gazed stolidly, though with rising annoyance, at a love scene which was being enacted between the two.
In the immediate background was the wall of the tower, and Michael saw for the first time that there was a little window which he did not remember having seen from the interior of the hall; it was particularly dark, and was lighted, even in daytime, by electric lamps.
“I never noticed that window before,” he said.
“It’s the window I want you to watch,” said Jack Knebworth, and, even as he spoke, there came stealthily into view a face.
At first it was indistinct and blurred, but later, it came into focus. It was the oval face of a girl, dark-eyed, her hair in disorder, a look of unspeakable terror on her face. She raised her hand as if to beckon somebody—probably Jack himself, who was directing the picture. That, at least, was Jack’s view. They had hardly time to get accustomed to the presence of the mystery girl when she disappeared, with such rapidity as to suggest that she had been dragged violently back.
===Chinese===
"这正是我不知道的，"对方挠着头说，"你自己看吧。"
银幕一闪，机声急响，那块放映用的小银幕上出现了两个人的画面。一个是阿黛尔，另一个是雷吉·康诺利，迈克尔面无表情地盯着两人演的一场爱情戏，心里越来越不耐烦。
紧贴他们身后的，是塔楼的墙面，迈克尔头一回注意到墙上有一扇小窗——他不记得从门厅里面见过它；那地方特别暗，即使白天也靠电灯照明。
"我从前没留意过那扇窗，"他说。
"我要你看的就是那扇窗，"杰克·尼布沃斯说，话音未落，一张脸悄悄探进了画面。
起初模糊一片，随后焦距对实了。那是一张姑娘的鹅蛋脸，黑眼睛，头发散乱，脸上一副难以言喻的惊恐。她抬起手，像在向什么人招呼——多半是杰克本人，当时正执导这场戏。至少杰克这么看。两人还没来得及看惯这位神秘姑娘的出现，她就没了——快得仿佛被人猛地拽了回去。

===Original===
“What do you make of that?” asked Knebworth.
Michael bit his lip thoughtfully.
“Looks almost as though friend Penne had a prisoner in his dark tower. Of course, the woman whose scream I heard, and who he said was a servant! But the window puzzles me. There’s no sign of it inside. The stairway leads out of the hall, but in such a position that it is impossible that the girl could have been standing either on the stairs or the landing. Therefore, there must be a fifth wall inside, containing a separate staircase. Does this mean you will have to retake?”
Jack shook his head.
“No, we can back her out: she’s only on fifty feet of the film; but I thought you’d like to see it.”
The lights came on again, and they went back to the director’s office.
===Chinese===
"这你怎么看？"尼布沃斯问。
迈克尔若有所思地咬了咬嘴唇。
"看样子，彭尼老兄的黑塔里几乎就是关着一名囚犯。当然喽，就是我听见尖叫、他说是什么女仆的那个女人！可那扇窗让我纳闷。屋里面不见它的踪影。楼梯是从门厅通出去的，位置摆在那儿，那姑娘绝不可能站在楼梯上，也不可能站在楼梯口。所以，里面必定还有第五道墙，墙里藏着另一道楼梯。这是不是意味着你们得重拍？"
杰克摇了摇头。
"不用，我们可以把她的镜头剪掉：她统共只占了五十英尺胶片；不过我想你该看看。"
灯又亮了，两人回到导演办公室。

===Original===
“I don’t like Penne, for more reasons than one,” said Jack Knebworth. “I like him less since I’ve found that he’s better friends with Mendoza than I thought he was.”
“Who is Mendoza—the deposed star?”
The other nodded.
“Stella Mendoza—not a bad girl and not a good girl,” he said. “I’ve been wondering why Penne always gave us permission to use his grounds for shooting, and now I know. I tell you that that house holds a few secrets!”
Michael smiled faintly.
“One, at least, of them will be revealed tonight,” he said. “I am going to explore Griff Towers, and I do not intend asking permission of Sir Gregory Penne. And if I can discover what I believe is there to be discovered, Gregory Penne will sleep under lock and key this night!”
===Chinese===
"我不喜欢彭尼，理由不止一条，"杰克·尼布沃斯说，"发现他跟门多萨的交情比我以为的深，我就更不喜欢他了。"
"门多萨是谁——那个被废的明星？"
对方点了点头。
"斯特拉·门多萨——不算坏姑娘，也算不得好姑娘，"他说，"我一直纳闷彭尼为什么总肯把园子借给我们拍戏，现在明白了。我告诉你，那座宅子里藏着好几个秘密！"
迈克尔淡淡一笑。
"至少其中一个，今夜就会揭晓，"他说，"我要去探一探格里夫塔楼，而且不打算征得格雷戈里·彭尼爵士的许可。只要我能找到我相信藏在那儿等着被找到的东西，今夜格雷戈里·彭尼就要在锁链和牢房里睡觉了！"

## XXVI The Hand / XXVI 那只手

===Original===
Jack looked at his watch.
“I guess she’ll be in bed by now, but it’s worth while trying. Would you like to see her?”
Michael hesitated. Stella Mendoza was a friend of Penne’s, and he was loath to commit himself irretrievably to the view that Penne was the murderer.
“Yes, I think we’ll see her,” he said. “After all, Penne knows that he is suspected.”
Jack Knebworth was ten minutes on the telephone before he succeeded in getting a reply from Stella’s cottage.
===Chinese===
杰克看了看表。
"我猜她这会儿已经上床了，不过试一试也值得。你想见她吗？"
迈克尔犹豫了。斯特拉·门多萨是彭尼的朋友，他不愿无可挽回地一口咬定彭尼就是凶手。
"好吧，我想我们就见见她。"他说，"毕竟，彭尼知道自己是嫌疑对象。"
杰克·尼布沃斯打了十分钟电话，才叫通斯特拉的小屋。

===Original===
“It’s Knebworth speaking, Miss Mendoza,” he said. “Is it possible to see you tonight? Mr. Brixan wants to speak to you.”
“At this hour of the night?” she said in sleepy surprise. “I was in bed when the bell rang. Won’t it do in the morning?”
“No, he wants to see you particularly tonight. I’ll come along with him if you don’t mind.”
“What is wrong?” she asked quickly. “Is it about Gregory?”
Jack whispered a query to the man who stood at his side, and Michael nodded.
“Yes, it is about Gregory,” said Knebworth.
===Chinese===
"我是尼布沃斯，门多萨小姐，"他说，"今晚能见你一面吗？布里克森先生有话要同你说。"
"都夜里这个钟点了？"她睡意未消地讶声道。"门铃响时我都上床了。明早不行吗？"
"不行，他特别要今晚见你。你不介意的话，我陪他一起来。"
"出什么事了？"她立刻问，"是为了格雷戈里？"
杰克低声问了句身旁的人，迈克尔点了点头。
"是的，是为了格雷戈里。"尼布沃斯说。

===Original===
“Will you come along? I’ll have time to dress.”
Stella was dressed by the time they arrived, and too curious and too alarmed to make the hour of the call a matter of comment.
“What is the trouble?” she asked.
“Mr. Foss is dead.”
“Dead?” She opened her eyes wide. “Why, I only saw him yesterday. But how?”
===Chinese===
"你们来吧。我来得及穿好衣服。"
他们到时，斯特拉已穿戴整齐，又好奇又惊慌，全顾不上计较来客的时辰。
"出了什么乱子？"她问。
"福斯先生死了。"
"死了？"她睁大了眼睛。"咦，我昨天还见过他呢。怎么死的？"

===Original===
“He has been murdered,” said Michael quietly. “His head has been found on Chobham Common.”
She would have fallen to the floor, had not Michael’s arm been there to support her, and it was some time before she recovered sufficiently to answer coherently the questions which were put to her.
“No, I didn’t see Mr. Foss again after he left the Towers, and then I only saw him for a few seconds.”
“Did he suggest he was coming back again?”
She shook her head.
===Chinese===
"他被人谋杀了。"迈克尔平静地说，"他的头颅在乔巴姆公地上被发现了。"
若不是迈克尔伸臂扶住，她早已跌倒在地；过了好一会儿，她才缓过神来，能把人家问她的有条有理地答上来。
"没有，福斯先生离开塔楼后我再没见过他，那回拢共也只照了几秒钟的面。"
"他说过他还要回来吗？"
她摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Did Sir Gregory tell you he was returning?”
“No.” She shook her head again. “He told me he was glad to see the last of him, and that he had borrowed fifty pounds until next week, when he expected to make a lot of money. Gregory is like that﻿—he will tell you things about people, things which they ask him not to make public. He is rather proud of his wealth and what he calls his charity.”
“You had a luncheon engagement with him?” said Michael, watching her.
She bit her lip.
“You must have heard me talking when I left him,” she said. “No, I had no luncheon engagement. That was camouflage, intended for anybody who was hanging around, and we knew somebody had been in the house that night. Was it you?”
Michael nodded.
===Chinese===
"格雷戈里爵士跟你说起他要回来吗？"
"没有。"她又摇头。"他跟我说，巴不得从此再不见这个人，还说借给了他 50 英镑，周转到下礼拜——那人指望到时发一笔大财。格雷戈里就是这脾气——他会把人家的事讲给你听，还是人家央求他不要外扬的事。他对自己的财富，还有他那套自诩的慈善，颇为沾沾自喜。"
"你同他约好了吃午饭？"迈克尔说，眼睛注视着她。
她咬了咬嘴唇。
"我离开他时说的话，你一定听见了。"她说，"不，我没同他约什么午饭。那是幌子，说给可能躲在近旁的人听的——我们知道那晚有人进过宅子。是你吗？"
迈克尔点了点头。

===Original===
“Oh, I’m so relieved!” She heaved a deep sigh. “Those few minutes in that dark room were terrible to me. I thought it was﻿—” She hesitated.
“Bhag?” suggested Michael, and she nodded.
“Yes. You don’t suspect Gregory of killing Foss?”
“I suspect everybody in general and nobody in particular,” said Michael. “Did you see Bhag?”
She shivered.
“No, not that time. I’ve seen him, of course. He gives me the creeps! I’ve never seen anything so human. Sometimes, when Gregory was a little﻿—a little drunk, he used to bring Bhag out and make him do tricks. Do you know that Bhag could do all the Malayan exercises with the sword! Sir Gregory had a specially made wooden sword for him, and the way that that awful thing used to twirl it round his head was terrifying.”
===Chinese===
"哦，我可算安心了！"她长出一口气。"在黑屋子里那几分钟，真把我吓坏了。我还以为是——"她欲言又止。
"巴格？"迈克尔替她说出来，她点了点头。
"就是。你们不会怀疑是格雷戈里杀了福斯吧？"
"我笼统地怀疑每一个人，却不特别怀疑哪一个人。"迈克尔说，"你见到巴格了吗？"
她打了个寒噤。
"没有，那回没见着。我当然见过它。它叫我毛骨悚然！我从没见过这么像人的东西。有时格雷戈里喝得微醺——微醺的时候，会把巴格带出来让它耍把戏。你可知道，巴格会使全套马来剑术！格雷戈里爵士特意给它定做了一把木头剑，那可怕的家伙把剑在头顶上抡得团团转的样子，真是吓人。"

===Original===
Michael stared at her.
“Bhag *could* use the sword, then? Penne told me he did, but I thought he was lying.”
“Oh, yes, he could use the sword. Gregory taught him everything.”
“What is Penne to you?” Michael asked the question bluntly, and she coloured.
“He has been a friend,” she said awkwardly, “a very good friend of mine﻿—financially, I mean. He took a liking to me a long time ago, and we’ve been﻿—very good friends.”
Michael nodded.
===Chinese===
迈克尔怔怔地望着她。
"这么说，巴格*真的*会使剑？彭尼跟我说过它会，我还以为他撒谎呢。"
"哦，会的，它会使剑。格雷戈里什么都教过它。"
"彭尼跟你是什么关系？"迈克尔单刀直入地问，她的脸红了。
"他一直是个朋友，"她局促地说，"我的一个很要好的朋友——我是说，钱财上的。很久以前他就对我有意，我们一直是——很要好的朋友。"
迈克尔点了点头。

===Original===
“And you are still?”
“No,” she answered shortly, “I’ve finished with Gregory, and am leaving Chichester tomorrow. I’ve put the house in an agent’s hands to rent. Poor Mr. Foss!” she said, and there were tears in her eyes. “Poor soul! Gregory wouldn’t have done it, Mr. Brixan, I’ll swear that! There’s a whole lot of Gregory that’s sheer bluff. He’s a coward at heart, and though he has done dreadful things, he has always had an agent to do the dirty work.”
“Dreadful things like what?”
She seemed reluctant to explain, but he pressed her.
===Chinese===
"现在还是？"
"不是了。"她简短地答道，"我同格雷戈里一刀两断了，明天就离开奇切斯特。房子已经交给中介去出租。可怜的福斯先生！"她说着，眼里噙着泪。"可怜的人！这事绝不会是格雷戈里干的，布里克森先生，我敢起誓！格雷戈里这个人，一多半全是虚张声势。他骨子里是个懦夫，虽然干过些可怕的事，脏活儿向来都是雇人替他干的。"
"可怕的事，比如什么？"
她似乎不愿细说，可他紧追不放。

===Original===
“Well, he told me that he used to take expeditions in the bush and raid the villages, carrying off girls. There is one tribe that have very beautiful women. Perhaps he was lying about that too, but I have an idea that he spoke the truth. He told me that only a year ago, when he was in Borneo, he ‘lifted’ a girl from a wild village where it was death for a European to go. He always said ‘lifted.’ ”
“And didn’t you mind these confessions?” asked Michael, his steely eye upon her.
She shrugged her shoulders.
“He was that kind of man,” was all she said, and it spoke volumes for her understanding of her “very good friend.”
Michael walked back to Jack Knebworth’s house.
===Chinese===
"唉，他跟我说过，他以前常带队进丛林扫荡村子，掳走姑娘。有一个部落的女人生得极美。这事兴许也是他吹牛，可我总觉得他说的是实话。他告诉我，就在一年前，他还在婆罗洲从一个野人村子里'提'了一个姑娘出来——欧洲人进了那村子是死罪。他总说'提'。"
"听了这些自供，你竟然不介意？"迈克尔问，冷峻的目光盯住她。
她耸了耸肩。
"他就是那样的人。"她只说了这一句——这倒把她对那位"很要好的朋友"的了解，表露得淋漓尽致。
迈克尔步行回到杰克·尼布沃斯的住处。

===Original===
“The story Penne tells seems to fit together with the information Mendoza has given us. There is no doubt that the woman at the top of the tower was the lady he ‘lifted,’ and less doubt that the little brown man was her husband. If they have escaped from the tower, then there should be no difficulty in finding them. I’ll send out a message to all stations within a radius of twenty-five miles, and we ought to get news of them in the morning.”
“It’s morning now,” said Jack, looking toward the greying east. “Will you come in? I’ll give you some coffee. This news has upset me. I was going to have a long day’s work, but I guess we’ll have to put it off for a day or so. The company is bound to be upset by this news. They all knew Foss, although he was not very popular with them. It only wants Adele to be off colour to complete our misery. By the way, Brixan, why don’t you make this your headquarters? I’m a bachelor; there’s a phone service here, and you’ll get a privacy at this house which you don’t get at your hotel.”
The idea appealed to the detective, and it was at Jack Knebworth’s house that he slept that night, after an hour’s conversation on the telephone with Scotland Yard.
Early in the morning he was again at the Towers, and now, with the assistance of daylight, he enlarged his search, without adding greatly to his knowledge. The position was a peculiar one, as Scotland Yard had emphasized. Sir Gregory Penne was a member of a good family, a rich man, a justice of the peace; and, whilst his eccentricities were of a lawless character, “you can’t hang people for being queer,” the Commissioner informed Michael on the telephone.
===Chinese===
"彭尼说的那套，同门多萨提供给我们的情况对得上。毫无疑问，塔楼顶上的那个女人就是他'提'来的那位夫人，而那矮小的棕色男人多半就是她丈夫。他们若是逃出了塔楼，要找到他们应当不难。我这就给方圆 25 英里之内所有的警局发文，明天早上应该就能得到他们的消息。"
"现在就是早上了。"杰克说着，望向东边泛白的天色。"进来坐坐？我给你弄杯咖啡。这消息把我搅得心神不宁。今天本打算大干一天的，看来只好推后一两天了。这消息一传开，全班人马肯定乱套。他们都认识福斯，虽说人缘不算好。只消阿黛尔再打个蔫儿，我们的霉运就齐了。对了，布里克森，你何不把这儿当你的总部？我是单身汉，家里有电话，住在这儿，能得着旅馆里得不着的清静。"
这个提议正中下怀。那晚他就在杰克·尼布沃斯家里歇宿——睡前同苏格兰场通了一个钟头的电话。
一大早他又去了塔楼，这回借着日光扩大了搜索范围，见识却没添多少。正如苏格兰场所强调的，局面颇为棘手。格雷戈里·彭尼爵士出身名门，家资巨万，还是个治安官；他的乖僻行径固然无法无天，"可总不能因为人行径古怪就把人绞死吧"——总监（Commissioner）在电话里对迈克尔如此说。

===Original===
It was a suspicious fact that Bhag had disappeared as completely as the brown man and his wife.
“He hasn’t been back all night: I’ve seen nothing of him,” said Sir Gregory. “And that’s not the first time he’s gone off on his own. He finds hiding-places that you’d never suspect, and he’s probably gone to earth somewhere. He’ll turn up.”
Michael was passing through Chichester when he saw a figure that made him bring the car to a standstill with such a jerk that it was a wonder the tyres did not burst. In a second he was out of the machine and walking to meet Adele.
“It seems ten thousand years since I saw you,” he said with an extravagance which at any other time would have brought a smile to her face.
===Chinese===
可疑的是，巴格也同那棕色男人和他的妻子一样，消失得无影无踪。
"它一整夜没回来：我连影子也没见着。"格雷戈里爵士说，"这也不是它头一回自己跑出去。它能找到你做梦也想不到的藏身处，多半是钻进哪个洞里蛰伏起来了。它会露面的。"
迈克尔正开车穿过奇切斯特，忽然瞥见一个人影，猛地一个急刹把车停住，轮胎没爆简直是奇迹。转瞬他已下了车，迎着阿黛尔走去。
"自打见了你，仿佛过了一万年。"他这话夸张至极，换在任何别的时候，准能引她一笑。

===Original===
“I’m afraid I can’t stop. I’m on my way to the studio,” she said, a little coldly, “and I promised Mr. Knebworth that I would be there early. You see, I got off yesterday afternoon by telling Mr. Knebworth that I had an engagement.”
“And had you?” asked the innocent Michael.
“I asked somebody to take tea with me,” and his jaw dropped.
She would have gone on, but he stopped her.
“I don’t want to shock you or hurt you, Adele,” he said gently, “but the explanation for my forgetfulness is that we’ve had another tragedy.”
===Chinese===
"恐怕我不能久留。我正赶去摄影棚。"她略带冷淡地说，"我答应过尼布沃斯先生早点到。您瞧，昨天下午我是托词有约，才跟尼布沃斯先生告的假。"
"那到底有没有约？"天真的迈克尔问。
"我请了人来喝茶。"他的下巴当时就耷拉下来。
她本还要往前走，他拦住了她。
"我不想吓着你，也不想让你难过，阿黛尔，"他柔声说，"可我之所以把这事忘了个精光，是因为我们又出了一桩惨案。"

===Original===
She stopped and looked at him.
“Another?”
He nodded.
“Mr. Foss has been murdered,” he said.
She went very white.
“When?” Her voice was calm, almost emotionless.
“Last night.”
“It was after nine,” she said.
His eyebrows went up in surprise.
“Why do you say that?”
“Because, Mr. Brixan”﻿—she spoke slowly﻿—“at nine o’clock I saw the hand of the man who murdered him!
===Chinese===
她停下脚步，望着他。
"又一桩？"
他点了点头。
"福斯先生被人谋杀了。"他说。
她的脸霎时惨白。
"什么时候？"她的声音很平静，几乎不带感情。
"昨天夜里。"
"那是九点以后的事。"她说。
他惊得扬起了眉毛。
"你为什么这么说？"
"因为，布里克森先生"——她说得很慢——"九点钟的时候，我看见了杀人凶手的那只手！

===Original===
“Two nights ago,” she went on, “I went out to buy some wool I wanted. It was just before the shops closed﻿—a quarter to eight, I think. In the town I saw Mr. Foss and spoke to him. He was very nervous and restless, and again made a suggestion to me which he had already made when he called on me. His manner was so strange that I asked him if he was in any trouble. He told me no, but he had had an awful premonition that something dreadful was going to happen, and he asked me if I’d lived in Chichester for any length of time, and if I knew about the caves.”
“The caves?” said Michael quickly.
She nodded.
“I was surprised. I’d never heard of the caves. He told me there was a reference to them in some old history of Chichester. He had looked in the guidebooks without finding anything about them, but apparently there were caves at some time or other near Chellerton, but there was a heavy subsidence of earth that closed the entrance. He was so rambling and so disjointed that I thought he must have been drinking, and I was glad to get away from him. I went on and did my shopping and met one of the extra girls I knew. She asked me to go home with her. I didn’t want to go a bit, but I thought if I refused she would think I was giving myself airs, and so I went. As soon as I could, I came away and went straight home.
===Chinese===
"前天晚上，"她接着说，"我出门去买一点要用的毛线。赶在店铺打烊之前——我记得是差一刻八点。在城里我碰见了福斯先生，还同他说了话。他神色慌张，坐立不安，又向我提起一个建议——他来拜访我的时候已经提过一回。他样子那么古怪，我便问他是不是招了什么麻烦。他说没有，只是有一种可怕的预感，觉得大祸就要临头，还问我在奇切斯特住得久不久，知不知道那些洞穴。"
"洞穴？"迈克尔急忙问。
她点了点头。
"我很吃惊。我从没听说过什么洞穴。他告诉我，某部奇切斯特老志书里提到过它们。他翻遍了导游手册也没找到相关记载，不过切勒顿（Chellerton）附近从前显然是有洞穴的，后来一场严重的地陷把洞口封死了。他说得颠三倒四、语无伦次，我还当他喝多了，巴不得赶紧脱身。我接着买完东西，遇见一个认识的临时女演员。她邀我去她家坐坐。我一点也不想去，可转念一想，若是回绝了，她该当我摆架子，就去了。一得脱身，我就赶紧告辞直接回了家。

===Original===
“It was then nine o’clock and the streets were empty. They are not very well lit in Chichester, but I was able to recognize Mr. Foss. He was standing at the corner of the Arundel Road, and was evidently waiting for somebody. I stopped because I particularly did not wish to meet Mr. Foss, but I was on the point of turning round when a car drove into the road and stopped almost opposite him.”
“What sort of a car?” asked Michael.
“It was a closed landaulette﻿—I think they call them sedans. As it came round the corner its lights went out, which struck me as being curious. Mr. Foss was evidently waiting for this, for he went up and leant on the edge of the window and spoke to somebody inside. I don’t know what made me do it, but I had an extraordinary impulse to see who was in the car, and I started walking toward them. I must have been five or six yards away when Mr. Foss stepped back and the sedan moved on. The driver put his hand out of the window as if he was waving goodbye. It was still out of the window and the only thing visible﻿—the interior was quite dark﻿—when it came abreast of me.”
“Was there anything peculiar about the hand?”
===Chinese===
"那时正九点，街上空无一人。奇切斯特的路灯不大亮堂，但我还是认出了福斯先生。他站在阿伦德尔路拐角，分明是在等人。我停住脚，因为我特别不想碰见福斯先生，正要转身往回走，一辆汽车驶进这条路，几乎正对着他停下。"
"什么样的一辆车？"迈克尔问。
"是一辆封闭的兰多车（landaulette）——我想如今管这类车叫轿车。它拐过街角时车灯灭了，我觉得好生奇怪。福斯先生分明就是在等这个，他走上前去，倚着车窗沿同里面的什么人说话。不知怎么的，我心里涌起一股强烈的冲动，想看看车里坐的是谁，便朝他们走去。大约离着五六码远时，福斯先生退后一步，轿车开动了。开车人把手伸出窗外，像是在挥手道别。等车与我并排时，那只手还伸在窗外，也是唯一看得见的东西——车里一片漆黑。"
"那只手有什么特别之处吗？"

===Original===
“Nothing, except that it was small and white, and on the little finger was a large diamond ring. The fire in it was extraordinary, and I wondered why a man should wear a ring of that kind. You will think I am silly, but the sight of that hand gave me a terrible feeling of fear﻿—I don’t know why, even now. There was something unnatural and abnormal about it. When I looked round again, Mr. Foss was walking rapidly in the other direction, and I made no attempt to overtake him.”
“You saw no number on the car?”
“None whatever.” She shook her head. “I wasn’t so curious.”
“You didn’t even see the silhouette of the man inside?”
“No, I saw nothing. His arm was raised.”
“What size was the diamond, do you think?”
She pursed her lips dubiously.
===Chinese===
"没什么特别，只是它又小又白，小指上戴着一枚大钻戒。钻石里的火彩亮得惊人，我纳闷一个男人为什么要戴那样一枚戒指。您会觉得我傻气，可那只手的模样让我生出一种可怕的恐惧——直到现在我也说不清为什么。它透着一种不自然、不正常的东西。等我再回头，福斯先生已快步朝另一个方向走了，我也没想赶上去同他说话。"
"车牌号看见了吗？"
"一点儿也没看见。"她摇了摇头。"我还没那么好奇。"
"车里那人的侧影也没看见？"
"没有，什么也没看见。只看见他举着胳膊。"
"依你看，那颗钻石有多大？"
她将信将疑地抿了抿嘴。

===Original===
“He passed me in a flash, and I can’t give you any very accurate information, Mr. Brixan. It may be a mistake on my part, but I thought it was as big as the tip of my finger. Naturally I couldn’t see any details, even though I saw the car again last night.”
She went on to tell him of what happened on the previous night, and he listened intently.
“The man spoke to you﻿—did you recognize his voice?”
She shook her head.
“No﻿—he spoke in a whisper. I did not see his face, though I have an idea that he was wearing a cap. The policeman said he should have taken the number of the car.”
“Oh, the policeman said that, did he?” remarked Michael sardonically. “Well, there’s hope for him.”
For a minute he was immersed in thought, and then:
===Chinese===
"车从我面前一闪而过，我提供不了很确切的情况，布里克森先生。兴许是我看岔了，可我觉得它有我指尖那么大。细处自然是看不清的，尽管昨晚我又见到了那辆车。"
她接着讲了头天夜里发生的事，他凝神听着。
"那人跟你搭话了——你听出他的嗓音了吗？"
她摇了摇头。
"没有——他是耳语着说的。我没看见他的脸，只觉得他戴着一顶便帽。警察说，他本该把车牌号记下来的。"
"哦，警察是这么说的，是吗？"迈克尔讥诮道，"嗯，这人还有指望。"
他沉思了片刻，随后说道：

===Original===
“I’ll take you to the studio if you don’t mind,” said Michael.
He left her to go to her dressing-room, there to learn that work had been suspended for the day, and went in search of Jack.
“You’ve seen everybody of consequence in this neighbourhood,” he said. “Do you know anybody who drives a sedan and wears a diamond ring on the little finger of the right hand?”
“The only person I know who has that weakness is Mendoza,” he said.
Michael whistled.
===Chinese===
"你不介意的话，我送你去摄影棚。"迈克尔说。
他把她留在化妆间门口——到了那里才知道当天的拍摄已经停工——自己去找杰克。
"这一带有头有脸的人物，你都见过了。"他说，"你可认识什么人，开一辆轿车，右手小指上戴一枚钻戒？"
"我认识的人里，有这毛病儿的只有门多萨。"他说。
迈克尔吹了声口哨。

===Original===
“I never thought of Mendoza,” he said, “and Adele described the hand as ‘small and womanly.’ ”
“Mendoza’s hand isn’t particularly small, but it would look small on a man,” said Jack thoughtfully. “And her car isn’t a closed sedan, but that doesn’t mean anything. By the way, I’ve just sent instructions to tell the company I’m working today. If we let these people stand around thinking, they’ll get thoroughly upset.”
“I thought that too,” said Michael with a smile, “but I didn’t dare make the suggestion.”
An urgent message took him to London that afternoon, where he attended a conference of the Big Five at Scotland Yard. And at the end of the two-hour discussion, the conclusion was reached that Sir Gregory Penne was to remain at large but under observation.
===Chinese===
"我从没往门多萨身上想过，"他说，"而阿黛尔形容那只手'又小又像女人的'。"
"门多萨的手不算特别小，不过安在男人身上，就显得小了。"杰克若有所思地说，"她的车也不是封闭轿车，可这说明不了什么。对了，我刚传下话去，告诉大家今天照常开工。若让这帮人闲站着瞎琢磨，非乱了阵脚不可。"
"我也是这么想的，"迈克尔笑着说，"只是不敢开这个口。"
当天下午，一封急电把他召去伦敦，他在苏格兰场列席了五巨头（Big Five）的会议。两个钟头讨论下来，得出的结论是：格雷戈里·彭尼爵士暂不动手，放其在外，加以监视。

===Original===
“We verified the story about the lifting of this girl in Borneo,” said the quiet-spoken Chief. “And all the facts dovetail. I haven’t the slightest doubt in my mind that Penne is the culprit, but we’ve got to walk very warily. I dare say in your department, Captain Brixan, you can afford to take a few risks, but the police in this country never make an arrest for murder unless they are absolutely certain that a conviction will follow. There may be something in your other theory, and I’d be the last man in the world to turn it down, but you’ll have to conduct parallel investigations.”
Michael ran down to Sussex in broad daylight. There was a long stretch of road about four miles north of Chichester, and he was pelting along this when he became aware of a figure standing in the middle of the roadway with its arms outstretched, and slowed down. It was Mr. Sampson Longvale, he saw to his amazement. Almost before the car had stopped, with an extraordinary display of agility Mr. Longvale jumped on the running-board.
===Chinese===
"他在婆罗洲'提'姑娘的事，我们查证过了。"那位说话轻声细语的长官说，"各项事实环环相扣。我心里毫不怀疑彭尼就是凶手，可我们务必步步谨慎。布里克森上尉，你们那个部门，我想是经得起冒几回险的，但这个国家的警察，若非十拿九稳定得了罪，绝不轻启谋杀逮捕。你的另一套推断或许有些道理，我也绝不是要一笔抹杀的人，但你必须两条线并行查下去。"
大白天里，迈克尔驱车南下萨塞克斯。奇切斯特以北四英里有一段长路，他正全速飞驰，忽然察觉路中央站着一个人，张开双臂，便放慢了车速。他惊讶地发现，那竟是桑普森·朗维尔先生。车子几乎还没停稳，朗维尔先生就身手矫健得出奇，一步跳上了踏脚板。

===Original===
“I have been watching for you this last two hours, Mr. Brixan,” he said. “Do you mind if I join you?”
“Come right in,” said Michael heartily.
“You are going to Chichester, I know. Would you mind instead coming to the Dower House? I have something important to tell you.”
The place at which he had signalled the car to stop was exactly opposite the end of the road that led to the Dower House and Sir Gregory’s domain. The old man told him that he had walked back from Chichester, and had been waiting for the passing of the car.
“I learnt for the first time, Mr. Brixan, that you are an officer of the law,” he said, with a stately inclination of his head. “I need hardly tell you how greatly I respect one whose duty it is to serve the cause of justice.”
“Mr. Knebworth told you, I presume?” said Michael with a smile.
===Chinese===
"我在这儿守了您足足两个钟头了，布里克森先生。"他说，"可否容我搭您的车？"
"快请上车。"迈克尔热忱地说。
"我知道您要去奇切斯特。可否请您改道先去一趟寡妇居（Dower House）？我有一件要紧的事要告诉您。"
他拦车的地方，正对着通往寡妇居和格雷戈里爵士地界的那条路的路口。老人告诉他，自己是徒步从奇切斯特走回来的，一直在等候这辆车经过。
"我头一回得知，布里克森先生，您是一位执法官员。"他说着，庄重地欠了欠头。"职责所在、伸张正义之人，我是何等敬重，自不待我赘言。"
"想必是尼布沃斯先生告诉您的？"迈克尔含笑说。

===Original===
“He told me,” agreed the other gravely. “I went in really to seek you, having an intuition that you had some more important position in life than what I had first imagined. I confess I thought at first that you were one of those idle young men who have nothing to do but to amuse themselves. It was a great gratification to me to learn that I was mistaken. It is all the more gratifying”﻿—(Michael smiled inwardly at the verbosity of age)﻿—“because I need advice on a point of law, which I imagine my lawyer would not offer to me. My position is a very peculiar one, in some ways embarrassing. I am a man who shrinks from the eye of the public and am averse from vulgar intermeddling in other people’s affairs.”
What had he to tell, Michael wondered﻿—this old man, with his habit of nocturnal strolls, might have been a witness to something that had not yet come out.
They stopped at the Dower House, and the old man got out and opened the gate, not closing it until Michael had passed through. Instead of going direct to his sitting-room, he went upstairs, beckoning Michael to come after, and stopped before the room which had been occupied by Adele on the night of her terrible experience.
“I wish you to see these people,” said Mr. Longvale earnestly, “and tell me whether I am acting in accordance with the law.”
===Chinese===
"是他告诉我的。"对方正色应道，"我登门原是寻您来的，凭一种直觉，觉得您的身份比我起初想象的要紧得多。恕我直言，起初我把您当成了游手好闲、专事寻欢的公子哥儿之流。得知我看走了眼，实乃一件快事。尤其令人欣慰的是"——（迈克尔暗自失笑，老年的絮叨啊）——"我正需要一条法律上的主意，而这主意，我想我的律师是不肯给我出的。我的处境颇为特殊，在某些方面甚是为难。我这人怕见公众的眼色，也不肯俗气地插手他人事务。"
他要告诉自己什么呢，迈克尔暗忖——这位惯于夜间散步的老人，说不定目击了什么尚未揭出的事情。
他们在寡妇居停下车，老人先下来开了大门，等迈克尔把车开进去才关上。他没径直去起居室，而是上了楼，招手引迈克尔跟来，在阿黛尔那夜可怕经历所住的房间门前停住。
"我想请您看看这两个人，"朗维尔先生恳切地说，"再告诉我，我的行事是否合乎法律。"

===Original===
He opened the door, and Mike saw that there were now two beds in the room. On one, heavily bandaged and apparently unconscious, was the brown-faced man; on the other, sleeping, was the woman Michael had seen in the tower! She, too, was badly wounded: her arm was bandaged and strapped into position.
Michael drew a long breath.
“That is a mystery solved, anyway,” he said. “Where did you find these people?”
At the sound of his voice the woman opened her eyes and frowned at him fearfully, then looked across to the man.
“You have been wounded?” said Michael in Dutch, but apparently her education had been neglected in respect of European languages, for she made no reply.
===Chinese===
他推开门，迈克看见屋里如今摆着两张床。一张床上躺着那个棕色面孔的男人，绷带缠得厚厚的，看样子不省人事；另一张床上睡着的，正是迈克尔在塔楼里见过的那个女人！她也伤得不轻：胳膊上了绷带，还被绑带固定住。
迈克尔长吸了一口气。
"不管怎么说，一桩谜算是解开了。"他说，"您是在哪儿发现这两个人的？"
听见他的声音，那女人睁开眼，惊惧地朝他皱起眉头，随即又望向那男人。
"你受伤了吗？"迈克尔用荷兰语说，可她的教养显然在欧语方面有所亏欠，因为她没有答话。

===Original===
She was so uncomfortable at the sight of him that Michael was glad to go out of the room. It was not until they were back in his sanctum that Mr. Longvale told his story.
“I saw them last night about half-past eleven,” he said. “They were staggering down the road, and I thought at first that they were intoxicated, but fortunately the woman spoke, and as I have never forgotten a voice, even when it spoke in a language that was unfamiliar to me, I realized immediately that it was my patient, and went out to intercept her. I then saw the condition of her companion, and she, recognizing me, began to speak excitedly in a language which I could not understand, though I would have been singularly dense if I had had any doubt as to her meaning. The man was on the point of collapse, but, assisted by the woman, I managed to get him into the house and to the room where he now is. Fortunately, in the expectation of again being called to attend her, I had purchased a small stock of surgical dressing and was able to attend to the man.”
“Is he badly hurt?” asked Michael.
===Chinese===
她一见他就浑身不自在，迈克尔乐得走出房去。直到回到老人自己的圣所，朗维尔先生才讲出原委。
"昨晚十一点半左右，我看见了他们。"他说，"两人踉踉跄跄走在路上，起初我还当他们喝醉了，幸亏那女人开了口。我这个人，听过的嗓音从不忘却，哪怕说的是我听不懂的语言，所以我立刻听出那是我的病人，便出门拦住她。接着我看清了她同伴的伤势，她也认出了我，急切地说起一种我听不懂的语言——不过，倘若我连她的意思都有疑，那我也未免太迟钝了。那男人眼看就要倒下，多亏那女人帮衬，我总算把他弄进屋来，安置在他如今躺着的那间房里。所幸我一直盼着再被请去给她看病，早备下了一小批外科敷料，才好为他料理伤处。"
"他伤得重吗？"迈克尔问。

===Original===
“He has lost a considerable quantity of blood,” said the other, “and, though there seems to be no arteries severed or bones broken, the wounds have an alarming appearance. Now, it has occurred to me,” he went on, in his oddly profound manner, “that this unfortunate native could not have received his injury except as the result of some illegal act, and I thought the best thing to do was to notify the police that they were under my care. I called first upon my excellent friend, Mr. John Knebworth, and opened my heart to him. He then told me your position, and I decided to wait your return before I took any further steps.”
“You have solved a mystery that has puzzled me, and incidentally, you have confirmed a story which I had received with considerable scepticism,” said Mike. “I think you were well advised in informing the police﻿—I will make a report to headquarters, and send an ambulance to take these two people to hospital. Is the man fit to be moved?”
“I think so,” nodded the old gentleman. “He is sleeping heavily now, and has the appearance of being in a state of coma, but that is not the case. They are quite welcome to stay here, though I have no convenience, and must do my own nursing, which is rather a bother, for I am not fitted for such a strain. Happily, the woman is able to do a great deal for him.”
“Did he have a sword when he arrived?”
Mr. Longvale clicked his lips impatiently.
“How stupid of me to forget that! Yes, it is in here.”
===Chinese===
"他失血不少，"对方说，"而且，虽说似乎没有断动脉、没有伤骨头，创口的样子却叫人心惊。后来我想到，"他以他那古怪深沉的口吻接着说，"这位不幸的土著所受的伤，必是某种非法行为的后果，我以为上策莫如通知警方，说明他们在我照看之下。我先去拜访了我的至交约翰·尼布沃斯先生，向他推心置腹。他便告诉我您的身份，我于是决定，等您回来再作下一步打算。"
"您解开了一桩让我百思不解的谜，顺带还证实了一个我原本颇怀疑的说法。"迈克说，"我认为您通报警方是明智的——我会向总部呈报，再派一辆救护车把这两位送进医院。那男人经得起移动吗？"
"我想经得起。"老先生点头道，"他现在睡得很沉，看似昏迷，实则不然。他们尽管住在这儿，只是我这里设备不周，看护诸事都得亲力亲为，相当烦累，我这把年纪实难胜任。所幸那女人能替他料理许多。"
"他来的时候带着剑吗？"
朗维尔先生不耐烦地咂了咂嘴。
"瞧我这记性，竟把这给忘了！带着呢，就在这里面。"

===Original===
He went to a drawer in an old-fashioned bureau, pulled it open and took out the identical sword which Michael had seen hanging above the mantelpiece at Griff Towers. It was spotlessly clean, and had been so when Mr. Longvale took it from the brown man’s hands. And yet he did not expect it to be in any other condition, for to the swordsman of the East his sword is his child, and probably the brown man’s first care had been to wipe it clean.
Michael was taking his leave when he suddenly asked:
“I wonder if it would give you too much trouble, Mr. Longvale, to get me a glass of water? My throat is parched.”
With an exclamation of apology, the old man hurried away, leaving Michael in the hall.
===Chinese===
他走到一张老式写字台前，拉开抽屉，取出的正是迈克尔在格里夫塔楼壁炉上方见过的那柄剑。它洁净无瑕，朗维尔先生从那棕色男人手里接过来时便是如此。其实他也不指望它会是别的样子，因为对东方的剑客而言，剑便是他的孩子，那棕色男人头一桩要事，多半就是把剑擦拭干净。
迈克尔正要告辞，忽然问道：
"朗维尔先生，可否劳您驾，给我倒杯水来？我嗓子干得很。"
老人连声致歉，匆匆去了，把迈克尔留在门厅里。

===Original===
Hanging on pegs was the long overcoat of the master of Dower House, and beside it the curly-rimmed beaver and a very prosaic derby hat, which Michael took down the moment the old man’s back was turned. It had been no ruse of his, this demand for a drink, for he was parched. Only Michael had the inquisitiveness of his profession.
The old gentleman returned quickly to find Michael examining the hat.
“Where did this come from?” asked the detective.
“That was the hat the native was wearing when he arrived,” said Mr. Longvale.
“I will take it with me, if you don’t mind,” said Michael after a long silence.
“With all the pleasure in life. Our friend upstairs will not need a hat for a very long time,” he said, with a whimsical little smile.
Michael went back to his car, put the hat carefully beside him, and drove into Chichester; and all the way he was in a state of wonder. For inside the hat were the initials “L. F.” How came the hat of Lawley Foss on the head of the brown man from Borneo?
===Chinese===
衣帽钩上挂着寡妇居主人的长外套，旁边是一顶卷檐海狸皮帽和一顶再平常不过的圆顶硬礼帽——老人一转背，迈克尔便把那顶礼帽摘了下来。讨水喝并非他的计策，他是真的口渴。只不过，迈克尔还有他那一行的好奇心。
老先生很快回来，见迈克尔正在端详那顶帽子。
"这是哪儿来的？"侦探问。
"那土著来的时候头上戴的，就是这顶帽子。"朗维尔先生说。
沉默了半晌，迈克尔说："您若不介意，我想把它带走。"
"求之不得。咱们楼上那位朋友，往后好一阵子都用不着帽子了。"他说着，露出一丝古怪的浅笑。
迈克尔回到车上，把帽子小心地放在身旁，驱车进了奇切斯特；一路上他满心诧异。原来帽子里面绣着 "L. F." 两个姓名开首字母。劳利·福斯的帽子，怎么会戴到那个婆罗洲来的棕色男人头上？

## XXII The Head / XXII 头颅

===Original===
When Mike went out, he found Stella at the gate of the studio, and remembered, seeing her, that she had been invited to lunch at Griff Towers. To his surprise she crossed the road to him.
“I wanted to see you, Mr. Brixan,” she said. “I sent in word to find if you were there.”
“Then your message was wrongly delivered to Mr. Knebworth,” smiled Mike.
She lifted one of her shoulders in demonstration of her contempt for Jack Knebworth and all his works.
“No, it was you I wanted to see. You’re a detective, aren’t you?”
“I am,” said Michael, wondering what was coming next.
===Chinese===
迈克走出来时，发现斯特拉（Stella）正站在制片厂大门口，一见她便想起，她已被邀去格里夫塔楼吃午饭。令他意外的是，她竟穿过马路朝他走来。
"我想见的是您，布里克森先生，"她说，"我让人传话进去，看看您在不在。"
"那您的口信可送错了人，送到尼布沃斯先生那儿去了。"迈克微笑道。
她耸起一边肩膀，以示对杰克·尼布沃斯及其一切作为的轻蔑。
"不，我想见的就是您。您是侦探，对吧？"
"正是。"迈克尔说，心里揣测着接下来会是什么。

===Original===
“My car is round the corner: will you come to my house?”
Michael hesitated. He was anxious, more than anxious, to speak to Adele, though he had nothing special to tell her, beyond the thing which he himself did not know and she could never guess.
“With pleasure,” he said.
She was a skilful motorist, and apparently so much engrossed in her driving that she did not speak throughout the journey. In the pretty little drawing-room from which he had a view of the lovely South Downs, he waited expectantly.
“Mr. Brixan, I am going to tell you something which I think you ought to know.”
===Chinese===
"我的车就在拐角那儿：您愿意到我家去一趟吗？"
迈克尔犹豫了一下。他急欲——岂止是急欲——同阿黛尔说话，尽管他并没有什么特别的话要告诉她，除了那件连他自己也不知道、她也永远猜不着的事。
"乐意之至。"他说。
她车技娴熟，一路显然全神贯注于驾驶，整个行程一言未发。在那间可以望见秀丽南唐斯（South Downs）山景的雅致小客厅里，他满怀期待地等待着。
"布里克森先生，我要告诉您一件事，一件我认为您应当知道的事。"

===Original===
Her face was pale, her manner curiously nervous.
“I don’t know what you will think of me when I have told you, but I’ve got to risk that. I can’t keep silence any longer.”
A shrill bell sounded in the hall.
“The telephone. Will you excuse me one moment?”
She hurried out, leaving the door slightly ajar. Michael heard her quick, angry reply to somebody at the other end of the wire, and then a long interregnum of silence, when apparently she listened without comment. It was nearly ten minutes before she returned, and her eyes were bright and her cheeks flushed.
===Chinese===
她脸色苍白，神情出奇地紧张。
"不知道听完之后您会怎么看我，可我也只好冒这个险了。我不能再沉默下去。"
门厅里响起一阵刺耳的铃声。
"是电话。失陪片刻，可以吗？"
她匆匆出去，房门虚掩着。迈克尔听见她对着电话那头的人急促而恼怒地回话，随后是一段长长的沉寂，显然她只是在听，一言不发。过了将近十分钟她才回来，双眼发亮，双颊绯红。

===Original===
“Would you mind if I told you what I was going to tell you a little later?” she asked.
She had been on the telephone to Sir Gregory: of that Michael was sure, though she had not mentioned his name.
“There’s no time like the present, Miss Mendoza,” he said encouragingly, and she licked her dry lips.
“Yes, I know, but there are reasons why I can’t speak now. Would you see me tomorrow?”
===Chinese===
"我想把要告诉您的事推迟一会儿再说，您介意吗？"她问。
她方才是在同格雷戈里爵士（Sir Gregory）通电话——这一点迈克尔很肯定，尽管她没有提到他的名字。
"要说什么时候合适，眼下就是，门多萨小姐。"他鼓励道。她舔了舔发干的嘴唇。
"是，我知道，可我有缘故，现在不能说。明天您能见我吗？"

===Original===
“Why, certainly,” said Michael, secretly glad of his release.
“Shall I drive you back?”
“No, thank you, I can walk.”
“Let me take you to the edge of the town: I’m going that way,” she begged.
Of course she was going that way, thought Michael. She was going to Griff Towers. He was so satisfied on this matter that he did not even trouble to inquire, and when she dropped him at his hotel, she hardly waited for him to step to the sidewalk before the car leapt forward on its way.
===Chinese===
"当然可以。"迈克尔说，心里暗自庆幸得了脱身之机。
"我开车送您回去吧？"
"不必了，谢谢，我可以走路。"
"至少让我把您送到镇边上：我正顺路。"她恳求道。
她当然是顺路，迈克尔心想。她这是要去格里夫塔楼。这件事他十拿九稳，连问都懒得问了。到了旅馆门口她把他放下，几乎没等他踏上人行道，汽车便一跃而去。

===Original===
“There’s a telegram for you, sir,” said the porter. He went into the manager’s office and returned with a buff envelope, which Michael tore open.
For a time he could not comprehend the fateful message the telegram conveyed. And then slowly he read it to himself.

> “A head found on Chobham Common early this morning. Come to Leatherhead Police Station at once.
> “Staines.”
===Chinese===
"先生，有您一封电报。"门房说。他进了经理办公室，拿回一只牛皮纸信封，迈克尔一把撕开。
一时间，他没能领会电文所传递的那条关系重大的消息。随后他慢慢地默读起来。

> "今晨在乔巴姆公地（Chobham Common）发现一颗头颅。速来莱瑟黑德（Leatherhead）警察局。
> "斯坦斯"

===Original===
An hour later a fast car dropped him before the station. Staines was waiting on the step.
“Found at daybreak this morning,” he said. “The man is so far unknown.”
He led the way to an outhouse. On a table in the centre of the room was a box, and he lifted the lid.
Mike took one glance at the waxen face and turned white.
“Good God!” he breathed.
It was the head of Lawley Foss.
===Chinese===
一小时后，一辆快车把他送到警察局门前。斯坦斯正在台阶上等候。
"今天黎明发现的，"他说，"死者身份至今不明。"
他领路来到一间外屋。房间中央的桌上放着一只盒子，他掀开了盖子。
迈克朝那张蜡白的脸看了一眼，顿时面色刷白。
"天哪！"他低呼一声。
那是劳利·福斯的头颅。

## I The Headhunter / I 猎头者

===Original===
Captain Mike Brixan had certain mild and innocent superstitions. He believed, for example, that if he saw a green crow in a field he would certainly see another green crow before the day was out. And when, at the bookstand on Aix la Chapelle station, he saw and purchased a dime novel that was comprehensively intituled *Only an Extra, or the Pride of Hollywood*, he was less concerned as to how this thrilling and dog-eared romance came to be on offer at half a million marks (this was in the days when marks were worth money) than as to the circumstances in which he would again hear or read the word “extras” in the sense of a supernumerary and unimportant screen actress.
The novel did not interest him at all. He read one page of superlatives and turned for relief to the study of a Belgian timetable. He was bored, but not so bored that he could interest himself in the sensational rise of the fictitious Rosa Love from modest obscurity to a press agent and wealth.
But “extra” was a new one on Michael, and he waited for the day to bring its inevitable companion.
To say that he was uninterested in crime, that burglars were less thrilling than golf scores, and the record of murders hardly worth the reading, might convey a wrong impression to those who knew him as the cleverest agent in the Foreign Office Intelligence Department.
===Chinese===
迈克·布里克森上尉（Captain Mike Brixan）有几分无害的小迷信。比如他相信，若在田野里看见一只绿乌鸦，当天之内必定还会看见第二只绿乌鸦。因此，当他在亚琛（Aix la Chapelle）车站的书报摊上看见并买下一本廉价小说——书名详尽备至，唤作《不过是个临时演员，或曰好莱坞的骄傲》（*Only an Extra, or the Pride of Hollywood*）——他并不太操心这本卷了边的惊险罗曼史何以标价五十万马克出售（那还是马克值钱的年头），倒是更琢磨起另一件事：自己将在怎样的情形下，再一次听到或读到"extra"这个词，用来指银幕上可有可无、无足轻重的临时女演员。
这本小说他全无兴致。读了一页满纸最高级的浮夸词句，他便转而研究起比利时列车时刻表来换换脑筋。他确实烦闷，却还没烦闷到会对虚构人物罗莎·洛芙（Rosa Love）从卑微的无名之辈发迹为报刊宣传员兼富婆的轰动史产生兴趣。
不过"extra"这个词对迈克尔（Michael）来说是个新鲜兆头，他便等着这一天带来它不可避免的同伴。
若说他本人对犯罪毫无兴趣，说夜盗还不如高尔夫球比分来得刺激，说凶案记录简直不值一读——对那些深知他是外交部情报司（Foreign Office Intelligence Department）最干练特工的人来说，这倒可能造成错误的印象。

===Original===
His official life was spent in meeting queer continentals in obscure restaurants and, in divers roles, to learn of the undercurrents that were drifting the barques of diplomacy to unsuspected ports. He had twice roamed through Europe in the guise of an open-mouthed tourist; had canoed many hundred miles through the gorges of the Danube to discover, in little riverside beer-houses, the inward meanings of secret mobilizations. These were tasks wholly to his liking.
Therefore he was not unnaturally annoyed when he was withdrawn from Berlin at a moment when, as it seemed, the mystery of the Slovak Treaty was in a way to being solved, for he had secured, at a cost, a rough but accurate draft.
“I should have had a photograph of the actual document if you had left me another twenty-four hours,” he reproached his chief, Major George Staines, when he reported himself at Whitehall next morning.
“Sorry,” replied that unrepentant man, “but the truth is, we’ve had a heart to heart talk with the Slovakian Prime Minister, and he has promised to behave and practically given us the text of the treaty—it was only a commercial affair. Mike, did you know Elmer?”
===Chinese===
他的公职生涯，是在不起眼的餐馆里会见形形色色古怪的欧陆人，以各种身份探听那些正把外交之舟冲向出人意料港口的暗流。他曾两度装扮成张大嘴巴的观光客漫游欧洲；也曾乘独木舟沿多瑙河峡谷漂流数百英里，在河边的小啤酒馆里探悉秘密动员的弦外之音。这些差事完全合他的胃口。
因此，当斯洛伐克条约（Slovak Treaty）之谜眼看似就要揭开之际，他却被从柏林召回，心里恼火也就不足为怪了——他已不惜代价弄到了一份粗略却准确的草案。
"你要是再给我二十四小时，我就能把正式文本拍照弄到手了。"第二天早晨他到白厅（Whitehall）向上司乔治·斯坦斯少校（Major George Staines）报到时，如此埋怨道。
"抱歉，"这位毫无悔意的人答道，"不过实情是，我们同斯洛伐克总理推心置腹谈了一回，他答应安分守己，实际上等于是把条约文本给了我们——那不过是一桩商务协定。迈克，你认识埃尔默（Elmer）吗？"

===Original===
The Foreign Office detective sat down on the edge of the table.
“Have you brought me from Berlin to ask me that?” he demanded bitterly. “Have you taken me from my favourite café on Unter den Linden—by the way, the Germans are making small arm ammunition by the million at a converted pencil factory in Bavaria—to discuss Elmer? He’s a clerk, isn’t he?”
Major Staines nodded.
“He *was*,” he said, “in the Accountancy Department. He disappeared from view three weeks ago, and an examination of his books showed that he had been systematically stealing funds which were under his control.”
Mike Brixan made a little face.
===Chinese===
这位外交部侦探在桌子边上坐了下来。
"你把我从柏林叫回来，就为了问这个？"他愤愤然道。"你把我从菩提树下大街（Unter den Linden）我最心爱的咖啡馆里拽走——顺便说一句，德国人正在巴伐利亚一家由铅笔厂改建的厂房里以百万计地生产轻武器弹药——就为了谈论埃尔默？他不过是个小职员，不是吗？"
斯坦斯少校点了点头。
"他*曾经*是，"他说，"在会计部。三个星期前他不见了踪影，一查他的账目，才发现他一直在有系统地盗取自己经手的公款。"
迈克·布里克森做了个小鬼脸。

===Original===
“I’m sorry to hear that,” he said. “He seemed to be a fairly quiet and inoffensive man. But surely you don’t want me to go after him? That is a job for Scotland Yard.”
“I don’t want you to go after him,” said Staines slowly, “because—well, he has been found.”
There was something very significant and sinister in his tone, and before he could take the little slip of paper from the portfolio on the desk, Michael Brixan knew what was coming.
“Not the Headhunter?” he gasped. Even Michael knew about the Headhunter.
===Chinese===
"听你这么说我很难过，"他说，"他看上去是个挺安静、不惹是非的人。不过你们总不至于要我去追查他吧？那是苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）的差事。"
"我不要你去追查他，"斯坦斯慢吞吞地说，"因为——呃，他已经找到了。"
他语气里透着一种意味深长的不祥，还没等他从桌上的公文包里取出那张小纸条，迈克尔·布里克森就知道要听到什么了。
"不会是"猎头者"（Headhunter）吧？"他倒抽一口气。就连迈克尔也知道猎头者其人。

===Original===
Staines nodded.
“Here’s the note.”
He handed the typewritten slip across to his subordinate, and Michael read:

> “You will find a box in the hedge by the railway arch at Esher.
> “The Headhunter.”

“The Headhunter!” repeated Michael mechanically, and whistled.
===Chinese===
斯坦斯点了点头。
"条子在这儿。"
他把那张打字机打的纸条递给下属，迈克尔念道：

> "你们会在埃舍尔（Esher）铁路桥洞旁的树篱里找到一只盒子。
> "猎头者"

"猎头者！"迈克尔机械地重复道，吹了声口哨。

===Original===
“We found the box, and of course we found the unfortunate Elmer’s head, sliced neatly from his body,” said Staines. “This is the twelfth head in seven years,” Staines went on, “and in almost every case—in fact, in every case except two—the victim has been a fugitive from justice. Even if the treaty question had not been settled, Mike, I should have brought you back.”
“But this is a police job,” said the young man, troubled.
“Technically you’re a policeman,” interrupted his chief, “and the Foreign Secretary wishes you to take this case in hand, and he does this with the full approval of the Secretary of State, who of course controls Scotland Yard. So far, the death of Francis Elmer and the discovery of his gruesome remains have not been given out to the press. There was such a fuss last time that the police want to keep this quiet. They have had an inquest—I guess the jury was picked, but it would be high treason to say so—and the usual verdict has been returned. The only information I can give you is that Elmer was seen by his niece a week ago in Chichester. We discovered this before the man’s fate was known. The girl, Adele Leamington, is working for the Knebworth Film Corporation, which has its studio in Chichester. Old Knebworth is an American and a very good sort. The girl is a sort of super-chorus-extra, that’s the word—”
===Chinese===
"我们找到了那只盒子，自然也找到了倒霉的埃尔默的脑袋，被人从躯体上利利索索地切了下来，"斯坦斯说。"这是七年来的第十二颗头，"他接着说，"几乎每一桩案子——确切说，除两桩之外——受害者都是逃避法律制裁的逃犯。迈克，就算条约的问题没有解决，我也会把你召回来的。"
"可这是警察的差事，"年轻人忧虑地说。
"从名义上说，你就是个警察，"上司打断他，"外交大臣希望你经手此案，而且是征得内政大臣完全同意的——内政大臣当然管着苏格兰场。到目前为止，弗朗西斯·埃尔默（Francis Elmer）之死及其可怕遗骸的发现还没有通报报界。上次那阵风波闹得太凶，这回警方想瞒住不动。死因研讯已经开过了——我猜陪审团是精心挑选的，不过这么说出去就是大逆不道了——照例的裁定也已经做出。我能告诉你的唯一情况是：一星期前，埃尔默在奇切斯特（Chichester）被他侄女看见过。我们发现这一点时，此人的下场还不为人知。那姑娘叫阿黛尔·利明顿（Adele Leamington），正在尼布沃斯电影公司（Knebworth Film Corporation）做事，公司的摄影棚就设在奇切斯特。老尼布沃斯是美国人，人是顶好的。那姑娘算是个超级群舞临时演员——说的就是这个词——"

===Original===
Michael gasped.
“Extra! I knew that infernal word would turn up again. Go on, sir—what do you wish me to do?”
“Go along and see her,” said the chief. “Here is the address.”
“Is there a Mrs. Elmer?” asked Michael as he put the slip into his pocket.
===Chinese===
迈克尔倒抽了一口气。
"临时演员！我就知道这个该死的词还会冒出来。说下去，长官——你要我做什么？"
"去找她谈谈，"上司说，"这是地址。"
"埃尔默有太太吗？"迈克尔一边把纸条塞进口袋一边问。

===Original===
The other nodded.
“Yes, but she can throw no light upon the murder. She, by the way, is the only person who knows he is dead. She had not seen her husband for a month, and apparently they had been more or less separated for years. She benefits considerably by his death, for he was well insured in her favour.”
Michael read again the gruesome note from the Headhunter.
“What is your theory about this?” he asked curiously.
“The general idea is that he is a lunatic who feels called upon to mete out punishment to defaulters. But the two exceptions disturb that theory pretty considerably.”
===Chinese===
对方点了点头。
"有，可她对这桩凶案提不出任何线索。顺便说一句，她是唯一知道他已死的人。她已有一个月没见过丈夫，而且显然两人多年来多少处于分居状态。他的死让她得了不少好处，因为他投了足额的人寿保险，受益人正是她。"
迈克尔又读了一遍猎头者那封可怕的短笺。
"对此你有什么看法？"他好奇地问。
"一般的看法是：他是个疯子，自觉负有惩治亏空卷逃者的天职。但那两桩例外，把这种理论搅得够呛。"

===Original===
Staines lay back in his chair, a puzzled frown on his face.
“Take the case of Willitt. His head was found on Clapham Common two years ago. Willitt was a well-off man, the soul of honesty, well liked, and he had a very big balance at his bank. Crewling, the second exception, who was one of the first of the Hunter’s victims, was also above suspicion, though in his case there is no doubt he was mentally unbalanced a few weeks before his death.
“The typewritten notification has invariably been typed out on the same machine. In every case you have the half-obliterated *u*, the faint *g*, and the extraordinary alignment which the experts are unanimous in ascribing to a very old and out-of-date Kost machine. Find the man who uses that typewriter and you have probably found the murderer. But it is very unlikely that he will ever be found that way, for the police have published photographs pointing out the peculiarities of type, and I should imagine that Mr. Hunter does not use this machine except to announce the demise of his victims.”
===Chinese===
斯坦斯向椅背上一靠，困惑地皱着眉头。
"就拿威利特（Willitt）的案子来说。两年前，他的脑袋在克拉彭公地（Clapham Common）被人发现。威利特家境殷实，诚实得挑不出毛病，人缘极好，银行里存款可观。第二个例外克鲁林（Crewling）是"猎手"最早的受害者之一，同样无可怀疑，尽管在他那桩案子里，毫无疑问他死前几个星期精神已经失常。
"那些打字的通告无一例外都出自同一部机器。每一张上都有那个半磨损的*u*、淡淡的*g*，还有那异样的字行排列——专家们一致断定，这出自一台老掉牙、过了时的科斯特（Kost）牌打字机。找到用那台打字机的人，十有八九就找到了凶手。但靠这个法子找到他的可能性微乎其微，因为警方早已公布照片，指明了字体的种种特征，依我看，那位猎手先生（Mr. Hunter）除了宣布受害者的死讯之外，是不会动用这台机器的。"

===Original===
Michael Brixan went back to his flat, a little more puzzled and a little more worried by his unusual commission. He moved and had his being in the world of high politics. The finesses of diplomacy were his peculiar study, and the normal abnormalities of humanity, the thefts and murders and larcenies which occupied the attention of the constabulary, did not come into his purview.
“Bill,” said he, addressing the small terrier that lay on the hearthrug before the fireless grate of his sitting-room, “this is where I fall down. But whether I do or not, I’m going to meet an extra—ain’t that grand?”
Bill wagged his tail agreeably.
===Chinese===
迈克尔·布里克森回到自己的公寓，这项不同寻常的差事让他多了一分困惑，也多了一分担忧。他生活和活动于高层政治的世界。外交的微妙手腕才是他的专门研究，而人性中那些寻常的非常态——那些占据着警察注意力的盗窃、凶杀与偷窃——从来不在他的视野之内。
"比尔（Bill），"他对那条趴在客厅无火炉栅前炉边地毯上的小梗犬说，"这回我可要栽跟头了。不过栽不栽得另说，我总算要去见一位临时演员了——这可不赖吧？"
比尔友善地摇了摇尾巴。

## IV The Leading Lady / IV 领衔女主角

===Original===
In the studio of the Knebworth Picture Corporation the company had been waiting in its street clothes for the greater part of an hour.
Jack Knebworth sat in his conventional attitude, huddled up in his canvas chair, fingering his long chin and glaring from time to time at the clock above the studio manager’s office.
It was eleven when Stella Mendoza flounced in, bringing with her the fragrance of wood violets and a small, unhappy Peke.
“Do you work to summertime?” asked Knebworth slowly. “Or maybe you thought the call was for afternoon? You’ve kept fifty people waiting, Stella.”
“I can’t help their troubles,” she said with a shrug of shoulder. “You told me you were going on location, and naturally I didn’t expect there would be any hurry. I had to pack my things.”
“Naturally you didn’t think there was any hurry!”
===Chinese===
在尼布沃斯电影公司的摄影棚里，全班人马穿着便装已经等了大半个钟头。
杰克·尼布沃斯以他惯常的姿势坐在那儿，蜷在帆布椅里，手指摩挲着长长的下巴，不时狠狠瞪一眼制片主任办公室上方挂着的钟。
十一点整，斯特拉·门多萨（Stella Mendoza）才大摇大摆地闯了进来，随身带来一股紫罗兰香气和一只闷闷不乐的小狮子狗。
"您是按夏令时间过日子的？"尼布沃斯慢悠悠地问，"还是您以为通告单写的是下午？您让五十个人干等着，斯特拉。"
"他们的难处我可管不着，"她耸了耸肩说，"你说过要出外景，我自然以为不用赶急。我总得收拾行李嘛。"
"您自然以为不用赶急！"

===Original===
Jack Knebworth reckoned to have three fights a year. This was the third. The first had been with Stella, and the second had been with Stella, and the third was certainly to be with Stella.
“I wanted you to be here at ten. I’ve had these boys and girls waiting since a quarter of ten.”
“What do you want to shoot?” she asked with an impatient jerk of her head.
“You mostly,” said Jack slowly. “Get into No. 9 outfit and don’t forget to leave your pearl earrings off. You’re supposed to be a half-starved chorus girl. We’re shooting at Griff Towers, and I told the gentleman who lent us the use of the house that I’d be through the day work by three. If you were Pauline Frederick or Norma Talmadge or Lillie Gish, you’d be worth waiting for, but Stella Mendoza has got to be on this lot by ten—and don’t forget it!”
Old Jack Knebworth got up from his canvas chair and began to put on his coat with ominous deliberation, the flushed and angry girl watching him, her dark eyes blazing with injured pride and hurt vanity.
===Chinese===
杰克·尼布沃斯给自己算的是一年吵三场架。这是第三场。第一场是跟斯特拉吵的，第二场也是跟斯特拉吵的，这第三场当然还是跟斯特拉。
"我要您十点到场。这些小伙子姑娘们从九点三刻就一直等着。"
"你要拍什么？"她不耐烦地把头一甩，问道。
"主要拍您，"杰克慢吞吞地说，"换上九号戏服，别忘了取下珍珠耳环。您要演的是个饿得半死的合唱队姑娘。我们去格里夫塔楼（Griff Towers）拍外景，我跟那位把宅子借给我们用的先生说好了，日场的活儿三点以前收工。您要是宝琳·弗雷德里克（Pauline Frederick）、诺玛·塔尔梅奇（Norma Talmadge）或者莉莉·吉什（Lillie Gish），倒还值得等，可斯特拉·门多萨十点就必须到场——您给我记住了！"
老杰克·尼布沃斯从帆布椅里站起来，带着不祥的从容开始穿外套；涨红了脸、怒气冲冲的姑娘盯着他，黑眼睛里燃烧着受辱的傲气与被刺伤的虚荣。

===Original===
Stella had once been plain Maggie Stubbs, the daughter of a Midland grocer, and old Jack had talked to her as if she were still Maggie Stubbs and not the great film star of coruscating brilliance, idol (or her press agent lied) of the screen fans of all the world.
“All right, if you want a fuss you can have it, Knebworth. I’m going to quit—now! I think I know what is due to my position. That part’s got to be rewritten to give me a chance of putting my personality over. There’s too much leading man in it, anyway. People don’t pay real money to see men. You don’t treat me fair, Knebworth: I’m temperamental, I admit it. You can’t expect a woman of my kind to be a block of wood.”
