“The only thing about you that’s a block of wood is your head, Stella,” grunted the producer, and went on, oblivious to the rising fury expressed in the girl’s face. “You’ve had two years playing small parts in Hollywood, and you’ve brought nothing back to England but a line of fresh talk, and you could have gotten that out of the Sunday supplements! Temperament! That’s a word that means doctors’ certificates when a picture’s half taken, and a long rest unless your salary’s put up fifty percent. Thank God this picture isn’t a quarter taken or an eighth. Quit, you mean-spirited guttersnipe—and quit as soon as you darn please!”
Boiling with rage, her lips quivering so that she could not articulate, the girl turned and flung out of the studio.
===Chinese===
斯特拉从前不过是个不起眼的玛吉·斯塔布斯（Maggie Stubbs），中部地区一个杂货店老板的女儿，而老杰克跟她说话的口气，仿佛她还是那个玛吉·斯塔布斯，而不是光彩夺目的超级影星、（至少她的宣传员没撒谎的话）全世界影迷的偶像。
"好啊，你要闹一场就闹吧，尼布沃斯。我不干了——现在就不干！我想我很清楚自己的身份该受什么待遇。那个角色必须重写，得给我机会施展我的个性。再说了，那戏里男主角的戏份也太多了。观众掏真金白银可不是来看男人的。你待我不公平，尼布沃斯：我这个人有艺术脾气，这我承认。你总不能指望我这样的女人当块木头。"
"你身上唯一像木头的就是你那颗脑袋，斯特拉，"制片人咕哝道，接着往下说，全不理会姑娘脸上节节攀升的怒火，"你在好莱坞跑了两年龙套，回英国什么都没带回来，就带回一套新鲜说辞，那玩意儿从星期天副刊上就能学到！艺术脾气！这词儿的意思就是：片子拍到一半就要医生证明，不加薪百分之五十就长期歇着。谢天谢地，这部片子连四分之一、连八分之一都没拍。走人吧，你这小心眼儿的街头丫头——爱多早走就多早走！"
姑娘怒火中烧，嘴唇哆嗦得说不出话来，一转身冲出了摄影棚。

===Original===
White-haired Jack Knebworth glared round at the silent company.
“This is where the miracle happens,” he said sardonically. “This is where the extra girl who’s left a sick mother and a mortgage at home leaps to fame in a night. If you don’t know that kinder thing happens on every lot in Hollywood you’re no students of fiction. Stand forth, Mary Pickford the second!”
The extras smiled, some amused, some uncomfortable, but none spoke. Adele was frozen stiff, incapable of speech.
“Modesty don’t belong to this industry,” old Jack sneered amiably. “Who thinks she can play ‘Roselle’ in this piece—because an extra’s going to play the part, believe me! I’m going to show this pseudo-actress that there isn’t an extra on this lot that couldn’t play her head off. Somebody talked about playing a part yesterday—you!”
His forefinger pointed to Adele, and with a heart that beat tumultuously she went toward him.
===Chinese===
白头发的杰克·尼布沃斯环视着鸦雀无声的全班人马。
"奇迹就是在这样的地方发生的，"他讥诮地说，"家里撇下病母和抵押债的临时演员姑娘，一夜之间跃身成名，说的就是这种地方。你们要是不知道这类好事在好莱坞每个片场天天都有，那就白读小说了。站出来吧，第二个玛丽·碧克馥（Mary Pickford）！"
临时演员们笑了，有的觉得好玩，有的浑身不自在，但谁也没吭声。阿黛尔整个人僵住了，连话都说不出来。
"谦虚这个行业里可没有，"老杰克和气地讥讽道，"谁觉得自己能在戏里演'罗塞尔'（Roselle）？——因为这角色注定要由临时演员来演，信我的话！我要让这位假明星瞧瞧，我片场里随便哪个临时演员都能把她比到地底下去。昨天有人跟我提过想演个角色——就是你！"
他的食指指向阿黛尔，她心潮翻涌地朝他走去。

===Original===
“I had a camera test of you six months ago,” said Jack suspiciously. “There was something wrong with her: what was it?”
He turned to his assistant. That young man scratched his head in an effort of memory.
“Ankles?” he hazarded a guess at random—a safe guess, for Knebworth had views about ankles.
“Nothing wrong with them—get out the print and let us see it.”
Ten minutes later, Adele sat by the old man’s side in the little projection room and saw her “test” run through.
“Hair!” said Knebworth triumphantly. “I knew there was something. Don’t like bobbed hair. Makes a girl too pert and sophisticated. You’ve grown it?” he added as the lights were switched on.
===Chinese===
"六个月前我给你试过一次镜，"杰克满腹狐疑地说，"那次有点不对劲：是什么来着？"
他转向助理。那年轻人挠着头努力回忆。
"脚踝？"他随口猜了一个——这猜法保险，因为尼布沃斯对脚踝自有一套看法。
"脚踝没毛病——把拷贝调出来瞧瞧。"
十分钟后，阿黛尔坐在小放映室里老人身旁，看着自己的"试镜"片子放了一遍。
"头发！"尼布沃斯得意地喊，"我就知道有地方不对。我不喜欢鲍伯头。那让姑娘显得太轻佻、太世故。你留长了？"灯亮起来时他又问了一句。

===Original===
“Yes, Mr. Knebworth.”
He looked at her in dispassionate admiration.
“You’ll do,” he said reluctantly. “See the wardrobe and get Miss Mendoza’s costumes. There’s one thing I’d like to tell you before you go,” he said, stopping her. “You may be good and you may be bad, but, good or bad, there’s no future for you—so don’t get heated up. The only woman who’s got any chance in England is the producer’s wife, and I’ll never marry you if you go down on your knees to me! That’s the only kind of star they know in English films—the producer’s wife; and unless you’re that, you haven’t—!”
He snapped his finger.
===Chinese===
"留长了，尼布沃斯先生。"
他以不带私心的欣赏打量着她。
"你行，"他不情愿地说，"去服装间领门多萨小姐的戏服。你走之前我有句话要说，"他拦住她说，"你也许行，也许不行，可不管行不行，你都没有前程——所以别太上心。在英国，唯一有出头机会的女人就是制片人的老婆，你就是跪下求我，我也绝不会娶你！英国电影里他们认的唯一一种明星就是这个——制片人的老婆；你要不是这个，就什么都不是——！"
他打了个响指。

===Original===
“I’ll give you a word of advice, kid. If you make good in this picture, link yourself up with one of those cute English directors that set three flats and a pot of palms and call it a drawing-room! Give Miss What’s-her-name the script, Harry. Say—go out somewhere quiet and study it, will you? Harry, you see the wardrobe. I give you half an hour to read that script!”
Like one in a dream, the girl walked out into the shady garden that ran the length of the studio building, and sat down, trying to concentrate on the typewritten lines. It wasn’t true—it could not be true! And then she heard the crunch of feet on gravel and looked up in alarm. It was the young man who had seen her that morning—Michael Brixan.
“Oh, please—you mustn’t interrupt me!” she begged in agitation. “I’ve got a part—a big part to read.”
Her distress was so real that he hastened to take his departure.
“I’m awfully sorry—” he began.
In her confusion she had dropped the loose sheets of the manuscript, and, stooping with her to pick them up, their heads bumped.
“Sorry—that’s an old comedy situation, isn’t it?” he began.
And then he saw the sheet of paper in his hand and began to read. It was a page of elaborate description of a scene.
===Chinese===
"给你一句忠告，小姑娘。你要是这部片子演成了，就赶紧跟上一个那种精明的英国导演——布景三板墙加一盆棕榈，他们就管那叫客厅！把剧本交给那位叫什么来着的小姐，哈里（Harry）。这样——找个清静地方把剧本好好读读，行吗？哈里，你去盯服装间。我给你半个小时读剧本！"
姑娘如坠梦中，走出摄影棚，来到沿厂房一路延伸的林荫花园里，坐下努力把心思集中在打字的台词上。这不是真的——这不可能是真的！就在这时，她听见砂石路上的脚步声，惊慌地抬起头。是那天早晨见过她的那个年轻人——迈克尔·布里克森。
"哦，求求你——你可别打搅我！"她激动地恳求道，"我拿到了一个角色——一个大角色，得好好读读。"
她的焦急如此真切，他赶紧抽身要走。
"实在对不起——"他开口道。
慌乱之中她把散页的剧本掉在了地上，两人同时俯身去捡，脑袋撞在了一起。
"抱歉——这是老掉牙的喜剧桥段了，对吧？"他开口说。
接着他看到了手里那页纸，便读了起来。那是一页详尽的场景说明。

===Original===

> “The cell is large, lighted by a swinging lamp. In centre is a steel gate through which a soldier on guard is seen pacing to and fro—”

“Good God!” said Michael, and went white.
The *u*’s in the type were blurred, the *g* was indistinct. The page had been typed on the machine from which the Headhunter sent forth his gruesome tales of death.
===Chinese===

> "牢房很大，由一盏吊灯照明。中央是一道铁栅门，透过它可以看见一名哨兵在来回踱步——"

"天哪！"迈克尔叫了一声，脸色发白。
打字字体里的*u*模糊不清，*g*若隐若现。这一页纸，正是出自猎头者用来发布他那一个个死亡故事的那台打字机。

## XXV The Man in the Car / XXV 汽车里的男人

===Original===
Life is largely made up of little things, but perspective in human affairs is not a gift common to youth. It had required a great effort on the part of Adele Leamington to ask a man to tea, but, once that effort was made, she had looked forward with a curious pleasure to the function.
At the moment Michael was speeding to London, she interviewed Jack Knebworth in his holy of holies.
“Certainly, my dear: you may take the afternoon off. I am not quite sure what the schedule was.”
He reached out his hand for the written timetable, but she supplied the information.
“You wanted some studio portraits of me﻿—‘stills,’ ” she said.
“So I did! Well, that can wait. Are you feeling pretty confident about the picture, eh?”
“I? No, I’m not confident, Mr. Knebworth; I’m in a state of nerves about it. You see, it doesn’t seem possible that I should make good at the first attempt. One dreams about such things, but in dreams it is easy to jump obstacles and get round dangerous corners and slur over difficulties. Every time you call ‘camera!’ I am in a state of panic, and I am so self-conscious that I am watching every movement I take, and saying to myself ‘You’re raising your hands awkwardly; you’re turning your head with a jerk.’ ”
“But that doesn’t last?” he said sharply, so sharply that she smiled.
“No: the moment I hear the camera turning, I feel that I *am* the character I’m supposed to be.”
===Chinese===
生活大半是由小事构成的，可是在人情事理上能拿捏分寸，却不是年轻人常见的天赋。对阿黛尔·利明顿来说，开口请一位男士喝茶已经费了老大的劲，可这口气一旦出了，她便怀着一种奇妙的喜悦期待着这场小小的茶会。
就在此刻迈克尔正驱车赶往伦敦，她则在杰克·尼布沃斯最神圣不可侵犯的圣殿里谒见他。
"当然可以，亲爱的：下午你就歇着吧。排期表上原先安排了什么，我记不太清了。"
他伸手去拿书面的时间表，她却直接替他答了。
"您要给我拍几张家棚人像——'剧照'（stills）。"她说。
"对了！那不急。对这部片子你挺有信心吧，嗯？"
"我？不，我没信心，尼布沃斯先生；我一想起来就紧张。您想啊，头一回出手就能成，这怎么看都不像可能的事。这种事人人都会做梦梦见，可在梦里，跨栏容易，绕开危险的转角容易，难处也都可以含糊过去。每回您一喊'开机！'我就慌了神，而且我自我意识太重，一边做动作一边盯着自己看，心里念叨着'你手抬得不自然；你转头转得太生硬'。"
"可这不会一直如此吧？"他不容分说地问，语气急得让她笑了。
"不会：一听见摄影机转动，我就觉得自己*就是*要演的那个角色。"

===Original===
He patted her on the shoulder.
“That is how you *should* feel,” he said, and went on: “Seen nothing of Mendoza, have you? She isn’t annoying you? Or Foss?”
“I’ve not seen Miss Mendoza for days﻿—but I saw Mr. Foss last night.”
She did not explain the curious circumstances, and Jack Knebworth was so incurious that he did not ask. So that he learnt nothing of Lawley Foss’s mysterious interview with the man in the closed car at the corner of Arundel Road, an incident she had witnessed on the previous night. Nor of the white and womanly hand that had waved him farewell, nor of the great diamond which had sparkled lustrously on the little finger of the unknown motorist.
===Chinese===
他拍了拍她的肩膀。
"这正是你*应当*有的感觉。"他说着，又问："门多萨没来找过你吧？她没惹你烦吧？福斯呢？"
"我好几天没见门多萨小姐了——不过昨晚我见到福斯先生了。"
她没有说明那桩蹊跷的经过，杰克·尼布沃斯也浑不好奇，并未追问。于是，劳利·福斯头天夜里在阿伦德尔路（Arundel Road）拐角同封闭汽车里那个人神秘会面的一幕——她亲眼目睹的那一幕——他便一无所知。同样也一无所知的，还有那只挥手道别的白皙女人手，以及那颗在无名车主小指上熠熠生辉的大钻石。

===Original===
Going home, Adele stopped at a confectioner’s and a florist’s, collected the cakes and flowers that were to adorn the table of Mrs. Watson’s parlour. She wondered more than a little just what attraction she offered to this man of affairs. She had a trick of getting outside and examining herself with an impartial eye, and she knew that, by self-repression and almost self-obliteration, she had succeeded in making of Adele Leamington a very colourless, characterless young lady. That she was pretty she knew, but prettiness in itself attracts only the superficial. Men who are worth knowing require something more than beauty. And Michael was not philandering﻿—he was not that kind. He wanted her for a friend at least: she had no thought that he desired amusement during his enforced stay in a very dull town.
Half-past four came and found the girl waiting. At a quarter to five she was at the door, scanning the street. At five, angry but philosophical, she had her tea and ordered the little maid of all work to clear the table.
Michael had forgotten!
===Chinese===
回家的路上，阿黛尔在一家糖果店和一家花店门口停下，取了蛋糕和鲜花，要去装点沃森太太（Mrs. Watson）家的客厅餐桌。她心里不止一点纳闷：自己对这位事务繁忙的男人究竟有什么吸引力。她有个习惯，能跳出来用旁观者的眼光打量自己，而且她清楚，凭着自我克制乃至近乎自我湮没，她已成功地把阿黛尔·利明顿塑造成了一位毫无色彩、毫无个性的小姐。自己生得漂亮，这她知道，可漂亮本身只能吸引肤浅之辈。值得结交的男人，要求的远不止美貌。何况迈克尔并不是逢场作戏的人——他不是那种人。他至少是想同她做朋友：她可不认为他是想在这沉闷小镇被迫逗留期间寻个消遣。
四点半到了，姑娘还在等着。差一刻五点，她已站到门口，张望着街面。到了五点，她又气又想得开，自顾自喝了茶，吩咐那个打杂的小女佣收拾桌子。
迈克尔忘记了！

===Original===
Of course, she made excuses for him, only to demolish them and build again. She was hurt, amused and hurt again. Going upstairs to her room, she lit the gas, took the script from her bag and tried to study the scenes that were to be shot on the following day, but all manner of distractions interposed between her receptive mind and the typewritten paper. Michael bulked largely, and the closed car, and Lawley Foss, and that waving white hand as the car drove off. Curiously enough, her speculations came back again and again to the car. It was new and its woodwork was highly polished and it moved so noiselessly.
At last she threw the manuscript down and rose, with a doubtful eye on the bed. She was not tired; the hour was nine. Chichester offered few attractions by night. There were two cinemas, and she was not in the mood for cinemas. She put on her hat and went down, calling en route at the kitchen door.
“I am going out for a quarter of an hour,” she told her landlady, who was in an approving mood.
===Chinese===
当然，她替他找理由开脱，找出来一条又推翻一条，推翻了再另找一条。她一阵委屈，一阵好笑，又是一阵委屈。上楼回到自己房里，她点亮煤气灯，从提包里取出剧本，想预习一下第二天要拍的几场戏，可是种种分心的念头不断插到她那敞开的头脑和这叠打字稿之间。迈克尔的影子占了老大一块地方，还有那辆封闭的汽车、劳利·福斯，还有汽车驶离时那只挥动的白手。说来奇怪，她的思绪一次又一次绕回那辆车上。它是崭新的，车身木料擦得锃亮，开起来悄无声息。
最后她把稿子一撂，站起身来，拿不准的眼光落在床上。她并不累；时针才指着九点。奇切斯特的夜里没什么可消遣的去处。城里有两家电影院，她又没有看电影的心思。她戴上帽子下了楼，路过厨房门口时招呼了一声。
"我出去一刻钟。"她对房东太太说，房东太太正心情宽和，满口应承。

===Original===
The house was situate in a street of small villas. It was economically illuminated, and there were dark patches where the light of the street lamps scarcely reached. In one of these a motorcar was standing﻿—she saw the bulk of it before she identified its character. She wondered if the owner knew that its tail light was extinguished. As she came up to the machine she identified the car she had seen on the previous night﻿—Foss had spoken to its occupant.
Glancing to the left, she could see nothing of its interior. The blinds on the road side were drawn, and she thought it was empty, and then﻿ ﻿…
“Pretty lady﻿—come with me!”
The voice was a whisper: she caught the flash and sparkle of a precious stone, saw the white hand on the edge of the half-closed window, and, in a fit of unreasoning terror, hurried forward.
She heard a whirr of electric starter and the purring of engines. The machine was following her, and she broke into a run. At the corner of the street she saw a man and flew toward him, as she made out the helmet of a policeman.
“What’s wrong, miss?”
As he spoke, the car flashed past, spun round the corner and was out of sight instantly.
“A man spoke to me﻿—in that car,” she said breathlessly.
The stolid constable gazed vacantly at the place where the car had been.
“He didn’t have lights,” he said stupidly. “I ought to have taken his number. Did he insult you, miss?”
She shook her head, for she was already ashamed of her fears.
“I’m nervy, officer,” she said with a smile. “I don’t think I will go any farther.”
She turned back and hurried to her lodgings. There were disadvantages in starring﻿—even on Jack Knebworth’s modest lot. It was nervous work, she thought.
She went to sleep that night and dreamt that the man in the car was Michael Brixan and he wanted her to come in to tea.
===Chinese===
这所房子坐落在一条小别墅街上。路灯装得节省，灯光照不到的地方便是一团团黑影。其中一团黑影里停着一辆汽车——她先看见那团黑影，随后才认出是什么。她还在想，车主知不知道车尾灯灭了。等走近车身，她认出了这正是头天夜里见过的那辆车——福斯跟车里的人说过话。
朝左边一瞥，车里什么也看不见。临街一侧的遮帘拉着，她以为车里是空的，接着……
"漂亮姑娘——跟我来吧！"
那声音是耳语：她瞥见一颗宝石的闪光，看见半开的车窗边搁着一只白手，登时一阵没来由的恐怖，加快脚步向前奔去。
她听见电启动器的嗡鸣和引擎的低吼。车子跟了上来，她撒腿就跑。在街角她望见一个人影，认出那是警察的头盔，便朝他飞奔过去。
"怎么了，小姐？"
说话间，那辆汽车一掠而过，拐过街角，眨眼便没了踪影。
"有个人跟我搭话——在那辆车里。"她上气不接下气地说。
那木讷的警察茫然望着汽车消失的地方。
"他没开车灯，"他傻乎乎地说，"我本该记下他的车牌号的。他非礼您了吗，小姐？"
她摇了摇头，因为这时她已经为自己的害怕感到羞愧了。
"我神经过敏，警官。"她笑了笑说，"我想我不往前走了。"
她转身匆匆回到住处。当担纲主角也有当主角的难处——哪怕是在杰克·尼布沃斯那座不起眼的摄影场里。这活儿叫人提心吊胆，她想。
那晚她睡着了，梦见汽车里的男人原来是迈克尔·布里克森，他要她进去喝茶。

===Original===
It was past midnight when Michael rang up Jack Knebworth with the news.
“Foss!” he gasped. “Good God! You don’t mean that, Brixan? Shall I come round and see you?”
“I’ll come to you,” said Michael. “There are one or two things I want to know about the man, and it will create less of a fuss than if I have to admit you to the hotel.”
Jack Knebworth rented a house on the Arundel Road, and he was waiting at the garden door to admit his visitor when Michael arrived.
Michael told the story of the discovery of the head, and felt that he might so far take the director into his confidence as to retail his visit to Sir Gregory Penne.
“That beats everything,” said Jack in a hushed tone. “Poor old Foss! You think that Penne did this? But why? You don’t cut up a man because he wants to borrow money.”
“My views have been switching round a little,” said Michael. “You remember a sheet of manuscript that was found amongst some of your script, and which I told you must have been written by the Headhunter?”
Jack nodded.
“I’m perfectly sure,” Michael went on, “and particularly after seeing the erasure in the scenario book, that Foss knew who was the author of that manuscript, and I’m equally certain that he resolved upon the desperate expedient of blackmailing the writer. If that is the case, and if Sir Gregory is the man﻿—again I am very uncertain on this point﻿—there is a good reason why he should be put out of the way. There is one person who can help us, and that is﻿—”
“Mendoza,” said Jack, and the two men’s eyes met.
===Chinese===
迈克尔打电话把消息告诉杰克·尼布沃斯时，已过午夜。
"福斯！"他倒抽一口气，"天哪！你不是当真的吧，布里克森？要我过去见你吗？"
"我到你那儿去。"迈克尔说，"关于这个人我想了解一两件事，再说，与其让我把你在旅馆里登名拜访、惹人注目，这样动静也小些。"
杰克·尼布沃斯在阿伦德尔路租了一所房子，迈克尔到时，他已等在花园门口迎接客人。
迈克尔讲了发现头颅的经过，并觉得不妨对这位导演稍稍交底，把自己造访格雷戈里·彭尼爵士的始末说了一遍。
"这可真是闻所未闻。"杰克压低嗓音说，"可怜的老福斯！你认为这是彭尼干的？可为什么呀？总不至于因为人家想借钱，就把人剁了吧。"
"我的看法有点转过来了。"迈克尔说，"你还记得在你们剧本堆里发现的那一页手稿吗？我跟你说过，那一定是猎头者写的。"
杰克点了点头。
"我完全确信，"迈克尔接着说，"尤其是在剧本本子上见到那处涂改之后——福斯知道那页手稿的作者是谁；而且我同样确信，他打定了那步险棋的主意，去敲诈执笔的人。倘若如此，倘若格雷戈里爵士就是那个人——这一点我依然很没有把握——那么就有一个充分的理由要除掉他。有一个人能帮我们的忙，那就是——"
"门多萨。"杰克说，两人的目光碰在了一起。

## XI The Mark on the Window / XI 窗上的标记

===Original===
The Thing was twittering at her, soft, birdlike noises, and she saw the flash of its white teeth in the darkness. It was not pulling, it was simply holding, one hand gripping the tendrils of the ivy up which it had climbed, the other hand firmly about her wrist. Again it twittered and pulled. She drew back, but she might as well have tried to draw back from a moving piston rod. A great, hairy leg was suddenly flung over the sill; the second hand came up and covered her face.
The sound of her scream was deadened in the hairy paw, but somebody heard it. From the ground below came a flash of fire and the deafening *tang!* of a pistol exploding. A bullet zipped and crashed amongst the ivy, striking the brickwork, and she heard the whirr of the ricochet. Instantly the great monkey released his hold and dropped down out of sight. Half swooning, she dropped upon the windowsill, incapable of movement. And then she saw a figure come out of the shadow of the laurel bush, and instantly recognized the midnight prowler. It was Michael Brixan.
“Are you hurt?” he asked in a low voice.
She could only shake her head, for speech was denied her.
“I didn’t hit him, did I?”
===Chinese===
那东西正朝她发出啾啾的叫声，轻柔如鸟鸣，她看见黑暗中它白牙一闪。它没有拉拽，只是攥着不放：一只手抓着它攀爬上来的常春藤藤蔓，另一只手牢牢箍着她的手腕。它又啾啾叫着拽了一下。她往后挣，可这跟想从运动的活塞杆底下挣脱一样徒劳。一条毛茸茸的大腿猛地跨上窗台；第二只手伸上来，捂住了她的脸。
她的尖叫闷死在毛茸茸的爪子里，但还是有人听见了。楼下的地面腾起一道火光，手枪炸响，*铛！*的一声震耳欲聋。子弹呼啸着打进常春藤，撞在砖墙上，她听见弹头弹开的呼啸。巨猿当即松手，落下去没了踪影。她半昏半醒地瘫在窗台上，动弹不得。随后她看见一条人影从月桂树的阴影里走出来，一眼认出这个午夜的巡夜人——是迈克尔·布里克森。
"伤着没有？"他低声问。
她只能摇头，一时说不出话来。
"我没打中它，是吧？"

===Original===
With an effort she found a husk of a voice in her dry throat.
“No, I don’t think so. He dropped.”
Michael had pulled an electric torch from his pocket and was searching the ground.
“No sign of blood. He was rather difficult to hit—I was afraid of hurting you, too.”
A window had been thrown up and Jack Knebworth’s voice bawled into the night.
“What’s the shooting? Is that you, Brixan?”
“It is I. Come down, and I’ll tell you all about it.”
===Chinese===
她费了半天劲，才从干涩的喉咙里挤出一线嘶哑的声音。
"没，我想没有。它自己掉下去的。"
迈克尔从口袋里掏出手电，照着地面搜寻。
"没有血迹。它不太好打——我还怕伤着你呢。"
一扇窗猛地推起，杰克·尼布沃斯的吼声冲进夜色。
"开的什么枪？是你吗，布里克森？"
"是我。下来吧，我把原委都告诉你。"

===Original===
The noise did not seem to have aroused Mr. Longvale, or, for the matter of that, any other member of the party; and when Knebworth reached the garden, he found no other audience than Mike Brixan.
In a few words Michael told him what he had seen.
“The monkey belongs to friend Penne,” he said. “I saw it this morning.”
“What do you think—that he was prowling round and saw the open window?”
Michael shook his head.
“No,” he said quietly, “he came with one intention and purpose, which was to carry off your leading lady. That sounds highly dramatic and improbable, and that is the opinion I have formed. This ape, I tell you, is nearly human.”
===Chinese===
这响动似乎没有惊醒朗维尔先生，也没惊醒班组里任何别人；尼布沃斯下到园子里时，发现听众只有迈克·布里克森一个。
迈克尔三言两语把见到的讲给他听。
"那猴子是彭尼老兄的，"他说，"我今天早上见过它。"
"你怎么看——是它四处乱逛，正好看见窗户开着？"
迈克尔摇了摇头。
"不，"他平静地说，"它是抱着一个明确的意图来的，就是劫走你的女主角。这话听着像戏文，听着难以置信，可我的判断就是这样。我告诉你，这只猿差不多就是个人。"

===Original===
“But he wouldn’t know the girl. He has never seen her.”
“He could smell her,” said Mike instantly. “She lost a pair of gloves at the Towers today, and it’s any odds that they were stolen by the noble Gregory Penne, so that he might introduce to Bhag an unfailing scent.”
“I can’t believe it; it is incredible! Though I’ll admit,” said Jack Knebworth thoughtfully, “that these big apes do some amazing things. Did you shoot him?”
“No, sir, I didn’t shoot him, but I can tell you this, that he’s an animal that’s been gunned before, or he’d have come for me, in which case he would have been now fairly dead.”
“What were you doing round here, anyway?”
“Just watching out,” said the other carelessly. “The earnest detective has so many things on his conscience that he can’t sleep like ordinary people. Speaking for myself, I never intended leaving the garden, because I expected Brer Bhag. Who is that?”
===Chinese===
"可它不认识那姑娘啊。它从没见过她。"
"它能闻出她来，"迈克当即说，"她今天在塔楼丢了一副手套，十有八九是被那位高贵的格雷戈里·彭尼偷走的，好把一帖万无一失的气味引荐给巴格。"
"我不信；这太离奇了！不过我承认，"杰克·尼布沃斯若有所思地说，"这些大猿确实干得出些惊人勾当。你打着它没有？"
"没有，先生，我没打着它，不过有一点我可以告诉你：这畜生挨过枪，否则它早就朝我扑过来了——真扑过来，它现在就已经死透了。"
"话说回来，你在这儿转悠什么？"
"放放哨而已，"对方满不在乎地说，"认真负责的侦探心里装的事太多，没法像常人那样睡觉。拿我自己说吧，我压根儿就没打算离开园子，因为我在等巴格老兄（Brer Bhag）大驾光临。那是谁？"

===Original===
The door opened, and a slim figure, wrapped in a dressing-gown, came out into the open.
“Young lady, you’re going to catch a very fine cold,” warned Knebworth. “What happened to you?”
“I don’t know.” She was feeling her wrist tenderly. “I heard something and went to the window, and then this horrible thing caught hold of me. What was it, Mr. Brixan?”
“It was nothing more alarming than a monkey,” said he with affected unconcern. “I’m sorry you were so scared. I guess the shooting worried you more?”
“You don’t guess anything of the kind. You know it didn’t. Oh, it was horrible, horrible!” She covered her face with her trembling hands.
Old Jack grunted.
“I think she’s right, too. You owe something to our friend here, young lady. Apparently he was expecting this visit and watched in the garden.”
===Chinese===
门开了，一个裹着晨衣的纤细身影走到屋外。
"小姐，您这可要染上一场好重的感冒，"尼布沃斯警告说，"您出什么事了？"
"我不知道。"她轻轻揉着手腕，"我听见动静，走到窗前，然后这个可怕的东西就抓住了我。那是什么，布里克森先生？"
"不过是只猴子罢了，没什么可怕的，"他装出若无其事的样子说，"让您受惊了。我猜枪声更吓着您了些？"
"您才不是猜呢。您明明知道枪声不吓人。哦，太可怕了，太可怕了！"她用颤抖的双手捂住脸。
老杰克哼了一声。
"我看她说得对。小姐，您欠咱们这位朋友一份情呢。看样子他早料到有这场夜访，一直守在园子里。"

===Original===
“You expected it?” she gasped.
“Mr. Knebworth has made rather more of the part I played than can be justified,” said Mike. “And if you think that this is a hero’s natural modesty, you’re mistaken. I did expect this gentleman, because he’d been seen in the fields by Mr. Longvale. And you thought you saw him yourself, didn’t you, Knebworth?”
Jack nodded.
“In fact, we all saw him,” Mike went on, “and as I didn’t like the idea of a coming star (if I may express that pious hope) being subjected to the annoyance of visiting monkeys, I sat up in the garden.”
With a sudden impulsive gesture she put out her little hand, and Michael took it.
“Thank you, Mr. Brixan,” she said. “I have been wrong about you.”
“Who isn’t?” asked Mike with an extravagant shrug.
===Chinese===
"您料到了？"她倒吸一口气。
"尼布沃斯先生把我扮的角色夸得过了头，"迈克说，"您要是以为这是英雄天生的谦虚，那可错了。我确实料到了这位先生会来，因为朗维尔先生在田里见过它。您自己也以为见过它，对吧，尼布沃斯？"
杰克点了点头。
"说起来，我们全都见过它，"迈克接着说，"我不愿意看到一颗明日之星（请容我许下这个虔诚的心愿）被半夜来访的猴子搅扰，就在园子里守了个通宵。"
她一时冲动，突然伸出小手，迈克尔握住了。
"谢谢您，布里克森先生，"她说，"我从前错看您了。"
"谁不错看我呢？"迈克夸张地一耸肩。

===Original===
She returned to her room, and this time she closed her window. Once, before she went finally to sleep, she rose and, peeping through the curtains, saw the little glowing point of the watcher’s cigar, and went back to bed comforted, to sleep as if it were only for a few minutes before Foss began knocking on the doors to waken the company.
The literary man himself was the first down. The garden was beginning to show palely in the dawn light, and he bade Michael Brixan a gruff good morning.
“Good morning to you,” said Michael. “By the way, Mr. Foss, you stayed behind at Griff Towers yesterday to see our friend Penne?”
“That’s no business of yours,” growled the man, and would have passed on, but Michael stood squarely in his path.
“There is one thing which is a business of mine, and that is to ask you why that little white disc appears on Miss Leamington’s window?”
He pointed up to the white circle that the girl had seen the night before.
===Chinese===
她回到房里，这一回关上了窗。最终入睡之前，她起来过一次，从窗帘缝里窥看，望见守夜人雪茄那一点小小的红光，便安心回床睡下——一觉仿佛只过了几分钟，福斯就开始挨个敲门叫醒全班组了。
这位舞文弄墨的先生自己第一个下了楼。园子在晨光里刚开始泛白，他没好气地跟迈克尔·布里克森道了声早安。
"您也早，"迈克尔说，"顺便问一句，福斯先生，您昨天在格里夫塔楼留下来，是去见咱们那位彭尼朋友？"
"那不关你的事，"那人咆哮道，本想径直走过去，迈克尔却端端正正拦住了他的去路。
"有一件事却关我的事，就是想问问您，利明顿小姐的窗上为什么会出现那个白色小圆片？"
他指向姑娘头天夜里见过的那个白圆片。

===Original===
“I don’t know anything about it,” said Foss with rising anger, but there was also a note of fear in his voice.
“If you don’t know, who will? Because I saw you put it there, just before it got dark last night.”
“Well, if you must know,” said the man, “it was to mark a vision boundary for the cameraman.”
That sounded a plausible excuse. Michael had seen Jack Knebworth marking out boundaries in the garden to ensure the actors being in the picture. At the first opportunity, when Knebworth appeared he questioned him on the subject.
“No, I gave no instructions to put up marks. Where is it?”
Michael showed him.
===Chinese===
"我一无所知，"福斯的火气眼看就上来，可话音里还带着一丝恐惧。
"您不知道谁知道？昨天傍晚天黑前，我亲眼看见您把它贴上去的。"
"好吧，非要知道的话，"那人说，"那是给摄影师标取景边界用的。"
这说法听着在理。迈克尔见过杰克·尼布沃斯在园子里划界，好让演员不走出画面。一等尼布沃斯露面，他就抓住机会问起这事。
"没有，我没吩咐过贴什么标记。在哪儿？"
迈克尔指给他看。

===Original===
“I wouldn’t have a mark up there, anyway, should I? Right in the middle of a window! What do you make of it?”
“I think Foss put it there with one object. The window was marked at Gregory’s request.”
“But why?” asked Knebworth, staring.
“To show Bhag Adele Leamington’s room. That’s why,” said Michael, and he was confident that his view was an accurate one.
===Chinese===
"反正我不会让人把标记贴在那儿，会吗？正正当当贴在窗户正中！你怎么看？"
"我看福斯贴它只有一个目的。那扇窗是应格雷戈里之请做的记号。"
"可是为什么？"尼布沃斯瞪着眼问。
"为了告诉巴格，阿黛尔·利明顿住哪间房。就这么回事，"迈克尔说，并且确信自己的判断错不了。

## XXIV The Marks of the Beast / XXIV 兽之印记

===Original===
“On the contrary, it is not the room,” said Michael quietly. “The room is at the end of the passage.”
The man hesitated.
“Can’t you believe me?” he asked in an almost affable tone of voice. “What a sceptical chap you are! Now come, Brixan! I don’t want to be bad friends with you. Let’s go down and have a drink and forget our past animosities. I’m feeling rotten﻿—”
“I want to see that room,” said Michael.
“I haven’t the key.”
“Then get it,” said Michael sharply.
Eventually the baronet found a passkey in his pocket, and, with every sign of reluctance, he opened the door.
“She went away in a bit of a hurry,” he said. “She was taken so ill that I had to get rid of her.”
“If she left here because she was ill she went into an institution of some kind, the name of which you will be able to give me,” said Michael, as he turned on the light.
One glance at the room told him that the story of her hasty departure may have been accurate. But that the circumstances were normal, the appearance of the room denied. The bed was in confusion; there was blood on the pillow, and a dark brown stain on the wall. A chair was broken; the carpet had odd and curious stains, one like the print of a bare foot. On a sheet was an indubitable hand-print, but such a hand as no human being had ever possessed.
“The mark of the beast,” said Michael, pointing. “That’s Bhag!”
===Chinese===
"恰恰相反，不是这间。"迈克尔不动声色地说，"那间房在走廊尽头。"
那人迟疑了。
"你就不能信我一回？"他的语气近乎讨好，"你这人疑心也太重了！得了，布里克森！我不想跟你交恶。走，下楼喝一杯，把咱们过去的过节都忘了。我现在难受得很——"
"我要看那间房。"迈克尔说。
"钥匙不在我手里。"
"那就去取。"迈克尔厉声道。
这位从男爵最终还是在口袋里摸出一把万能钥匙，满脸老大不情愿地开了门。
"她走得有点仓促，"他说，"她病得太厉害，我只好打发她走。"
"她若是因病离开这里，就一定进了什么机构，那机构的名字你应该说得出。"迈克尔说着，拧亮了灯。
只消朝屋里望上一眼，他便知道她仓促离去之说或许不假。可要说情形一切正常，屋内的景象却断然否认。床上乱作一团；枕头上留着血迹，墙上有一块深褐色的污渍。一把椅子断了腿；地毯上有些古怪的斑渍，其中一处像是一个赤脚的脚印。床单上有一枚确凿无疑的手印，可那样的手，世人从未生过。
"兽之印记，"迈克尔指着说，"这是巴格留下的！"

===Original===
Again the baronet licked his lips.
“There was a bit of a fight here,” he said. “The man came up and pretended to identify the servant as his wife﻿—”
“What happened to him?”
There was no reply.
“What happened to him?” asked Michael with ominous patience.
“I let him go, and let him take the woman with him. It was easier﻿—”
With a sudden exclamation, Michael stooped and picked up from behind the bed a bright steel object. It was the half of a sword, snapped clean in the middle, and unstained. He looked along the blade, and presently found the slightest indent. Picking up the chair, he examined the leg and found two deeper dents in one of the legs.
“I’ll reconstruct the scene. You and your Bhag caught the man after he had got into this room. The chair was broken in the struggle, probably by Bhag, who used the chair. The man escaped from the room, ran downstairs into the library and got the sword from the wall, then came up after you. That’s when the real fighting started. I guess some of this blood is yours, Penne.”
“Some of it!” snarled the other. “All of it, damn him!”
There was a long silence.
“Did the woman leave this room﻿—alive?”
“I believe so,” said the other sullenly.
“Did her husband leave your library﻿—alive?”
“You’d better find that out. So far as I know﻿—I was unconscious for half an hour. Bhag can use a sword﻿—”
===Chinese===
从男爵又舔了舔嘴唇。
"这儿是打过一场，"他说，"那人上来，硬把那女仆认作他的老婆——"
"他后来怎么了？"
无人应声。
"他后来怎么了？"迈克尔又问，耐心里透着不祥。
"我放他走了，也让他把那女人带走了。这样省事——"
迈克尔忽然低呼一声，俯身从床后捡起一件亮闪闪的钢器。那是半截剑，从正中干脆利落地折断，不见血迹。他顺着剑刃细看，很快发现一处极浅的凹痕。他又拾起那把椅子，细察椅腿，在其中一条腿上找到两处更深的凹坑。
"让我来复原现场。那男人进了这间房之后，你和你养的巴格逮住了他。椅子是在扭打中断掉的，多半是巴格拿它当的家伙。那人逃出房间，跑下楼进了书房，从墙上摘下剑，再上来找你。真正的恶斗就是那时候开的头。我猜这些血里有一部分是你的，彭尼。"
"一部分！"对方咬牙切齿，"全是我的，那该死的家伙！"
接下来是长久的沉默。
"那女人离开这间房时——还活着吗？"
"我想是活着。"对方阴沉着脸说。
"她丈夫离开你的书房时——还活着吗？"
"这你最好自己去查。就我所知——我昏迷了半个钟头。巴格会使剑——"

===Original===
Michael did not leave the house till he had searched it from attic to basement. He had every servant assembled and began his interrogation. Each of them except one spoke Dutch, but none spoke the language to such purpose that they made him any wiser than he had been.
Going back to the library, he put on all the lights.
“I’ll see Bhag,” he said.
“He’s out, I tell you. If you don’t believe me﻿—” Penne went to the desk and turned the switch. The door opened and nothing came out.
A moment’s hesitation and Michael had penetrated into the den, a revolver in one hand, his lamp in another. The two rooms were scrupulously clean, though a strange animal smell pervaded everything. There was a small bed, with sheets and blankets and feather pillow, where the beast slept; a small larder, full of nuts; a running water tap (he found afterwards that, in spite of his cleverness, Bhag was incapable of turning on or off a faucet); a deep, well-worn settee, where the dumb servitor took his rest; and three cricket balls, which were apparently the playthings of this hideous animal.
Bhag’s method of entering and leaving the house was now apparent. His exit was a square opening in the wall, with neither window nor curtain, which was situated about seven feet from the ground; and two projecting steel rungs, set at intervals between the window and the floor, made a sort of ladder. Michael found corresponding rungs on the garden side of the wall.
There was no sign of blood, no evidence that Bhag had taken any part in the terrible scene which must have been enacted the night before.
===Chinese===
迈克尔把这所宅子从阁楼搜到地窖，方才离开。他把仆役统统召集起来，逐一讯问。除一人之外，他们个个讲荷兰话，却没有一个人讲出个所以然来，没能让他比先前多明白半分。
回到书房，他把灯全部拧亮。
"我要见巴格。"他说。
"他出去了，我跟你说。你要是不信——"彭尼走到书桌旁，扳动开关。门开了，什么也没有出来。
迟疑了片刻，迈克尔便闯进了那间兽窟，一手握着左轮手枪，一手提着灯。两间屋子都干净得一尘不染，只是处处弥漫着一股怪异的兽腥气。有一张小床，铺着床单、毯子和羽绒枕头，是那头畜生睡觉的地方；有一只小食橱，装满了坚果；有一只常流水的龙头（他后来才发现，巴格尽管机灵，却不会自己拧开或关上水龙头）；一张坐得凹陷下去的深长沙发，是那哑巴仆役歇息之处；还有三只板球，显见得是这头可怕畜生的玩物。
巴格进出宅子的路数如今一目了然。它的出口是墙上一方洞口，既无窗扇也无窗帘，离地约七英尺高；窗与地面之间隔距钉着两枚突出的钢蹬，权当梯子。迈克尔在墙的花园一侧找到了对应的钢蹬。
这里没有血迹，没有任何迹象表明巴格参与过昨夜必定上演过的那可怕的一幕。

===Original===
Going back to the library, he made a diligent search, but found nothing until he went into the little drawing-room where he had hidden the night before. Here on the windowsill he found traces enough. The mark of a bare foot, and another which suggested that a heavy body had been dragged through the window.
By this time his chauffeur, who, after dropping him at Griff Towers, went on to Chichester, had returned with the two police officers, and they assisted him in a further search of the grounds. The trail of the fugitive was easy to follow: there were bloodstains across the gravel, broken plants in a circular flowerbed, the soft loam of which had received the impression of those small bare feet. In the vegetable field the trail was lost.
“The question is, who carried whom?” said Inspector Lyle, after Michael, in a few words, had told him all that he had learnt at the Towers. “It looks to me as if these people were killed in the house and their bodies carried away by Bhag. There’s no trace of blood in his room, which means no more than that in all probability he hasn’t been there since the killing,” said Inspector Lyle. “If we find the monkey we’ll solve this little mystery. Penne is the Headhunter, of course,” the Inspector went on. “I had a talk with him the other day, and there’s something fanatical about the man.”
“I am not so sure,” said Michael slowly, “that you’re right. Perhaps my ideas are just a little bizarre; but if Sir Gregory Penne is the actual murderer, I shall be a very surprised man. I admit,” he confessed, “that the absence of any footprints in Bhag’s quarters staggered me, and probably your theory is correct. There is nothing to be done but to keep the house under observation until I communicate with headquarters.”
At this moment the second detective, who had been searching the field to its farthermost boundary, came back to say that he had picked up the trail again near the postern gate, which was open. They hurried across the field and found proof of his discovery. There was a trail both inside and outside the gate. Near the postern was a big heap of leaves, which had been left by the gardener to rot, and on this they found the impression of a body, as though whoever was the carrier had put his burden down for a little while to rest. In the field beyond the gate, however, the trail was definitely lost.
===Chinese===
回到书房，他又细细搜了一遍，却一无所获，直到走进头天夜里他藏身的那间小客厅。就在这里的窗台上，他找到了足够的痕迹。一个赤脚的脚印，还有一道痕迹，表明有一具沉重的身躯被人从窗户拖了出去。
这时他的司机——先前把他送到格里夫塔楼后便去了奇切斯特——已经带着两名警官回来了，他们协助他进一步搜查园地。逃亡者的踪迹不难追循：砾石路上血迹斑斑，圆形花坛里的花木折损，松软的壤土上印着那双小小的赤脚印。到了菜地里，踪迹便断了。
"问题是，谁背着谁？"莱尔探长（Inspector Lyle）听迈克尔三言两语讲完他在塔楼的所见所闻后，说道，"依我看，这两个人是在宅子里遇害的，尸体由巴格运走的。它房里没有血迹，这只能说明，十有八九它行凶之后没再回去过。"莱尔探长说，"只要找到那只猿猴，这桩小小的疑案便可告破。彭尼就是猎头者，这没什么可说的。"探长接着说，"前几天我同他谈过一回，这人有股狂热劲儿。"
"你说得对不对，我可没那么有把握。"迈克尔慢吞吞地说，"也许我的想法有点古怪；不过倘若格雷戈里·彭尼爵士真是凶手，我会大吃一惊。我承认，"他坦白道，"巴格住处没有脚印这一点让我心里发慌，或许你的推断是对的。眼下别无他法，只能派人监视这座宅子，等我跟总部联系再说。"
正在此时，第二名警探一直搜到田地最远的边界，回来说他在后门附近重新找到了踪迹——那扇后门开着。他们急忙穿过田野，证实了他的发现。门的里外都有拖痕。后门旁有一大堆树叶，是园丁堆在那里任其腐烂的，树叶堆上压着一个身形的印子，仿佛搬运的人曾把负担当下来歇了片刻。然而在门外那片田野上，踪迹彻底消失了。

## VI The Master of Griff / VI 格里夫的主人

===Original===
Adele did not speak to him for a long time. Resentment that he should force his company upon her, and nervousness at the coming ordeal—a nervousness which became sheer panic as they grew nearer and nearer to their destination—made conversation impossible.
“I see your Mr. Lawley Foss is with us,” said Michael, glancing over his shoulder, and by way of making conversation.
“He always goes on location,” she said shortly. “A story has sometimes to be amended while it’s being shot.”
“Where are we going now?” he asked.
“Griff Towers first,” she replied. She found it difficult to be uncivil to anybody. “It is a big place owned by Sir Gregory Penne.”
===Chinese===
阿黛尔好半天没跟他说话。她恼恨他硬要凑上来同行，又为即将到来的考验紧张——车子越开越近目的地，这紧张干脆变成了纯粹的恐慌——两人实在无从交谈。
"我看你们那位劳利·福斯先生也同行呢，"迈克尔回头瞥了一眼，没话找话说。
"出外景他回回都去，"她简短地说，"故事有时拍到一半还得改。"
"我们现在去哪儿？"他问。
"先去格里夫塔楼，"她答道。她对谁都难以失礼。"那是格雷戈里·彭尼爵士（Sir Gregory Penne）的一处大宅子。"

===Original===
“But I thought we were going to the Dower House?”
She looked at him with a little frown.
“Why did you ask if you knew?” she demanded, almost in a tone of asperity.
“Because I like to hear you speak,” said the young man calmly. “Sir Gregory Penne? I seem to know the name.”
She did not answer.
===Chinese===
"可我以为我们是去遗孀宅？"
她微微皱眉看了他一眼。
"既然知道，何必还问？"她质问道，语气近乎刻薄。
"因为我爱听你说话，"年轻人心平气和地说，"格雷戈里·彭尼爵士？这名字我好像听说过。"
她没有答话。

===Original===
“He was in Borneo for many years, wasn’t he?”
“He’s hateful,” she said vehemently. “I detest him!”
She did not explain the cause of her detestation, and Michael thought it discreet not to press the question, but presently she relieved him of responsibility.
“I’ve been to his house twice. He has a very fine garden, which Mr. Knebworth has used before—of course, I only went as an extra and was very much in the background. I wish I had been more so. He has queer ideas about women, and especially actresses—not that I’m an actress,” she added hastily, “but I mean people who play for a living. Thank heaven there’s only one scene to be shot at Griff, and perhaps he will not be at home, but that’s unlikely. He’s always there when I go.”
Michael glanced at her out of the corner of his eye. His first impression of her beauty was more than confirmed. There was a certain wistfulness in her face which was very appealing; an honesty in the dark eyes that told him all he wanted to know about her attitude toward the admiration of the unknown Sir Gregory.
“It’s queer how all baronets are villains in stories,” he said, “and queerer still that most of the baronets I’ve known have been men of singular morals. I’m bothering you, being here, aren’t I?” he asked, dropping his tone of banter.
===Chinese===
"他在婆罗洲待了好些年，对吧？"
"他讨厌死了，"她激烈地说，"我看他就恶心！"
她没有解释厌恶的缘由，迈克尔觉得不便追问，可没过多久她自己卸下了他的顾虑。
"我去过他家两次。他有一座非常好的花园，尼布沃斯先生以前用过——当然，我只是作为临时演员去的，完全是个背景。真希望当时藏得更深些才好。他对女人有一套怪念头，对女演员尤其如此——倒不是说我是演员，"她急忙补了一句，"我是说靠演戏吃饭的人。谢天谢地，在格里夫只拍一场戏，也许他不在家，不过这指望不大。我去的时候他回回都在。"
迈克尔用眼角打量她。他对她美貌的第一印象非但得到印证，更有过之。她脸上有一种令人心动的怅惘；那双黑眼睛里的诚实，把他对她如何看待素未谋面的彭尼爵士的追求所想了解的一切，全都说尽了。
"说来奇怪，小说里的从男爵个个都是恶棍，"他说，"更奇怪的是，我认识的大多数从男爵偏偏都是品行格外端正的人。我在这儿碍着你了，是吧？"他收起打趣的口吻问道。

===Original===
She looked round at him.
“You are a little,” she said frankly. “You see, Mr. Brixan, this is my big chance. It’s a chance that really never comes to an extra except in stories, and I’m frightened to death of what is going to happen. You make me nervous, but what makes me more panic-stricken is that the first scene is to be shot at Griff. I hate it, I hate it!” she said almost savagely. “That big, hard-looking house, with its hideous stuffed tigers and its awful looking swords—”
“Swords?” he asked quickly. “What do you mean?”
“The walls are covered with them—Eastern swords. They make me shiver to see them. But Sir Gregory takes a delight in them: he told Mr. Knebworth, the last time we were there, that the swords were as sharp now as they were when they came from the hands of their makers, and some of them were three hundred years old. He’s an extraordinary man: he can cut an apple in half on your hand and never so much as scratch you. That is one of his favourite stunts—do you know what ‘stunt’ means?”
“I seem to have heard the expression,” said Michael absently.
===Chinese===
她回过头看他。
"是有一点，"她坦率地说，"您瞧，布里克森先生，这是我一辈子的大机会。这种机会除了小说里，现实中从来轮不到临时演员，我对接下来要发生的事怕得要死。您让我紧张，可更让我胆战心惊的是头一场戏偏偏要在格里夫拍。我恨那地方，恨死了！"她几乎是恶狠狠地说，"那座又大又冷硬的房子，里面摆着丑陋的老虎标本，还有那些看着就吓人的刀剑——"
"刀剑？"他赶忙问，"什么意思？"
"墙上挂满了——都是东方的刀剑。看一眼我就发抖。可格雷戈里爵士却乐在其中：上回我们在那儿，他告诉尼布沃斯先生，这些剑如今还跟当年出了匠人之手时一样锋利，有几把已经三百年了。他是个非同寻常的人：能把苹果放在你手背上劈成两半，连一点皮都划不着你。那是他最得意的绝技之一——你知道'绝技'（stunt）这个词是什么意思吗？"
"这个词我好像听说过，"迈克尔心不在焉地说。

===Original===
“There is the house,” she pointed. “Ugh! It makes me shiver.”
Griff Towers was one of those bleak looking buildings that it had been the delight of the early Victorian architects to erect. Its one grey tower, placed on the left wing, gave it a lopsided appearance, but even this distortion did not distract attention from its rectangular unloveliness. The place seemed all the more bare, since the walls were innocent of greenery, and it stood starkly in the midst of a yellow expanse of gravel.
“Looks almost like a barracks,” said Michael, “with a parade ground in front!”
They passed through the lodge gates, and the charabanc stopped halfway up the drive. The gardens apparently were in the rear of the building, and certainly there was nothing that would attract the most careless of directors in its uninteresting façade.
Michael got down from his seat and found Jack Knebworth already superintending the unloading of a camera and reflectors. Behind the charabanc came the big dynamo lorry, with three sun arcs that were to enhance the value of daylight.
===Chinese===
"那就是那宅子了，"她指了指，"啧！看得我直打哆嗦。"
格里夫塔楼是那种阴森森的房子，早年的维多利亚建筑师最爱盖这种玩意儿。它仅有的一座灰色塔楼坐落在左侧翼房上，使整座建筑显得歪斜失衡，可就连这份失衡也分毫转移不了它那四四方方的丑陋。四壁不见一点绿意，宅子赤裸裸地矗立在一片黄砂砾中间，愈发显得荒秃。
"简直像座兵营，"迈克尔说，"前头还带个操场！"
游览车穿过门楼驶进庄园大门，在车道半途停下。园子显然在宅子背后，这正面门脸平淡无趣，再马虎的导演也挑不出可取之处。
迈克尔下了车，见杰克·尼布沃斯已经在指挥卸运摄影机和反光板。游览车后面跟着那辆大发电车，载着三盏用来给日光添彩的太阳弧光灯。

===Original===
“Oh, you’re here, are you?” growled Jack. “Now you’ll oblige me, Mr. Brixan, by not getting in the way? I’ve got a hard morning’s work ahead of me.”
“I want you to take me on as a—what is the word?—extra,” said Michael.
The old man frowned at him.
“Say, what’s the great idea?” he asked suspiciously.
“I have an excellent reason, and I promise you that nothing I do will in any way embarrass you. The truth is, Mr. Knebworth, I want to be around for the remainder of the day, and I need an excuse.”
Jack Knebworth bit his lip, scratched his long chin, scowled, and then:
“All right,” he said gruffly. “Maybe you’ll come in handy, though I’ll have quite enough bother directing one amateur, and if you get into the pictures on this trip you’re going to be lucky!”
===Chinese===
"哦，你也来了？"杰克咕哝道，"那就劳您大驾别碍事，布里克森先生？我这一上午的活儿可够呛。"
"我想让你雇我当一名——那词儿怎么说来着？——临时演员，"迈克尔说。
老头朝他皱起眉头。
"喂，你这是打的什么算盘？"他狐疑地问。
"我有充足的理由，而且我向你保证，我做的事绝不会让你为难。实情是，尼布沃斯先生，今天剩下的时间我都想待在这儿，我需要个借口。"
杰克·尼布沃斯咬了咬嘴唇，挠了挠长下巴，沉着脸，然后说：
"行吧，"他粗声说，"没准儿你还用得上，不过带一个业余的已经够我头疼的了，这趟你要是还能混进画面，那算你走运！"

===Original===
There was a man of the party, a tall young man whose hair was brushed back from his forehead, and was so tidy and well arranged that it seemed as if it had originally been stuck by glue and varnished over. A tall, somewhat good-looking boy, who had sat on Adele’s left throughout the journey and had not spoken once, he raised his eyebrows at the appearance of Michael, and, strolling across to the harassed Knebworth, his hands in his pockets, he asked with a hurt air:
“I say, Mr. Knebworth, who is this johnny?”
“Which johnny?” growled old Jack. “You mean Brixan? He’s an extra.”
“Oh, an extra, is he?” said the young man. “I say, it’s pretty desperately awful when extras hobnob with principals! And this Leamington girl—she’s simply going to mess up the pictures, she is, by Jove!”
“Is she, by Jove?” snarled Knebworth. “Now see here, Mr. Connolly, I ain’t so much in love with your work that I’m willing to admit in advance that even an extra is going to mess up this picture.”
“I’ve never played opposite to an extra in my life, dash it all!”
===Chinese===
队伍里有个人，一个高个子青年，头发从额前往后梳，梳理得整齐服帖至极，仿佛是先用胶水粘住又上了一层清漆。这个高挑、模样还算英俊的小伙子一路坐在阿黛尔左边，一句话也没说过；迈克尔一露面，他扬起了眉毛，双手插兜，踱到焦头烂额的尼布沃斯跟前，一脸委屈地问：
"我说，尼布沃斯先生，这位老兄是何方神圣？"
"哪个老兄？"老杰克咕哝道，"你是说布里克森？他是临时演员。"
"哦，临时演员，是吗？"青年说，"我说，临时演员跟主角混在一起，这可真是糟透了！还有那个利明顿丫头——她准会把整部片子搅黄，一定的，老天爷作证！"
"老天爷作证，是吗？"尼布沃斯怒道，"你给我听着，康诺利先生（Mr. Connolly），我还没爱你的表演爱到那份上，肯事先承认连个临时演员都会毁掉这部片子。"
"我这辈子还没跟临时演员搭过戏呢，见鬼！"

===Original===
“Then you must have felt lonely,” grunted Jack, busy with his unpacking.
“Now, Mendoza is an artiste—” began the youthful leading man, and Jack Knebworth straightened his back.
“Get over there till you’re wanted, you!” he roared. “When I need advice from pretty boys, I’ll come to you—see? For the moment you’re *de trop*, which is a French expression meaning that you’re standing on ground there’s a better use for.”
The disgruntled Reggie Connolly strolled away with a shrug of his thin shoulders, which indicated not only his conviction that the picture would fail, but that the responsibility was everywhere but under his hat.
From the big doorway of Griff Towers, Sir Gregory Penne was watching the assembly of the company. He was a thickset man, and the sun of Borneo and an unrestricted appetite had dyed his skin a colour which was between purple and brown. His face was covered with innumerable ridges, his eyes looked forth upon the world through two narrow slits. The rounded feminine chin seemed to be the only part of his face that sunshine and stronger stimulants had left in its natural condition.
Michael watched him as he strolled down the slope to where they were standing, guessing his identity. He wore a golf suit of a loud check in which red predominated, and a big cap of the same material was pulled down over his eyes. Taking the stub of a cigar from his teeth, with a quick and characteristic gesture he wiped his scanty moustache on his knuckles.
===Chinese===
"那你一定很孤独，"杰克咕哝一声，只顾拆箱。
"要知道，门多萨是位艺术家——"年轻的男主角刚开口，杰克·尼布沃斯就直起了腰。
"滚到那边等着叫你！"他吼道，"等我要听小白脸的高见，自会去找你——明白？眼下你*多余*（de trop），这是句法国话，意思是你站着的这块地方另有更好的用处。"
满腹牢骚的雷吉·康诺利（Reggie Connolly）耸了耸瘦削的肩膀踱开了，这一耸肩表明：他不仅确信这部片子必败无疑，而且认定责任人人有份，唯独没藏在他帽子底下。
格雷戈里·彭尼爵士正站在格里夫塔楼的大门口，望着这群人集结。他是个矮壮汉子，婆罗洲的烈日加上不加节制的口腹之欲，把他的皮肤染成了一种介于紫色和棕色之间的颜色。他脸上布满数不清的横肉棱褶，两眼透过两道细缝打量世界。那张圆润得近乎女性的下巴，似乎是他脸上唯一未被阳光和更烈的刺激物改了本色的部位。
迈克尔望着他沿坡道踱下来，朝他们站的地方走来，一面猜着他的身份。他穿一套花哨格子呢的高尔夫球服，红色占了主调，一顶同样料子的大帽子压到眼上。他把雪茄烟蒂从牙间取下，用一个急促而特有的手势，用指节抹了抹他那两撇稀疏的胡子。

===Original===
“Good morning, Knebworth,” he called.
His voice was harsh and cruel; a voice that had never been mellowed by laughter or made soft by the tendernesses of humanity.
“Good morning, Sir Gregory.”
Old Knebworth disentangled himself from his company.
“Sorry I’m late.”
“Don’t apologize,” said the other. “Only I thought you were going to shoot earlier. Brought my little girl, eh?”
“Your little girl?” Jack looked at him, frankly nonplussed. “You mean Mendoza? No, she’s not coming.”
“I don’t mean Mendoza, if that’s the dark girl. Never mind: I was only joking.”
===Chinese===
"早安，尼布沃斯，"他喊道。
他的嗓音刺耳而冷酷；一副从未被笑声温润、也从未被人间温情软化过的嗓子。
"早安，格雷戈里爵士。"
老尼布沃斯从人堆里脱身出来。
"抱歉来晚了。"
"用不着道歉，"对方说，"只是我以为你们会早些开拍。把我的小姑娘带来了，嗯？"
"您的小姑娘？"杰克看着他，一副货真价实的困惑相，"您是说门多萨？不，她不来。"
"我不是说门多萨，如果那个黑头发姑娘就是的话。不碍事：我不过开个玩笑。"

===Original===
Who the blazes was his little girl, thought Jack, who was ignorant of two unhappy experiences which an unconsidered extra girl had had on previous visits. The mystery, however, was soon cleared up, for the baronet walked slowly to where Adele Leamington was making a pretence of studying her script.
“Good morning, little lady,” he said, lifting his cap an eighth of an inch from his head.
“Good morning, Sir Gregory,” she said coldly.
“You didn’t keep your promise.” He shook his head waggishly. “Oh, woman, woman!”
“I don’t remember having made a promise,” said the girl quietly. “You asked me to come to dinner with you, and I told you that that was impossible.”
“I promised to send my car for you. Don’t say it was too far away. Never mind, never mind.” And, to Michael’s wrath, he squeezed the girl’s arm in a manner which was intended to be paternal, but which filled the girl with indignant loathing.
She wrenched her arm free, and, turning her back upon her tormentor, almost flew to Jack Knebworth with an incoherent demand for information on the reading of a line which was perfectly simple.
Old Jack was no fool. He watched the play from under his eyelids, recognizing all the symptoms.
“This is the last time we shall shoot at Griff Towers,” he told himself.
For Jack Knebworth was something of a stickler on behaviour, and had views on women which were diametrically opposite to those held by Sir Gregory Penne.
===Chinese===
他嘴里的小姑娘到底是哪路神仙？杰克心里嘀咕——他并不知道，那个无人在意的临时演员姑娘先前两次登门时有过怎样不快的遭遇。不过谜底很快揭开：从男爵缓缓走向阿黛尔·利明顿装模作样研读剧本的地方。
"早安，小女士，"他说着把帽子从头上抬起八分之一英寸。
"早安，格雷戈里爵士，"她冷冷地说。
"你可没守约。"他滑头滑脑地摇着头，"唉，女人啊，女人！"
"我不记得许过什么约，"姑娘平静地说，"您请我同您吃饭，我告诉过您那不可能。"
"我说过要派车去接你的。可别说是路太远。罢了，罢了。"接着，让迈克尔怒火中烧的是，他捏了一把姑娘的胳膊，那样子本想装出父辈的慈爱，却只让姑娘满心憎恶。
她猛地抽回胳膊，掉头背对这个纠缠者，几乎是小跑着奔向杰克·尼布沃斯，语无伦次地请教一句再简单不过的台词该怎么念。
老杰克不是傻子。他垂着眼皮把这出戏看在眼里，种种征候一望便知。
"这是我们最后一回在格里夫塔楼拍片了，"他暗自说。
因为杰克·尼布沃斯在品行上也是个一丝不苟的人，他对女人的看法，与格雷戈里·彭尼爵士所持的那一套恰恰背道而驰。

## XIX The Midnight Visit / XIX 午夜潜入

===Original===
Michael Brixan had had sent down to him from town a heavy suitcase, which contained precious little clothing. He was busy with its contents for half an hour, when the boots of the hotel announced the arrival of the motorcycle that had been hired for him.
With a canvas bag strapped to his back, he mounted the machine, and was soon clear of the town, swerving through the twisting lanes of Sussex until he arrived at the Dower House, behind which he concealed his machine.
It was eleven o’clock when he crossed the fields to the postern gate, on the alert all the time for the soft-footed Bhag. The postern was closed and locked—a contingency for which he was prepared. Unstrapping his bag, he took therefrom a bundle of rods, and screwed three together. To the top he fastened a big, blunt hook, and, replacing the remainder of the rods, he lifted the hook till it rested on the top of the high wall, tested its stability, and in a few seconds had climbed his “ladder” and had jumped to the other side.
He followed the path that he had taken before, keeping close to the bushes, and all the time watching left and right for Penne’s monstrous servant. As he came to the end of the hedge, the hall door opened and two men came out. One was Penne, and for a moment he did not recognize the tall man by his side, until he heard his voice. Mr. Sampson Longvale!
===Chinese===
迈克尔·布里克森让人从伦敦送下来一只沉重的手提箱，里面衣物少得可怜。他跟箱中之物忙了半个钟头，旅馆的杂役来通报：替他雇的摩托车到了。
他背上捆好帆布包，跨上摩托，很快便出了城，在萨塞克斯弯弯曲曲的小巷间左拐右绕，一直骑到遗孀宅，把车藏在了宅子后面。
他穿过田野走向那道便门时，已是十一点，一路上提防着脚步无声的巴格。便门关着，还上了锁——这一手他早有准备。他解下帆布包，取出一捆金属杆，把三根拧接起来，顶端装上一只粗钝的大挂钩，把剩下的杆子收好，然后将挂钩高高举起搭上高墙墙头，试了试稳不稳，几秒钟内便顺着这架"梯子"爬上去，翻身跳到了墙那一边。
他沿着上回走过的路线前进，紧贴灌木，左右提防着彭尼那骇人的仆役。走到树篱尽头时，宅门开了，走出两个人。一个是彭尼，旁边那个高个子他一时没认出来，直到听见那声音——桑普森·朗维尔先生！

===Original===
“I think she will be all right. The wounds are very peculiar. It looks almost as if she had been scratched by some huge claw,” said Longvale. “I hope I have been of assistance, Sir Gregory, though, as I told you, it is nearly fifty years since I engaged in medical work.”
So old Longvale had been a doctor! Somehow this news did not surprise Michael. There was something in the old man’s benevolence of countenance and easy manner which would have suggested a training in that profession, to one less analytical than Michael Brixan.
“My car will take you down,” he heard Sir Gregory say.
“No, no, thank you; I will walk. It is not very far. Good night, Sir Gregory.”
The baronet growled a good night and went back into the dimly-lit hall, and Michael heard the rattle of chains as the door was fastened.
===Chinese===
"我想她会没事的。伤口很特别，看着几乎像被什么巨大的爪子抓过，"朗维尔说，"但愿我帮上了忙，格雷戈里爵士，不过正如我所说，我行医已是将近五十年前的事了。"
原来老朗维尔当过医生！不知怎的，这消息并不让迈克尔意外。老人那副慈祥的面容、从容的举止里自有一种东西，哪怕分析力不如迈克尔·布里克森的人，也会想到他受过那一行的训练。
"我的车送您回去，"他听见格雷戈里爵士说。
"不，不，谢谢，我走着回去。不算很远。晚安，格雷戈里爵士。"
从男爵咕哝了一声晚安，回到灯光昏暗的门厅里，迈克尔听见门上锁链哗啦作响，把门锁牢了。

===Original===
There was no time to be lost. Almost before Mr. Sampson Longvale had disappeared into the darkness, Michael had opened his canvas bag and had screwed on three more links to his ladder. From each rod projected a short, light, steel bracket. It was the type of hook-ladder that firemen use, and Michael had employed this method of gaining entrance to a forbidden house many times in his chequered career.
He judged the distance accurately, for when he lifted the rod and dropped the hook upon the sill of the little window, the ladder hung only a few inches short of the ground. With a tug to test the hook, he went up hand over hand, and in a few seconds was prying at the window sash. It needed little opening, for the catch was of elementary simplicity, and in another instant he was standing on the step of a dark and narrow stairway.
He had provided himself with an electric torch, and he flashed a beam up and down. Below, he saw a small door which apparently led into the hall, and, by an effort of memory, he remembered that in the corner of the hall he had seen a curtain hanging, without attaching any importance to the fact. Going down, he tried the door and found it locked. Putting down his lantern, he took out a leather case of tools and began to manipulate the lock. In an incredibly short space of time the key turned. When he had assured himself that the door would open, he was satisfied. For the moment his work lay upstairs, and he climbed the steps again, coming to a narrow landing, but no door.
A second, a third and a fourth flight brought him, as near as he could guess, to the top of the tower, and here he found a narrow exit. Listening, after a while he heard somebody moving about the room, and by the sound they made, he supposed they wore slippers. Presently a door closed with a thud, and he tried the handle of the wicket. It was unlocked, and he opened it gently a fraction of an inch at a time, until he secured a view of the greater part of the chamber.
===Chinese===
一刻也不能耽搁。桑普森·朗维尔先生的身影几乎还没没入黑暗，迈克尔已打开帆布包，往梯子上又拧接了三节。每根杆上都伸出一个轻巧的短钢支架。这是消防员用的那种钩梯，在他跌宕起伏的生涯里，迈克尔靠这套办法溜进禁宅已不止一次。
距离他估得极准：举起杆子把挂钩搭上小窗的窗台时，梯子离地不过几英寸。他往下一坠试了试钩子，随即双手交替攀了上去，几秒钟后已在撬那窗扇。窗子几乎不用怎么开——窗闩简单得可笑——转眼间他已站在一道又黑又窄的楼梯的踏步上。
他备了手电，朝上朝下各照了一圈。下面有一扇小门，显然通门厅；他努力回忆，记起门厅一角曾挂着一块帘子，当时没多想。下去一试，门锁着。他放下灯，取出皮工具包，动手对付那把锁。没到什么工夫，锁芯一转就开了。确信门打得开后，他就放了心。眼下的活儿在楼上，于是他又爬上台阶，来到一处狭窄的楼梯口——却没有门。
第二段、第三段、第四段楼梯爬完，他估计已到了塔顶，这里有一道窄窄的出口。他侧耳听了一阵，听见屋里有人在走动，听那动静，穿的是拖鞋。不久一扇门砰地关上，他试了试小门的把手。没锁，他便一次一丝丝地轻轻推开，直到能望见屋里的大半。

===Original===
It was a small, lofty room, unfurnished with the exception of a low bed in one corner, on which a woman lay. Her back was toward him, fortunately; but the black hair and the ivory yellow of the bare arm that lay on the coverlet told him that she was not European.
Presently she turned and he saw her face, recognizing her immediately as the woman whose face he had seen in the picture. She was pretty in her wild way, and young. Her eyes were closed, and presently she began crying softly in her sleep.
Michael was halfway in the room when he saw the handle of the other door turn, and, quick as a flash, stepped back into the darkness of the landing.
It was Bhag, in his old blue overall, a tray of food in his great hands. He reached out his foot and pulled the table toward him, placing the viands by the side of the bed. The girl opened her eyes and sank back with a little cry of disgust; and Bhag, who was evidently used to these demonstrations of her loathing, shuffled out of the room.
Again Michael pushed the door and crossed the room, unnoticed by the girl, looking out into the passage—not six feet away from him, Bhag was squatting, glaring in his direction.
Michael closed the door quickly and flew back to the secret staircase, pulling the door behind him. He felt for a key, but there was none, and, without wasting another second, he ran down the stairs. The one thing he wished to avoid was an encounter which would betray his presence in the house.
===Chinese===
那是一间高而小的房间，除了角落里一张矮床别无陈设，床上躺着一个女人。所幸她背朝着他；但那乌黑的头发和搭在被面上的象牙黄裸臂告诉他，她不是欧洲人。
过了一会儿她翻过身，他看清了那张脸——立刻认出正是影片画面里见过的那个女人。她有种野性的俏丽，还年轻。眼睛闭着，不多时，她在睡梦中低声啜泣起来。
迈克尔半个人已在屋里，忽见另一扇门的把手转动，他疾如闪电地退回楼梯口的黑暗里。
是巴格，穿着那身旧蓝工装裤，大手端着一托盘吃食。它伸出脚把桌子勾到跟前，把饭菜摆在床边。姑娘睁开眼，嫌恶地轻呼一声，缩了回去；巴格显然惯了她这一套憎恶的表示，趿拉着步子出了屋。
迈克尔再次推门穿过房间，姑娘没有察觉；他朝走廊里望去——不出六英尺远，巴格正蹲在那儿，朝他这个方向瞪视。
迈克尔迅速关上门，飞身折回暗梯，随手带上门。他摸了摸想上锁，却没有钥匙，便一秒也不再耽搁，直奔楼下。他最想避免的，就是一场暴露他身在此宅的遭遇。

===Original===
He made no attempt to get out of the window, but continued his way to the foot of the stairs, and passed through into the hall. This time he was able to close the door, for there were two large bolts at the top and the bottom. Pulling aside the curtain, he stepped gingerly into the hall. For a while he waited, and presently heard the shuffle of feet on the stairs and a sniff beneath the door.
His first act was to ensure his retreat. Noiselessly he drew the bolts from the front door, slipped off the chain and turned the key. Then, as noiselessly, he made his way along the corridor toward Sir Gregory’s room.
The danger was that one of the native servants would see him, but this he must risk. He had observed on each of his previous visits that, short of the library, a door opened into what he knew must be an anteroom of some kind. It was unlocked and he stepped into complete darkness. Groping along the wall, he found a row of switches, and pulled down the first. This lit two wall-brackets, sufficient to give him a general view of the apartment.
It was a small drawing-room, apparently unused, for the furniture was sheeted with holland, and the fire-grate was empty. From here it was possible to gain access to the library through a door near the window. He switched off the light, locked the door on the inside, and tried the shutters. These were fastened by iron bars and were not, as in the case of the library, locked. He pulled them back, let the blind up, and gingerly raised a window. His second line of retreat was now prepared, and he could afford to take risks.
===Chinese===
他没试图从窗户出去，而是一路下到楼梯脚，穿门进了门厅。这一回他能把门关上了，因为门上下各有一道大门闩。他掀开帘子，蹑手蹑脚走进门厅。他等了一阵，不久便听见楼梯上的脚步趿拉声，还有门底下一记嗅闻。
他的第一件事是保住退路。他悄无声息地抽开前门的门闩，滑下锁链，拧开锁。随后又悄无声息地沿走廊朝格雷戈里爵士的房间摸去。
危险在于被哪个土著仆人撞见，但这风险必须冒。前两回来他都留意到，快到书房时有一扇门，通向他判断是某种前厅的屋子。门没锁，他跨进去，里面漆黑一片。沿墙摸索，他找到一排开关，拉下第一个，点亮了两盏壁灯，足够把这屋子的全貌看个大概。
那是间小客厅，显然久不使用，家具都罩着荷兰麻布防尘罩，炉膛空着。从这儿经窗边一扇门可以进书房。他关了灯，从里面锁上门，试了试窗板。窗板由铁闩固定，不像书房那样上锁。他拉开铁闩，卷起百叶窗，轻轻推起一扇窗。第二条退路备好了，他冒得起险了。

===Original===
Kneeling down, he looked through the keyhole. The library was illuminated, and somebody was talking. A woman! Turning the handle, he opened the door the fraction of an inch, and had a view of the interior.
Gregory Penne was standing in his favourite attitude, with his back to the fire, and before him was a tray of those refreshments without which life was apparently insupportable. Seated on the low settee, drawn up at one side of the fireplace, was Stella Mendoza. She was wearing a fur coat, for the night was chilly, and about her neck was such a sparkle of gems as Michael had never seen before on a woman.
Evidently the discussion was not a pleasant one, for there was a heavy scowl on Gregory’s face, and Stella did not seem too pleased.
“I left you because I had to leave you,” growled the man, answering some complaint she had made. “One of my servants is ill and I brought in the doctor. And if I had stayed it would have been the same. It’s no good, my girl,” he said harshly. “The goose doesn’t lay golden eggs more than once—this goose doesn’t, at any rate. You were a fool to quarrel with Knebworth.”
She said something which did not reach Michael’s ears.
===Chinese===
他跪下身，从锁眼往里看。书房亮着灯，有人在说话。是个女人！他拧动把手，把门开出一线，望见了屋内。
格雷戈里·彭尼正以他惯常的姿势站着，背对炉火，面前摆着一托盘提神饮食——看来离了这些东西日子就没法过。矮长椅拉到壁炉一侧，上面坐着斯特拉·门多萨。夜里有凉意，她裹着皮草，颈间宝石的光彩，迈克尔在女人身上从未见过这等阵仗。
这番谈话显然不愉快，格雷戈里脸上阴云密布，斯特拉也不见得多高兴。
"我丢下你，是因为不得不丢下，"那人吼道，回应她的某句抱怨，"我的一个仆人病了，我把医生请了来。就算我不走，结局也一样。没用的，我的姑娘，"他狠声说，"鹅不会下第二回金蛋——这只鹅反正不会。你跟尼布沃斯闹翻，是犯傻。"
她回了句什么，迈克尔听不见。

===Original===
“I dare say your own company would be fine,” said Penne sarcastically. “It would be fine for me, who footed the bill, and finer for you, who spent the money! No! Stella, that cat doesn’t jump. I’ve been very good to you, and you’ve no right to expect me to bankrupt myself to humour your whims.”
“It’s not a whim,” she said vehemently, “it’s a necessity. You don’t want to see me going round the studios taking any kind of job I can get, do you, Gregory?” she pleaded.
“I don’t want to see you work at all, and there’s no reason why you should. You’ve enough to live on. Anyway, you’ve got nothing against Knebworth. If it hadn’t been for him, you wouldn’t have met me, and if you hadn’t met me, you’d have been poorer by thousands. You want a change.”
There was a silence. Her head was drooped, and Michael could not see the girl’s face, but when she spoke, there was that note of viciousness in her voice which told him her state of mind.
“You want a change too, perhaps! I could tell things about you that wouldn’t look good in print, and you’d have a change too! Get that in your mind, Gregory Penne! I’m not a fool—I’ve seen things and heard things, and I can put two and two together. You think I want a change, do you—I do! I want friends who aren’t murderers—”
He sprang at her, his big hand covering her mouth.
===Chinese===
"我敢说你自己的公司会很美妙，"彭尼讥讽道，"对掏钱的我美妙，对花钱的你更美妙！不行！斯特拉，这招不灵。我待你不薄，你没权利指望我倾家荡产去惯你的性子。"
"这不是任性，"她激动地说，"是必要的事。你总不想看我挨个片厂跑，什么活儿都揽吧，格雷戈里？"她恳求道。
"我根本不想看你干活，你也没有干活的道理。你的钱够活一辈子。再说，你没什么可怨尼布沃斯的。没有他，你遇不上我；遇不上我，你如今少说穷几千。你需要换换环境。"
一阵沉默。她低着头，迈克尔看不见姑娘的脸，可她一开口，嗓音里那股狠劲，把她的心绪告诉了他。
"或许你也该换换环境！我能说出些你的事，印在报上可不好看，到时候你也换换环境！把这话记牢了，格雷戈里·彭尼！我不是傻子——我见过的事、听过的事多了，一加一我也会加。你以为我就想换环境？对——我想！我要的朋友，不能是杀人凶手——"
他扑向她，大手捂住她的嘴。

===Original===
“You little devil!” he hissed, and at that instant somebody must have knocked, for he turned to the door and said something in the native dialect.
The answer was inaudible to Mike.
“Listen.” Gregory was speaking to the girl in a calmer tone. “Foss is waiting to see me, and I’ll discuss this little matter with you afterwards.”
He released her, and, going to his desk, touched the spring that operated the mechanism of the secret door that led to Bhag’s quarters.
“Go in there and wait,” he said. “I’ll not keep you longer than five minutes.”
She looked suspiciously at the door which had suddenly opened in the panelling.
“No,” she said, “I’ll go home. Tomorrow will do. I’m sorry I got rough, Gregory, but you madden me sometimes.”
“Go in there!”
===Chinese===
"你这个小妖精！"他嘶声骂道。就在这时，想必有人敲门，因为他转向门口，用土话说了句什么。
回答是什么，迈克听不见。
"听着。"格雷戈里换上平静些的口气对姑娘说，"福斯等着见我，这点小事咱们回头再谈。"
他放开她，走到书桌前，按动那个操纵暗门机关的弹簧——暗门通向巴格的住处。
"进去等着，"他说，"不会让你超过五分钟。"
她狐疑地望着镶板墙上突然打开的那扇门。
"不，"她说，"我回家。明天再谈也行。抱歉我方才凶了，格雷戈里，可你有时候真能把人逼疯。"
"进去！"

===Original===
He pointed to the den, his face working.
“I’ll not!” Her face was white. “You beast, don’t you think I know? That is Bhag’s den! Oh, you beast!”
His face was horrible to see. It was as though all the foulness in his mind found expression in the demoniacal grimace.
Breathless, terrified, the girl stared at him, shrinking back against the wall. Presently Gregory mastered himself.
“Then go into the little drawing-room,” he said huskily.
Mike had time to switch out the lights and flatten himself against the wall, when the door of the room was flung open and the girl thrust in.
===Chinese===
他指着那兽穴，脸都变了形。
"我不进！"她的脸白了，"你这畜生，你以为我不知道？那是巴格的窝！哦，你这畜生！"
他的脸狰狞可怖。仿佛他心里全部的污秽，都在那副魔鬼般的狞笑里现了形。
姑娘大气不敢出，惊恐地瞪着他，缩身退到墙边。片刻之后，格雷戈里按住了自己。
"那就去小客厅，"他嗓音沙哑地说。
迈克来得及熄掉灯，把身子紧贴在墙上——房门随即被猛地推开，姑娘被推了进来。

===Original===
“It is dark!” she wailed.
“You’ll find the switches!”
The door banged.
Michael Brixan was in a dilemma. He could see her figure groping along the wall, and stealthily he moved to avoid her. In doing so he stumbled over a stool.
“Who’s there?” she screamed. “Gregory! Don’t let him touch me, Gregory!”
Again the piercing scream.
Mike leapt past her and through the open window, and, the sound of her shrill agony in his ears, fled along the hedge. Swift as he was, something sped more quickly in pursuit, a great, twittering something that ran bent double on hands and feet. The detective heard and guessed. From what secret hiding-place Bhag had appeared, whether he was in the grounds at the moment Mike jumped, he had no time even to guess. He felt a curious lightness of pocket at that moment and thrust in his hand. His pistol was gone. It must have fallen when he jumped.
He could hear the pad of feet behind him as he darted at a tangent across the field, blundering over the cabbage rows, slipping in furrows, the great beast growing closer and closer with every check. Ahead of him the postern. But it was locked, and, even if it had not been, the wall would have proved no obstacle to the ape. The barrier of the wall held Michael. Breathless, turning to face his pursuer, in the darkness he saw the green eyes shining like two evil stars.
===Chinese===
"好黑！"她哀叫。
"自己找开关！"
门砰地关上。
迈克尔·布里克森进退两难。他看得见她的身影沿墙摸索，便悄悄挪动想躲开她，却绊上了一张小凳。
"谁在那儿？"她尖叫，"格雷戈里！别让它碰我，格雷戈里！"
又是一声刺耳的尖叫。
迈克从她身边一跃而过，钻出敞开的窗，耳中灌满她凄厉的惊叫，沿树篱狂奔。他跑得再快，也有一样东西追得更快——一个庞大的、啾啾叫着的东西，手脚并用地弓着身子飞跑。侦探听见了，也明白了。巴格是从哪处暗藏的巢穴冒出来的，迈克跳窗时它是不是就在园子里，他连猜的工夫都没有。这时他只觉口袋莫名一轻，伸手一摸——手枪没了。准是跳窗时掉的。
他斜刺里穿过田地奔逃，跌跌撞撞撞过一垄垄甘蓝，在犁沟里打滑，身后噗噗的脚步声步步紧逼——每绊一下，那巨兽便又逼近一截。前方就是便门。可门锁着；就算没锁，那道墙对猿来说也根本不算障碍。墙这道屏障困住了迈克尔。他喘着气转身面对追兵，黑暗中，看见那双绿眼睛亮着，像两颗凶星。

## III The Niece / III 侄女

===Original===
Adele Leamington occupied a small room in a small house, and there were moments when she wished it were smaller, that she might be justified in plucking up her courage to ask from the stout and unbending Mrs. Watson, her landlady, a reduction of rent. The extras on Jack Knebworth’s lot were well paid but infrequently employed; for Jack was one of those clever directors who specialized in domestic stories.
She was dressing when Mrs. Watson brought in her morning cup of tea.
“There’s a young fellow been hanging round outside since I got up,” said Mrs. Watson. “I saw him when I took in the milk. Very polite he was, but I told him you weren’t awake.”
“Did he want to see me?” asked the astonished girl.
“That’s what he said,” said Mrs. Watson grimly. “I asked him if he came from Knebworth, and he said no. If you want to see him, you can have the use of the parlour, though I don’t like young men calling on young girls. I’ve never let theatrical lodgings before, and you can’t be too careful. I’ve always had a name for respectability and I want to keep it.”
Adele smiled.
===Chinese===
阿黛尔·利明顿在一幢小房子里租住一个小房间，有些时候她倒巴不得房间更小些，好名正言顺地鼓起勇气，向那位身材敦实、说一不二的房东沃森太太（Mrs. Watson）请求减租。杰克·尼布沃斯片场的临时演员薪水不低，活计却稀少；因为杰克是那种专拍家庭题材的聪明导演。
她正在穿衣，沃森太太端着她早晨那杯茶走了进来。
"打我起床起，就有个小伙子在外头转悠，"沃森太太说，"我取牛奶的时候瞧见的。人倒挺客气，可我告诉他你还没醒。"
"他要见我？"姑娘吃惊地问。
"他是这么说的，"沃森太太板着脸道，"我问他是不是尼布沃斯那边来的，他说不是。你要是想见他，客厅可以借你们用，不过我不喜欢小伙子来找年轻姑娘。我从没出租过给戏子住的房子，这种事小心不为过。我向来有规矩正经的好名声，我想保住它。"
阿黛尔笑了。

===Original===
“I cannot imagine anything more respectable than an early morning caller, Mrs. Watson,” she said.
She went downstairs and opened the door. The young man was standing on the sidewalk with his back to her, but at the sound of the door opening he turned. He was good-looking and well-dressed, and his smile was quick and appealing.
“I hope your landlady did not bother to wake you up? I could have waited. You are Miss Adele Leamington, aren’t you?”
She nodded.
“Will you come in, please?” she asked, and took him into the stuffy little front parlour, and, closing the door behind her, waited.
===Chinese===
"我想不出还有比清晨来访更规矩的事了，沃森太太，"她说。
她下楼开了门。年轻人正背对着她站在人行道上，听见开门声便转过身来。他相貌英俊，衣着考究，笑容来得快而动人。
"没让房东太太特意叫醒您吧？我本来可以等的。您是阿黛尔·利明顿小姐，对吧？"
她点了点头。
"请进来好吗？"她问，把他领进那间闷热的小前厅，随手掩上门，等着来意。

===Original===
“I am a reporter,” he said untruthfully, and her face fell.
“You’ve come about Uncle Francis? Is anything really wrong? They sent a detective to see me a week ago. Have they found him?”
“No, they haven’t found him,” he said carefully. “You knew him very well, of course, Miss Leamington?”
She shook her head.
“No, I have only seen him twice in my life. My dear father and he quarrelled before I was born, and I only saw him once after daddy died, and once before mother was taken with her fatal illness.”
She heard him sigh, and sensed his relief, though why he should be relieved that her uncle was almost a stranger to her, she could not fathom.
===Chinese===
"我是个记者，"他撒谎道，她的脸色顿时沉了下来。
"您是为弗朗西斯伯伯的事来的？真出什么事了吗？一星期前他们派了个侦探来见我。找到他了吗？"
"没有，还没找到，"他字斟句酌地说，"您跟他很熟吧，利明顿小姐？"
她摇了摇头。
"不熟，我一辈子只见过他两次。亲爱的父亲和他吵翻时我还没出生，父亲去世后我见过他一次，母亲染上那场致命的病之前又见过一次。"
她听见他叹了口气，也觉察到他如释重负，可他为什么因为她伯伯几乎等于陌生人就放下心来，她实在捉摸不透。

===Original===
“You saw him at Chichester, though?” he said.
She nodded.
“Yes, I saw him. I was on my way to Goodwood Park—a whole party of us in a charabanc—and I saw him for a moment walking along the sidewalk. He looked desperately ill and worried. He was just coming out of a stationer’s shop when I saw him; he had a newspaper under his arm and a letter in his hand.”
“Where was the store?” he asked quickly.
She gave him the address, and he jotted it down.
“You didn’t see him again?”
She shook her head.
===Chinese===
"不过您在奇切斯特见过他？"他说。
她点了点头。
"是的，我见过。那天我们一伙人坐游览车去古德伍德公园（Goodwood Park），我一眼瞥见他在人行道上走。他看上去病得厉害，一脸愁容。我看见他时他刚从一家文具店里出来，胳膊底下夹着一份报纸，手里拿着一封信。"
"店在哪儿？"他赶紧问。
她把地址告诉了他，他匆匆记下。
"后来没再见过他？"
她摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Is anything really very badly wrong?” she asked anxiously. “I’ve often heard mother say that Uncle Francis was very extravagant, and a little unscrupulous. Has he been in trouble?”
“Yes,” admitted Michael, “he has been in trouble, but nothing that you need worry about. You’re a great film actress, aren’t you?”
In spite of her anxiety she laughed.
“The only chance I have of being a great film actress is for you to say so in your paper.”
“My what?” he asked, momentarily puzzled. “Oh yes, my newspaper, of course!”
“I don’t believe you’re a reporter at all,” she said with sudden suspicion.
“Indeed I am,” he said glibly, and dared to pronounce the name of that widely-circulated sheet upon which the sun seldom sets.
===Chinese===
"是不是真的出了大乱子？"她焦急地问，"我常听母亲说，弗朗西斯伯伯花钱大手大脚，做事也不大有底线。他惹上麻烦了吗？"
"是的，"迈克尔承认道，"他是惹了麻烦，不过没什么需要您操心的。您是位了不起的电影女演员，对吧？"
尽管满心焦虑，她还是笑了。
"我要想当上了不起的电影女演员，唯一的指望就是您在您那份报上这么写一句。"
"我的什么？"他一时没反应过来，"哦对，我那份报纸，当然！"
"我压根儿不信你是什么记者，"她突然起了疑心。
"千真万确，"他油嘴滑舌地说，还大着胆子报出了那份行销全球、太阳几乎从不在其版面上落下的畅销报纸的名字。

===Original===
“Though I’m not a great actress, and fear I never shall be, I like to believe it is because I’ve never had a chance—I’ve a horrible suspicion that Mr. Knebworth knows instinctively that I am no good.”
Mike Brixan had found a new interest in the case, an interest which, he was honest enough to confess to himself, was not dissociated from the niece of Francis Elmer. He had never met anybody quite so pretty and quite so unsophisticated and natural.
“You’re going to the studio, I suppose?”
She nodded.
“I wonder if Mr. Knebworth would mind my calling to see you?”
She hesitated.
===Chinese===
"虽说我不算什么了不起的女演员，恐怕永远也成不了，可我宁愿相信那是因为我从没得到过机会——我心里有个可怕的怀疑：尼布沃斯先生凭着本能就知道我不行。"
迈克·布里克森对这桩案子生出了新的兴致——他老实承认，这份兴致与弗朗西斯·埃尔默的侄女不无关系。他还从没遇见过这么漂亮、又这么不谙世事、浑然天成的人。
"您这就去摄影棚了吧？"
她点了点头。
"不知道尼布沃斯先生会不会介意我去片场看您？"
她犹豫了一下。

===Original===
“Mr. Knebworth doesn’t like callers.”
“Then maybe I’ll call on him,” said Michael, nodding. “It doesn’t matter whom I call on, does it?”
“It certainly doesn’t matter to me,” said the girl coldly.
“In the vulgar language of the masses,” thought Mike as he strode down the street, “I have had the bird!”
His inquiries did not occupy very much of his time. He found the little news shop, and the proprietor, by good fortune, remembered the coming of Mr. Francis Elmer.
“He came for a letter, though it wasn’t addressed to Elmer,” said the shopkeeper. “A lot of people have their letters addressed here. I make a little extra money that way.”
===Chinese===
"尼布沃斯先生不喜欢来客。"
"那我就去拜访他，"迈克尔点了点头说，"我拜访谁都不碍事吧？"
"反正与我无关，"姑娘冷冷地说。
"用老百姓的粗话说，"迈克大步走在街上时想道，"我这是吃了个闭门羹！"
他的查访没花多少时间。他找到了那家小报刊店，幸运的是，店主还记得弗朗西斯·埃尔默先生光顾的事。
"他是来取信的，不过那封信不是写给埃尔默的，"店主说，"好些人都把信寄到我这儿。这样我也能多挣几个钱。"

===Original===
“Did he buy a newspaper?”
“No, sir, he did not buy a newspaper; he had one under his arm—the *Morning Telegram*. I remember that, because I noticed that he’d put a blue pencil mark round one of the agony advertisements on the front page, and I was wondering what it was all about. I kept a copy of that day’s *Morning Telegram*: I’ve got it now.”
He went into the little parlour at the back of the shop and returned with a dingy newspaper, which he laid on the counter.
“There are six there, but I don’t know which one it was.”
Michael examined the agony advertisements. There was one frantic message from a mother to her son, asking him to return and saying that “all would be forgiven.” There was a cryptogram message, which he had not time to decipher. A third, which was obviously the notice of an assignation. The fourth was a thinly veiled advertisement for a new hair-waver, and at the fifth he stopped. It ran:
===Chinese===
"他买报纸了吗？"
"没有，先生，他没买报纸；他自己胳膊底下夹着一份——是《晨间电讯报》（Morning Telegram）。这个我记得清楚，因为我瞧见他用蓝铅笔在头版的一条私人启事广告上画了个圈，我当时还纳闷是怎么回事。那天的《晨间电讯报》我留了一份：现在还在。"
他走进店铺后头的小客厅，拿来一份发黄的报纸，摊在柜台上。
"上头一共六条启事，可我说不准是哪一条。"
迈克尔细看那些私人启事。有一条是一位母亲发给儿子的急切留言，求他回家，说"一切都会原谅"。有一条是密语文告，他来不及破译。第三条显然是幽会通知。第四条是一条几乎不加掩饰的新式卷发器广告。到第五条他停住了。启事写道：

===Original===

> “Troubled Final directions at address I gave you

 Courage. Benefactor.”

“Some ‘benefactor,’ ” said Mike Brixan. “What was he like—the man who called? Was he worried?”
“Yes, sir: he looked upset—all distracted like. He seemed like a chap who’d lost his head.”
“That seems a fair description,” said Mike.
===Chinese===

> "致『受困者』：到我给你的那个地址取最后指示。

 鼓起勇气。——恩主"

"好一个'恩主'，"迈克·布里克森说，"来取信的那人什么样——很发愁吗？"
"可不是嘛，先生：他看上去心烦意乱——六神无主的样子。就像个丢了魂的人。"
"这描述倒贴切，"迈克说。

## X The Open Window / X 敞开的窗

===Original===
The dynamo wagon was humming as he walked down the garden path, and with a hiss and a splutter from the arcs, the front of the cottage was suddenly illuminated by their fierce light. Outside on the road a motorist had pulled up to look upon the unusual spectacle.
“What is happening?” he asked curiously.
“They’re taking a picture,” said Michael.
“Oh, is that what it is? I suppose it is one of Knebworth’s outfits?”
“Where are you going?” demanded Michael suddenly. “Forgive my asking you, but if you’re heading for Chichester you can render me a very great service if you give me a lift.”
“Jump in,” said the man. “I’m going to Petworth, but it will not be much out of my way to take you into the city.”
Until they came to the town he plied Michael with questions betraying that universal inquisitiveness which picture-making invariably incites amongst the uninitiated.
===Chinese===
他沿园中小径往回走时，发电车嗡嗡作响，弧光灯嘶的一声喷出火花，小屋的正面霎时被刺目的强光照亮。外面路上，一个开车人停了车，看这新奇的场面。
"这是干什么呢？"他好奇地问。
"他们在拍电影，"迈克尔说。
"哦，原来是这样？想必是尼布沃斯的班子吧？"
"您去哪儿？"迈克尔突然问，"恕我冒昧，要是您去奇切斯特方向，捎我一程，就是帮了我一个大忙。"
"上车吧，"那人说，"我去佩特沃斯（Petworth），不过送你进城也绕不了多少路。"
一路进城，他连珠炮似的向迈克尔发问，流露出拍电影这行当总会在外行人中间激起的那种人人皆有的好奇心。

===Original===
Michael got down near the marketplace and made his way to the house of a man he knew, a former master at his old school, now settled down in Chichester, who had, amongst other possessions, an excellent library. Declining his host’s pressing invitation to dinner, Michael stated his needs, and the old master laughed.
“I can’t remember that you were much of a student in my days, Michael,” he said, “but you may have the run of the library. Is it some line of Virgil that escapes you? I may be able to save you a hunt.”
“It’s not Virgil, maestro,” smiled Michael. “Something infinitely more full-blooded!”
He was in the library for twenty minutes, and when he emerged there was a light of triumph in his eye.
“I’m going to use your telephone if I may,” he said, and he got London without delay.
For ten minutes he was speaking with Scotland Yard, and, when he had finished, he went into the dining-room where the master, who was a bachelor, was eating his solitary dinner.
===Chinese===
迈克尔在市场附近下了车，去投奔一个熟人——他母校的一位老教师，如今定居奇切斯特，家藏甚丰，其中有一座极好的图书室。迈克尔谢了主人殷勤的晚餐之邀，说明来意，老教师笑了。
"我记得你在我门下时可算不上好学，迈克尔，"他说，"不过图书室随你翻。是维吉尔（Virgil）的哪一句忘了？兴许能省你一番查找。"
"不是维吉尔，老师，"迈克尔笑道，"是比那有血有肉得多的东西！"
他在图书室里待了二十分钟，出来时眼里闪着得胜的光。
"可以的话，我要用用您的电话，"他说，随即毫无耽搁地接通了伦敦。
他和苏格兰场讲了十分钟，讲完便走进饭厅——独身的老师正在那儿吃他一个人的晚饭。

===Original===
“You can render me one more service, mentor of my youth,” he said. “Have you in this abode of peace an automatic pistol that throws a heavier shell than this?”
And he put his own on the table. Michael knew Mr. Scott had been an officer of the Territorial Army, and incidentally an instructor of the Officers’ Training Corps, so that his request was not as impossible of fulfilment as it appeared.
“Yes, I can give you a heavier one than that. What are you shooting—elephants?”
“Something a trifle more dangerous,” said Michael.
“Curiosity was never a weakness of mine,” said the master, and went out to return with a Browning of heavy calibre and a box of cartridges.
They spent five minutes cleaning the pistol, which had not been in use for some time, and, with his new weapon weighing down his jacket pocket, Mike took his leave, carrying a lighter heart and a clearer understanding than he had enjoyed when he had arrived at the house.
===Chinese===
"我年轻时的恩师，还得请您再帮个忙，"他说，"您这座安宁的居所里，有没有一支口径比这个大的自动手枪？"
说着把自己的那支放在桌上。迈克尔知道斯科特先生（Mr. Scott）当过本土军的军官，还兼过军官训练团的教官，所以这要求并不像看上去那么难以办到。
"有，我能给你一支比这大的。你要打什么——大象？"
"比那稍微危险一点的东西，"迈克尔说。
"好奇从来不是我的毛病，"老师说，出去拿来一支大口径勃朗宁和一盒子弹。
两人花了五分钟擦净那支久未使用的枪，迈克把新家伙揣进外套口袋坠得沉沉的，起身告辞——比起进这屋子时，他心境更轻快，头脑也更明白了。

===Original===
He hired a car from a local garage and drove back to the Dower House, dismissing the car just short of his destination. Jack Knebworth had not even noticed that he had disappeared. But old Mr. Longvale, wearing a coat with many capes, and a soft silk cap from which dangled a long tassel, came to him almost as soon as he entered the garden.
“May I speak to you, Mr. Brixan?” he said in a low voice, and they went into the house together. “Do you remember Mr. Knebworth was very perturbed because he thought he saw somebody peering in at the window—something with a monkey’s head?”
Michael nodded.
“Well, it is a most curious fact,” said the old gentleman impressively, “that a quarter of an hour ago I happened to be walking in the far end of my garden, and, looking across the hedge toward the field, I suddenly saw a gigantic form rise, apparently from the ground, and move toward these bushes”—he pointed through the window to a clump in a field on the opposite side of the road. “He seemed to be crouching forward and moving furtively.”
“Will you show me the place?” said Michael quickly.
He followed the other across the road to the bushes, a little clump which was empty when they reached it. Kneeling down to make a new skyline, Michael scanned the limited horizon, but there was no sign of Bhag. For that it was Bhag he had no doubt. There might be nothing in it. Penne told him that the animal was in the habit of taking nightly strolls, and that he was perfectly harmless. Suppose …
===Chinese===
他从本地车行雇了辆车开回遗孀宅，快到地方便把车打发了。杰克·尼布沃斯压根儿没留意他失踪过。倒是老朗维尔先生——他披一件多层披肩的外套，戴一顶垂着长流苏的软绸帽——几乎在他刚进园子时就迎了上来。
"能跟您说句话吗，布里克森先生？"他低声说，两人一同进了屋，"您还记得吗，尼布沃斯先生看见有什么东西从窗口往里窥视——一个长着猴子脑袋的东西——吓得够呛？"
迈克尔点了点头。
"呃，有件顶蹊跷的事，"老先生郑重其事地说，"一刻钟前，我恰好在园子最那头散步，隔着树篱朝田里望，突然看见一个庞大的身影——像是从地里冒出来的——朝那丛灌木挪过去。"他隔着窗指向路对面田野里的一小丛灌木。"它似乎弓着身子，鬼鬼祟祟地移动。"
"能带我去看看那地方吗？"迈克尔赶紧说。
他跟着老人穿过马路来到灌木丛前，那一小丛灌木到时已空无一物。迈克尔跪下身换一个天际线的视角，扫视那有限的地平线，却不见巴格的踪影。那正是巴格，他毫不怀疑。也许这只是无谓之举。彭尼告诉过他，这畜生习惯夜里出来溜达，而且全然无害。可万一……

===Original===
The thought was absurd, fantastically absurd. And yet the animal had been so extraordinarily human that no speculation in connection with it was quite absurd.
When he returned to the garden, he went in search of the girl. She had finished her scene and was watching the stealthy movements of two screen burglars, who were creeping along the wall in the subdued light of the arcs.
“Excuse me, Miss Leamington, I’m going to ask you an impertinent question. Have you brought a complete change of clothes with you?”
“Why ever do you ask that?” she demanded, her eyes wide open. “Of course I did! I always bring a complete change in case the weather breaks.”
“That’s one question. Did you lose anything when you were at Griff Towers?”
“I lost my gloves,” she said quickly. “Did you find them?”
===Chinese===
这念头荒唐，荒唐透顶。可那畜生又人得如此出奇，跟它沾边的任何猜想便都不算荒唐了。
他回到园子里，去找那姑娘。她的戏已拍完，正看着两个银幕窃贼悄没声地行动——他们在弧光灯压暗的光里沿着墙根爬行。
"恕我冒昧，利明顿小姐，我要问您一个唐突的问题。您带了一整套换洗衣物吗？"
"您问这个干什么？"她瞪大了眼睛问，"当然带了！我总带一整套换的，万一变天好替换。"
"这是头一个问题。在格里夫塔楼时，您丢了什么东西没有？"
"我的手套丢了，"她赶紧说，"您捡到了？"

===Original===
“No. When did you miss them?”
“I missed them immediately. I thought for a moment—” She stopped. “It was a foolish idea, but—”
“What did you think?” he asked.
“I’d rather not tell you. It is a purely personal matter.”
“You thought that Sir Gregory had taken them as a souvenir?”
Even in the half-darkness he saw her colour come and go.
===Chinese===
"没有。您什么时候发现丢的？"
"一离开就发现了。我一度以为——"她停住了，"那念头很傻，可是——"
"您以为什么？"他问。
"我宁愿不说。这是纯属私人的事。"
"您以为是被格雷戈里爵士拿去当了纪念品？"
即使在这半明半暗里，他也看见她红了脸又褪下去。

===Original===
“I did think that,” she said, a little stiffly.
“Then it doesn’t matter very much—about your change of clothing,” he said.
“Whatever are you talking about?”
She looked at him suspiciously. He guessed she thought that he had been drinking, but the last thing in the world he wanted to do at that moment was to explain his somewhat disjointed questions.
“Now everybody is going to bed!”
It was old Jack Knebworth talking.
