“Everybody! Off you go! Mr. Foss has shown you your rooms. I want you up at four o’clock tomorrow morning, so get as much sleep as you can. Foss, you’ve marked the rooms?”
===Chinese===
"我确实那么想过，"她说，语气有点生硬。
"那么，换洗衣服的事就不大要紧了，"他说。
"您到底在说什么呀？"
她狐疑地打量他。他猜她准以为自己喝多了，而他此刻天下头一件不愿做的事，就是解释自己那串没头没脑的问题。
"好了，都睡觉去！"
说话的是老杰克·尼布沃斯。
"所有人！都走！福斯先生已把各位的房间分派好了。明早四点我就要叫你们起床，能睡多少睡多少。福斯，房间都标好了？"

===Original===
“Yes,” said the man. “I’ve put the names on every door. I’ve given this young lady a room to herself—is that right?”
“I suppose it is,” said Knebworth dubiously. “Anyway, she won’t be there long enough to get used to it.”
The girl said good night to the detective and went straight up to her apartment. It was a tiny room, smelling somewhat musty, and was simply furnished. A truckle bed, a chest of drawers with a swinging glass on top, and a small table and chair was all that the apartment contained. By the light of her candle, the floor showed signs of having been recently scrubbed, and the centre was covered by a threadbare square of carpet.
She locked the door, blew out the candle and, undressing in the dark, went to the window and threw open the casement. And then, for the first time, she saw, on the centre of one of the small panes, a circular disc of paper. It was pasted on the outside of the window, and at first she was about to pull it off, when she guessed that it might be some indicator placed by Knebworth to mark an exact position that he required for the morning picture-taking.
She did not immediately fall asleep, her mind for some curious reason, being occupied unprofitably with a tumultuous sense of annoyance directed towards Michael Brixan. For a long time a strong sense of justice fought with a sense of humour equally powerful. He was a nice man, she told herself; the sixth sense of woman had already delivered that information, heavily underlined. He certainly had nerve. In the end humour brought sleep. She was smiling when her eyelids closed.
She had been sleeping two hours, though it did not seem two seconds. A sense of impending danger wakened her, and she sat up in bed, her heart thumping wildly. She looked round the room. In the pale moonlight she could see almost every corner, and it was empty. Was it somebody outside the door that had wakened her? She tried the door handle: it was locked, as she had left it. The window? It was very near to the ground, she remembered. Stepping to the window, she pulled one casement close. She was closing the other when, out of the darkness below, reached a great hairy arm and a hand closed like a vice on her wrist.
She did not scream. She stood breathless, dying of terror, she felt. Her heart ceased beating, and she was conscious of a deadly cold. What was it? What could it be? Summoning all her courage, she looked out of the window down into a hideous, bestial face and two round, green eyes that stared into hers.
===Chinese===
"标好了，"那人说，"每扇门上都贴了名字。我给这位小姐单分了一间——对吧？"
"应该吧，"尼布沃斯将信将疑地说，"反正她也住不了多久，习惯了也白搭。"
姑娘向侦探道了晚安，径直上楼进了自己的房间。那是间小屋，有点霉味，陈设简单。一张滚轮矮床，一座顶上装着转镜的五斗柜，外加一张小桌一把椅子，便是全部家当。借着烛光看得出，地板刚擦洗过，正中铺着一块磨秃了的小方地毯。
她锁上门，吹灭蜡烛，在黑暗中脱了衣裳，走到窗前推开窗扉。这时她第一次看见，一块小窗玻璃的正中贴着一枚圆形纸片。它是从外面贴在窗上的，起初她刚要伸手去撕，随即想到这也许是他尼布沃斯做的什么标记，用来标出明早拍摄所需的准确位置。
她没有立刻睡着，也不知为什么，满脑子翻来覆去都是对迈克尔·布里克森的一股无名火。很长一段时间里，强烈的正义感和同样强烈的幽默感打了个平手。他是个好人，她对自己说；女人的第六感早已把这条情报送达，还重重画了着重线。他的胆子确实够大。最后是幽默感送她入了梦。合上眼皮时，她脸上还带着笑。
她已睡了两小时，却仿佛只睡了眨眼工夫。大祸临头的感觉惊醒了她，她坐起身，心口狂跳。她环视屋内。淡淡的月光下，几乎每个角落都看得见，屋里空无一人。是门外有什么人吵醒了她吗？她拧了拧门把手：锁着，和她离开时一样。是窗户？她记得窗子离地面很近。她走到窗前，掩上一扇窗扉。正要去关另一扇时，底下的黑暗里探出一条毛茸茸的巨臂，一只手像老虎钳一样扣住了她的手腕。
她没有喊。她站在那里喘不上气，觉得自己快要吓死了。心脏停了跳，浑身感到一种彻骨的死寒。那是什么？会是什么呢？她鼓起全部勇气，朝窗外望去——下面是一张狰狞的兽脸，两只滚圆的绿眼睛正瞪着她的眼睛。

## XXXIV The Search / XXXIV 搜寻

===Original===
“Yes, it’s me,” said Michael bitterly. “All right, officer, you needn’t wait. Jack, I’ll come up to the house to get this makeup off.”
“For the Lord’s sake!” breathed Knebworth, staring at the detective. “I’ve never seen a man made up so well that he deceived me.”
“I’ve deceived everybody, including myself,” said Michael savagely. “I thought I’d caught him with a dummy letter, instead of which the devil caught me.”
“What was it?”
“Ammonia, I think﻿—a concentrated solution thereof,” said Michael.
It was twenty minutes before he emerged from the bathroom, his eyes inflamed but otherwise his old self.
===Chinese===
"是，是我。"迈克尔苦涩地说，"好了，警官，你不用等了。杰克，我上你家把这身装扮洗掉。"
"我的老天！"尼布沃斯喘着气，直勾勾望着侦探，"我从没见过谁的化装这么到家，连我都骗过了。"
"我谁都骗过了，连我自己都骗了。"迈克尔狠狠地说，"我本以为用一封假信钓住了他，到头来倒是那魔鬼钓住了我。"
"那是什么东西？"
"氨水，我猜——浓缩的。"迈克尔说。
过了二十分钟他才从浴室里出来，双眼还是红的，其余已恢复了原样。

===Original===
“I wanted to trap him in my own way, but he was too smart for me.”
“Do you know who he is?”
Michael nodded.
“Oh, yes, I know,” he said. “I’ve got a special force of men here, waiting to effect the arrest, but I didn’t want a fuss, and I certainly did not want bloodshed. And bloodshed there will be, unless I am mistaken.”
“I didn’t seem to recognize the car, and I know most of the machines in this city,” said Jack.
“It is a new one, used only for these midnight adventures of the Headhunter. He probably garages it away from his house. You asked me if I’d have something to eat just now, and I lied and told you I was living on the fat of the land. Give me some food, for the love of heaven!”
===Chinese===
"我本想按我自己的路数擒他，不想他比我更精。"
"你知道他是谁了？"
迈克尔点了点头。
"哦，知道，我知道。"他说，"我手下有一支特别人马在这儿候着，单等动手拿人，可我不想兴师动众，更不想见血。而血怕是免不了，除非我看走眼。"
"那辆车我像是认不出来，城里大半的车我都认得。"杰克说。
"是辆新车，专供猎头者干这些午夜勾当用的。多半停在离他宅子远远的车库里。方才你问我要不要吃点什么，我撒了谎，说我吃得讲究。看在老天的份上，给我弄点吃的吧！"

===Original===
Jack went into the larder and brought out some cold meat, brewed a pot of coffee, and sat in silence, watching the famished detective dispose of the viands.
“I feel a man now,” said Michael as he finished, “for I’d had nothing to eat except a biscuit since eleven this morning. By the way, our friend Stella Mendoza is staying at Griff Towers, and I’m afraid I rather scared her. I happened to be nosing round there an hour ago, to make absolutely sure of my bird, and I looked in upon her﻿—to her alarm!”
There came a sharp rap at the door, and Jack Knebworth looked up.
“Who’s that at this time of night?” he asked.
“Probably the policeman,” said Michael.
Knebworth opened the door and found a short, stout, middle-aged woman standing on the doorstep with a roll of paper in her hand.
===Chinese===
杰克进了食品间，端出些冷肉，煮上一壶咖啡，便默默坐着，看那饿瘪了的侦探风卷残云。
"这下我觉得自己又是个人了。"迈克尔吃完说，"打今早十一点起，我就啃了一块饼干。对了，咱们那位朋友斯特拉·门多萨眼下正住在格里夫塔楼，恐怕我还把她吓着了。一个钟头前我恰好在那一带嗅探，想把我那只鸟拿个十拿九稳，顺道朝她窗里望了一眼——把她的魂都吓掉了！"
门上响起急促的敲击声，杰克·尼布沃斯抬起头来。
"夜里这个钟点，会是谁？"他问。
"多半是那个警察。"迈克尔说。
尼布沃斯开了门，见台阶上站着一个矮胖的中年妇人，手里拿着一卷纸。

===Original===
“Is this Mr. Knebworth’s?” she asked.
“Yes,” said Jack.
“I’ve brought the play that Miss Leamington left behind. She asked me to bring it to you.”
Knebworth took the roll of paper and slipped off the elastic band which encircled it. It was the manuscript of “Roselle.”
“Why have you brought this?” he asked.
“She told me to bring it up if I found it.”
===Chinese===
"这儿是尼布沃斯先生家吗？"她问。
"是。"杰克说。
"利明顿小姐落下的剧本，我给送来了。是她托我带给您的。"
尼布沃斯接过那卷纸，褪下箍着的橡皮筋。是《罗塞尔》的手稿。
"你为什么送这个来？"他问。
"她吩咐我，找着了就送来。"

===Original===
“Very good,” said Jack, mystified. “Thank you very much.”
He closed the door on the woman and went back to the dining-room.
“Adele has sent up her script. What’s wrong, I wonder?”
“Who brought it?” asked Michael, interested.
“Her landlady, I suppose,” said Jack, describing the woman.
“Yes, that’s she. Adele is not turning in her part?”
Jack shook his head.
“That wouldn’t be likely.”
===Chinese===
"很好。"杰克满腹狐疑地说，"多谢了。"
他关上门，送走妇人，回到餐室。
"阿黛尔把剧本送上来了。这是出了什么事，我想不透？"
"谁送来的？"迈克尔来了兴致。
"她的房东太太，我估摸着。"杰克说着，把那妇人的模样描述了一遍。
"对，是她。阿黛尔不是要辞角色吧？"
杰克摇了摇头。
"那倒不至于。"

===Original===
Michael was puzzled.
“What the dickens does it mean? What did the woman say?”
“She said that Miss Leamington wanted her to bring up the manuscript if she found it.”
Michael was out of the house in a second, and, racing down the street, overtook the woman.
“Will you come back, please?” he said, and escorted her to the house again. “Just tell Mr. Knebworth why Miss Leamington sent this manuscript, and what you mean by having ‘forgotten’ it.”
“Why, when she came up to you﻿—” began the woman.
===Chinese===
迈克尔纳闷起来。
"这究竟是什么名堂？那妇人怎么说的？"
"她说，利明顿小姐嘱咐她，找着剧本就送来。"
转眼间迈克尔已冲出门去，沿街飞奔，追上了那妇人。
"请您回来一趟好吗？"他说着，又把她领回屋里，"请您对尼布沃斯先生讲清楚，利明顿小姐为什么要送这部手稿来，您说的'落下了'又是怎么回事。"
"嗐，她上您这儿来的时候——"妇人开口道。

===Original===
“Came up to me?” cried Knebworth quickly.
“A gentleman from the studio called for her, and said you wanted to see her,” said the landlady. “Miss Leamington was just going to bed, but I took up the message. He said you wanted to see her about the play, and asked her to bring the manuscript. She had mislaid it somewhere and was in a great state about it, so I told her to go on, as you were in a hurry, and I’d bring it up. At least, she asked me to do that.”
“What sort of a gentleman was it who called?”
“A rather stout gentleman. He wasn’t exactly a gentleman, he was a chauffeur. As a matter of fact, I thought he’d been drinking, though I didn’t want to alarm Miss Leamington by telling her so.”
“And then what happened?” asked Michael quickly.
“She came down and got in the car. The chauffeur was already in.”
===Chinese===
"上我这儿来？"尼布沃斯急忙喊道。
"摄影棚来了位先生接她，说是您要见她。"房东太太说，"利明顿小姐正要上床，是我把话捎上去的。他说您为剧本的事要见她，请她把手稿带上。她不知把稿子搁哪儿了，急得不行，我便叫她先走，说您赶时间，稿子我随后送来。起码，是她求我这么办的。"
"来接的是位什么样的先生？"
"一位胖乎乎的先生。也算不上什么先生，是个司机。说实话，我看他是喝过了酒，只是不想说破，怕利明顿小姐着慌。"
"后来呢？"迈克尔急问。
"她下了楼，上了车。司机已经坐在车里了。"

===Original===
“A closed car, I suppose?”
The woman nodded.
“And then they drove off? What time was this?”
“Just after half-past ten. I remember, because I heard the church clock strike just before the car drove up.”
Michael was cool now. His voice scarcely rose above a whisper.
“Twenty-five past eleven,” he said, looking at his watch. “You’ve been a long time coming.”
===Chinese===
"是辆封闭的汽车吧？"
妇人点了点头。
"然后车就开走了？那是几点的事？"
"十点半刚过。我记得清楚，车开到之前，我听见教堂的钟正敲着。"
迈克尔此刻反倒冷静下来，声音低得几如耳语。
"十一点二十五。"他看了看表，"你可来得真够久的。"

===Original===
“I couldn’t find the paper, sir. It was under Miss Leamington’s pillow. Isn’t she here?”
“No, she’s not here,” said Michael quietly. “Thank you very much; I won’t keep you. Will you wait for me at the police station?”
He went upstairs and put on his coat.
“Where do you think she is?” asked Jack.
“She is at Griff Towers,” replied the other, “and whether Gregory Penne lives or dies this night depends entirely upon the treatment that Adele has received at his hands.”
===Chinese===
"我找不着稿子呀，先生。它在利明顿小姐的枕头底下。她不在这儿吗？"
"不，她不在这儿。"迈克尔平静地说，"多谢您，不留您了。请到警察局等我，好吗？"
他上楼穿上外衣。
"你觉得她在哪儿？"杰克问。
"她在格里夫塔楼。"对方答道，"而格雷戈里·彭尼今夜是死是活，全看他是怎么对待阿黛尔的。"

===Original===
At the police station he found the landlady, a little frightened, more than a little tearful.
“What was Miss Leamington wearing when she went out?”
“Her blue cloak, sir,” whimpered the woman, “that pretty blue cloak she always wore.”
Scotland Yard men were at the station, and it was a heavily loaded car that ran out to Chichester﻿—too heavy for Michael, in a fever of impatience, for the weight of its human cargo checked its speed, and every second was precious. At last, after an eternity of time, the big car swung into the drive. Michael did not stop to waken the lodge-keeper, but smashed the frail gates open with the buffers of its machine, mounted the slope, crossing the gravel parade, and halted.
There was no need to ring the bell: the door was wide open, and, at the head of his party, Mike Brixan dashed through the deserted hall, along the corridor into Gregory’s library. One light burnt, offering a feeble illumination, but the room was empty. With rapid strides he crossed to the desk and turned the switch. Bhag’s den opened, but Bhag too was an absentee.
===Chinese===
在警察局，他见到了房东太太，她有些惊惶，更有些抹泪。
"利明顿小姐出门时穿的什么？"
"她的蓝斗篷，先生，"妇人呜咽道，"她常穿的那件漂亮蓝斗篷。"
苏格兰场的人已到了局里，驶往奇切斯特的汽车满载而行——于焦灼如焚的迈克尔来说，载得太重了，这一车人的分量拖住了车速，而每一秒都贵如金玉。终于，熬过了仿佛无穷无尽的时辰，大车拐进了宅前车道。迈克尔没停车去叫门房，径直用车头的保险杠撞开了那扇单薄的铁门，冲上坡地，碾过砾石广场，停了下来。
不必按铃：大门洞开。迈克·布里克森领着人马，冲过空荡荡的门厅，沿走廊直奔格雷戈里的书房。一盏灯亮着，光线微弱，屋里却空无一人。他大步抢到书桌前，扳动开关。巴格的兽窟开了，可巴格也不在。

===Original===
He pressed the bell by the side of the fireplace, and almost immediately the brown-faced servitor whom he had seen before came trembling into the room.
“Where is your master?” asked Michael in Dutch.
The man shook his head.
“I don’t know,” he replied, but instinctively he looked up to the ceiling.
“Show me the way.”
They went back to the hall, up the broad stairway on to the first floor. Along a corridor, hung with swords, as was its fellow below, he reached another open door﻿—the great dance hall where Gregory Penne had held revel that evening. There was nobody in sight, and Michael came out into the hall. As he did so, he was aware of a frantic tapping at one of the doors in the corridor. The key was in the lock: he turned it and flung the door wide open, and Stella Mendoza, white as death, staggered out.
===Chinese===
他按了壁炉旁的电铃，几乎是立刻，他从前见过的那个棕色面孔的哑仆浑身颤抖着走了进来。
"你的主人在哪儿？"迈克尔用荷兰语问。
那人摇了摇头。
"不知道。"他答道，可本能地朝天花板望了一眼。
"给我带路。"
他们回到门厅，登上宽阔的楼梯，上了二楼。沿一条走廊——同楼下那条一样挂满了刀剑——他走到又一扇敞开的门前：那是格雷戈里·彭尼当晚纵酒作乐的大舞厅。不见人影，迈克尔又折回门厅。就在此时，他察觉走廊里某扇门内传出狂乱的敲击声。钥匙就插在锁上：他拧开门，把门甩开，斯特拉·门多萨脸色惨白如死，跌跌撞撞地扑了出来。

===Original===
“Where is Adele?” she gasped.
“I want to ask you that,” said Michael sternly. “Where is she?”
The girl shook her head helplessly, strove to speak, and then collapsed in a swoon.
He did not wait for her to recover, but continued his search. From room to room he went, but there was no sign of Adele or the brutal owner of Griff Towers. He searched the library again, and passed through into the little drawing-room, where a table was laid for two. The cloth was wet with spilt wine; one glass was half empty﻿—but the two for whom the table was laid had vanished. They must have gone out of the front door﻿—whither?
He was standing tense, his mind concentrated upon a problem that was more vital to him than life itself, when he heard a sound that came from the direction of Bhag’s den. And then there appeared in the doorway the monstrous ape himself. He was bleeding from a wound in the shoulder; the blood fell drip-drip-drip as he stood, clutching in his two great hands something that seemed like a bundle of rags. As Michael looked, the room rocked before his eyes.
===Chinese===
"阿黛尔在哪儿？"她喘着气问。
"这话正要问你。"迈克尔厉声道，"她在哪儿？"
姑娘无助地摇头，挣扎着想说话，随即晕厥过去。
他没等她苏醒，继续搜寻。一间屋一间屋地找过去，却不见阿黛尔，也不见格里夫塔楼那残忍的主人。他又搜了一遍书房，穿进小客厅——里面摆着两人的餐具。桌布上洒湿了酒；一只杯子剩着半杯——可那两位对坐之人已无影无踪。他们必是从正门出去的——去了哪里？
他绷紧了身子站在那里，全副心神都凝在一个比他性命还紧要的问题上，忽听得一阵声响，从巴格兽窟的方向传来。随即，那头巨猿的身影出现在门口。它肩上一处伤口淌着血，站着的地方血珠一滴一滴往下落，两只大手攥着一件像是一团破布的东西。迈克尔一看，眼前的屋子在他眼中晃了一晃。

===Original===
The tattered, stained garment that Bhag held was the cloak that Adele Leamington had worn!
For a second Bhag glared at the man who he knew was his enemy, and then, dropping the cloak, he shrank back toward his quarters, his teeth bared.
Three times Michael’s automatic spat, and the great, manlike thing disappeared in a flash﻿—and the door closed with a click.
Knebworth had been a witness of the scene. It was he who ran forward and picked up the cloak that the ape had dropped.
“Yes, that was hers,” he said huskily, and a horrible thought chilled him.
Michael had opened the door of the den, and, pistol in hand, dashed through the opening. Knebworth dared not follow. He stood petrified, waiting, and then Michael reappeared.
===Chinese===
巴格攥着的那件破碎染污的衣裳，正是阿黛尔·利明顿出门穿的那件斗篷！
巴格瞪了那男人一秒钟——它知道那是它的仇敌——随后丢下斗篷，龇着牙朝自己的巢穴缩了回去。
迈克尔的自动手枪连吐三下火，那庞然的人形之物一闪而没——门咔哒一声合上了。
这一幕尼布沃斯全看在眼里。冲上去拾起猩猩丢下的斗篷的正是他。
"是，这是她的。"他嗓音沙哑地说，一个可怕的念头让他遍体生寒。
迈克尔已拉开兽窟的门，提着手枪冲了进去。尼布沃斯不敢跟进，僵立等候，随后迈克尔又现了身。

===Original===
“There’s nothing here,” he said.
“Nothing?” asked Jack Knebworth in a whisper. “Thank God!”
“Bhag has gone﻿—I think I may have hit him; there is a trail of blood, but I may not be responsible for that. He had been shot recently,” he pointed to stains on the floor. “He wasn’t shot when I saw him last.”
“Have you seen him before tonight?”
Michael nodded.
“For three nights he has been haunting Longvale’s house.”
“Longvale’s!”
===Chinese===
"这里什么也没有。"他说。
"什么也没有？"杰克·尼布沃斯耳语般地问，"谢天谢地！"
"巴格跑了——我想我兴许打中了它；有一道血迹，不过那也未必是我打的。它新近挨过枪。"他指了指地上的污渍，"我上回见到它时，它还没中过枪。"
"今晚之前你还见过它？"
迈克尔点了点头。
"接连三夜，它一直在朗维尔家附近出没。"
"朗维尔家！"

===Original===
Where was Adele? That was the one dominant question, the one thought uppermost in Michael Brixan’s mind. And where was the baronet? What was the meaning of that open door? None of the servants could tell him, and for some reason he saw that they were speaking the truth. Only Penne and the girl﻿—and this great ape﻿—knew, unless﻿—
He hurried back to where he had left a detective trying to revive the unconscious Stella Mendoza.
“She has passed from one fainting fit to another,” said the officer. “I can get nothing out of her except that once she said ‘Kill him, Adele.’ ”
“Then she has seen her!” said Michael.
One of the officers he had left outside to watch the building had a report to make. He had seen a dark figure climbing the wall and disappear apparently through the solid brickwork. A few minutes later it had come out again.
“That was Bhag,” said Michael. “I knew he was not here when we arrived. He must have come in through the opening while we were upstairs.”
===Chinese===
阿黛尔在哪儿？这是压倒一切的问题，是迈克尔·布里克森心里翻腾的头一桩事。那位从男爵又在哪里？那扇敞开的门是什么意思？仆役们没有一个说得上，而且不知怎的，他看得出他们说的是实话。只有彭尼和那姑娘——还有这头巨猿——知情，除非——
他匆匆折回先前留下的那名侦探处，那人正设法救醒不省人事的斯特拉·门多萨。
"她一阵晕厥接着一阵晕厥。"那警探说，"什么也问不出来，只有一回，她说了一句'杀了他，阿黛尔。'"
"那她见过她了！"迈克尔说。
留在外面监视宅子的警员中，有一个要作报告。他看见一条黑影攀墙而上，竟仿佛从整堵实心砖墙里钻了进去，几分钟后又钻了出来。
"那是巴格。"迈克尔说，"我们到的时候我就知道它不在宅里。它必是在我们上楼那会儿，从那洞口进来的。"

===Original===
The car that had carried Adele had been found. It was Stella’s, and at first Michael suspected that the girl was a party to the abduction. He learnt afterwards that, whilst the woman’s chauffeur had been in the kitchen, virtually a prisoner, Penne himself had driven the car to the girl’s house, and it was the sight of the machine, which she knew belonged to Stella, that had lulled any suspicions she may have had.
Michael was in a condition bordering upon frenzy. The Headhunter and his capture was insignificant compared with the safety of the girl.
“If I don’t find her I shall go mad,” he said.
Jack Knebworth had opened his lips to answer when there came a startling interruption. Borne on the still night air came a scream of agony which turned the director’s blood to ice.
“Help, help!”
Shrill as was the cry, Michael knew that it was the voice of a man, and knew that that man was Gregory Penne!
===Chinese===
载走阿黛尔的那辆汽车已经找到。那是斯特拉的车，起初迈克尔疑心那姑娘也是劫持的同谋。他后来才得知，趁她的司机被变相软禁在厨房里之际，彭尼亲自驾车去了姑娘的住处，而正是那辆车——她认得是斯特拉的——打消了她可能生出的任何疑心。
迈克尔此刻的状态已近癫狂。猎头者，抓不抓得住，比起姑娘的安危来，已无足轻重。
"再找不到她，我就要疯了。"他说。
杰克·尼布沃斯张口正要回答，一个骇人的声音打断了他。一声痛苦的惨叫划破寂静的夜空，导演听得血液凝成冰。
"救命，救命！"
那呼救声虽尖利，迈克尔却听出那是一个男人的嗓音，而且知道，那人就是格雷戈里·彭尼！

## VII The Swords and Bhag / VII 刀剑与巴格

===Original===
The little party moved away, leaving Michael alone with the baronet. For a period, Gregory Penne watched the girl, his eyes glittering; then he became aware of Michael’s presence and turned a cold, insolent stare upon the other.
“What are you?” he asked, looking the detective up and down.
“I’m an extra,” said Michael.
“An extra, eh? Sort of chorus boy? Put paint and powder on your face and all that sort of thing? What a life for a man!”
“There are worse,” said Michael, holding his antagonism in check.
===Chinese===
那一小群人走开了，只剩迈克尔和从男爵单独在一起。格雷戈里·彭尼盯着那姑娘看了一阵，眼里闪着光；随后他察觉到迈克尔在场，把冷冰冰、目中无人的目光转向他。
"你是干什么的？"他上下打量着侦探问道。
"我是临时演员，"迈克尔说。
"临时演员，嗯？合唱队小伙子那一类？往脸上涂脂抹粉什么的？一个大男人过这种日子！"
"还有更糟的呢，"迈克尔说，强压着心头的敌意。

===Original===
“Do you know that little girl—what’s her name, Leamington?” asked the baronet suddenly.
“I know her extremely well,” said Michael untruthfully.
“Oh, you do, eh?” said the master of Griff Towers with sudden amiability. “She’s a nice little thing. Quite a cut above the ordinary chorus girl. You might bring her along to dinner one night. She’d come with you, eh?”
The contortions of the puffy eyelids suggested to Michael that the man had winked. There was something about this gross figure that interested the scientist in Michael Brixan. He was elemental; an animal invested with a brain; and yet he must be something more than that if he had held a high administrative position under Government.
“Are you acting? If you’re not, you can come up and have a look at my swords,” said the man suddenly.
===Chinese===
"你认识那个小姑娘吗——她叫什么来着，利明顿？"从男爵突然问。
"熟得很，"迈克尔言不由衷地说。
"哦，熟得很，嗯？"格里夫塔楼的主人忽然和气起来，"她是个可人的小东西。比寻常的合唱队姑娘高出一大截。哪天晚上你可以带她来吃饭。她肯跟你来，嗯？"
那对浮肿眼皮的抽动让迈克尔明白，这人是在挤眼睛。这个臃肿粗蠢的人物身上，有某种东西勾起了迈克尔·布里克森身上那股科学家式的兴致。他是原始的造物；一头顶着脑子的兽；可他既然曾在政府手下担任过高位，就必定还远不止如此。
"你今天出镜吗？要是不出，可以上来看看我的刀剑，"那人突然说。

===Original===
Michael guessed that, for a reason of his own, probably because of his claim to be Adele’s friend, the man wished to cultivate the acquaintance.
“No, I’m not acting,” replied Michael.
And no invitation could have given him greater pleasure. Did their owner realize the fact, Michael Brixan had already made up his mind not to leave Griff Towers until he had inspected that peculiar collection.
“Yes, she’s a nice little girl.”
Penne returned to the subject immediately as they paced up the slope toward the house.
===Chinese===
迈克尔猜想，此人是出于自己的一番算盘——多半是因为他自称是阿黛尔的朋友——才有意结纳这份交情。
"我不出镜，"迈克尔答道。
再没有比这更叫他心花怒放的邀请了。哪怕主人没有意识到，迈克尔·布里克森其实早已打定主意：不亲眼查验过那批奇特的藏品，绝不离开格里夫塔楼。
"是啊，她是个可人的小姑娘。"
两人沿坡道朝宅子踱去，彭尼立刻又回到了这个话题。

===Original===
“As I say, a cut above chorus girls. Young, unsophisticated, virginal! You can have your sophisticated girls: there is no mystery to ’em! They revolt me. A girl should be like a spring flower. Give me the violet and the snowdrop: you can have a bushel of cabbage roses for one petal of the shy dears of the forest.”
Michael listened with a keen sense of nausea, and yet with an unusual interest, as the man rambled on. He said things which were sickening, monstrous. There were moments when Brixan found it difficult to keep his hands off the obscene figure that paced at his side; and only by adopting toward him the attitude with which the enthusiastic naturalist employs in his dealings with snakes, was he able to get a grip of himself.
The big entrance hall into which he was ushered was paved with earthen tiles, and, looking up at the stone walls, Michael had his first glimpse of the famous swords.
There were hundreds of them—poniards, scimitars, ancient swords of Japan, basket-hilted hangers, two-handed swords that had felt the grip of long-dead Crusaders.
“What do you think of ’em, eh?” Sir Gregory Penne spoke with the pride of an enthusiastic collector. “There isn’t one of them that could be duplicated, my boy; and they’re only the rag, tag and bobtail of my collection.”
===Chinese===
"就像我说的，比合唱队姑娘高出一截。年轻，不谙世事，冰清玉洁！那些世故的女人你尽管拿去：她们身上没有一点神秘！让我恶心。姑娘就该像春天的花。我只要紫罗兰和雪花莲：林子里那些羞答答的小宝贝，一片花瓣就能换你一蒲式耳的卷心玫瑰。"
那人絮絮地说下去，迈克尔听得一阵阵强烈作呕，却又怀着异乎寻常的兴趣。他说出的东西令人作呕、骇人听闻。有几回，布里克森几乎按捺不住，想对身边这具淫邪的行尸动手；全靠拿出热忱的博物学家对付毒蛇的那种态度，他才勉强控制住自己。
他被引进的大门厅铺着陶砖，抬眼望向石墙，迈克尔第一次瞥见了那些大名鼎鼎的刀剑。
足有几百件——匕首、弯刀、日本古剑、护手猎剑，还有早已作古的十字军骑士握过的双手巨剑。
"你觉得怎么样，嗯？"格雷戈里·彭尼爵士带着狂热收藏家的自豪说道，"这里面没有一件能找出第二件来，我的孩子；而这还只是我藏品的下脚料。"

===Original===
He led his visitor along a broad corridor, lighted by square windows set at intervals, and here again the walls were covered with shining weapons. Throwing open a door, Sir Gregory ushered the other into a large room which was evidently his library, though the books were few, and, so far as Michael could see at first glance, the conventional volumes that are to be found in the houses of the country gentry.
Over the mantelshelf were two great swords of a pattern which Michael did not remember having seen before.
“What do you think of those?”
Penne lifted one from the silver hook which supported it, and drew it from its scabbard.
“Don’t feel the edge unless you want to cut yourself. This would split a hair, but it would also cut you in two, and you would never know what had happened till you fell apart!”
===Chinese===
他领着客人沿一条宽阔的走廊走去，间隔镶嵌的方窗透着光，这里的墙上同样挂满了寒光闪闪的兵器。格雷戈里爵士推开一扇门，把客人让进一个大房间，那显然是他的书房，尽管书寥寥无几，而且迈克尔第一眼望去，尽是乡绅宅第里惯见的那几部装点门面的书。
壁炉架上方悬着两柄巨剑，样式迈克尔不记得见过。
"这两柄你觉得如何？"
彭尼从承托剑身的银钩上取下一柄，抽出剑鞘。
"别摸刃口，除非你想割了手。这剑能把头发劈成两半，也能把你拦腰斩断，等你身首分离都不知道出了什么事！"

===Original===
Suddenly his manner changed, and he almost snatched the sword from Michael’s hand, and, putting it back in its sheath, he hung it up.
“That is a Sumatran sword, isn’t it?”
“It comes from Borneo,” said the baronet shortly.
“The home of the headhunters.”
Sir Gregory looked round, his brows lowered.
“No,” he said, “it comes from Dutch Borneo.”
===Chinese===
他的态度突然一变，几乎是从迈克尔手里夺过剑，插回鞘中，挂了回去。
"那是苏门答腊剑，对吧？"
"它出自婆罗洲，"从男爵简短地说。
"猎头人的老家。"
格雷戈里爵士环顾左右，眉头压了下来。
"不，"他说，"它出自荷属婆罗洲。"

===Original===
Evidently there was something about this weapon which aroused unpleasant memories. He glowered for a long time in silence into the little fire that was burning on the hearth.
“I killed the man who owned that,” he said at last, and it struck Michael that he was speaking more to himself than to his visitor. “At least, I hope I killed him. I hope so!”
He glanced round, and Michael Brixan could have sworn there was apprehension in his eyes.
“Sit down, What’s-your-name,” he commanded, pointing to a low settee. “We’ll have a drink.”
He pushed a bell, and, to Michael’s astonishment, the summons was answered by an undersized native, a little copper-coloured man, naked to the waist. Gregory gave an order in a language which was unintelligible to Michael—he guessed, by its sibilants, it was Malayan—and the servant, with a quick salaam, disappeared, and came back almost instantly with a tray containing a large decanter and two thin glasses.
“I have no white servants—can’t stand ’em,” said Penne, taking the contents of his glass at a gulp. “I like servants who don’t steal and don’t gossip. You can lick ’em if they misbehave, and there’s no trouble. I got this fellow last year in Sumatra, and he’s the best butler I’ve had.”
===Chinese===
这兵器显然勾起了他不愉快的记忆。他阴沉着脸，久久默然瞪着炉膛里的一小簇火。
"我杀了这剑原先的主人，"他终于开口，迈克尔察觉这话与其说是对客人讲，不如说是对他自己讲的，"至少，我希望我杀了他。但愿如此！"
他四下看了一眼，迈克尔·布里克森几乎可以发誓，他眼里有一丝惊惶。
"坐下吧，你叫什么来着的，"他指着一张矮长椅吩咐道，"咱们喝一杯。"
他按了铃，令迈克尔吃惊的是，应声而来的是个矮小的土著，一个铜色皮肤、赤着上身的男人。格雷戈里用一种迈克尔听不懂的语言吩咐了一句——听那咝咝的辅音，他猜是马来语——仆人快速行了个额手礼，转身不见，几乎立刻就端着托盘回来，上面是一大瓶酒和两只细薄的酒杯。
"我不用白人仆人——受不了，"彭尼说着，把杯中酒一仰而尽，"我喜欢不偷东西、不多嘴的仆人。他们不听话你尽管抽，绝不惹麻烦。这家伙是我去年在苏门答腊弄来的，是我用过最好的管家。"

===Original===
“Do you go to Borneo every year?” asked Michael.
“I go almost every year,” said the other. “I’ve got a yacht: she’s lying at Southampton now. If I didn’t get out of this cursed country once a year, I’d go mad. There’s nothing here, nothing! Have you ever met that dithering old fool, Longvale? Knebworth said you were going on to him—pompous old ass, who lives in the past and dresses like an advertisement for somebody’s whisky. Have another?”
“I haven’t finished this yet,” said Michael with a smile, and his eyes went up to the sword above the mantelpiece. “Have you had that very long? It looks modern.”
“It isn’t,” snapped the other. “Modern! It’s three hundred years old if it’s a day. I’ve only had it a year.” Again he changed the subject abruptly. “I like you, What’s-your-name. I like people or I dislike them instantly. You’re the sort of fellow who’d do well in the East. I’ve made two millions there. The East is full of wonder, full of unbelievable things.” He screwed his head round and fixed Michael with a glittering eye. “Full of good servants,” he said slowly. “Would you like to meet the perfect servant?”
There was something peculiar in his tone, and Michael nodded.
“Would you like to see the slave who never asks questions and never disobeys, who has no love but love of me”—he thumped himself on the chest—“no hate but for the people I hate—my trusty—Bhag?”
He rose, and, crossing to his table, turned a little switch that Michael had noticed attached to the side of the desk. As he did so, a part of the panelled wall at the farther end of the room swung open. For a second Michael saw nothing, and then there emerged, blinking into the daylight, a most sinister, a most terrifying figure. And Michael Brixan had need for all his self-control to check the exclamation that rose to his lips.
===Chinese===
"您每年都去婆罗洲？"迈克尔问。
"差不多年年去，"对方说，"我有一艘游艇：现在就停在南安普敦（Southampton）。要不是一年逃出这个可咒的国家一回，我非疯不可。这儿什么都没有，什么都没有！你见过朗维尔那个哆哆嗦嗦的老傻子吗？尼布沃斯说你们接着要去他那儿——自命不凡的老蠢驴，活在过去里，穿得像哪家威士忌的活广告。再来一杯？"
"这杯还没喝完呢，"迈克尔微笑着说，目光抬向壁炉架上方的那柄剑，"它您得了很久了吗？看着挺现代的。"
"现代个屁，"对方厉声道，"三百年都不止！我到手才一年。"他又猛地岔开话题，"我喜欢你，你叫什么来着的。我看人要么喜欢要么讨厌，一眼定夺。你是那种在东方能混出名堂的家伙。我在那儿挣了两百万。东方遍地是奇迹，遍地是难以置信的东西。"他拧过脑袋，用一只放光的眼睛盯住迈克尔，"遍地是好仆人，"他缓缓地说，"想不想见见最完美的仆人？"
他话音里有种古怪的意味，迈克尔点了点头。
"想不想看看那个从不发问、从不违逆的奴隶？他没有爱，只爱我一个"——他捶了捶自己的胸口——"没有恨，只恨我恨的人——我的心腹——巴格（Bhag）？"
他站起身，走到桌前，扳动了一个迈克尔先前留意到装在书桌侧面的开关。随着他的动作，房间尽头那面镶板墙的一部分应声旋开。一时间迈克尔什么也没看见，随后，一个身影眨着眼睛从暗处走进天光里——一个再阴森不过、再骇人不过的身影。迈克尔·布里克森用上了全部的自制力，才把冲到嘴边的一声惊呼压了回去。

## XXVIII The Tower / XXVIII 高塔

===Original===
Michael was a little perturbed in mind. He took a more serious view of the closed car than did the girl, and the invitation to the “pretty lady” to step inside was particularly disturbing. Since the events of the past few days it had been necessary to withdraw the detective who was watching the girl’s house, and he decided to reestablish the guard, employing a local officer for the purpose.
After he had driven Adele home, he went to the police station and made his wishes known; but it was too late to see the chief constable, and the subordinate officer in charge did not wish to take the responsibility of detaching an officer for the purpose. It was only when Michael threatened to call the chief on the telephone that he reluctantly drew on his reserves and put a uniformed officer to patrol the street.
Back again at Knebworth’s house, Michael examined the two articles which the girl had found. Butyl chloride was a drug and a particularly violent one. What use would the Headhunter have for that, he wondered.
As for the handcuff, he examined it again. Terrific force must have been employed to snap the connecting links. This was a mystery to him, and he gave it up with a sense of annoyance at his own incompetence.
Before going to bed he received a phone message from Inspector Lyle, who was watching Griff Towers. There was nothing new to report, and apparently life was pursuing its normal round. The inspector had been invited into the house by Sir Gregory, who had told him that Bhag was still missing.
===Chinese===
迈克尔心里有些不安。他对那辆封闭汽车的看法，比姑娘要严重得多，而那句请"漂亮姑娘"上车的招呼，尤其叫他心惊。由于前几日的种种变故，原先监视姑娘住宅的那名侦探不得不撤走，他决定重新布岗，改用一名本地警官担当此任。
他送阿黛尔回家之后，便去警察局说明来意；可要见郡警察局长已经太晚，值班的下属警官又不愿承担抽调人手的责任。直到迈克尔扬言要打电话给局长本人，他才老大不情愿地动用后备警力，派了一名穿制服的警官去巡那条街。
回到尼布沃斯家，迈克尔又察看了姑娘找到的那两样东西。丁基氯是一种麻醉药，还是药性特别猛烈的一种。猎头者要这东西有什么用呢，他想不透。
至于那副手铐，他又端详了一遍。要挣断连接的链环，非得有惊人的膂力不可。这对他是个谜，他只得放下，一面恼恨自己无能。
临睡前，他接到莱尔探长的电话，对方正监视着格里夫塔楼。无可奉报的新情况，那边的日子显然一切照旧。格雷戈里爵士请探长进过宅子，还告诉他巴格仍然下落不明。

===Original===
“I’ll keep you there tonight,” said Michael. “Tomorrow we will lift the watch. Scotland Yard is satisfied that Sir Gregory had nothing to do with Foss’s death.”
A grunt from the other end of the phone expressed the inspector’s disagreement with that view.
“He’s in it somehow,” he said. “By the way, I’ve found a bloodstained derby hat in the field outside the grounds. It has the name of Chi Li Stores, Tjandi, inside.”
This was news indeed.
“Let me see it in the morning,” said Michael after long cogitation.
Soon after breakfast the next morning the hat came and was inspected. Knebworth, who had heard most of the story from Michael, examined the new clue curiously.
===Chinese===
"今夜你再守一晚，"迈克尔说，"明天我们就撤哨。苏格兰场已经认定，福斯之死与格雷戈里爵士无关。"
电话那头哼了一声，表达了探长对这一判断的不以为然。
"他总归脱不了干系。"他说，"对了，我在园子外面的田里捡到一顶染血的圆顶硬礼帽。帽子里面印着'芝厘商店，强迪（Chi Li Stores, Tjandi）'的字样。"
这可是实打实的新闻。
"明早拿给我看看。"迈克尔思忖了半晌，说道。
第二天早晨，早饭后不久帽子送到了，众人查验一番。尼布沃斯已从迈克尔口中听说了事情的大概，好奇地端详着这条新线索。

===Original===
“If the coon wore Lawley’s hat when he arrived at Mr. Longvale’s, where, in the name of fate, did the change take place? It must have been somewhere between the Towers and the old man’s house, unless﻿—”
“Unless what?” asked Michael. He had a great respect for Knebworth’s shrewd judgment.
“Unless the change took place at Sir Gregory’s house. You see that, although it is bloodstained, there are no cuts in it. Which is rum.”
“Very rum,” agreed Mike ruefully. “And yet, if my first theory was correct, the explanation is simple.”
He did not tell his host what his theory was.
===Chinese===
"要是那马来佬到达朗维尔先生家时戴的是劳利的帽子，那看在老天的份上，这调换是在哪儿发生的？必定在塔楼和老头儿家之间的什么地方，除非——"
"除非什么？"迈克尔问。他对尼布沃斯敏锐的判断力向来十分敬重。
"除非这调换就发生在格雷戈里爵士的宅子里。你看，帽子虽然染了血，却没有破口。这事邪门。"
"邪门得很。"迈克懊然附和道，"不过，倘若我最初那套推断成立，解释倒也简单。"
他却没有告诉主人，他的推断究竟是什么。

===Original===
Accompanying Knebworth to the studio, he watched the charabanc drive off, wishing that he had some excuse and the leisure to accompany them on their expedition. It was a carefree, cheery throng, and its very association was a tonic to his spirits.
He put through his usual call to London. There was no news. There was really no reason why he should not go, he decided recklessly; and as soon as his decision was taken his car was pounding on the trail of the joy wagon.
He saw the tower a quarter of an hour before he came up to it: a squat, ancient building, for all the world like an inordinately high sheepfold. When he came up to them the charabanc had been drawn on to the grass, and the company was putting the finishing touches to its makeup. Adele he did not see at once﻿—she was changing in a little canvas tent, whilst Jack Knebworth and the camera man wrangled over light and position.
Michael had too much intelligence to butt in at this moment, and strolled up to the tower, examining the curious courses which generation after generation had added to the original foundations. He knew very little of masonry, but he was able to detect the Roman portion of the wall, and thought he saw the place where Saxon builders had filled in a gap.
One of the hands was fixing a ladder up which Roselle was to pass. The story which was being filmed was that of a girl who, starting life in the chorus, had become the wife of a nobleman with archaic ideas. The poor but honest young man who had loved her in her youth (Michael gathered that a disconsolate Reggie Connolly played this part) was ever at hand to help her; and now, when shut up in a stone room of the keep, it was he who was to rescue her.
===Chinese===
他随尼布沃斯到了摄影棚，望着游览大巴（charabanc）驶远，心里巴不得有个由头、有份闲暇，好随他们一同远征。那是一群无忧无虑的快活人，光是同他们做伴，就足以提神醒脑。
他照例给伦敦挂了电话。没有消息。他豁出去一想：实在没有理由不去。主意一定，他的车便追着那辆欢乐之车的辙印飞驰而去。
离塔还有一刻钟路程，他就望见了它：一座矮墩墩的古老建筑，活像个高得出奇的羊圈。等他赶到，大巴已开上草地，全班人马正在给妆容收尾。阿黛尔一时没看见——她正在一顶小帆布帐里换装，杰克·尼布沃斯则同摄影师为光线和机位争得不可开交。
迈克尔识趣得很，没有在这当口插嘴，信步走向高塔，细细打量一代又一代人在原有地基上垒起的奇特砖层。他对砌石之术所知甚少，却认得出墙体上的罗马部分，还自认看出了撒克逊工匠填补缺口的位置。
一名场务正在架一架梯子，罗塞尔（Roselle）待会儿要从那儿上去。正在拍的故事讲的是一个出身歌队的姑娘，后来嫁给了一位满脑子古老公爵做派的贵族。她年轻时爱过的那个贫寒却诚实的青年（迈克尔听得出来，垂头丧气的雷吉·康诺利（Reggie Connolly）演的就是这个角色）总在她身边随时效劳；如今她被关在城堡的一间石屋里，前去搭救的自然又是他。

===Original===
The actual castle tower had been shot in Arundel. Old Griff Tower was to serve for a closeup, showing the girl descending from her prison in the arms of her lover, by the aid of a rope of knotted sheets.
“It’s going to be deuced awkward getting down,” said Reggie lugubriously. “Of course, they’ve got a rope inside the sheet, so there’s no chance of it breaking. But Miss Leamington is really fearfully awfully heavy! You try and lift her yourself, old thing, and see how you like it!”
Nothing would have given Michael greater pleasure than to carry out the instructions literally.
“It’s too robust a part for me, it is really,” bleated Reggie. “I’m not a cave man, I’m not indeed! I’ve told Knebworth that it isn’t the job for me. And besides, why do they want a closeup? Why don’t they make a dummy that I could carry and sling about? And why doesn’t she come down by herself?”
“It’s dead easy,” said Knebworth, who had walked up and overheard the latter part of the conversation. “Miss Leamington will hold the rope and take the weight off you. All you’ve got to do is to look brave and pretty.”
===Chinese===
真正的城堡塔楼已在阿伦德尔拍过。老格里芬塔这回是用来拍特写：姑娘靠一条结了扣的床单绳，从囚室里下来，落进情郎的怀抱。
"抱下去这活儿可要命地别扭。"雷吉哭丧着脸说。"当然啦，床单里缝了绳子，绝没有断的可能。可利明顿小姐实在重得吓死人！老兄，你自己去抱抱她试试，看你还乐意不乐意！"
天底下再没有比照字面执行这道指令更让迈克尔快活的事了。
"这角色对我太猛了，真是的。"雷吉哀声叫道。"我又不是原始人，真的不是！我跟尼布沃斯说了，这活儿不适合我。再说了，干吗非要拍特写？干吗不做个假人，让我扛着甩来甩去？她干吗不自己下来？"
"简单得很。"尼布沃斯走了过来，听见了后半段对话，说道。"利明顿小姐会抓着绳子，把分量卸掉。你只消显出又勇猛又漂亮的样子就成。"

===Original===
“That’s all very well,” grumbled Reggie, “but climbing down ropes is not the job I was engaged for. We all have our likes and our dislikes, and that’s one of my dislikes.”
“Try it,” said Jack laconically.
The property man had fixed the rope to an iron staple which he had driven to the inside of the tower, the top of which would not be shown in the picture. The actual descent had been acted by “doubles” in Arundel on a long shot: it was only the closeup that Jack needed. The first rehearsal nearly ended in disaster. With a squeak, Connolly let go his burden, and the girl would have fallen but for her firm grip on the rope.
“Try it again,” stormed Jack. “Remember you’re playing a man’s part. Young Coogan would hold her better than that!”
They tried again, with greater success, and after the third rehearsal, when poor Reggie was in a state of exhaustion﻿—
===Chinese===
"话是这么说，"雷吉嘟囔道，"可顺绳子爬下去，不是我签约时说好的活计。人各有所好，各有所恶，这就是我所恶的一桩。"
"试试。"杰克言简意赅地说。
道具员已把绳子拴在一枚钉进塔身内侧的铁搭扣上，画面里不会拍到塔顶。实拍的下坠动作已在阿伦德尔由"替身"在远景里演过：杰克需要的只是特写。头一回排练差点以灾难收场。康诺利吱的一声撒了手，若不是姑娘抓绳抓得紧，早摔下去了。
"再来一遍！"杰克大发雷霆，"记住你演的是个男子汉。小库根（Coogan）抱得都比这像样！"
他们又试了一遍，这回顺利得多。到第三遍排练完毕，可怜的雷吉已是筋疲力尽——

===Original===
“Camera!” said Knebworth shortly, and then began the actual taking of the picture.
Whatever his other drawbacks were, and whatever his disadvantages, there was no doubt that Connolly was an artist. Racked with agony at this unusual exertion though he was, he could smile sweetly into the upturned face of the girl, whilst the camera, fixed upon a collapsible platform, clicked encouragingly as it was lowered to keep pace with the escaping lovers. They touched ground, and with one last languishing look at the girl, Connolly posed for the final three seconds.
“That’ll do,” said Jack.
Reggie sat down heavily.
“My heavens!” he wailed, feeling his arms painfully. “I’ll never do that again, I won’t really. I’ve had as much of that stuff as ever I’m going to have, Mr. Knebworth. It was terrible! I thought I should die!”
===Chinese===
"开机！"尼布沃斯简短地喊道，实拍就此开始。
无论康诺利还有多少短处、多少不足，他无疑是一位艺术家。尽管这番不寻常的消耗折磨得他死去活来，他仍能对着姑娘仰起的脸甜美地微笑；架在折叠平台上的摄影机一路下降、追着这对逃命的恋人，咔咔地响着为他鼓劲。两人落到地面，康诺利朝姑娘投去最后一记缠绵的目光，为最后三秒钟摆好了姿势。
"行了。"杰克说。
雷吉一屁股坐倒在地。
"我的老天！"他哀号着，痛苦地揉着两条胳膊。"这种事我再也不干了，真的不干了。这种苦头我算受够了，尼布沃斯先生。太可怕了！我以为自己要死了！"

===Original===
“Well, you didn’t,” said Jack good-humouredly. “Now have a rest, you boys and girls, and then we’ll shoot the escape.”
The camera was moved off twenty or thirty yards, and whilst Reggie Connolly writhed in agony on the ground, the girl walked over to Michael.
“I’m glad that’s over,” she said thankfully. “Poor Mr. Connolly! The awful language he was using inside nearly made me laugh, and that would have meant that we should have had to take it all over again. But it wasn’t easy,” she added.
Her own arm was bruised, and the rope had rubbed raw a little place on her wrist. Michael had an insane desire to kiss the raw skin, but restrained himself.
“What did you think of me? Did I look anything approaching graceful? I felt like a bundle of straw!”
“You looked﻿—wonderful!” he said fervently, and she shot a quick glance at him and dropped her eyes.
===Chinese===
"喏，你不是没死嘛。"杰克和气地说，"现在歇一歇吧，姑娘小伙们，回头我们拍逃亡那场。"
摄影机挪开二三十码，雷吉·康诺利还在地上痛苦扭动，姑娘已朝迈克尔走了过来。
"总算熬过去了，真谢天谢地。"她说。"可怜的康诺利先生！他在里面骂的那些难听话，差点把我逗笑，一笑可就得整个重来了。不过这差事实在不容易。"她补了一句。
她自己的胳膊磕青了，绳子还在腕子上磨破了一小块皮。迈克尔生出一股疯狂的冲动，想去吻那块破皮，到底忍住了。
"你觉得我演得如何？看上去有没有半点优雅的意思？我自己觉得像一捆稻草！"
"你看上去——美极了！"他热切地说。她飞快瞟了他一眼，垂下了眼帘。

===Original===
“Perhaps you’re prejudiced,” she said demurely.
“I have that feeling too,” said Michael. “What is inside?” He pointed.
“Inside the tower? Nothing, except a lot of rock and wild bush, and a pathetic dwarf tree. I loved it.”
He laughed.
“Just now you said you were glad it was over. I presume you were referring to the play and not to the interior of the tower?”
She nodded, a twinkle in her eye.
===Chinese===
"也许你带着偏见。"她端庄地说。
"我也有同感。"迈克尔说，"里面是什么？"他指了指塔。
"塔里面？什么也没有，只有一堆乱石、一片野树丛，还有一棵可怜巴巴的小矮树。我喜欢得很。"
他笑了。
"你刚才说总算熬过去了。我猜你说的是拍戏，不是塔的里面吧？"
她点了点头，眼里闪着顽皮的光。

===Original===
“Mr. Knebworth says he may have to take a night shot if he’s not satisfied with the day picture. Poor Mr. Connolly! He’ll throw up his part.”
At that moment Jack Knebworth’s voice was heard.
“Don’t take the ladder, Collins,” he shouted. “Put it down on the grass behind the tower. I may have to come up here tonight, so you can leave anything that won’t be hurt by the weather, and collect it again in the morning.”
Adele made a little face.
“I was afraid he would,” she said. “Not that I mind very much﻿—it’s rather fun. But Mr. Connolly’s nervousness communicates itself in some way. I wish you were playing that part.”
===Chinese===
"尼布沃斯先生说，要是白天拍的不满意，可能还得来个夜景镜头。可怜的康诺利先生！他会撂挑子的。"
正在此时，传来了杰克·尼布沃斯的声音。
"梯子别搬走，柯林斯（Collins）。"他喊道，"放在塔后头的草地上。今晚我可能还得来，凡是不怕风吹雨淋的，都可以留下，明早再来收。"
阿黛尔做了个小鬼脸。
"我就怕他来这一手。"她说，"倒不是我很在乎——其实挺好玩的。可康诺利先生的紧张不知怎么就会传染给人。我倒希望你演那个角色。"

===Original===
“I wish to heaven I were!” said Michael, with such sincerity in his voice that she coloured.
Jack Knebworth came toward them.
“Did you leave anything up there, Adele?” he asked, pointing to the tower.
“No, Mr. Knebworth,” she said in surprise.
“Well, what’s that?”
He pointed to something round that showed above the edge of the tower top.
===Chinese===
"我巴不得如此！"迈克尔说，语气里的诚挚让她红了脸。
杰克·尼布沃斯朝他们走来。
"你把什么东西落在上头了吗，阿黛尔？"他指着塔问。
"没有啊，尼布沃斯先生。"她讶然道。
"那这是什么？"
他指着塔顶边缘上方露出的一个圆乎乎的东西。

===Original===
“Why, it’s moving!” he gasped.
As he spoke a head came slowly into view. It was followed by a massive pair of hairy shoulders, and then a leg was thrown over the wall.
It was Bhag!
His tawny hair was white with dust, his face was powdered grotesquely. All these things Michael noticed. Then, as the creature put out his hand to steady himself, Michael saw that each wrist was encircled by the half of a broken pair of handcuffs!
===Chinese===
"哎呀，它在动！"他倒吸一口气。
说话间，一颗脑袋慢慢探了出来。随后是一副魁伟多毛的肩膀，接着一条腿跨过了墙头。
是巴格！
它黄褐色的毛发蒙着尘土，白花花一片，脸上扑满了白粉，滑稽古怪。这些迈克尔都看在眼里。随后，那畜生伸出手去扶稳身子，迈克尔看见它的两只手腕上，各扣着半副挣断的手铐！

## XIII The Trap That Failed / XIII 失手的陷阱

===Original===
And then Michael heard a faint creak in one corner of the room. It was followed by the almost imperceptible sound of bare feet on the thick pile carpet, and the noise of quick breathing.
He did not hesitate. Feeling again for the keyhole of the shutter, he pulled out his pistol and fired twice at the lock. The sound of the explosion was deafening in the confined space of the room. It must have had an electrical effect upon the intruder, for when, with a wrench, the shutter opened, and at a touch the white blind sprang up, flooding with light the big, ornate room, it was empty.
Almost immediately afterwards the door opened through which the baronet had passed. If he had been panic-stricken before, his condition was now pitiable.
“What’s that? What’s that?” he whimpered. “Did somebody shoot?”
===Chinese===
接着，迈克尔听见房间一角传来轻微的吱呀声。随后是赤脚踩过厚绒地毯那几不可闻的声响，还有急促的呼吸。
他毫不迟疑。摸到窗板的锁眼，他拔出手枪朝锁开了两枪。枪声在密闭的房间里震耳欲聋。这对闯入者想必如电击一般，因为等他一使劲拉开窗板，一碰百叶窗便弹了上去，晨光灌满这间华丽的大房时，屋里已空无一人。
紧接着，从男爵出去的那扇门开了。如果他先前已是惊惶失措，此刻的样子简直可怜。
"怎么了？怎么了？"他呜咽道，"有人开枪？"

===Original===
“Somebody shot,” said Michael calmly, “and I was the somebody. And the gentlemen you sent into the room to settle accounts with me are very lucky that I confined my firing practice to the lock of your shutter, Penne.”
He saw something white on the ground, and, crossing the room with quick strides, picked it up. It was a scarf of coarse silk, and he smelt it.
“Somebody dropped this in their hurry,” he said. “I guess it was to be used.”
“My dear fellow, I assure you I didn’t know.”
“How is the interesting invalid?” asked Michael with a curl of his lip. “The lunatic lady who screams?”
===Chinese===
"是有人开了枪，"迈克尔平静地说，"那人就是我。还有，你派进屋来跟我算账的那几位先生，实在运气不错——我只在你窗板的锁上练了练枪法，彭尼。"
他看见地上有个白东西，几大步跨过房间，捡了起来。那是一条粗绸围巾，他闻了闻。
"有人走得仓促，落下了这个，"他说，"我猜它是预备派用场的。"
"亲爱的伙计，我向你保证，我事先不知道。"
"那位有意思的病人怎么样了？"迈克尔撇着嘴问，"就是那位会尖叫的疯女士？"

===Original===
The man fingered his trembling lips for a moment as though he were trying to control them.
“She’s all right. It was as I—as I thought,” he said; “she had some sort of fit.”
Michael eyed him pensively.
“I’d like to see her, if I may,” he said.
“You can’t.” Penne’s voice was loud, defiant. “You can’t see anybody! What the hell do you mean by coming into my house at this hour of the morning and damaging my property? I’ll have this matter reported to Scotland Yard, and I’ll get the coat off your back, my man! Some of you detectives think you own the earth, but I’ll show you you don’t!”
The blustering voice rose to a roar. He was smothering his fear in weak anger, Michael thought, and looked up at the swords above the mantelpiece. Following the direction of his eyes, Sir Gregory wilted, and again his manner changed.
===Chinese===
那人用手指按了按哆嗦的嘴唇，仿佛想让它安分下来。
"她没事。跟我想的一样——跟我料想的一样，"他说，"她犯了点病。"
迈克尔若有所思地打量着他。
"要是可以，我想见见她，"他说。
"不行。"彭尼嗓门大起来，带着挑衅。"你谁也别想见！一大早闯进我家，还毁坏我的财物，你他妈到底是什么意思？这事我要报告苏格兰场，剥了你的皮，我的伙计！你们这帮侦探，有的以为自己天下都是自家的，我倒要让你见识见识未必！"
咆哮的嗓门升成了吼。他这是拿色厉内荏的怒火捂住恐惧，迈克尔想道，一面抬眼望向壁炉架上方的刀剑。顺着他的视线看去，格雷戈里爵士顿时蔫了，态度又变了一副。

===Original===
“My dear fellow, why exasperate me? I’m the nicest man in the world if you only treat me right. You’ve got crazy ideas about me, you have indeed!”
Michael did not argue. He walked slowly down the passage and out to meet the first sector of a blazing sun. As he reached the door he turned to the man.
“I cannot insist upon searching your house because I have not a warrant, as you know, and, by the time I’d got a warrant, there would be nothing to find. But you look out, my friend!” He waved a warning finger at the man. “I hate dragging in classical allusions, but I should advise you to look up a lady in mythology who was known to the Greeks as Adrastia!”
And with this he left, walking down the drive, watched with eyes of despair by a pale-faced girl from the upper window of the tower, whilst Sir Gregory went back to his library and, by much diligent searching, discovered that Adrastia was another name for Nemesis.
===Chinese===
"亲爱的伙计，何必惹恼我呢？只要你好好待我，我是天底下最好的人。你对我有一堆荒唐念头，真是的！"
迈克尔不与之争辩。他缓步沿走廊走出去，迎头撞上一轮喷薄旭日的第一道光。走到门口，他回身对那人说：
"我没法坚持搜你的宅子，因为你知道，我没有搜查令，等我拿到搜查令，早没什么可搜的了。不过你自己当心，我的朋友！"他冲那人晃了晃警告的手指，"我最不爱掉书袋，但还是奉劝你去查一查神话里的一位女士——希腊人管她叫阿德拉斯特亚（Adrastia）！"
说完他便走了，沿车道而去；塔楼高层的一扇窗前，一个脸色苍白的姑娘用绝望的眼睛目送他远去，而格雷戈里爵士回到书房，一番勤勉的查阅之后，发现阿德拉斯特亚正是涅墨西斯（Nemesis）的别名。

===Original===
Michael was back at the Dower House in time for breakfast. It was no great tribute to his charm that his absence had passed unnoticed—or so it appeared, though Adele had marked his disappearance, and had been the first to note his return.
Jack Knebworth was in his most cheery mood. The scenes had been, he thought, most successful.
“I can’t tell, of course, until I get back to the laboratory and develop the pictures; but so far as young Leamington is concerned, she’s wonderful. I hate predicting at this early stage, but I believe that she’s going to be a great artiste.”
“You didn’t expect her to be?” said Michael in surprise.
Jack laughed scornfully.
===Chinese===
迈克尔赶回遗孀宅，正赶上早餐。他不在竟无人察觉——至少表面如此——这对他的人缘实在算不得恭维，尽管阿黛尔留意到了他的失踪，也第一个注意到他回来了。
杰克·尼布沃斯心情正好。他觉得那几场戏拍得相当成功。
"当然，得等回洗印房把片子洗出来才作数；可就小利明顿而言，她妙极了。我不爱这么早下断语，可我相信她将成为一位了不起的艺术家。"
"您原本没料到她会这样？"迈克尔诧异地说。
杰克轻蔑地笑了。

===Original===
“I was very annoyed with Mendoza, and when I took this outfit on location, I did so quite expecting that I should have to return and retake the picture with Mendoza in the cast. Film stars aren’t born, they’re made; they’re made by bitter experience, patience and suffering. They have got to pass through stages of stark inefficiency, during which they’re liable to be discarded, before they win out. Your girl has skipped all the intervening phases, and has won at the first time of asking.”
“When you talk about ‘my girl,’ ” said Michael carefully, “will you be good enough to remember that I have the merest and most casual interest in the lady?”
“If you’re not a liar,” said Jack Knebworth, “you’re a piece of cheese!”
“What chance has she as a film artiste?” asked Michael, anxious to turn the subject.
Knebworth ruffled his white hair.
===Chinese===
"那阵子我正跟门多萨怄气，带班子出外景时，我满心以为还得回去请门多萨入组重拍。电影明星不是生出来的，是造出来的；是靠惨痛的教训、耐心和磨难造出来的。他们必得穿过一段彻底不入流的阶段——那阵子随时可能被一脚踢开——然后才能出人头地。你那位姑娘把中间这些阶段全跳了过去，头一回开口就赢了。"
"您开口闭口'你那位姑娘'，"迈克尔斟酌着字句说，"可否劳驾记住，我对那位女士的兴趣，淡薄得不能再淡薄，随便得不能再随便？"
"你要不是骗子，"杰克·尼布沃斯说，"就是一块奶酪！"
"她当电影艺术家有多少前途？"迈克尔急于岔开话题，问道。
尼布沃斯抓乱了一头白发。

===Original===
“Precious little,” he said. “There isn’t a chance for a girl in England. That’s a horrible thing to say, but it’s true. You can count the so-called English stars on the fingers of one hand; they’ve only a local reputation and they’re generally married to the producer. What chance has an outsider got of breaking into the movies? And even if they break in, it’s not much good to them. Production in this country is streets behind production either in America or in Germany. It is even behind the French, though the French films are nearly the dullest in the world. The British producer has no ideas of his own; he can adopt and adapt the stunts, the tricks of acting, the methods of lighting, that he sees in foreign films at trade shows; and, with the aid of an American cameraman, he can produce something which might have been produced a couple of years ago at Hollywood. It’s queer, because England has never been left behind as she has been in the cinema industry. France started the motorcar industry: today, England makes the finest motorcar in the world. America started aviation: today, the British aeroplanes have no superior. And yet, with all the example before them, with all the immense profits which are waiting to be made, in the past twenty years England has not produced one film star of international note, one film picture with an international reputation.”
It was a subject upon which he was prepared to enlarge, and did enlarge, throughout the journey back to Chichester.
“The cinema industry is in the hands of showmen all the world over, but in England it is in the hands of peep-showmen, as against the Barnums of the States. No, there’s no chance for your little friend, not in this country. If the picture I’m taking makes a hit in America—yes. She’ll be playing at Hollywood in twelve months’ time in an English story—directed by Americans!”
In the outer lobby of his office he found a visitor waiting for him, and gave her a curt and steely good morning.
===Chinese===
"少得可怜，"他说，"在英国，姑娘家没有机会。这话难听，却是实情。所谓英国明星，一只手数得过来；无非地方上有点名气，而且多半嫁给了制片人。一个外来者想打进电影界，机会在哪里？就算打进去，也没什么好处。这个国家的制片，比美国、比德国都差着好几条街。甚至比法国还落后，虽说法国电影几乎算全世界最沉闷的。英国制片人自己没有点子；他只会把行业放映会上看到的外国片里的噱头、表演技巧、布光手法搬过来改一改；再靠一位美国摄影师帮忙，捣鼓出好莱坞两年前就可能拍出来的东西。怪就怪在，英国从来没有在哪一行像在电影业这样落伍过。汽车工业是法国起的头：如今英国造出全世界最好的汽车。航空是美国起的头：如今英国飞机举世无双。然而榜样摆在眼前，泼天的利润等人来赚，过去二十年里，英国却没有出过一位有国际声望的电影明星，没有出过一部有国际声誉的影片。"
这个题目他本就准备大加发挥，回奇切斯特的一路也确实发挥了个够。
"电影业在哪儿都攥在耍把式的手里，可在英格兰，它攥在拉洋片的手里，对面美国可是巴纳姆（Barnum）那样的人物。不行，你那位小朋友没有机会，在这个国家没有。要是我这部片子在美国打响——那另说。十二个月后她就会在好莱坞演一部英国故事片——导演还得是美国人！"
在外间办公室的门厅里，他发现一位访客在等他，便冷冰冰、硬邦邦地回了声早安。

===Original===
“I want to see you, Mr. Knebworth,” said Stella Mendoza, with a smile at the leading man who had followed Knebworth into his office.
“You want to see me, do you? Why, you can see me now. What do you want?”
She was pulling at a lace handkerchief with a pretty air of penitence and confusion. Jack was not impressed. He himself had taught her all that handkerchief stuff.
“I’ve been very silly, Mr. Knebworth, and I’ve come to ask your pardon. Of course, it was wrong to keep the boys and girls waiting, and I really am sorry. Shall I come in the morning? Or I can start today?”
A faint smile trembled at the corner of the director’s big mouth.
===Chinese===
"我要见您，尼布沃斯先生，"斯特拉·门多萨说，又冲跟进办公室的男主角嫣然一笑。
"你要见我，是吗？嗬，现在不就见着了。想干什么？"
她揪着一块花边手帕，一副又悔怯又慌乱的媚态。杰克不为所动。手帕这一套把式，正是他亲口教给她的。
"我那回太傻了，尼布沃斯先生，我是来请您原谅的。让大家干等当然不对，我实在抱歉。要我明早来吗？要不今天就能开工？"
导演的大嘴角颤出一丝浅笑。

===Original===
“You needn’t come in the morning and you needn’t stay today, Stella,” he said. “Your substitute has done remarkably well, and I don’t feel inclined to retake the picture.”
She flashed an angry glance at him, a glance at total variance with her softer attitude.
“I’ve got a contract: I suppose you know that, Mr. Knebworth?” she said shrilly.
“I’d ever so much rather play opposite Miss Mendoza,” murmured a gentle voice. It was the youthful Reggie Connolly, he of the sleek hair. “It’s not easy to play opposite Miss—I don’t even know her name. She’s so—well, she lacks the artistry, Mr. Knebworth.”
Old Jack didn’t speak. His gloomy eyes were fixed upon the youth.
===Chinese===
"你明早不必来，今天也不必留，斯特拉，"他说，"你的替角干得漂亮极了，我不打算重拍这部片子。"
她朝他甩去一道怒火腾腾的眼风，与她方才那副温软姿态判若两人。
"我可是有合同的：这点您知道吧，尼布沃斯先生？"她尖声说。
"我可是万分愿意跟门多萨小姐搭戏，"一个温和的嗓音嗫嚅道。正是那位头发油光的青年雷吉·康诺利。"跟这位小姐对戏真不容易——我连她名字都不知道。她那么——唉，她欠缺艺术性，尼布沃斯先生。"
老杰克没说话。他那双阴沉的眼睛钉在这青年身上。

===Original===
“What’s more, I don’t feel I can do myself justice with Miss Mendoza out of the cast,” said Reggie. “I really don’t! I feel most awfully, terribly nervous, and it’s difficult to express one’s personality when one’s awfully, terribly nervous. In fact,” he said recklessly, “I’m not inclined to go on with the picture unless Miss Mendoza returns.”
She shot a grateful glance at him, and then turned with a slow smile to the silent Jack.
“Would you like me to start today?”
“Not today, or any other day,” roared the old director, his eyes flaming. “As for you, you nut-fed chorus boy, if you try to let me down I’ll blacklist you at every studio in this country, and every time I meet you I’ll kick you from hell to Halifax!”
===Chinese===
"再说，门多萨小姐一退出，我觉得自己根本发挥不出来，"雷吉说，"真的发挥不出来！我觉得怕得要命、慌得要死，而一个人怕得要命、慌得要死的时候，是很难展现自己的个性的。说白了，"他豁出去道，"门多萨小姐不回来，我就不打算接着拍这部片子。"
她投去感激的一瞥，然后带着缓缓绽开的笑转向沉默的杰克。
"您要不要我今天开工？"
"今天不开，哪天也不开，"老导演吼道，两眼喷火，"至于你，吃坚果长大的合唱队小子，你要敢拆我的台，我就让这个国家每一家片厂都把你列进黑名单，而且每回见你一回，就把你从地狱一路踢到哈里法克斯！"

===Original===
He came stamping into the office, where Michael had preceded him, a raging fury of a man.
“What do you think of that?” he asked when he had calmed down. “That’s the sort of stuff they try to get past you! He’s going to quit in the middle of a picture! Did you hear him? That cissy-boy! That mouse! Say, Brixan, would you like to play opposite this girl of mine? You can’t be worse than Connolly, and it would fill in your time whilst you’re looking for the Headhunter.”
Michael shook his head slowly.
“No, thank you,” he said. “That is not my job. And as for the Headhunter”—he lit a cigarette and sent a ring of smoke to the ceiling—“I know who he is and I can lay my hands on him just when I want.”
===Chinese===
他跺着脚冲进办公室——迈克尔已先在里面——整个人是一团熊熊怒火。
"你瞧瞧这算什么？"他平静下来后问，"他们就想拿这种货色糊弄你！他要拍到一半撂挑子！听见没有？那个娘娘腔！那只耗子！哎，布里克森，愿不愿意跟我的姑娘搭个戏？你总不会比康诺利更糟，反正你找猎头者的空当，正好拿它填填时间。"
迈克尔缓缓摇头。
"不了，谢谢，"他说，"那不是我的本行。至于猎头者嘛"——他点起一支烟，朝天棚吐出一个烟圈——"我知道他是谁，想抓他的时候，随时伸手就能抓到。"

## XL “The Widow” / XL “寡妇”

===Original===
A guillotine!
Standing in the middle of the cave, its high framework lifted starkly. It was painted bloodred, and its very simplicity had a horror of its own.
Michael looked, fascinated. The basket, the bright, triangular knife suspended at the top of the frame, the tilted platform with its dangling straps, the black-painted lunette shaped to receive the head of the victim and hold it in position till the knife fell in its oiled groove. He knew the machine bolt by bolt, had seen it in operation on grey mornings before French prisons, with soldiers holding back the crowd, and a little group of officials in the centre of the cleared space. He knew the sound of it, the *clop!* as it fell, sweeping to eternity the man beneath.
“‘The Widow’!” said Longvale humorously. He touched the frame lovingly.
===Chinese===
一架断头台！
它立在洞穴正中，高高的骨架森然耸立。通体漆成血红色，而它那极度的简洁，自有一种恐怖。
迈克尔看呆了。那接头的篮筐，悬在架顶的亮闪闪的三角铡刀，斜置的断头台板连着垂挂的皮扣，漆成黑色的颈圈——塑形得正好嵌进受刑者的头颅，把它稳稳卡住，直到铡刀顺着上足油的凹槽落下。这部机器他熟到了每一颗螺栓，曾在灰蒙蒙的清晨、在法国监狱门前见过它开动：士兵拦着人群，清出的一圈空地中央立着一小群官员。他熟悉它的声音——那*咔噗*一声——落下时，把底下那人一挥送进永恒。
"'寡妇'！"朗瓦尔风趣地说。他爱抚地摸了摸机架。

===Original===
“Oh God, I’m not fit to die!” It was Penne’s agonized wail that went echoing through the hollow spaces of the cavern.
“The Widow,” murmured the old man again.
He was without a hat; his bald head shone in the light, yet there was nothing ludicrous in his appearance. His attitude toward this thing he loved was in a sense pathetic.
“Who shall be her first bridegroom?”
“Not me, not me!” squealed Penne, wriggling back against the wall, his face ashen, his mouth working convulsively. “I’m not fit to die﻿—”
Longvale walked slowly over to him, stooped and raised him to his feet.
“Courage!” he murmured. “It is the hour!”
===Chinese===
"哦上帝，我不配死啊！"那是彭尼痛苦的哀嚎，在洞窟的空穴间回荡。
"寡妇。"老人又低声念了一遍。
他没戴帽子；秃顶在灯光下泛着光，可他的样子没有半点滑稽。他对这件心爱之物的态度，某种意义上近乎凄恻。
"谁来做她的第一位新郎？"
"不是我，不是我！"彭尼尖叫着朝墙根缩去，面如死灰，嘴抽搐个不停。"我不配死啊——"
朗瓦尔慢慢走到他跟前，俯身把他扶了起来。
"拿出勇气！"他低语道，"时辰到了！"

---

===Original===
Jack Knebworth was pacing the road when the police car came flying back from Chichester.
“He’s not there, hasn’t been to the station at all,” said the driver breathlessly as he flung out of the car.
“He may have gone into Longvale’s house.”
“I’ve seen Mr. Longvale: it was he who told me that the Captain had gone into Chichester. He must have made a mistake.”
Knebworth’s jaw dropped. A great light suddenly flashed upon his mind. Longvale! There was something queer about him. Was it possible﻿—?
===Chinese===
警车从奇切斯特飞驰而回时，杰克·尼布沃斯正在路上踱步。
"他不在，压根没去过警察局。"司机跳下车，气喘吁吁地说。
"他兴许进朗维尔家去了。"
"我见过朗维尔先生了：正是他告诉我，上尉进奇切斯特去了。他一定是弄错了。"
尼布沃斯下巴耷拉下来。一道亮光霎时照进他脑中。朗维尔！那人身上有古怪。莫非——？

===Original===
He remembered now that he had been puzzled by a contradictory statement the old man had made; remembered that, not once but many times, Sampson Longvale had expressed a desire to be filmed in a favourite part of his own, one that he had presented, an episode in the life of his famous ancestor.
“We’ll go and knock him up. I’ll talk to him.”
They hammered at the door without eliciting a response.
“That’s his bedroom.” Jack Knebworth pointed to a latticed window where a light shone, and Inspector Lyle threw up a pebble with such violence that the glass was broken. Still there was no response.
“I don’t like that,” said Knebworth suddenly.
===Chinese===
他这才想起，老人有一句前后矛盾的话曾让他犯疑；也想起桑普森·朗维尔不止一次、而是屡屡表示，想把自己偏爱的一出戏拍成影片——那出戏还是他自己提供的——演的正是他那位赫赫有名的先人生平中的一幕。
"咱们去把他叫起来。我来同他谈。"
他们把门擂得山响，无人应答。
"那是他的卧室。"杰克·尼布沃斯指着一扇亮着灯的格窗。莱尔探长抛上一枚石子，力道之大，玻璃应声而碎。仍旧无人应答。
"这事不对劲。"尼布沃斯忽然说。

===Original===
“You don’t like it any better than I do,” growled the officer. “Try that window, Smith.”
“Do you want me to open it, sir?”
“Yes, without delay.”
A second later, the window of the long dining-room was prized open; and then they came upon an obstacle which could not be so readily forced.
“The shutter is steel-lined,” reported the detective. “I think I’d better try one of the upper rooms. Give me a leg up, somebody.”
===Chinese===
"你不喜欢，我也好受不了多少。"警官咆哮道，"试试那扇窗，史密斯（Smith）。"
"要我打开它吗，长官？"
"是，立刻。"
一秒钟后，长餐厅的窗被撬开了；随即他们遇上了一道没那么容易攻破的障碍。
"百叶窗夹了钢板。"侦探报告道，"我看最好从楼上的房间试试。谁搭把手，托我一把。"

===Original===
With the assistance of a fellow, he reached up and caught the sill of an open window, the very window from which Adele had looked down into the grinning face of Bhag. In another second he was in the room, and was reaching down to help up a second officer. A few minutes’ delay, and the front door was unbarred and opened.
“There’s nobody in the house, so far as I can find out,” said the officer.
“Put a light on,” ordered the inspector shortly.
They found the little vapour lamp and lit it.
“What’s that?” The detective officer pointed to the hook that still hung in the beam with the pulley beneath, and his eyes narrowed. “I can’t understand that,” he said slowly. “What was that for?”
===Chinese===
借一名同伴之托，他探身抓住一扇敞窗的窗台——正是阿黛尔当日俯视巴格那张狞笑面孔的那扇窗。转眼他已翻进屋内，又探手把第二名警员拉了上来。耽搁了几分钟，前门下了闩，开了。
"就我查看的结果，屋里一个人也没有。"警官说。
"点个灯。"探长简短地下令。
他们找到那盏小汽灯，点着了。
"那是什么？"侦探指着仍挂在梁上的钢钩和下方的滑轮，眯起了眼。"这个我看不明白。"他慢吞吞地说，"那是干什么用的？"

===Original===
Jack Knebworth uttered an exclamation.
“Here’s Brixan’s gun!” he said, and picked it up from the floor.
One glance the inspector gave, and then his eyes went back to the hook and the pulley.
“That beats me,” he said. “See if you fellows can find anything anywhere. Open every cupboard, every drawer. Sound the walls﻿—there may be secret doors; there are in all these old Tudor houses.”
The search was futile, and Inspector Lyle came back to a worried contemplation of the hook and pulley. Then one of his men came in to say that he had located the garage.
===Chinese===
杰克·尼布沃斯失声一呼。
"布里克森的枪在这儿！"他说着，把它从地板上拾起。
探长瞥了一眼，目光又回到钢钩和滑轮上。
"这下我真懵了。"他说，"你们几个看看能不能在哪儿找出点什么。每个柜子、每个抽屉都打开。敲敲墙壁——兴许有暗门；这些老都铎宅子，哪一所都少不了暗门。"
搜查一无所获，莱尔探长又回到钢钩和滑轮前，忧心忡忡地端详。这时一名手下进来报告，说他找到了车库。

===Original===
It was an unusually long building, and when it was opened, it revealed no more than the old-fashioned car which was a familiar object in that part of the country. But obviously, this was only half the accommodation. The seemingly solid whitewashed wall behind the machine hid another apartment, though it had no door, and an inspection of the outside showed a solid wall at the far end of the garage.
Jack Knebworth tapped the interior wall.
“This isn’t brickwork at all, it’s wood,” he said.
Hanging in a corner was a chain. Apparently it had no particular function, but a careful scrutiny led to the discovery that the links ran through a hole in the roughly plastered ceiling. The inspector caught the chain and pulled, and, as he did so, the “wall” opened inwards, showing the contents of the second chamber, which was a second car, so sheeted that only its radiator was visible. Knebworth pulled off the cover, and:
===Chinese===
那是一栋长得反常的屋舍，打开之后，里面不过是那辆老式汽车——那一带人人都眼熟的老古董。可这显然只占了一半的地方。车后那堵看似严整的粉墙，后面还藏着另一间屋，却没有门；从外面察看，车库远端也是一堵实墙。
杰克·尼布沃斯敲了敲内墙。
"这根本不是砖砌的，是木头。"他说。
角落里挂着一条链子。乍看没什么特别的用处，细看之下才发现，链环穿过粗糙抹灰顶棚上的一个洞。探长抓住链子一拉——"墙"应声向内开启，露出第二间暗室里的物件：又一辆汽车，罩着车衣，只露出散热器。尼布沃斯扯下车罩——

===Original===
“That’s the car.”
“What car?” asked the inspector.
“The car driven by the Headhunter,” said Knebworth quickly. “He was in that machine when Brixan tried to arrest him. I’d know it anywhere! Brixan is in the Dower House somewhere, and if he’s in the hands of the Headhunter, God help him!”
They ran back to the house, and again the hook and pulley drew them as a magnet. Suddenly the police officer bent down and jerked back the carpet. The trapdoor beneath the pulley was plainly visible. Pulling it open, he knelt down and gazed through. Knebworth saw his face grow haggard.
“Too late, too late!” he muttered.
===Chinese===
"就是这辆车。"
"哪辆车？"探长问。
"猎头者开的那辆。"尼布沃斯急道，"布里克森动手抓他时，他坐的就是这辆车。它化成灰我也认得！布里克森就在寡妇居里的什么地方，他要是在猎头者手里——上帝保佑他吧！"
他们跑回宅子，那钢钩和滑轮又像磁石般把他们吸了过去。那警官忽然俯身，一把掀开地毯。滑轮正下方的活动门赫然在目。他拉开活门，跪下去往里张望。尼布沃斯看见他的脸一下子憔悴了。
"迟了，迟了！"他喃喃道。

## XV Two from the Yard / XV 场里来的两位

===Original===
Chichester is not famous for its restaurants, but the dining-room of a little hotel, where three people foregathered that afternoon, had the advantage of privacy.
When Mike Brixan got back to his hotel he found two men waiting to see him, and, after a brief introduction, he took them upstairs to his sitting-room.
“I’m glad you’ve come,” he said, when the inspector had closed the door behind him. “The fact is that sheerly criminal work is a novelty to me, and I’m afraid that I’m going to make it a mystery to you,” he smiled. “At the moment I’m not prepared to give expression to all my suspicions.”
Detective Inspector Lyle, the chief of the two, laughed.
“We have been placed entirely under your orders, Captain Brixan,” he said, “and neither of us are very curious. The information you asked for, Sergeant Walters has brought.” He indicated his tall companion.
===Chinese===
奇切斯特的餐馆没什么名气，不过那天下午三个人聚首的一家小旅店的饭厅，倒有清静无人扰的好处。
迈克·布里克森回到旅馆，发现两个人在等他，简短引见之后，他把他们让到楼上的客厅。
"你们来了我很高兴，"督察在身后关上门后，他说，"实情是，纯粹的刑案活计对我是个新行当，恐怕到你们这儿，我也得把它办成一桩谜案，"他笑道，"眼下我还不打算把全部怀疑都摊出来。"
两人中领头的侦探督察莱尔（Detective Inspector Lyle）笑了。
"我们已完全听候您的差遣，布里克森上尉，"他说，"我们俩都不多问。您要的情报，沃尔特斯警士（Sergeant Walters）带来了。"他指了指高个子的同伴。

===Original===
“Which information—about Penne? Is he known to the police?” asked Michael, interested.
Sergeant Walters nodded.
“He was convicted and fined a few years ago for assaulting a servant—a woman. Apparently he took a whip to the girl, and he very narrowly escaped going to prison. That was the first time our attention was attracted to him, and we made inquiries both in London and in the Malay States and found out all about him. He’s a very rich man, and, being a distant cousin of the late baronet, you may say he fluked his title. In Borneo he lived upcountry, practically in the bush, for fifteen or twenty years, and the stories we have about him aren’t particularly savoury. There are a few of them which you might read at your leisure, Mr. Brixan—they’re in the record.”
Michael nodded.
===Chinese===
"哪方面的情报——关于彭尼的？警方有他的底吗？"迈克尔来了兴致，问道。
沃尔特斯警士点了点头。
"几年前他因殴打一名仆人——一个女人——被定罪罚款。据说他拿鞭子抽了那姑娘，差一点就进了监狱。那是他第一次引起我们注意，我们在伦敦和马来各邦都做了查访，把他的底细摸了个透。他是个非常有钱的人，又因为是已故从男爵的远房堂亲，这个头衔可以说纯属侥幸到手。在婆罗洲，他在内地住了十五二十年，几乎常年待在莽林里，我们掌握的有关他的传闻都不怎么体面。有几件您有空可以看看，布里克森先生——都录在案卷里。"
迈克尔点了点头。

===Original===
“Is anything known of an educated orangutan which is his companion?”
To his surprise, the officer answered:
“Bhag? Oh yes, we know all about him. He was captured when he was quite a baby by Penne, and was brought up in captivity. It has been rather difficult to trace the man, because he never returns to England by the usual steamship line, so that it’s almost impossible to have a tag on him. He has a yacht, a fine seagoing boat, the *Kipi*, which is practically officered and manned by Papuans. What comes and goes with him I don’t know. There was a complaint came through to us that the last time he was abroad Penne nearly lost his life as the result of some quarrel he had with a local tribesman. Now, Mr. Brixan, what would you like us to do?”
Michael’s instructions were few and brief. That evening, when Adele walked home to her lodgings, she was conscious that a man was following her, and after her previous night’s adventure this fact would have played havoc with her nerves but for the note she found waiting when she got indoors. It was from Michael.
===Chinese===
"关于他养的那只调教过的红毛猩猩，有什么情况吗？"
令他意外的是，那警官答道：
"巴格？哦有，它的底细我们全知道。它还是幼崽时就被彭尼捕获，是圈养长大的。这个人一直很难盯，因为他回英国从不坐寻常的轮船航线，想给他盯梢几乎不可能。他有一艘游艇，一条上好的远洋船，叫'基皮号'（Kipi），船员几乎全由巴布亚人充任。他来来往往带了些什么，我不清楚。曾有一桩投诉转到我们这里：上次他出国，因跟当地一个部族人争执，差点送了命。好了，布里克森先生，您要我们做什么？"
迈克尔的指示寥寥数语。当天傍晚，阿黛尔走回住处时，察觉有个男人跟在身后；若不是进门时发现等着她的一张字条，头天夜里的遭遇早让这件事把她的神经搅个稀碎了。字条是迈克尔写的。

===Original===

> “Would you mind if I put a Scotland Yard man to watch you, to see that you do not get into mischief! I don’t think there’s any danger that you will, but I shall feel ever so much easier in my mind if you will endure this annoyance.”

She read the letter and her brows knit. So she was being shadowed! It was an uncomfortable experience, and yet she could not very well object, could not indeed feel anything but a sense of warm gratitude toward this ubiquitous and pushful young man, who seemed determined not to let her out of his sight.
===Chinese===

> "我派了一名苏格兰场的人跟着您，看着您别出乱子，您不会见怪吧！我想您不会有危险，可只要您肯受这点叨扰，我心里就踏实多了。"

她读完信，眉头拧了起来。原来她在被人盯梢！这滋味不好受，可她又不好反对，心里对这个无处不在、说干就干的年轻人，只剩下一股暖乎乎的感激——他似乎打定了主意，不肯让她离开自己的视线。

## XXXV What Happened to Adele / XXXV 阿黛尔的遭遇

===Original===
There were moments when Adele Leamington had doubts as to her fitness for the profession she had entered; and never were those periods of doubt more poignant than when she tried to fix her mind upon the written directions of the scenario. She blamed Michael, and was immediately repentant. She blamed herself more freely; and at last she gave up the struggle, rolled up the manuscript book, and, putting an elastic band about it, thrust it under her pillow and prepared for bed. She had rid herself of skirt and blouse when the summons came.
“From Mr. Knebworth?” she said in surprise. “At this time of night?”
“Yes, miss. He’s going to make a big alteration tomorrow and he wants to see you at once. He has sent his car. Miss Mendoza is coming into the cast.”
“Oh!” she said faintly.
Then she had been a failure, after all, and had lived in a fool’s paradise for these past days.
===Chinese===
阿黛尔·利明顿时时会怀疑自己是否入错了行；而这类疑心最锥心的时刻，莫过于她想把心神集中在剧本的书面指示上的时候。她怨迈克尔，随即又懊悔；她更毫不客气地怨自己；最后她索性放弃挣扎，把手稿卷起来，箍上橡皮筋，塞到枕头底下，预备就寝。裙子衬衫刚褪下，传话就来了。
"尼布沃斯先生派来的？"她讶然道，"夜里这个钟点？"
"是，小姐。他明天要大改一场戏，想立刻见您。车是他派的。门多萨小姐要进组了。"
"哦！"她有气无力地应了一声。
这么说，她终究还是失败了，这些天一直活在痴人的天堂里。

===Original===
“I’ll come at once,” she said.
Her fingers trembled as she fastened her dress, and she hated herself for such a display of weakness. Perhaps Stella was not coming into the cast in her old part; perhaps some new character had been written in; perhaps it was not for “Roselle” at all that she had been re-engaged. These and other speculations rioted in her mind; and she was in the passage and the door was opened when she remembered that Jack Knebworth would want the manuscript. She ran upstairs, and, by an aberration of memory, forgot entirely where the script had been left. At last, in despair, she went down to the landlady.
“I have left some manuscripts which are rather important. Would you bring them up to Mr. Knebworth’s house when you find them? They’re in a little brown jacket﻿—” She described the appearance as well as she could.
It was Stella Mendoza’s car; she recognized the machine with a pang. So Jack and she were reconciled!
===Chinese===
"我这就去。"她说。
系裙扣时她指尖发颤，她恨自己这般露出软相。也许斯特拉进组演的不是原先那个角色；也许添写了什么新人物；也许重新续聘她，压根不是为了《罗塞尔》。这一个个念头连同别的猜测在她心里翻江倒海；人都走到过道、门也开了，她才想起杰克·尼布沃斯多半要用那部手稿。她跑上楼去，偏偏一时记性失灵，怎么也想不起稿子搁在了哪儿。最后没了指望，只得下楼找房东太太。
"我落下了一些手稿，还挺要紧的。您要是找着了，能送到尼布沃斯先生家去吗？装在一只棕色小夹子里——"她尽力把样子描述了一遍。
那是斯特拉·门多萨的车；她认出那辆车，心里一沉。这么说，杰克同她和好了！

===Original===
In a minute she was inside the machine, the door closed behind her, and was sitting by the driver, who did not speak.
“Is Mr. Brixan with Mr. Knebworth?” she asked.
He did not reply. She thought he had not heard her, until he turned with a wide sweep and set the car going in the opposite direction.
“This is not the way to Mr. Knebworth’s,” she said in alarm. “Don’t you know the way?”
Still he made no reply. The machine gathered speed, passed down a long, dark street, and turned into a country lane.
“Stop the car at once!” she said, terrified, and put her hand on the handle of the door.
===Chinese===
转眼她已坐进车里，车门在身后合上，她坐在开车人身旁，那人一言不发。
"布里克森先生也同尼布沃斯先生在一起吗？"她问。
他不答话。她只当他没听见，直到他大回了一记方向盘，让车朝相反的方向开去。
"这不是去尼布沃斯先生家的路。"她惊慌地说，"你不认得路吗？"
他仍不作声。车越开越快，驶过一条长长的黑黢黢的街，拐进了一条乡间小路。
"马上停车！"她吓坏了，一面说，一面把手搭上车门的把手。

===Original===
Instantly her arm was gripped.
“My dear, you’re going to injure your pretty little body, and probably spoil your beautiful face, if you attempt to get out while the car is in motion,” he said.
“Sir Gregory!” she gasped.
“Now don’t make a fuss,” said Gregory. There was no mistaking the elation in his voice. “You’re coming up to have a little bit of supper with me. I’ve asked you often enough, and now you’re going willy-nilly! Stella’s there, so there’s nothing to be afraid of.”
She held down her fears with an effort.
“Sir Gregory, you will take me back at once to my lodgings,” she said. “This is disgraceful of you!”
===Chinese===
她的胳膊立刻被一把攥住。
"亲爱的，车还在走你就想下去，会伤了你这漂亮的小身段，多半还要毁了这张美丽的脸。"他说。
"格雷戈里爵士！"她倒抽一口气。
"别大惊小怪。"格雷戈里说。他话音里的得意错认不了。"你是来陪我吃一顿小小夜宵的。我请你请得够多了，这回你是愿不愿意都得去！斯特拉在那儿，没什么好怕的。"
她费了好大的劲才把恐惧压住。
"格雷戈里爵士，请您立刻送我回住处。"她说，"您这样做太不光彩了！"

===Original===
He chuckled loudly.
“Nothing’s going to happen to you; nobody’s going to hurt you, and you’ll be delivered safe and sound; but you’re going to have supper with me first, little darling. And if you make a fuss, I’m going to turn the car into the first tree I see and smash us all up!”
He was drunk﻿—drunk not only with wine, but with the lust of power. Gregory had achieved his object, and would stop at nothing now.
Was Stella there? She did not believe him. And yet it might be true. She grasped at the straw which Stella’s presence offered.
“Here we are,” grunted Gregory, as he stopped the car before the Towers door and slipped out on to the gravel.
===Chinese===
他呵呵地笑出声来。
"你什么事儿也不会有；没人会伤你，到时候把你毫发无损地送回去；不过得先陪我吃这顿夜宵，小宝贝儿。你要是不识抬举，我就把车一头撞上我瞧见的第一棵树，咱们同归于尽！"
他醉了——不光是酒醉，更是权欲的醉。格雷戈里已如愿以偿，如今再没有任何事能让他却步。
斯特拉真在那儿吗？她不信。可这也许是真的。她抓住了斯特拉在场这根救命稻草。
"到了。"格雷戈里咕哝一声，把车停在塔楼门前，溜下车，踏上砾石地。

===Original===
Before she realized what he was doing, he had lifted her in his arms, though she struggled desperately.
“If you scream I’ll kiss you,” growled his voice in her ear, and she lay passive.
The door opened instantly. She looked down at the servant standing stolidly in the hall, as Gregory carried her up the wide stairway, and wondered what help might come from him. Presently Penne set her down on her feet and, opening a door, thrust her in.
“Here’s your friend, Stella,” he said. “Say the good word for me! Knock some sense into her head if you can. I’ll come back in ten minutes, and we’ll have the grandest little wedding supper that any bridegroom ever had.”
The door was banged and locked upon her before she realized there was another woman in the room. It was Stella. Her heart rose at the sight of the girl’s white face.
“Oh, Miss Mendoza,” she said breathlessly, “thank God you’re here!”
===Chinese===
她还没弄清他要做什么，已被他拦腰抱起，任凭她拼命挣扎也无济于事。
"你敢叫唤，我就吻你。"他的声音在她耳边低吼，她只得软软地伏着不动。
门应声而开。格雷戈里抱着她走上宽阔的楼梯时，她低头望着呆立在门厅里的仆役，不知道能指望他帮上什么忙。不一会儿，彭尼把她放下，推开一扇门，把她塞了进去。
"你的朋友来了，斯特拉。"他说，"替我说句好话吧！能把她那脑袋敲出点道理来就敲。我十分钟后回来，咱们要吃一顿任何新郎都不曾享用过的、顶顶盛大的小小婚宴。"
门砰地关上，落了锁，她这才发觉屋里还有一个女人。是斯特拉。一见那张苍白的脸，她的心才提了起来又落了下去。
"哦，门多萨小姐，"她上气不接下气地说，"谢天谢地你在这儿！"
